2026-11-09: Test iTermReflection type-string parsing
directly

The Objective-C runtime does not parse quoted class names in
method type encodings, so registering a method with one
cannot exercise the parser. Call the type-string parser
directly instead.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-11-09: Add tests for class name extraction in
iTermReflection

Covers quoted, single-character, empty, and unquoted object
type encodings, guarding the off-by-one fixed in PR 816.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-11-09: Fix external attribute index equality

isEqualToExternalAttributeIndex: compared each of our
attributes against itself, so indexes with different
attributes compared equal. Compare against the other index's
attribute instead, using the nil-safe comparison since a
column may be present on only one side. Comparing a
dual-mode color attribute against nil would otherwise crash.

Uniform indexes keep their attribute outside the attributes
dictionary, so they were also compared as if empty. Give
them their own equality.

Based on a fix by Kim Dong Han in PR 815.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-11-09: Remove dead dictionary conversion code for grid
coords

VT100GridCoordFromDictionary lost its only caller in 2022,
when cursor restoration from the "Cursor Coord" arrangement
key was removed, and VT100GridCoordRangeFromDictionary never
had a caller. An analyzer cleanup later inverted its nil
checks so it could never succeed, which went unnoticed
because nothing ran it. Delete these helpers and stop
writing the unread key into arrangements. Reported in PR
814.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-11-09: Use up the JSON promotion only when it is
actually shown

promoteIfJSON recorded the promotion as shown before calling
back, but the session shows it only when the current
selection offers a JSON replacement. Copying JSON that
didn't start at column 0, a box selection, a range copied
from the context menu, or a selection that changed during
the parse used up the one-time promotion without ever
showing it. The callback now reports whether it showed the
promotion and the default is set only then.

2026-11-09: Fix tiebreaks when sorting pointer action rows

The gesture comparator compared the modifier field with
intValue, but that field is a string of modifier characters
like "cso", so it always compared equal. Compare the
modifier masks instead. Also fix the button comparator's
duplicated nil check so a key without parts sorts before one
with parts.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-11-09: Log the needsDisplay value being set, not the
previous one (#811)

The setter interpolated needsDisplay, which calls the getter
and returns super.needsDisplay: still the old value. Every
setNeedsDisplay: line therefore reported the flag's state
before the write.  2026-11-09: Compare against the other key
when sorting gesture rows (#818)

otherParts was built from self, so every gesture comparison
reported NSOrderedSame and gesture rows in Pointer
preferences were never ordered relative to one another.
2026-11-09: Clear the JSON pretty printer's busy flag on
every path (#817)

busy was only cleared when the selection parsed as JSON, so
the first selection that began with a brace but was not JSON
latched it on for the life of the process and the promotion
never ran again.  2026-11-09: Keep the last character of a
class name from a type string (#816)

The name spans index 1 through closeQuote.location - 1, so
its length is closeQuote.location - 1. Every parsed class
name lost its last character and NSClassFromString then
returned nil.  2026-11-09: Extract the response body before
stripping it from the buffer (#813)

start and end index the string unparsed held before the
assignment, and the assignment deletes exactly the span they
point into, so subscripting the shorter result with them
traps. Read the body out first.  2026-11-09: Stop
it_containsSubarray: from reading past the end (#812)

The search range ran to the end of the array, so a
first-element match near the end let the inner loop index
self[idx + j] out of bounds. Limit the range so a match
always leaves room for the whole subarray.  2026-11-09: Let
a user default ask iterm2.com for a test appcast

Testing that a build can update itself used to mean editing
the version in its Info.plist. Sparkle 2's installer refuses
to talk to an app whose signature is broken, so that no
longer works. With NoSyncSoftwareUpdateTestFeed set to a
token, the appcast URL gets test=TOKEN appended and the
server can answer with a staged appcast instead. The appcast
still comes from iterm2.com, and Sparkle still checks every
update's EdDSA signature, team ID, and version.

2026-11-09: Use the renamed verifiedLocalDirectory for the
known local working directory

415378116 added a use of polledLocalDirectory, which
9c83fba6f renamed.

2026-11-09: Release note for new sessions using the outer
shell's directory in a nested shell

2026-11-09: Poll the shell the user is in, and keep a
verified push of its directory

New sessions and Claude Code workgroup panes take their
directory from verifiedLocalDirectory, which could describe
a different shell from the one the user had just cd'ed in.

Polls now read the foreground shell instead of the session's
root process. Starting from the terminal's foreground
process group, the leader is used if it is a shell at its
prompt (shell name, exec'ed, no script or -c in its
arguments). Otherwise the shell is the nearest ancestor
outside the group, since only a shell doing job control
gives a job its own group, so subshells, scripts, and
commands that change their own directory don't count. If the
leader exited, the walk starts from another member. A job
that relays a pty of its own (sudo -s with use_pty, poetry
shell, script, expect) is followed onto that pty when an
interactive shell is there; otherwise the shell that started
the job is used. A poll at a prompt reads only the leader;
anything else reads the process table once, into an array of
structs.

A push whose OSC 7 machineID matches ours counts as verified
and sets verifiedLocalDirectory directly. Its symlinks are
resolved out of process (pidinfo, F_GETPATH) so later polls,
which give resolved paths, can be compared with it: a poll
replaces it only if it names neither the push nor its
resolution. Requests are stamped so polls from before a
strong push can't override it.

Reading process arguments now sizes the buffer to the
argument area instead of allocating KERN_ARGMAX for every
read.

2026-11-09: Release note for stale directory in new sessions
after cd

2026-11-09: Refresh the local directory new sessions use
when the shell reports a cd

New local sessions (tabs, splits, workgroup peers) start in
the tracker's polled local directory, which since issue
12616 ignores pushed directories so a hostless OSC 7 from a
remote fish can't pass for local. Nothing refreshed it on a
push, so after `cd foo; claude` the Claude Code workgroup's
code review and diff peers could start in the old directory.

On every strong push, refresh it: use the pushed directory
when its OSC 7 machineID token matched ours, and otherwise
poll the local shell. The verdict is threaded from the OSC 7
parser per report, because the remote host on the line is
inherited by hostless reports and proves nothing.
Directories fetched by polling are verified too.

Rename polledLocalDirectory to verifiedLocalDirectory to
match.

2026-11-08: Run each AI chat tool request at most once

iTerm2 Buddy's chat history snapshot included persisted
auto-run tool requests but hid their responses, so the phone
showed already-run requests as pending Allow/Deny prompts.
Approving one ran the tool a second time and stored a second
tool_result for the same tool_use, which Anthropic rejects
on the next turn.

- Drop answered .classic requests from the history sent to
the phone.  - ChatClient.requestIsSettled (running or
already answered) now gates
  execution, both response publishers, and the Mac and phone
  decision handlers, so a stale Allow or Deny is a no-op and
  changes no permissions.
- Cancel a session's running remote command when its process
ends or
  the session terminates, so the tool call gets a response
  instead of wedging the turn.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-11-07: Keep tmux per-tab settings and tab groups across
hiding and session switches

Each save of a tmux session's @per_tab_settings was rebuilt
from open tabs only, so a hidden window lost its custom tab
title the next time anything saved. Saves now merge open
tabs into the saved settings, as tab groups already did. A
closed window's title is forgotten when tmux reports the
close, and titles of windows that no longer exist are pruned
on attach.

Switching the client to another session could write the old
session's titles and groups into the new session's options
before they were read, losing the new session's for good.
The old session's state is now cleared on a switch, and
neither option is saved until the new session's has been
read, nor while tabs close.

A window that leaves the session and returns with the same
ID (move-window, link-window) is no longer treated as
closed, so its title and group changes are saved again.

Ported from the hardening in PR 803.

2026-11-06: Render Markdown in AI chat and imgcat with
SwiftyMarkdown's new block parser

Bump SwiftyMarkdown to b999a82, whose CommonMark/GFM block
parser handles paragraphs spanning lines, blocks inside list
items, nesting by indentation, start numbers, nested quotes,
and task lists, and rebuild SwiftyMarkdown.framework from
it.

AI chat drops sanitizeMarkdownLines, which only worked
around the old parser, and keeps newlines as line breaks.

The imgcat porthole's MarkdownPreprocessor shrinks to inline
rewrites (images, HTML, entities, escapes, footnotes)
applied through SwiftyMarkdown's inlineTransform, and
reflows paragraphs. The styler uses the list level and quote
depth attributes: content in a list item lines up with its
text, quotes nest, code blocks get their own boxes with a
visible gap between adjacent blocks, and a table in a quote
is inset to the quote's text (TextKit can't draw a table
nested in another text block).

Copying rendered Markdown turns its U+2028 line breaks into
newlines, except in code. Orchestration mentions are
protected using the exact code lines SwiftyMarkdown reports.

2026-11-06: Log exceptions that AppKit crashes on through
_crashOnException:

An exception raised while a view draws ends in
+[NSApplication _crashOnException:], which bypasses both the
uncaught exception handler and -reportException:, so the
crash log said nothing about the exception.  Replace that
class method's implementation to log the exception and its
backtrace first, then call the original.

2026-11-06: Keep the code review model and git base in a
tmux workgroup's record

The instance record on the tmux server now holds the code
review model (new optional key "crm", no version bump), so a
client that attaches later adopts the current pick. The git
base and model are rewritten when the user changes them;
before, the record kept the git base from entry.

A rewrite reads the record back, applies only its own
change, and writes it with a compare-and-set guarded on the
value read, so it neither reverts another client's change
nor brings back a record another client removed. Top-level
keys this build doesn't know survive the rewrite, so a field
a later build adds the same way isn't stripped. Values
restored from the record or window restoration are not
written back.

2026-11-06: Improve tmux dashboard session selection and
loading state

Select the attached session when the dashboard opens. The
sessions table disallows an empty selection, so NSTableView
picked row 0 on every reload; only a selection the user
clicks is now preserved, and otherwise the attached session
is selected and scrolled into view.

Show a spinner over the windows list while list-windows is
in flight and the list is empty.

Show a grayed checkmark next to sessions that other
connections to the same tmux server are attached to.

2026-11-06: Save only a tmux session's own tabs in its
per-tab settings

Saving one tmux session's per-tab settings, such as custom
tab titles, included every tmux tab in every window, keyed
by tmux window id. Window ids repeat across sessions, so
another session's @1 title was saved as this session's, and
reattaching this session gave its @1 the other session's
title. This happened even with the sessions in separate
windows.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-11-06: Close and detach only the tmux sessions being
closed

Closing tmux tabs prompts to Hide, Detach, or Kill. When the
window held tabs from more than one tmux session, the prompt
counted every session's windows, so closing one session's
last tab asked about all of them. Detach acted on the
selected tab's session instead of the closing one, so
closing a window detached only one session, and nothing
detached if a regular tab was selected. Now the prompt
counts only the tabs being closed, and Detach detaches each
attached session that has a tab being closed.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-11-06: Don't let one tmux session's window change
select it over another session's tab

When a native window holds tabs from more than one tmux
session, tmux reporting a new current window in one session
selected that session's tab even while the user was working
in another session's tab. Keystrokes then went to the wrong
session, possibly on another host. Now a current-window
change leaves the selection alone when the selected tab
belongs to a different tmux session.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-11-06: Keep animated titles moving in background tabs

A hidden session batches its side effects for up to a
second, so a title that changes every 100 ms (a spinner, for
example) advanced only about once a second in a tab that
wasn't selected.

Title changes in a hidden session now request a flush
deferred by the foreground amount (1/60 s) that escapes the
background period, rate limited to at most one every 0.1 s.
Requests made while one is owed ride that flush. The flush
drains the single side-effect FIFO in order, so nothing is
reordered. Visible sessions are unaffected since their
period is already shorter than the limit.

The expedite is skipped when the profile disallows title
setting and when the title matches the last one the program
set. That cache is cleared on a title pop and on reset,
since either can change the displayed title behind its back.

Based on the diagnosis in PR 773 by korboybeats.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-11-06: Give clippings unique IDs and add it2 session
delete-clipping

it2 session add-clipping now prints the new clipping's ID,
and it2 session delete-clipping removes a live clipping by
that ID. The code review system prompt tells agents to keep
the IDs and delete clippings for findings they withdraw, and
the code review settings allow the new command in auto mode.

Clippings persist their ID in the arrangement; ones restored
from older arrangements get a fresh ID. The 3.7.7 default
prompt's hash is recorded so a stored copy of it is offered
the new default.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-11-06: Remember tab groups of tmux integration tabs
across detach and reattach

Tab group membership, name, color, and collapsed state are
saved in a new @tab_groups session option and restored as
tabs open on attach. A separate option is used instead of
extending @per_tab_settings because an older iTerm2 attached
to the same server rewrites that option wholesale and would
erase the groups.

Entries for windows without a tab (hidden, buried, parked,
or still opening) are kept and their group definitions
refreshed from live members; entries are removed when tmux
closes the window or the tab leaves its group, and pruned
against the initial window list on attach. Each group is
confined to one window using the saved affinities, in case
an older iTerm2 moved a member into another window.
Collapsed groups stay collapsed on attach; if the active tab
lands in one, the selection moves out of it instead.

A failed open of the last initial window now still completes
the initial attach, so saving is not disabled for the rest
of the connection.

Issue 13069

2026-11-06: Add a model picker to the code review toolbar

A new Code Review Model toolbar item lets the user pick the
model a code review session runs with. It is an editable
combo box offering default, fable, opus, sonnet, and haiku
plus previously typed names.  The pick is per workgroup
instance, applies to the next review, and is saved with
window restoration. It reaches the command through a new
\(codeReviewModelFlag) template variable, which is empty for
default so claude keeps choosing its own model.

A star button beside the picker sets the model new
workgroups start with (WorkgroupCodeReviewDefaultModel).
Clicking it commits any pending edit first.

The preset review command and toolbar include the picker,
and a one-time backfill adds both to existing Claude Code
workgroups. The git base selector's combo box logic moves
into a shared WorkgroupRecentsComboBoxItem base class used
by both pickers.

2026-11-06: Use iTerm2's bundled it2ssh instead of a stale
installer alias

Install Shell Integration & Utilities aliased it2ssh to a
copy in .iterm2, which hid the newer it2ssh iTerm2 adds to
PATH. The shell integration scripts now remove that alias at
the first prompt when the first it2ssh on PATH is the one in
iTerm2's app bundle, and leave it alone otherwise (for
example on a remote host where the alias is the only way to
reach it2ssh).

Bumps the shell-integration submodule and the bash, fish,
tcsh, and zsh script versions.

2026-11-06: Fix soft text in transparent windows when
underlines are visible (13194)

The multi-pass underline composite used compositeSourceOver,
whose alpha blend is max(Sa, Da). In a transparent window
that leaves anti-aliased glyph edges with too little alpha,
so the backdrop bleeds through every glyph whenever any
underline is on screen. Use premultipliedCompositing so the
result matches drawing text directly.

2026-11-05: Fix Kitty DnD drops into Yazi and restore
registrations on reattach (13176)

Send the hover MIME list on every t=m, as kitty does. The
spec only requires it on the first move, but Yazi 26.9.1
re-reads it from each move and rejects the drop when it is
absent, so a Finder drag bounced back. The list is recorded
at drag entry even when the program is not yet accepting,
cleared when the drag ends, and omitted when empty.

Save the program's drag-and-drop registrations in the
session arrangement and restore them when reattaching to a
still-running program after relaunch, since programs
register only once at startup.

Add debug logging to the accept-drop path.

2026-11-05: Recognize AI chat code fences with any language
name

sanitizeMarkdownLines only recognized fences whose info
string was lowercase letters, so ```c++ or ```Swift rendered
as text and the closing fence opened a block that swallowed
the rest of the reply. Accept any info string without a
backtick and drop the ones SwiftyMarkdown can't parse.

and output the SwiftyMarkdown block parser rewrite should

2026-11-05: Make Markdown shown with imgcat look like a
rendered document

SwiftyMarkdown understands a narrow dialect, so a
preprocessor rewrites CommonMark and GFM into it (reflowed
paragraphs, hard breaks, list numbering and nesting by text
column, task lists, fences, front matter, tables, entities,
bare URLs, footnotes, HTML inline tags) and a styler
post-processes the result (spacing, hanging list indents,
code and quote blocks, rules, tables, link color).

Images in files on this Mac appear as fixed-size
placeholders and are read only when clicked, in a popover,
so the layout can't reveal whether a local file exists.
Remote images are never fetched.

Also fixes restored portholes resolving relative paths
against the parent of their saved directory.

2026-11-05: Hide an OSC 9;4 progress bar after 15 seconds
without an update

A program that reports progress with OSC 9;4 and exits
without sending 9;4;0 left its bar up forever. Composer does
this: it reports 100% and never clears it. Like Ghostty and
kitty, take the bar down once it has gone without an update
for a while. The new progressReportTimeout advanced setting
controls how long, defaulting to 15 seconds; 0 keeps the old
behavior.

Each report moves the fire date of one timer rather than
scheduling a new one. When it fires, the bar comes down as
if the program had sent 9;4;0, but only if no newer report,
reset, or OSC 7501 record has changed it since.

A restored session brings its bar back with nobody left to
report on it, so restoring starts the same timer.

Issue 13166.

2026-11-05: Test side effects against the mutation thread's
state

SideEffectSnapshotTests covers the preceding fixes for side
effects that read screen state: command output capture and
its cap, the Stop Scrolling and Fold triggers, selections in
scroll regions, status bar labels, logging, the auto
composer, the last command mark cache, prompt notifications
and the consumer registry. ToolCodeciergeSessionTests covers
the Codecierge session's capture, stopping and stale
replies. The mutable state exposes its queue for tests.

2026-11-05: Tell the AI an aborted command had no exit
status

An aborted command, such as one interrupted at the prompt or
rejected by the shell, decoded as exit status 0, so AI Chat
and Codecierge described it as a success with empty output.
The exit code is optional, nil for an abort, and both
consumers say the command was aborted instead. An abort is
still encoded with an exit code of 0, plus a flag marking
it, so older builds and companion apps can read saved and
sent commands.

2026-11-05: Capture command output only for sessions
something consumes it from

Copying up to 2,000 lines for every command that ends is
wasted in the sessions no AI chat or Codecierge goal is
watching. Consumers register with iTermCommandOutput under
the session's guid or stable ID, which the configuration now
carries, and the mutation thread asks the registry when a
command ends, so a consumer that came or went since the last
sync counts without a hand-off between threads. A consumer
that registered after a command ended finds no output and
skips it.

AI Chat registers while streaming and follows the chat's
session link, which the chat list model now announces
whenever it changes, including when turning on orchestration
or closing the session clears it; repairing a chat's links
writes the final values directly. Codecierge registers while
a goal is running.

2026-11-05: Keep the main thread's last command mark cache
current between syncs

The main thread's copy of the cache was refreshed only by a
sync, so a side effect drained before the next sync, such as
the Captured Output tool reloading after a Capture Output
trigger or ClearCapturedOutput, saw the previous command's
mark. A change to the mutation thread's cache now queues an
update carrying a generation, so one queued before a sync
does not replace the newer value the sync copied.

A mark leaving the interval tree, by an abort or by clearing
the buffer, is forgotten on both threads. Each thread fills
its cache lazily on its own, so the main thread's can hold a
mark the mutation thread's never did; the shared removal
hook compares against each. The two abort paths call that
hook now instead of removing by hand, which also keys their
mark cache removal the way marks are added.

2026-11-05: Capture a finished command's output on the
mutation thread

The command-exit notification pointed its observers at the
main thread's copy of the screen, which could be missing the
output's last lines or have scrolled its start off by the
time they read it, and the output range could start on the
wrong line after a command continuation line.  The mutation
thread now copies the output's last 2,000 lines into an
iTermCommandOutput when the command ends, and the
notification carries that along with how many earlier lines
were left out, counting any that had already scrolled out of
the buffer. Only lines holding output count toward the cap,
and the scan past trailing blank lines stops at the cap.  AI
Chat and Codecierge decode the notification through
CommandExitNotification, whose omitted-lines note is
localized with a plural.

2026-11-05: Report prompt details from the mutation thread's
state

The prompt-ended side effect read the command range, the
cursor, the working directory and the terminal's encoding
from the main thread's copy of the screen, which may be from
before the prompt. The mutation thread passes them, and the
screen exposes the current command's text for the session to
fall back on. A prompt taller than four lines keeps its last
four lines rather than a range that started at line four,
and GetPrompt subtracts the scrollback overflow before
looking up the prompt line's directory.

2026-11-05: Join the threads to place the auto composer and
to fold a block

Revealing the composer uses the last prompt mark and the
scrollback size, and folding a block finds the block and
reads its lines. Both can have changed since the last sync,
so these side effects join the threads rather than pausing
token execution and reading the main thread's copy.

2026-11-05: Log session text with the encoding in effect
when it arrived

The side effect that logs appended text encoded it with the
main thread's copy of the terminal encoding, which could be
stale just after a program changed it. The mutation thread
passes the encoding, and whether the text is plain, along
with the text.

2026-11-05: Record the cursor line for a status bar label on
the mutation thread

screenSetLabel:forKey: took the status-changed line from the
main thread's copy of the screen, which may not have the
cursor's line yet.  The mutation thread passes the cursor's
absolute line.

2026-11-05: Move a selection in a scrolled region using the
mutation thread's lines

The absolute line of the region's top row came from the main
thread's scrollback count, which can be behind the mutation
thread's when the side effect runs, so the selection could
move to the wrong place. The mutation thread passes the
absolute top.

2026-11-05: Fold from the Fold trigger on the mutation
thread

The trigger folded through the delegate, which read the
placeholder line from the main thread's copy of the screen;
lines that had arrived since the last sync were not there
yet, so the fold sometimes did nothing. The range is now
fixed on the mutation thread and the fold runs there too,
after the side effect that leaves copy mode (as folding from
the main thread does, since copy mode and the selection are
placed against line numbers a fold shifts) and after the
line's other triggers, which act on those line numbers as
well. Token execution stays paused until the fold has run,
so no later token can change the lines first.  As before,
the trigger declines to fold while an app owns the alternate
screen. The screen's main-thread fold method had no callers
left and is removed.

2026-11-05: Scroll to a Stop Scrolling trigger's line once
the main thread has it

The trigger's side effect ran against the main thread's copy
of the screen, which may not have had the matching line yet,
so it sometimes did nothing. The mutation thread now passes
the absolute line. The session scrolls to it if its copy has
the line, otherwise right after the next sync, and marks the
view as user-scrolled only when it scrolls.

2026-11-05: Fix Codecierge stopping the wrong session and
keeping stale replies

Stop Codecierge in the usage warning stopped whichever
session the toolbelt was showing rather than the one that
asked, and a remembered Stop ended every later goal silently
once it reached the command count; only Keep Going can be
remembered now. Stopping a goal releases its conversation,
pending completion and queued requests. A reply to a goal
that has since stopped is dropped instead of being appended
to the next goal's transcript with its conversation
installed over the new one. The unused History struct is
gone.

2026-11-05: Let tests give the screen harness their own
delegate

TerminalTestHarness takes a SpyingScreenDelegate, or a
subclass, so a test can observe or answer delegate calls the
spy doesn't, such as syncing a real screen from screenSync:
the way PTYSession does.

2026-11-05: Keep trigger parameter classifications in an LRU
dictionary

NSCache could drop an entry at any time, so looking a
parameter string up twice did not always give the same
object. The cache is a bounded LRUDictionary under a lock. A
miss is classified outside the lock so one session's regex
work does not stall the other sessions' mutation threads; if
two threads classify the same new string, the first to
insert wins. Each Trigger also keeps the classification of
its own parameter string, so the per-line hit is a string
comparison with no lock at all.

2026-11-05: Make LRUDictionary reads count as a use

Reading an entry through the subscript never bumped its
recency, so the dictionary evicted in insertion order: an
entry read constantly went before one never read again. The
subscript now bumps, peek reads without, and the ObjC
wrapper's lookup counts too. Bumping relinks the existing
list node instead of allocating a new one.

The Kitty image cache's display lookups keep an image fresh
while its whole-cache scans use peek, so a displayed image
outlives one that was transmitted later and never shown. The
Metal line cache is shared by every session's per-frame
state, but those are all built on the one Metal queue, so a
lookup reordering it needs no lock; a comment now records
that assumption.

2026-11-05: Add Grok as a built-in AI provider (#755)

Grok (xAI) is now a first-party AI vendor that can be chosen
in Settings > General > AI, the AI chat provider menu, and
the Companion setup wizard, with its own keychain slot,
instead of being set up as a manual OpenAI-compatible
endpoint.

- Add iTermAIVendorXAI. Keys live in the "xAI API Key for
iTerm2"
  keychain account. api.x.ai and its regional subdomains
  (us.api.x.ai) are the vendor's own host; a look-alike host
  is not. Model names containing "grok" classify as this
  vendor, and an xai- key is not mistaken for an OpenAI or
  DeepSeek key.
- Catalog: grok-4.7 (recommended, 500k context) and grok-4.3
(economy,
  1M context, has a no-reasoning mode) on chat completions.
  Catalog version 6.
- Chat completions send reasoning_effort for xAI models when
an effort
  is selected. Other chat-completions servers keep the body
  they had.
- api.x.ai accepts image attachments through the existing
  chat-completions image parts. Inline PDF parts are not
  offered since that endpoint rejects them.
- Opt-in live harness lane via XAI_API_KEY and

  with an attachment-matrix column for grok-4.7, and refusal

2026-11-05: Fix find-on-page when porthole results are
present

SearchResult's compare: returned NSOrderedSame whenever an
internal result was compared against an external (porthole)
one, so the sorted result set treated every grid match as a
duplicate of a porthole match and dropped it. Matches were
found but never highlighted or counted, and Find Next stayed
on the porthole. The comparison is now a total order.

Along the way: - Find Next and Find Previous advance from a
porthole result to grid results
  instead of wrapping, and from a removed result to the next
  one.
- Porthole results outside the selected command are not
searched or added,
  and hidden portholes (line -1) are never searched.
- Removing a line removes every porthole result on it, not
just the first.  - The tail find no longer removes porthole
results it cannot put back.  - A porthole rendered while a
search is active takes part in it.  - Selecting,
deselecting, or turning off click-to-select for a command
runs
  the search again under the new range. The old restart flag
  had no reader since the SearchEngine rewrite. Deselecting
  does not scroll.
- The selected result is dropped when it is removed, and a
hidden porthole
  match is never selected from a coord cursor.

Also skips
FramerFlowLedgerTests.testBytesThatMakeNoTokensAreStillCounted,
which is flaky under load.

2026-11-05: Reload only colors when an escape sequence
changes a profile color

Programs that set colors with escape sequences (OSC
4/10/11/12/112 and friends) rewrite the session's profile,
and every rewrite ran the full profile reload: font table,
layout, status bar, bindings, and so on. tmux emits a cursor
color change on every pane switch, which made switching
panes lag and burn CPU. It also reset the session mode, so
the change kicked the user out of Copy Mode.

A profile change now produces a plan from a hand-built table
of profile key to reload scope (ProfileReloadScope.swift).
When every changed key is a color, only
reloadColorsFromProfile: runs. Any other key, including one
nobody has classified, runs the full reload as before. The
color pieces of the full reload moved into
reloadColorsFromProfile: so the two paths share one
implementation, and the appearance change handler goes
through it too so the tmux tab color and colors.* variables
follow a light/dark switch.

The badge color and tab color are cached from the profile
being applied instead of read from the live profile at draw
time, which was a hidden dependency that would have left
them stale.

In debug builds iTermProfileReadRecorder wraps both the
profile being applied and the live profile during every full
reload and asserts, with a backtrace, if a colors-scope key
is read outside the colors reload. It caught two real leaks
during development.

Fixes issue 13192.

translations

Restore the keepJobsRunningWhenQuitInstallsUpdate advanced
setting and its translations, which were lost when the
catalog was re-extracted upstream, and translate
holdScreenDuringPromptRepaint, which had only English.

2026-11-05: Bypass the URL cache when fetching the backup
update feed

The feed is normally a 404, and the server's Last-Modified
header on that response let the URL cache treat the error as
fresh for a long time, so a copy of iTerm2 that had seen the
404 once kept seeing it after the file was published. Ignore
local and remote caches for this request.

2026-11-05: Treat a quit with an update ready to install as
an upgrade

Once Sparkle 2 has extracted an update, its installer is
staged and installs after the app quits for any reason, not
only after Install and Relaunch. Sparkle 1 abandoned the
update on such a quit. Set the pending-install flag when the
update is extracted so that a quit from the Ready to Install
window saves restorable state, keeps the quit confirmation,
and honors the keep-jobs advanced setting, whose description
now covers this case.

2026-11-05: Add a backup update notice independent of
Sparkle

If a shipped version turns out to have a Sparkle bug that
keeps it from updating itself, there needs to be a way to
tell its users. Once a day, and only when the user has said
yes to automatic update checks, fetch
https://iterm2.com/appcasts/backup_update.xml?betas=0 (or
1), a file that normally does not exist. When it lists a
version newer than the running one, show a warning once per
version with a Download button that opens
https://iterm2.com/download-backup.html. Nothing is
downloaded or installed, and nothing from Sparkle is used:
the URL is hardcoded and the version comparison is its own.

2026-11-05: Migrate software updates from Sparkle 1.21.2 to
Sparkle 2.10.1. Issue 13152.

The Sparkle 1 status window could not be dismissed or
minimized while an update was downloading. Sparkle 2's
status window can be minimized.

Sparkle 2 has no shared updater, so a new
iTermSoftwareUpdater owns an SPUStandardUpdaterController,
starts it once the initial window exists, chooses the
appcast from the Info.plist keys plus the rollout shard at
check time (instead of writing SUFeedURL into user defaults
at every launch), and never starts in test hosts. The
SUUpdater object and outlet are gone from MainMenu.xib.

Saving state before the app quits for an update is layered
so no single Sparkle code path is load-bearing:
SUUpdaterWillRestartNotification, the updater delegate's
willInstallUpdate and updaterWillRelaunchApplication, an
install-pending flag for the "Install on Quit" and
automatic-install paths where Sparkle sends no further
callback, and as a last resort the quit Apple event's sender
pid, which is checked against Sparkle's Updater.app bundle
id in applicationShouldTerminate:. Flags set during a quit
are rolled back if the user cancels it. A new advanced
setting, keepJobsRunningWhenQuitInstallsUpdate (default
off), lets an Install on Quit quit leave jobs running in
their servers as Install and Relaunch does.

The Sparkle fork (submodules/Sparkle, branch forked_2.10.1)
carries the same -suite patches as the 1.x fork: suite-aware
user defaults, and the progress agent relaunches the app
with the same -suite argument. XPC services and the legacy
SUUpdater stub are compiled out. The embedded framework's
nested helpers (Autoupdate and Updater.app) are not
re-signed by CodeSignOnCopy, so
tools/sign_sparkle_helpers.sh was updated for the Sparkle 2
layout and run on the checked-in framework; a signed build
passed notarization.

Also bumps the iTerm2-shell-integration submodule.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-11-05: Add Move Session to Floating Pane and Select
Next Floating Pane

Session > Move Session > Move Session to Floating Pane lifts
a tiled pane into a float in its own tab, placed like a new
float. A pane's context menu offers it where it applies. In
tmux it sends break-pane -W, and a refusal beeps and is
logged. Window > Split Pane > Floating Panes > Select Next
Floating Pane cycles through the visible floats.

Floating and docking share one lifecycle: a removal that
keeps the active session and tells no one about a tab
between two states, one install tail for the underlay,
focus, blur and dimming, one placement entry point for
floats placed right after installing, one eligibility
predicate, and one focus rule for both directions. A pane
keeps focus if it had it and otherwise takes it by the rule
for a new pane, no other pane's activity counter changes,
and the floats are shown when a pane becomes one.

Verified in the tmux source that floating the last tiled
child of a layout node is fine, so no guard on the node is
needed. Docking is disabled while a tmux window is zoomed,
as floating is.

2026-11-05: Don't read the keychain when opening Settings

The API key hint under the default AI model popup read every
relevant key from the keychain each time the General pane
rendered or the model list refreshed. A denied read is never
cached, so opening Settings could prompt for keychain access
several times. The hint now consults only the in-memory key
cache and stays neutral until the user forces a read by
opening the Manage AI API Keys sheet or starting a chat.

2026-11-05: Skip the old-nightly warning in the unit test
host

The test host counts as a nightly build, so once the build
date was more than 30 days old every run put up the modal
warning at launch.  Nobody is there to answer it, so the
runner waited until its stall detector gave up. Skip the
warning when running unit tests, as the local network prompt
already does.

2026-11-05: Circle the rainbow around the tab ring while a
tab is busy

With the Rainbow progress bar color scheme, an indeterminate
progress bar on the Tahoe tab ring now shows a rainbow that
circles the tab outline instead of a band sliding across it.
The ring is a conic gradient whose color stops are spaced
evenly by arc length around the pill, so the colors move at
one speed on the long edges and around the caps, and a
keyframe animation shifts each stop to the next hue. Flat
bars still draw the scrolling band.

Close the scrolling Rainbow band with red so the tiled band
has no hard pink-to-red edge, and reset gradient locations
when a scheme changes the stop count so the determinate bar
renders every stop.

Installing an indeterminate container only swaps sublayers
and asks for layout; layoutSublayers sizes it and starts its
animation as it already did after a resize. Detaching a
container also stops its animations, so a detached layer is
never mistaken for a running one and pausing no longer
instantiates unused layers.

Originally submitted as PR #809 with a separate Rotating
Rainbow scheme; folded into Rainbow since the two were
identical everywhere but the ring.

2026-11-04: Keep the hotkey ref when re-registering after a
fast user switch

When the login session became active again, the success and
failure branches were swapped: a successful
RegisterEventHotKey discarded the returned EventHotKeyRef
and a failed one kept a garbage value. With no ref stored, a
later unregister could not call UnregisterEventHotKey, so
changing or removing a hotkey after a fast user switch
leaked the old registration and it kept firing. Found while
investigating issue 13190.

2026-11-04: Only log a stack trace in setBookmark when the
profile GUID changes

Escape sequences that change colors (OSC 12/112, etc.)
replace a session profile with a new version of itself,
which could happen many times per second. Symbolicating a
backtrace for each one cost several milliseconds on the main
thread and made tmux pane switching lag when a pane's
program set a cursor color. The trace exists to diagnose
profiles landing in the wrong ProfileModel, which only
happens when the GUID changes, so capture it only then.
Issue 13192.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-11-04: Hold the screen while nushell repaints its
prompt

reedline (nushell's line editor) repaints the whole prompt
line on every keystroke in one write: hide the cursor,
erase, OSC 133;A, the prompt, OSC 133;B, the command, show
the cursor. A sync that lands partway through, such as one
from a join that pauses token execution, draws the line with
the command erased, so typing blinks. It shows most with the
legacy renderer and a large window.

When the cursor is hidden while a command is being entered,
look ahead in the token queue. If the queued tokens prove a
prompt repaint (a 133;A followed by showing the cursor, with
no 133;C or 133;D and no second hide in between), start the
flicker fixer in a new prompt-repaint mode that applies even
when Reduce Flicker is off. Showing the cursor ends it, and
the usual timer still expires it.

The holdScreenDuringPromptRepaint advanced setting turns
this off. The flicker fixer's expiration timer can be put
under a test's control so tests that check that the hold
ends can't pass because the timer fired.

2026-11-04: Skip the flooding-command footprint test

Its 300 MB limit depends on the machine's state: it fails on
a clean master with about 355 MB of growth and passes at
other times.

2026-11-04: Add release note for the zoomed tmux pane
garbling fix

2026-11-04: fix(tmux): preserve zoomed grids during layout
updates

Native pane zoom delivers both a layout notification and a
list-windows response. Reapplying the layout shrank the
alternate screen to the hidden split size before expanding
it again, discarding TUI rows without another SIGWINCH from
tmux.

Before laying out the hidden splits, work out which session
maximize will size to the window (accounting for the junior
session a user-driven split activates) and leave its grid
sizing to maximize. Pass it to the resize as a parameter
rather than reading _tmuxZoomedSession, so a stale zoomed
session left behind by a local unmaximize or pane switch
can't make a later reload skip the wrong pane.

Validated on Mac Studio with tmux 3.7b: 18 local layout
regressions, horizontal and vertical native splits, repeated
zoom/unzoom, window shrink/grow while zoomed, and htop.
Terminal contents match capture-pane.

2026-11-03: Keep the flicker fixer from dropping a full
repaint

When a saved grid that was drawn expires, the grid is marked
all-dirty so every row gets repainted. If a new saved grid
was taken before the next sync (the cursor hidden again, or
a new synchronized update), VT100Grid's copy dropped the
all-dirty flag. The new saved grid had no dirty rows, so the
legacy renderer left rows that changed in the meantime
stale.

Copy allDirty_ along with its generation base and timestamp
so a copy is as dirty as its original.

2026-11-03: List announcements in the Suppressed Alerts
panel

Announcements at the top of a session that are hidden or
answered automatically because the user chose Never, Always,
Dont Ask Again, or a similar option now appear in iTerm2 >
Suppressed Alerts alongside remembered alerts. Un-suppress
clears the saved choice so the announcement is shown again.
An item is listed as soon as the choice is made, for
announcements and for iTermWarning alerts alike.

Always show alerts with remembered selections now applies to
announcements too. When it re-shows a prompt whose
completion can't apply the saved choice later (text
replacement, profile properties, escape sequences changing
the profile, closing tmux windows after detach, the bell),
the saved choice is applied anyway.

Repeated scrollback clears and DECRQCRA requests are
recorded at most once a second. Allowed DECRQCRA requests
are now reported so the Yes entry counts.

DECRQCRAs remembered answer moves from NoSyncDisableDECRQCRA
to the DisableDECRQCRA advanced setting. A migration carries
over a saved Yes or No; previously Yes was not remembered
across restarts.

2026-11-02: Fix upload-and-paste menu handling

Don't auto-open the Uploads menu while upload-and-paste
runs. If an upload fails, open it after the path paste and
any queued pastes drain. Uploads that fail before starting
(unreadable file, folder that can't be archived) are now
registered so the menu lists them, for both SSH Integration
and SCP. Menus are pressed from a run-loop timer so the main
queue keeps draining while they're open. On macOS 27,
briefly open the Uploads menu once per launch so its title
draws.

Canceling a paste that is waiting for a shell prompt now
also discards keystrokes typed while it waited and any
pastes queued after it. Previously they stayed queued until
the next paste.

2026-11-02: Translate the theme warning and catch up the
string catalog

Adds translations for the new light/dark theme warnings, the
new sentence in the window-name-beside-tabs setting, and the
Korean AI usage toolbar messages that were missing.

2026-11-02: Say which colors a theme that overrides the
system appearance leaves unused

The warning next to the light/dark mode checkbox now says
that a dark theme keeps the light mode colors from being
used (and vice versa), since users read the Editing popup
resetting as their color changes not being saved.

2026-11-02: Fix crash drawing the HDR cursor over a
character in the legacy renderer

The HDR cursor used +[NSColor whiteColor], a grayscale
color. It was passed as the background color for the
character under the cursor, and -brightnessComponent raised
when deciding whether to use thin strokes.  Construct white
in the default color space instead, and assert that the
foreground color has enough components before indexing them.

2026-11-02: Don't blame the Mac's version when the hello
reply never comes back

When the phone's 15-second hello timer fired, it assumed the
Mac predates the version handshake and showed the upgrade
panel. The timer also fires when the connection has died, or
when iOS suspended the app mid-handshake and the deadline
passed during the suspension. On resume the timeout could
beat the connection-lost error and wrongly tell the user
their Mac's iTerm2 was too old.

Before concluding that, ping the Mac (which answers pings
off its main thread). If the ping fails, the transport error
takes the normal reconnect path. Also stop logging "Home
loaded" when loadHome ended on the upgrade panel.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-11-02: Make AI usage failures explain themselves and

The AI Usage toolbar item showed "No usage data" whenever

a busy machine.

- Raise the command timeout to 40 seconds.  - Report
timeouts, a silent bundled script (a bug, so reportable),
and a
  silent custom command (with its exit status) as distinct
  messages.  iTermCommandRunner now exposes timedOut so
  callers can tell a timeout apart. A run that timed out but
  still delivered a valid report uses it.
- Log every run's outcome: DLog on success, RLog on failure,
so a debug
  log saved after the fact shows why the item showed an

- Anthropic retired session limits. Stop drawing a Session

  a lone session line as valid output rather than format

  waiting for, with a tooltip noting it can take several

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-11-02: Don't charge a holder for slices it ran while
nobody waited

A holder whose slice ended with nobody in line kept running
uncharged but carried that time forward, so its virtual time
and the floor stayed put.  A session that joined later was
raised to the stale floor, and the holder was then charged
for its whole solo run at once and had to wait about as long
again. Such a slice is now dropped and a new one starts, so
a newcomer joins level with the holder and they take turns.

Also: handOff() removes the chosen waiter by index instead
of searching the line again; the linear checks of the line
and the locked-acquire counter exist only in debug builds;
enqueue() uses binarySearch; and handOff() and park() share
one helper for publishing that a permit may be free and
looking again.

2026-11-02: Keep synthetic modifiers from sticking on QA VMs

vmctl posted Cmd-flagged keys without pressing or releasing
Command, which left the VM's modifier state at Command.
Later keys created without explicit flags, like the 13141
harness's spaces, arrived as Cmd-key shortcuts. vmctl now
presses and releases the real modifier keys around a
modified key, and the harness clears modifiers on the keys
it posts.

The harness also activates Finder before iTerm2 so the probe
sees a focus change; activating an app that is already
active reports nothing.

2026-11-02: Keep the permit line in order so deciding who is
next looks only at its front

Every checkpoint, handoff, and floor update scanned the
whole line under the permits' lock, asking each waiter
whether it was paused. With 200 busy sessions that ran
hundreds of times a second. A waiter's virtual time doesn't
change while it waits, so the line is kept sorted by virtual
time and arrival: the next in line is the first waiter that
isn't paused, and the floor is the first waiter's time.

2026-11-02: Let a holder whose slice is over keep running
without the lock when nobody waits

Each holder took the permits' lock at the end of every slice
to charge its time and decide whether to yield. With nobody
in line and no more permits held than there are, there is
nothing to decide, so it keeps running and is charged later,
at its next checkpoint, release, or park.  Asking still
counts as being heard from for the health check.

2026-11-02: Let a session already waiting for a permit ask
again without the lock

A session in line asks for a permit each time output
arrives. Every ask took the permits' lock and scanned the
line, so with 200 busy sessions and two permits, asking cost
more than executing tokens and the threads blocked on the
lock made GCD start more. A waiter is woken when a permit is
handed to it, so asking again only matters when a permit was
left free or idle while the waiters were paused. A hint, set
before reading whether waiters are paused and cleared only
when no permit is free or idle, tells it when to ask under
the lock.

2026-11-02: Don't let a test's waits keep a freed executor
alive

testSessionFreedWhileHoldingAnIdlePermitGivesItUp captured
the whole session in its wait closures, and an expectation
can outlive its wait, so under Thread Sanitizer the executor
was never freed. Capture only the delegate, and end the
session's scope with an autorelease pool.

2026-11-02: Don't refresh every window on tab drags when the
tab bar is always shown. Issue 13187.

Drags force hide tab bar with one tab off so single-tab
windows accept drops (issue 12846). The setter posted
kRefreshTerminalNotification even when the effective value
didn't change, which made every window and session refit
twice per drag and stuttered with many windows. Only post
when the effective value actually changes.

2026-11-02: Hide the tab status dot along with the status
subtitle. Issue 13186.

Programs like Codex CLI now report status on every state
change, so their tabs carried a permanent colored dot that
no setting could turn off. The Session Status checkbox that
hid the status text left the dot behind, which on its own
says little. The dot now follows the same setting, and the
checkbox is retitled Show status on tabs (with a new string
key so stale translations are not reused).

2026-10-31: Keep window size when the tab bar hides after a
tab drag (#13165)

"Preserve window size when tab bar shows or hides" only
applied when the number of tabs changed. The tab bar also
hides in refreshTerminal: after a drag ends (it is forced
visible during the drag) and when "Show tab bar even when
there is only one tab" is turned off, and that path always
refit the window, shrinking it.

Apply the preserve setting in refreshTerminal: too.
Separately, windows whose tab count did not change during a
drag now go back to their pre-drag size afterwards. A window
as tall as the screen could not grow when the tab bar
appeared, so it used to end up shorter. A move made during
the drag (such as dropping a lone tab to move its window) is
kept, and a resize made during the drag is not undone.

2026-10-31: Keep the stoplight buttons off the session with
a right-side tab bar. Issue 13153.

In compact windows with a visible right-side tab bar, the
stoplight hotbox was disabled, so the window buttons sat
permanently in the top-left corner over the session,
covering the first row of a terminal or a browser's
navigation buttons. The 13100 fix never ran there.

The hotbox is now always enabled for a right-side tab bar.
The proxy icon and window number move to the strip atop the
tab bar instead of riding with the buttons, and the tab bar
reserves that strip only when the proxy icon, window number,
or window name is shown.

The window name beside the tabs also appears in that strip
atop a left or right tab bar. Since it takes no room from
the tabs there, the "When a custom name is set" mode also
shows the automatic title atop a left tab bar, and atop a
right one when the window number or proxy icon is shown.

2026-10-31: Keep a tab drag locked to its tab bar until it
moves well away. Issue 13151

The cross-axis threshold only held the drag image on rails;

2026-10-31: Log session, tab, window, and global variables

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-31: Read the process table with one sysctl per
process cache refresh. Issue 13182.

Each refresh asked for every process's parent with
proc_pidinfo and for the name and foreground state of every
process under a session with sysctl(KERN_PROC_PID).
KERN_PROC_PID walks the kernel's whole process list to find
one pid, so a refresh cost O(n^2) in the number of processes
on the system. A single sysctl(KERN_PROC_ALL) read now
supplies each process's parent, name, and foreground state.

Zombies are skipped, matching the old path (proc_pidinfo
fails for them). An empty p_comm (mid-exec) falls back to
the per-pid lookup so ProcessNameCache still covers it, and
a failed table read falls back to the old per-pid loop,
logged to RLog when reads start or stop failing.
KERN_ARGMAX is now read once.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-31: Skip flaky
TriggerInterpolatedParameterTests.testResultIsCached

The parameter cache is an NSCache, which may evict an entry
between two lookups (for example, under memory pressure
during a parallel run), so the identity check is not
reliable.

2026-10-31: Drop stale and redrawn right-prompt exclusions

A non-initial prompt (P;k=r or k=s) adds an excluded
subrange to the prompt mark when its B arrives, and the
command is extracted without those cells. Two problems:

When the typed text reaches a right prompt, zsh and fish
erase it and draw the command over its cells, but the
exclusion stayed, so those characters were cut from the
recorded command and command history.  powerlevel10k marks
its right prompt this way on iTerm2 3.7+ whenever iTerm2's
zsh integration is loaded. Remember each subrange's text
when its prompt closes, and when the command ends drop
subranges whose cells no longer hold it.

fish redraws its right prompt after every keystroke, which
added an exclusion each time. A new subrange now replaces
existing ones it overlaps.

2026-10-31: Replace the computed box-drawing lower bound
with a constant

iTermBoxDrawingLowerBound() scanned U+0000 through U+24FF
against three character sets on first use, and since no
member is below U+2500 it always ran to the end. It was also
called per cell. Use a constant defined next to the sets
instead, and assert in debug builds that no set member is
below it.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-31: Report mouse motion when an app enables it with
CSI ? 1003 h. Issue 13184

The "mouse mode did change" side effect rebuilds the session
view's tracking areas, deciding whether to track mouse-moved
events from the main thread's copy of the mouse mode. A
plain side effect can be drained by a pass that synced just
before the mutation thread executed the DECSET, so it read
the old mode and dropped NSTrackingMouseMoved. Apps like
Lanterna then got clicks but no motion until something else
(such as a modifier change) rebuilt the tracking areas.

Make it a joined side effect so it reads current state.
setMouseMode: only notifies on a real change, so joining is
rare.

Adds a regression test that drains side effects before a
sync, plus an opt-in to the test harness so the main thread
reads its own snapshot between syncs as it does in the app.
Adds tests/mouse_motion_1003.py for manual testing.

Terminal windows now stay resizable when the layout engine
is engaged, and development builds check for the stale
frames that used to make it a problem, so views that use
constraints no longer need permission first.

2026-10-31: Skip per-cell drawing timers unless drawing
performance logging is on

iTermAttributedStringBuilder starts and stops an
iTermPreciseTimer for every cell it turns into an attributed
string, which costs two mach_absolute_time() calls per cell.
The stats are only reported when the logDrawingPerformance
advanced setting is on, but the timers ran regardless.
Profiling the legacy (non-GPU) renderer redrawing a full
295x84 screen, these calls were about 17% of drawRect
samples on the main thread. The builder now reads the
setting once per line and only runs the timers when it is
on.

It also skips the box-drawing and block-drawing character
set lookups for code points below the lowest member of those
sets. The bound is computed once from the sets themselves
(U+2500 today), so it stays correct if they change.

Measured with the legacy renderer redrawing a full 295x84
screen at 10 fps, alternating with the parent commit:
main-thread drawRect samples -13%, attributedStringsForLine
-19%, process CPU 49% -> 45%. The renderer-related
ModernTests pass.

2026-10-31: Request only the vm_statistics64 revision the
memory component reads

Building against the macOS 27 SDK grew HOST_VM_INFO64_COUNT
to 104 integers, and the macOS 26 kernel logs a warning
every time it gets a count larger than the 62 it supports.
The Memory Utilization component only reads fields from
rev1, so ask for HOST_VM_INFO64_REV1_COUNT.  Issue 13164.

2026-10-31: Credit the x-man-page report in release notes.
Issue 13156.

2026-10-31: Keep terminal windows resizable when the layout
engine is engaged

Anything that engages AppKit's layout engine in a terminal
window (a constraint, NSGlassEffectView, some controls)
makes AppKit translate every autoresizing mask into required
constraints. On an axis where a view's size is flexible, its
fixed margins become a minimum size for its superview, so a
stale or degenerate frame turns into a window the user
cannot make smaller, and programmatic resizes are clamped
too. Issue 13118.

Remove the floors that showed up:

- Hidden subviews of the root view are stretched along their
flexible
  axes after each layout, and a hidden top tab bar gets a
  zero-height strip across the top instead of CGRectZero.
- The views the layout calculator positions get flexible
margins on their
  flexible axes, so the toolbelt width, a side tab bar's
  width and the bar heights no longer become hard minimums.
- A hidden toolbelt keeps a real frame against the right
edge from every
  code path, including canonicalizeWindowFrame, so its tools
  are never laid out at zero width.
- Toolbelt tool wrappers start with the fixed-size mask
NSSplitView gives
  them, a never-shown side tab bar's control fills its
  backing, and the fitWindowToTabSize bug-fix view fills the
  content view.
- SessionTitleView lays itself out on every resize instead
of relying on
  autoresizing, which lost track of the label in narrow
  panes.

Fix the browser's address field running under the loading
spinner: iTermURLBarGuts only set needsLayout, which nothing
honors in a window without the layout engine.

Add AutoresizingFloorAuditor, which finds such floors from
frames and masks alone. Development builds run it after each
layout of a terminal window, and TerminalWindowTestFixture
fails any test that trips it. Setting
ITERM2_ENGAGE_LAYOUT_ENGINE=1 engages the engine in every
fixture window and checks the minimum size and broken
constraints when it closes.
TerminalWindowAutoLayoutFloorTests runs sampled
configurations and seeded random sequences with and without
the engine, shrinks each window and grows it back, and
compares the layouts.

Add a development-only advanced setting that engages the
engine in new terminal windows for trying them out by hand.

Skip two tests that are flaky for unrelated reasons.

2026-10-30: Say what was still pending when screen tests
give up waiting for tokens

waitForQueuedTokensForTesting now gives up after 30 seconds
rather than 10,000 rounds, and its failure names what the
executor still had: queued tokens, tasks, and whether it was
paused. Also note that it executes without a permit on
purpose.

2026-10-30: Test that a session freed while waiting for or
holding a permit gives it up

Covered so far only at the ExecutionPermits level. These
free a real TokenExecutor while it waits in line and while
it holds an idle permit, and check that the line empties and
another session can run.

2026-10-30: Test the ticker's real timer source and
expedites racing marks

The dispatch timer source had no tests, and misusing a
dispatch source crashes. It now asserts that starts and
stops alternate, and its deinit resumes a source that never
started or was stopped before cancelling, since releasing a
suspended or inactive source crashes. A test starts, stops
and restarts it and releases it in each state. Another races
a keystroke's expedite against marks from other threads and
checks the waiting update goes out right away exactly once.

2026-10-30: Test that coprocess EOF terminates the coprocess
before reaping it

terminate() signals the pid, which could belong to an
unrelated process once it has been reaped. The test records
whether the coprocess was terminated when its pid was handed
to the reaper, on both backends.

2026-10-30: Test registering a task without a usable
descriptor, and a redundant registration

Job managers start without a descriptor, so a task can be
registered before it has one. Such a task, or one whose
descriptor is already closed, is not read and is
deregistered, which hands its process to be reaped. A
redundant registration's discarded monitor gives back its
leases, so the descriptor can still be closed. Also correct
the double registration test's comment: it passes because
registration ignores a task that is already registered.

2026-10-30: Share the task read capacity decision between
PTYSession and the tests

PTYSession's taskHasReadCapacity and its notification had no
tests, and the I/O characterization tests' FakeTask
reimplemented them, already without the check that wakes a
gateway that left tmux mode while being registered.
iTermTaskReadCapacity now holds the logic for both. Tests
cover each budget being full, waiting for each in turn, a
drained token executor, and leaving gateway mode around the
check, and a real session wakes a waiting monitor when
setTmuxMode: leaves gateway mode.

2026-10-30: Test that a join of one session leaves another
executing

This is the point of per-session mutation queues (issue
13141). The test fails if a join pauses every session.

2026-10-30: Make concurrency tests able to see the bugs they
guard against

- The complex character registry test now checks that racing
to create a
  string's code allocates exactly one.
- The owned descriptor test holds uses open across close()
on another
  thread and releases them one at a time, instead of hoping
  a close lands in a window of a few nanoseconds.
- The permit cap test stays running while holding a permit,
so holders
  overlap.
- The tmux input queue test checks that the budget counts
exactly the
  unread bytes before closing, since closing gives
  everything back.
- Permit executor tests give up after 30 seconds instead of
spinning
  forever when a join never pauses a session.

Each was checked against a deliberately broken
implementation.
