Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-30: Read a tmux pane's tty with if-shell instead of
run-shell

To learn whether claude in a tmux pane is interactive,
iTerm2 sent run-shell 'stty -a < tty; ps ...' to the tmux
server. In control mode tmux ends that command's reply right
away and prints the output afterward, outside any reply. The
gateway took the stray lines for a broken connection
("Unrecognized command from tmux") and detached, so running
claude in a tmux integration pane could end the session.

Use a guarded if-shell instead. Its branch replies in a
block of its own: YES when the pane's tty is in raw mode and
a process whose argv[0] is claude is in its foreground
process group, NO otherwise. NO can't lead to an offer; it
reads as canonical and keeps checking until the policy gives
up. A host where stty or ps -o ... -t doesn't work falls
back to waiting until claude has run for three seconds.

Before tmux 3.2 the reply to an if-shell branch is flagged
as server-originated, so the gateway doesn't match it to the
command and hands it the next command's reply instead. Older
servers keep the three-second wait.

Checked against every tmux release from 2.1 through 3.8-rc3
with the exact command strings, and end to end through the
gateway on 2.0, 2.1, 3.0a, 3.1c, 3.2 and 3.8.

2026-10-29: Expect the process group column in the framer's
ps command

afce7c434 added pgid to the columns the framer asks ps for.

2026-10-29: Read the Claude session's terminal mode through
the task's owned descriptor

PTYTask no longer exposes a raw fd. Add
-withFileDescriptor:, which keeps the descriptor open while
tcgetattr reads it.

2026-10-29: Point the shell integration submodule back at
0296291

8634ad695 (Bump companion version to 1.5) accidentally moved
it back to 20a786c, eleven commits older than the copies
bundled in Resources.

2026-10-29: Read advanced settings without racing a change
on another thread

Each setting was a strong static object that the main thread
replaced while mutation queues and parser threads read it,
so a reader could retain a value as it was being released.
Each value now lives in an atomic slot that readers load
without a lock. A replaced value is kept alive, and values
are swapped only when they change.

2026-10-29: Keep the workgroup list behind a lock so
triggers can read it off the main thread

EnterWorkgroupTrigger reads iTermWorkgroupModel.workgroups
on the session's mutation queue while the main thread
replaces it. Readers now get a whole list from a
MutableAtomicObject. The main thread is still the only
writer.

2026-10-29: Wait for character width tables to finish
initializing before reading them

ensureInitialized() returned as soon as the state was
nonzero, so a thread that arrived while another was filling
the full-width and ambiguous-width bitmaps (state 1) read
them half-filled and could give characters the wrong width.
Now it returns only when initialization is done (state 2);
otherwise it waits, as the code already intended.

Sessions convert text on their own reader and mutation
queues, so several threads can reach this at once right
after launch.

2026-10-29: Hash and compare complex character strings
outside the registry's lock

Every combining mark, emoji, and other multi-code-point
character goes through one process-wide registry behind a
mutex. In a profile of four sessions flooding mixed output
it took about 12% of token execution, more than half of it
waiting for the lock, and hashing and comparing the NSString
keys took most of the time the lock was held.

Now a string's hash is computed before taking the lock, the
lock is held only to fetch the entries with that hash, and
the string is compared after releasing it. Codes are stored
as unichar rather than NSNumber. In an optimized build, a
lookup of a registered string is about 30% faster on one
thread and 25% faster with four threads contending; with
eight, the lock's handoffs still dominate.

The registry takes an injectable hash function and maximum
code, so tests can make hashes collide and make the codes
wrap.

2026-10-29: Load the terminal's weak delegate once per token

VT100Terminal's delegate is weak, so each message to it
loads it through the Objective-C runtime's weak reference
table. Executing a token sent it at least two messages, and
VT100Grid sent its own weak delegate gridCursorDidMove on
every cursor move; together about 3% of token execution in a
profile of four sessions flooding mixed output.
-reallyExecuteToken: now loads the delegate into a strong
local once (it changes only in a join, between tokens), and
gridCursorDidMove, whose implementations were all empty, is
gone.

2026-10-29: Check the execution permit time slice every 8
tokens instead of every token

Reading the clock for every token took about 1.6% of token
execution in a profile of four sessions flooding mixed
output. The holder now reads it on the first token after its
clock (re)starts and then every 8 tokens, so a slice can run
a few tokens long, which takes microseconds. The holder's
clock is injectable, for the test.

2026-10-29: Add a script that generates different kinds of
terminal output

tests/13141/loadgen.py writes a chosen kind of output to
stdout, for loading iTerm2 while profiling or measuring:
short, long, and prose-like lines; empty and mostly empty
lines; wide, emoji, combining, and right-to-left text;
16-color and 24-bit SGR; tabs; carriage-return progress
bars; full-screen redraws; OSC 8 hyperlinks; or all of them
mixed. It can stop after a size or a time, limit its rate,
and write line by line instead of in 64 KB writes. Writes
end on character boundaries, and it resets the terminal when
it stops.

workloads.py now generates its data with loadgen's
generators and accepts any loadgen kind as a workload. Its
"color" workload is now "sgr".

2026-10-29: Don't make a UUID for every line block

Each new line block got a fresh UUID as its identifier, and
making one takes a global lock in the system's random number
generator. With 16 sessions scrolling heavy output, that was
about 3% of token execution time, mostly waiting for the
lock. Blocks now get a UUID made once per process plus a
counter. The identifier is only compared and stored, so its
format doesn't matter, and restored blocks keep the
identifiers they were saved with.

2026-10-29: Add a workload script for measuring output
throughput and its CPU cost

tests/13141/workloads.py runs a fixed set of workloads, each
aimed at a different cost (line-at-a-time seq, short lines,
long lines with and without wrapping, frequent color
changes, wide and combining characters, and full-screen
redraws), at several session counts. It reports executed
MB/s, iTerm2's CPU, CPU-seconds per GB, and the execution
overhead from the token execution stats, so builds can be
compared on the same workload.

2026-10-29: Time each keystroke's echo in the token
execution stats

The 13141 harness measured keystroke to pty and DSR round
trips, which can't show whether the echo reaches the screen
promptly. Each session's executor update ticker client now
times the update that follows a keystroke: from the
keystroke until its output finished executing, from then
until the update started on the main thread (the ticker and
the dispatch), and how long main took to handle it,
including the redraw it starts. With
NoSyncTokenExecutionStatsFile set, each block reports
percentiles of all three, and run_experiment.py prints them
per phase.

The harness's process and stats helpers move to
tests/13141/harness.py so other scripts can use them.

2026-10-29: Add an advanced setting to turn off the limit on
sessions processing output

If the limit causes trouble, a beta user had to turn off the
whole new input and output implementation, losing the fix
for typing lag that came with it. The new
limitSessionsProcessingOutput setting turns off just the
limit (after a restart): sessions then execute tokens
without taking permits, as before the limit existed, and the
Settings control for it is disabled.

2026-10-29: Put execution permit state in debug logs

Debug logs now list every session's place: holding a running
or parked permit (and when it was last heard from) or
waiting (for how long, and whether it is paused), with each
one's virtual time, plus the capacity and the floor. Waits
of over a second are summarized in the debug log at most
every ten seconds.

Subsystems add their state to the header and footer of debug
logs with iTermRegisterDebugLogStateProvider, so
DebugLogging doesn't depend on them. Providers are called
with the debug log lock held, so nothing logs while holding
the permits' lock.

2026-10-29: Recover from lost execution permit wakeups

A lost wakeup would leave a session frozen with no crash and
no clue.  Once a second, while anyone waits for a permit or
holds a running one, ExecutionPermits now hands free and
idle permits to waiters that can run and have waited over a
second, and wakes running holders that haven't checked in
for five seconds (a holder that is really running checks in
every slice). An unneeded wake only costs an extra
execute().  Each repair is written to the debug log, since
it means a bug. A silent holder is never expected, so debug
builds crash on one.

2026-10-29: Send executor updates to the main thread from
one shared ticker

Each session ran its own 120 Hz timer while it had output,
to tell the main thread how much it executed. With 16
sessions flooding, re-arming those timers took about a core,
mostly blocked in the kernel, and sent about 2,000 blocks a
second to the main thread.

With per-session queues (UseTaskIOMonitors), one ticker now
runs while any session has an update, and each tick sends
every pending update in one dispatch. A session's update
goes out right away if it hasn't sent one within the last
period, so the first sign of new output isn't delayed by
other sessions' output. After a keystroke, the session's
next update (normally the echo) goes out right away even if
one just did, so the redraw that shows it isn't throttled.

VT100Screen owns the main-thread side, and the mutable state
only marks that an update is needed. Sessions on the shared
queue send updates with their own timers, as before.

2026-10-29: Report bytes executed per second in the token
execution stats

Each block in the NoSyncTokenExecutionStatsFile file now
says how many MB of input a second finished executing, so a
run shows whether iTerm2 or the kernel's pty layer is the
limit. Every execute() is sampled, not only those of
sessions with execution permits, so runs with and without
per-session queues can be compared.
tests/13141/run_experiment.py reports it per phase.

2026-10-29: Keep execution permits off the lock when
sessions aren't competing

With 19 sessions flooding on 16 cores, nobody ever waited
for a permit, yet 750 to 1000 ms of every second went to the
permits' lock: each execute() took it twice, and execute()
runs once per chunk read.

- A session that runs out of tokens parks its permit instead
of giving
  it up: it keeps it, idle, and resumes it lock-free when
  tokens arrive. A session that needs a permit when none is
  free takes one from an idle holder under the lock. A
  parking holder marks itself idle before checking for
  waiters, and a waiter gets in line before looking for idle
  holders, so a waiter that can run is never left in line
  beside an idle permit. Paused waiters don't make a holder
  give its permit up; they take the idle permit when they
  ask again.
- Wakes happen after the lock is released.  - A holder
releasing with work left keeps its permit when nobody else
  is owed it, instead of handing the permit to itself and
  waking up.
- A yield owed to a waiter happens even if a join pauses the
holder as
  its slice ends.
- After the cap is lowered, idle permits go first, so only a
running
  holder that is really over the cap stops.

Permit time fell to about 10 ms a second in the same test.

2026-10-29: Limit how many sessions execute tokens at once

With per-session mutation queues, GCD ran about one thread
per busy session, so heavy output in many sessions used
every core. Token execution now takes an execution permit
first. Sessions waiting for a permit hold no thread and are
woken when one is handed to them.

Permits are shared by stride scheduling: each session's
virtual time goes up by the time it ran divided by its
weight, and the next permit goes to the waiter with the
lowest. Visible sessions weigh more (advanced setting,
default 4), so they get more turns without starving
background sessions. A holder gives up its permit at a token
boundary after a 5 ms slice when a waiter is owed it. Joins
never take a permit or the permits' lock; a session paused
only for joins keeps its permit, and any other pause gives
it up.

The limit is "Sessions processing output at once" in
Settings > General > Magic: automatic (2 to 4, by core
count) or 2 up to the core count. It applies right away.

The hidden default NoSyncTokenExecutionStatsFile names a
file that gets a summary of token execution time, permit
overhead, permit waits, and join waits once a second.
tests/13141/run_experiment.py reads it with --exec-stats.

2026-10-29: Add a tmux control-mode backlog repro and record
threads and memory in the 13141 harness

2026-10-29: Turn on task I/O monitors and per-session
mutation queues in beta builds

2026-10-29: Keep tmux from queuing a backlog when the
gateway pauses reading

With task I/O monitors on, the gateway's byte budget was
charged only when main appended %output to a pane, so it
read far ahead of main and then stopped reading for seconds.
A tmux bug makes tmux keep reading a pane without limit
during such a stop, so keystrokes to tmux panes ran a minute
or more late after a flood.

Charge each tmux line against the budget from when it is
handed to main until main has handled it, resume the gateway
once its budget drops 16 KiB below capacity rather than at
half, and wake a reader waiting on the token budget as soon
as one slot frees.

2026-10-29: Wait for queued tokens to execute in screen
tests instead of relying on an empty join

Many tests injected bytes and then did an empty join,
expecting the join to run after the injected tokens
executed. That held only while a join on the shared queue
with Maximize Throughput on first waited for the queue to go
idle. A join that pauses right away, as it does for a
session with a queue of its own or with Maximize Throughput
off, stops the scheduled execution, so the test read the
screen before its input ran.

waitForQueuedTokensForTesting() joins until the executor has
no pending tokens or tasks, letting the main queue run side
effects that are delivered outside a join and executing
pending work directly on the executor's queue between
rounds.

2026-10-29: Add the issue 13141 keystroke latency harness

Measures keystroke and DSR round-trip latency in an idle
session while other sessions flood output, adapted from the
reporter's reproducer.

2026-10-29: Don't wait for a busy session to go idle before
joining it when it has its own queue

With Maximize Throughput on, a join first gave the mutation
queue up to 30 ms to go idle before pausing it, because
pausing the shared queue stops every session. A session with
a queue of its own pauses right away and waits only for its
current batch: pausing costs only that session, and a queue
that is busy with a flood never goes idle, so every join
would block the main thread for the whole grace period
(issue 13141).

2026-10-29: Give each session a mutation queue of its own
when task I/O monitors are on

Every session's mutable state ran on one serial queue, so
joining any session, which every refresh does, waited for
and paused every other session's token execution. With a few
sessions producing heavy output, all sessions lagged (issue
13141).

With task I/O monitors on, each session's mutable state now
gets its own mutation queue, and a join pauses only that
session. With them off, every session still shares one
queue, as before.

2026-10-29: Fix more state that is unsafe once sessions have
mutation queues of their own

- scheduleTriggerCallback: ran its block inline on any
mutation queue.
  It now does so only on its own state's queue.
- iTermImageInfo's decode completion wrote its ivars without
the
  instance lock.
- The DEBUG-only OSC 1337 Debug=RemapFn and QueryFnState
wrote a
  preference and read the modifier remapper on the mutation
  queue. They run on main now and reply through the
  terminal's queue.
- The regex cache was a static dictionary with no lock.  -
The per-process "joined" flag, used only to label debug log
lines,
  was wrong with nested joins and other sessions' queues.
  The label comes from the per-queue safety markers instead.
- Mutation queue assertions accepted main while it had
joined any
  queue. The mutable state and token executor now assert for
  their own queue.

2026-10-29: Always join in
performBlockWithJoinedThreadsReentrantSafe:

It ran its block inline, without joining, whenever it wasn't
called from inside an unpaused side effect, which is nearly
always. The block then changed mutable state while token
execution ran on the mutation queue. This has been the case
since it was added, and became common when the color setters
started using it. Setting colors and the echo probe delegate
(broadcast password input) were affected.

It now always joins. It differs from
performBlockWithJoinedThreads: only in also being allowed
inside an unpaused side effect. A comment spells out what a
block passed to it may do there.

2026-10-29: Restore the main-thread state after joins in
screen tests, and assert it

The test FakeSession ignored screenRestore:, so after the
first join a test screen kept reading mutable state through
the sanitizing adapter while its mutation queue changed it.
Thread Sanitizer reported this as a race on the interval
tree. It now restores like PTYSession does, and VT100Screen
asserts after its outermost join that the delegate restored
the state.

Three tests relied on the missing restore, because ending a
join syncs and marks the grid clean. They now check inside
the join.

A join can also release the last owner of the screen's
session, so the screen keeps itself alive until it returns.

2026-10-29: Pause only the joined session when it has a
mutation queue of its own

Joining a session pauses token execution so its queue
reaches the join quickly. On the shared queue that must stop
every session, but a session with a queue of its own now
pauses only itself, and background sessions no longer yield
to active ones they don't share a queue with.  Every session
still uses the shared queue, so nothing changes yet.

2026-10-29: Make process-wide caches and counters safe to
use from several mutation queues

Sessions' mutation queues will run concurrently, and these
were only safe because they all shared one queue: - The
"last made" caches in iTermExternalAttribute and iTermURL
were
  read and replaced without a lock, which could free an
  object another queue was using. They are locked now, read
  as a unit, and release the old object after unlocking. A
  URL decoded from data is no longer reused for a lookup by
  code.
- iTermImageInfo's lazy-decode list is shared by every
instance but was
  locked per instance, so it already raced with the decode
  queue.
- Kitty image sequence numbers and button mark IDs use
atomics.

2026-10-29: Track mutation queue join state per queue
instead of per process

Groundwork for giving each session its own mutation queue.
Whether the main queue is inside a joined block now lives in
an iTermMutationQueueJoinState stored on each mutation
queue. Sessions on the shared queue share it, so behavior is
unchanged: a nested join of another session still takes the
reentrancy shortcut, as it must when the one queue is
already paused. With a queue per session, such a join will
pause that session's queue too.

The queue-safety markers are per queue, and the main queue
stays marked until the outermost of nested joins ends. A
token executor runs a task synchronously only on its own
queue, and PathSniffer uses the session's queue instead of
the shared one.

2026-10-29: Log task I/O monitor lifecycle events and stalls

Rare lifecycle events on the task I/O monitor path are
RLogged so debug logs from the field show them: a monitor
finishing its stop, coprocess start and end on a monitor, a
pane input queue closing, the reaper waiting for and reaping
a pid, and a pid that stays unreapable.

Waits for read capacity happen many times a second under
load, so they stay DLog. A monitor that has had no room to
read for 5 seconds logs once, and again when the stall ends,
with how long it lasted.

2026-10-29: Give owned file descriptors pipe and flag
helpers, and use them in place of fcntl

iTermOwnedFileDescriptor gains +pipe, which returns both
ends wrapped, and nonblocking and closeOnExec properties
that change one flag inside withFileDescriptor and do
nothing once closed. The job managers, the multiserver
child, Coprocess, and tests use them instead of fcntl.

Coprocess still makes its pipes with pipe() because the
child needs plain descriptors after fork(), but it now
checks for failure, and its fork() failure path no longer
leaks the pipes or the environment copy.

2026-10-29: Read tmux pane output from memory, with gateway
backpressure, when task I/O monitors are on

A tmux pane's session appends %output to a TaskInputQueue
instead of writing it to a pipe, and the pane's
TaskIOMonitor reads it through a user-data dispatch source
that the queue signals. Closing the queue ends the task's
input like closing a pipe's write end.

Unread pane output counts against a ByteBudget owned by the
gateway session. While it is full the gateway stops reading
from tmux (writes continue) until the panes drain it to
half. Without this, a flood in a pane grew memory without
bound and starved the main thread. Leaving gateway mode
wakes a gateway waiting on the budget.

With task I/O monitors off, panes keep using the pipe.

2026-10-29: Restart sessions only after the old task's
monitor stops

With task I/O monitors on, restarting a session deregisters
the old task and waits on the main queue until its monitor
has stopped before resetting the parser and starting the new
program. This keeps two tasks from reading into one token
executor. Tokens the old program sent that haven't been
executed yet are discarded; injected tokens are kept.

Each pid is handed to the reaper once per task, however many
times the task is deregistered. The record lives on the task
because a weak reference to it can't be formed while it
deallocates.

A restart requested while one is already pending is ignored.

2026-10-29: Replace the SIGCHLD handler with logging when
task I/O monitors are on

With useTaskIOMonitors on, the SIGCHLD handler only woke a
select loop that no longer runs, so don't install it. Child
exit is detected by EOF on the pty, and ChildReaper reaps. A
dispatch signal source RLogs each SIGCHLD instead, which
needs no signal handler.

Remove the multiserver job manager's extra brokenPipe on a
failed attach; finishAttaching:task: already reports it.

Add a characterization test that a child exiting causes
exactly one broken pipe on every backend, with no SIGCHLD
handler installed.

2026-10-29: Give task file descriptors an owner that
monitors lease

iTermOwnedFileDescriptor owns an fd and closes it exactly
once: when its owner calls close() (owners also do so in
dealloc), or as a backstop when it is deallocated. Closing
waits until no Lease or withFileDescriptor: block is using
the fd, so it is never closed under a TaskIOMonitor's
dispatch sources and its number can't be reused while one
watches it. A close that fails with EINTR is not retried;
EBADF asserts.

- The job managers, the multiserver child, and Coprocess own
their fds through
  the wrapper. Raw int fd getters and setters are gone from
  iTermJobManager, iTermTask, PTYTask, the multiserver
  child, and Coprocess. TaskNotifier's select loop is the
  only code that reads a raw number.
- PTYTask's reads, writes, password detection, and
window-size ioctl go
  through the wrapper.
- -[PTYTask setReadOnlyFileDescriptor:] is replaced by
  -readTmuxPaneOutputFrom:, which takes a wrapper.
- Each monitor source holds a lease until its cancel handler
runs.
  Registration reports whether the task had an open fd.

2026-10-29: Add TaskIOMonitor behind the useTaskIOMonitors
gate

With the gate on, each registered task is serviced by its
own TaskIOMonitor instead of TaskNotifier's select loop: a
serial queue with dispatch sources for the pty and the
coprocess. Before each read the monitor checks the session's
token capacity, and when there is none it suspends reading
until the TokenExecutor drains, so a slow session never
blocks another's reads.

- TaskIOMonitorRegistry owns the monitors. TaskNotifier
forwards register,
  deregister, and the new unblockTask: to it and doesn't
  start the select thread.
- PTYTask calls unblockTask: where it called unblock, and
gets read capacity
  from its delegate (PTYSession -> VT100Screen ->
  TokenExecutor).
- TokenBudget: registering a capacity handler while one is
pending now runs
  the pending one instead of asserting, so a session's next
  task isn't stranded by a handler its previous task left
  behind.
- The coprocess ending path signals the coprocess before
reaping it.  - The characterization suite also runs against
monitors, with backpressure
  tests and a multi-threaded stress test.

The monitor does not own its file descriptors yet.

2026-10-29: Replace TokenExecutor's semaphore with
TokenBudget

TokenBudget limits how many token arrays from the pty a
TokenExecutor buffers, as the DispatchSemaphore did.
acquire() blocks the same way, so the select-based reader is
unchanged. It also supports a reader that must never block:
hasTokenCapacity tells it whether reading is safe, and
notifyWhenTokenCapacityAvailable(on:_:) dispatches a
one-shot block onto the reader's queue once the executor has
drained to half full. The per-task readers that will use
this come later.

TokenArray now releases its slot through a single method
that clears it, so no path can release twice. A bufferDepth
of zero or less is treated as one rather than blocking reads
forever.

2026-10-29: Add ChildReaper behind the useTaskIOMonitors
gate

Adds the useTaskIOMonitors advanced setting (default NO),
latched once per process by +[TaskNotifier
usesTaskIOMonitors]. When it is on, the shared TaskNotifier
hands pids to ChildReaper instead of its polled dead pool.
ChildReaper watches each pid with a kqueue process-exit
source and never blocks in waitpid, since macOS has been
seen to hang there on an exited child; it retries with
backoff instead.

Every dead pool insertion now goes through -reapPid:, which
ignores pids <= 0. Previously the monoserver could pass pid
0, and the dead pool then called waitpid(0), reaping
arbitrary children in our process group.

The characterization suite now also runs with ChildReaper
doing the reaping. Removes the unused -lock and -unlock.

2026-10-29: Fix task I/O bugs ahead of the TaskNotifier
migration

- processRead treats read() == 0 as EOF. A pty master reads
0 (not EIO)
  when its slave has no openers; that already ended sessions
  via the select exception set, but a tmux pane's pipe at
  EOF spun forever. Bytes read before EOF or an error are
  now delivered before brokenPipe.
- brokenPipe always deregisters but reports to the session
only once.
  It can be called twice (EOF plus the exception set, or the
  multiserver attach path), and after a restart the second
  report would end the new shell.
- writeToCoprocess: wakes TaskNotifier. Bytes written from
the SSH path
  could otherwise sit in the coprocess buffer until
  something unrelated woke the select loop.
- registerTask: ignores a task that is already registered,
and tmux panes
  no longer register twice (registerTmuxTask becomes
  markAsTmuxTask).
- The coprocess-change notification no longer blocks the I/O
thread on
  the main thread.
- VT100Parser: conductor recovery mutates the SSH parser
table under the
  parser lock; the debug histogram is locked; the async
  preconvert budget can no longer be overshot by concurrent
  parsers.
- Coprocess closes the child's stderr after EOF instead of
leaking an fd
  per coprocess.

2026-10-29: Make TaskNotifier testable and add
characterization tests

TaskNotifier was only reachable through a never-stopping
singleton with a static unblock pipe. Test instances can now
be created with an injectable select() and coprocess-change
handler, started on their own thread, and stopped. The
unblock pipe moves into ivars; the shared instance publishes
its write end to a static that the SIGCHLD handler reads, so
it stays async-signal-safe. UnblockTaskNotifier() now
preserves errno and does nothing before the shared instance
exists.

The new tests pin down the select loop's current behavior
(ordering, pause, broken pipe, reaping, coprocesses, the
global stall) so the planned per-task GCD backend can be
checked against the same contract.

2026-10-29: Offer the Claude Code integration only for
interactive claude sessions

The offer appeared for any claude in the foreground,
including short-lived non-interactive runs like claude
plugin list, even ones started by a shell script.

This began as a three-second wait: the offer came only after
claude had been in the foreground that long, using a
per-session pending check cancelled when claude exits, when
the session closes, or when claude is relaunched. That
structure is kept, but a wait alone still offered the
integration for a claude -p that ran longer than three
seconds. The check now decides from the run itself:

- claude's command line rules out one-off runs right away:
-p,
  --version, --help, --bg, or a management subcommand
  (plugin, mcp, doctor, ...) as the first argument. attach
  is left out because it opens a session. Only raw argv is
  classified, which a local claude has. SSH Integration's
  argv is split from ps output, which has lost the argument
  boundaries, so those runs are left to the tty check.
- Otherwise, offer once claude's tty is in raw mode while
claude's own
  process group has the tty's foreground. Interactive Claude
  Code puts its tty in raw mode, and claude -p never does.
  Every common shell also goes raw at its prompt, so a raw
  reading only counts when claude, not a shell nested
  anywhere, has the foreground.

The session is judged by the outermost claude, the one the
user ran, not one its hooks or tools started. The tty is
read from whatever owns claude's pty: tcgetattr and the
process table for a session on this Mac; framer over SSH
Integration, which now reports the foreground process group
with the modes, checks the login shell's tty once a second
rather than only when it writes, and puts each process's
group in its ps rows; and for a tmux pane, run-shell with
stty and ps -t on the server's host, which needs tmux 2.4.
The three-second wait remains as the fallback when the tty
can't be read, and every check ends within fifteen seconds.

Co-authored-by: George Nachman <gnachman@gmail.com>

2026-10-29: Keep right prompts out of command history and
fix stale autocomplete prefix. Issue 13168.

Since OSC 133 k= support, the executed command was extracted
with contentInRange:excludingSubranges:, which skipped nulls
and kept going.  A right prompt that the shell doesn't mark
with P;k=r (such as zsh's RPROMPT with iTerm2's own shell
integration) was therefore glued onto the command and saved
in the mark and in command history. Add
kiTermTextExtractorNullPolicyMidlineEndsLine, under which a
null after content on a line drops the rest of that line
while later lines are still extracted, and rebuild the
excluding extractor on locatedStringInRange: with that
policy.

The auto command history popup inserted the chosen command
minus the length of the prefix it last loaded with. The
session only reloads it while the cell under the cursor is
empty, so a zsh autosuggestion drawn after the cursor left
the length stale and the last typed character was repeated
("aws" became "awss"). The popup now asks its delegate for
what is typed when a row is chosen, matches it
case-insensitively as history lookup does, and inserts
nothing if the command no longer matches.

2026-10-29: Keep a session's profile type fixed when its
shared profile changes type

The Profile Type control in Settings > Profiles > General
rewrites KEY_CUSTOM_COMMAND in the shared profile. That
change flowed into sessions already using the profile, so a
terminal session's profile could claim to be a browser (or
the reverse) while its view stayed as it was. Among other
things, this made open sessions lose their tab and window
names.

A session's type is now fixed when it is created. When a
shared profile changes type, sessions on the other side of
the line divorce and override KEY_CUSTOM_COMMAND, keeping
their command type while still following the rest of the
shared profile. Session-specific writes, such as from the
Python API, can't change the type either.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-29: Keep Claude Code hooks from piling up behind an
unanswered consent prompt. Issue 13175.

it2 handle-agent-hook got its API cookie by running
osascript on every hook event. When macOS needs the user's
consent before an AppleEvent may reach iTerm2, AE.framework
in iTerm2 blocks a serial queue on tccd until the prompt is
answered, and each later AppleEvent strands a dispatch
thread behind it.  With hooks firing on every tool call in
every session, an unanswered prompt overnight reached the
512-thread limit and hung iTerm2.

handle-agent-hook now asks macOS first, without prompting,
whether the event would be allowed
(AEDeterminePermissionToAutomateTarget). If it is, it sends
the event itself instead of running osascript. If consent is
needed and no prompt is pending, it starts a detached helper
(the hidden "it2 request-consent") that prompts while
holding an advisory lock, and waits for it. Hooks that find
the lock held give up at once. The helper runs in its own
session with no inherited descriptors, so the agent killing
the hook neither cancels the prompt nor waits on the helper.
On this path the cookie is fetched before connecting,
because iTerm2 drops a connection that sends nothing for 30
seconds.

Every other it2 command keeps the osascript behavior, so
existing scripts see no change.

tests/ae_permission_guard/aeguard.swift is the manual test
tool used to check that the precheck predicts what a real
send does.

2026-10-28: Run sheet warning completions after the sheet
has ended

AppKit calls a sheet's completion handler while it is still
ending the sheet. When that completion showed another
warning on the same window, the new sheet began before the
old one detached, and after it was answered it stayed
attached as a dead sheet. The window then ignored all input
until the app was force-quit. This happened when choosing
Paste as Text for a pasted file whose contents needed a
paste confirmation.

Defer the completion of a sheet-style iTermWarning to the
next pass of the main queue. App-modal and silenced warnings
still complete synchronously.

Issue 13169

2026-10-28: Skip call stack logging when reloading dynamic
profiles. Issue 13174.

A reload replaces every dynamic profile, and
setBookmark:atIndex:sideEffects: symbolicated a call stack
for each one, about 5 ms per profile. With hundreds of
dynamic profiles this froze the app for a second on every
reload.

2026-10-28: Opt out of AppKit window and tab title indexing

On macOS 26 AppKit donates every distinct window and tab
title to CoreSpotlight, which runs each through on-device
text embedding.  Terminal titles change constantly, so this
burned a lot of CPU in mediaanalysisd and
spotlightknowledged. Set NSEnableWindowTabIndexing to NO
with the other system preference overrides. Issue 13066.

2026-10-27: Make a held Delete key repeat when typing into a
session in iTerm2 Buddy

The system keyboard only auto-repeats Delete while the
focused document has text before the caret. The hidden
pass-through text view kept its document empty, so a held
Delete sent a single backspace. Keep an invisible sentinel
there instead and strip it from the composer draft.

2026-10-27: Add 3.7.7beta2 release notes

2026-10-27: Don't recover an adopted orphan job's SSH
connection whose ssh ended

When iTerm2 was killed before it ever saved state, a
relaunch adopts the session's job as an orphan, so no
restored conductor exists and the saved-arrangement check
for a missing ssh client never ran. If ssh died while iTerm2
was down, the dead connection's unread framer output then
started framer recovery, typing base64 recover into the
local shell and leaving a recovery conductor holding the
keyboard. The first framer recovery request in an adopted
orphan now checks the job for an ssh client and, if there is
none, refuses recovery as is done for a restored connection
whose ssh ended.

QA finding F-970.

2026-10-27: Expect a bare ^C for an EndSSH no connection
started in the abort test

21bc38e28 answers such an EndSSH with ^C (never a newline);
the abort test still expected nothing to be written.

2026-10-27: Put the F-920, F-921 and F-960 download code
where F-724 moved it

The F-920, F-921 and F-960 fixes were ported to the inline
download code before F-724 moved it into
ConductorDownload.swift.
ConductorDownload.downloadOrViewChoice now lives in
ConductorDownload, and the F-921 and F-960 tests are in
SSHRemoteZipReplyTests.swift and
ConductorDownloadTests.swift, as on master-fixes (05041a50d,
6c374cb88). No behavior changes.

2026-10-27: Put a floor of 15 under the screen's refresh
rate for Metal views

If a screen reports a maximum of 0 frames per second, the
Metal view divided by it (a crash) and its interval between
frames became infinite, so it would stop drawing. Use at
least 15.

QA finding F-726 (one possible cause; the reported freeze
was not reproduced).

2026-10-27: Clean up right away when a stalled SSH
Integration download is stopped

QA finding F-728. Stopping a Download using SSH Integration
download whose connection had stalled left the partial
hidden file .iTerm2.<UUID> in the Downloads folder, and the
download stayed listed as stopping. Stop only marked the
transfer stopped; removing the file and reporting the stop
waited until the download task got its next reply, which
never came.

Stop now removes the file and reports the download as
cancelled immediately, and cancels its fetches that haven't
been sent yet. When replies that were already sent arrive
later, they are ignored: nothing more is written, and a late
failure doesn't replace the cancellation. A download stopped
before its task starts no longer creates the file at all,
and a folder stopped while it is being zipped is not
downloaded and its archive is removed from the host.

2026-10-27: Translate the SSH Integration download
move-failure reason

Adds the catalog entry for
ConductorFileTransfer.CouldNotMoveIntoPlace (tangential
finding T-180 from the QA fix pass) with German, Japanese,
Brazilian Portuguese, Russian, and Simplified Chinese
translations, and renormalizes recently added entries.

2026-10-27: End a failed SSH Integration download in the
failed state

Tangential finding T-186 from the QA fix pass. When a
finished Download using SSH Integration file couldn't be
quarantined, or its attributes couldn't be read, the
transfer reported an error but never left the downloading
state, so it stayed listed as in progress with Stop
available. Both now go through fail(reason:), which removes
the temporary file and ends the transfer as failed.

A file that couldn't be quarantined is deleted rather than
kept, matching SCP and inline file downloads (SCPFile and
TerminalFile), and the user gets the existing could not be
quarantined or deleted warning if it can't be removed.

2026-10-27: Fail an SSH Integration download that can't be
moved into place

Tangential finding T-180 from the QA fix pass. When a
download started with Download using SSH Integration
finished but its hidden temporary file couldn't be moved to
its final name in the Downloads folder, the transfer
recorded the error yet still reported success, posted
Download finished, and pointed at a file that didn't exist.
It now fails with a reason naming the destination and the
system's error, removes the temporary file, and posts no
success notification.

2026-10-27: Download over SSH Integration with pipelined 4
KiB requests

QA finding F-725. A download started from Download using SSH
Integration (or by Command-clicking a remote file) asked for
1 KiB and waited for the reply before asking for the next,
so its speed was one round trip per kilobyte. A 12 MB folder
archive took about 17 minutes over loopback, while a file
fetched through the file panel, which asks for 4 KiB with 8
requests in flight, moved about 32 times faster. Both paths
now use the same chunk size and number of requests in
flight. Data is still written in order, a file that grew
since it was examined is still read to its end, and a stop
is noticed after each reply.

The stopped-during-the-last-request test now stops during
the request for the file's last bytes, since a file that
ends in a short chunk no longer needs an extra empty
request.

A folder download stopped while the folder is examined no
longer zips it, and one stopped while a request is
outstanding ends as stopped even if that request then fails,
instead of being reported as failed.

2026-10-27: Remove the hidden temp file when an SSH
Integration download fails

QA finding F-724. A download started from Download using SSH
Integration saves to a hidden file
~/Downloads/.iTerm2.<UUID> and renames it when done. When
the download failed (the folder couldn't be listed or
zipped, the name was mistyped, the connection dropped) or
was stopped, the file was left behind, empty or partly
written. The transfer now removes it whenever it fails or is
stopped before it is renamed, including when quarantining or
moving the finished file fails. A download stopped during
its last request now ends as stopped instead of staying
listed, and a zip reply naming a folder no longer goes on to
download after failing.

The download flow moved into ConductorDownload, with the
remote operations passed in, so it can be tested with fakes.

2026-10-27: Keep a long download status from running into
its size

QA finding F-723. In the Downloads and Uploads menus the
status and the size were each drawn across the whole bottom
row, so a long status such as Finished, with some items left
out ran into the size, and in German the two were drawn over
each other. The size now keeps the room it needs on the
right and the status gets the rest, truncating with an
ellipsis if it doesn't fit.

2026-10-27: Use the Russian word for sending in upload
strings

QA finding F-722. In Russian the upload notifications said
. and   ., which read as downloads, while the Uploads menu
is . Every Russian string about uploading now uses /,
matching   : the upload notifications, the Uploads menu
status, the upload indicator, SSH Integration and attachment
upload messages, the upload failure heading, the it2ul
advanced setting, and two tips.

2026-10-27: Say when a folder download over SSH Integration
left out files

QA finding F-721. Downloading a folder over SSH Integration
zips it on the remote host, and the framer silently skipped
any file it couldn't read, so the download reported success
and the archive just lacked the file. iTerm2 now asks the
framer to report what it left out (an extra "report-skipped"
argument that an older framer ignores; without it a newer
framer still replies with the bare archive path). The
download still finishes, but its notification says what
couldn't be read, for example 2 items couldnt be read and
were left out., naming up to two of them, and the Downloads
menu says Finished, with some items left out. Unreadable
subfolders are counted too, and a folder that can't be
listed at all now fails with a permission error instead of
downloading an empty archive.

2026-10-27: Give the Uploads menu a Russian title distinct
from Downloads

QA finding F-719 follow-up. Russian translated both
Downloads and Uploads as . Now that the menu bar shows the
translated submenu titles, that put two identical menus side
by side. Uploads is now , matching the existing Russian
Upload ().

2026-10-27: Give a folder download's archive permission
error the generic reason

QA finding F-718 follow-up. Downloading a folder over SSH
Integration first zips it into a temporary file in the
remote home folder. A permission error there said the file
couldn't be read, which is not what went wrong; it now gets
the generic permission reason.

2026-10-27: Discard a declined terminal-initiated file
instead of drawing it

QA finding F-720. Answering No to Allow Terminal-Initiated
Download? (or Allow Terminal-Initiated Display?) left the
terminal out of file-receiving mode, so the rest of the OSC
1337 File= payload was drawn as text: thousands of lines of
base64 for a large it2dl. A declined file is now consumed up
to its terminator and dropped, the way the
untrusted-terminal path already hid it, and output after it
is drawn normally. The untrusted path now drops the payload
in the terminal too instead of passing it to a download that
does not exist. A declined multipart file is unchanged,
since its parts are control sequences that are never drawn.

2026-10-27: Localize the Downloads and Uploads menu titles
and sizes

QA finding F-719. The size column of a file in the Downloads
and Uploads menus was built as %@ of %@ with an English of,
so German showed 1,2 MB of 20 MB. It is now a localized
phrase with positional arguments, translated for de, ja,
pt-BR, ru, and zh-Hans. The menu bar also kept showing
Downloads and Uploads in Japanese because it shows the
submenu's title, which was created in English; the submenus
now get the localized title.

2026-10-27: Say when an SSH Integration transfer fails for
lack of permission

QA finding F-718. A download of an unreadable remote file,
or an upload into a folder you can't write to, failed with
only The file operation failed on the remote host, so a
permission problem looked like any other failure. The framer
reported EACCES as a generic failure (its PermissionError
branch was unreachable behind OSError), and iTerm2 folded
every code other than ENOENT into one error. EACCES and
EPERM now come back as permission denied, and the
notification says the file couldn't be read or the
destination folder isn't writable. A path with a non-folder
in it also gets its own reason.

The framer's create command passed the wrong arguments to
file_error, so a failed create (such as the first step of an
upload into a read-only folder) never ended and the upload
waited forever. Malformed file commands no longer report
code 1, which now means permission denied, and zip of a
missing path reports ENOENT.

2026-10-27: Localize SSH Integration file transfer failure
reasons

QA finding F-717 follow-up. Several reasons an SSH
Integration download or upload can fail were English-only,
and since F-717 they appear as the body of the failure
notification. They are now localized complete sentences,
with translations for de, ja, pt-BR, ru, and zh-Hans. A
chunked download stopped from the Downloads menu now reports
the existing localized cancellation error instead of a bare
Canceled.

2026-10-27: Treat a canceled file transfer as canceled, not
failed

QA finding F-717 follow-up. Canceling the save panel of an
inline download, or declining to connect to a new host for
an SCP transfer, was reported as a failure, so the Downloads
menu said Failed with error and the notification read
Download failed for . with Canceled. as the reason. These
now stop the transfer the way the Stop menu item does: it is
listed as canceled and posts no notification. An error
reported for a transfer the user already stopped is also
treated as the cancellation it is, and a stopped browser
download now leaves Waiting to cancel once WebKit confirms.
An inline download whose save panel was canceled is named by
its suggested file name instead of Unnamed file.

2026-10-27: Name the file in failed transfer notifications

QA finding F-717. When a file transfer failed (for example
an SSH Integration download whose remote file was deleted
mid-transfer), the notification was only the bare reason,
such as File not found. It now says Download failed for
name. (or Upload failed) with the reason as its body,
matching the success notification. Browser download failures
now include the reason too. Stopping a transfer still posts
no notification.

Tests can intercept transfer notifications instead of
posting them.

2026-10-27: Start an injected remote bash as a login shell

Over SSH Integration, a bash login shell that got shell
integration injected was started as `-bash -c "/bin/bash
--posix"`. The outer shell sourced ~/.profile
non-interactively and exec'd the interactive one, which was
not a login shell, so what ~/.profile defined (aliases such
as the shell integration utilities) was gone.

For a plain interactive session whose injection added
arguments, the conductor now sends the framer `login <cwd>
--iterm2-exec-login <shell> <args>`, and the framer execs
that shell with argv[0] "-bash" so it is the login shell
itself and its profile runs exactly once, in the interactive
shell. Custom commands and env-only injections (zsh, fish,
xonsh, nu) are unchanged.

2026-10-27: Bump shell integration: it2ssh stops on a bare
^C and returns ssh's status

Pairs with the app answering an EndSSH it didn't start with
a bare ^C, so it2ssh no longer leaves the terminal waiting
for Return when ssh fails before connecting. Regenerates the
bundled it2ssh copies.

2026-10-27: Answer an it2ssh EndSSH no connection started
with a bare ^C

When ssh fails before connecting (a host that doesn't
resolve, a bad option), it2ssh still prints EndSSH and
waits, but no connection of the session started it, so
iTerm2 sent nothing and the terminal looked stuck until
Return. A newline can't be sent, since anything can print
EndSSH and a newline could run a command. Send a bare ^C
instead; a current it2ssh reads bytes and stops on it, and
an older one is no worse off.

2026-10-27: Log an unpauser released while still paused only
when that happens

Unpauser's deinit logged "Unpause in deinit! This should
never happen." every time, even after unpause() had already
run, which made normal paused side effects (such as starting
tmux mode) look like they leaked a pause.

2026-10-27: Explain why tmux integration can't start inside
a tmux integration session

Running tmux -CC in a tmux integration pane (for example
through it2ssh) reached startTmuxMode:, which returned
silently because the session is a tmux client. The remote
tmux client was left waiting in control mode with no
explanation. Show a warning that says so and that an empty
line ends the waiting client.

2026-10-27: Send nothing but the quit after an SSH
connection's login shell exits

When the remote login shell exited, the conductor told the
framer to quit, but an autopoll report that arrived
meanwhile was still answered.  The request waited for the
quit's reply and was then written, by which time ssh could
have exited, so the local terminal showed YXV0b3BvbGw=
(base64 autopoll), and its blank line could release it2ssh's
wait for the newline answering EndSSH. Once the login shell
exits the conductor now stops flow control, drops its queued
commands, and sends nothing but the quit.

Ported from gap-ssh-port 915f9caa2. The review fix that
keeps keys typed in that window for the shell that takes
over (F-783, b180a4483) is not included: it depends on the
let-go handling that is not on this branch.

QA findings F-709 and F-951.

2026-10-27: Frame keys typed in a connection nested in one
without a framer

An outer SSH Integration connection without a framer runs
the login shell straight on the tty and unhooks, so it
passes writes through. Its unhooked state still vetoed
queueWrites for a connection nested in it, so a framer the
inner connection started never got typed keys framed: they
reached it raw, it took them for malformed commands, and the
session took no input. A parent without a framer no longer
has a say.

QA finding F-971.

2026-10-27: Don't recover a restored SSH connection whose
ssh ended

When iTerm2 restarted after a crash and reattached to a job
whose ssh died while it was down, it forgot the restored SSH
Integration state (F-786), but the dead connection's unread
framer output then arrived outside any hook and started
framer recovery. That typed base64 recover into the local
shell and left a recovery conductor holding the keyboard.
Framer recovery is now refused for that connection, as it is
after Disconnect, until a new connection hooks.

QA finding F-970.

2026-10-27: Send tmux integration's opening ^C through the
SSH Integration framer

startTmuxMode wrote its ^C straight to the pty. With tmux
-CC running over SSH Integration, the session held it for
the framer, since the conductor queues writes, and flushed
it to the local tty when the framer unhooked, just before
the newline answering it2ssh's EndSSH.  it2ssh then read the
line ^C^C instead of the one it waits for and kept the
terminal at -echo until Return was pressed. The ^C now goes
where tmux commands go: through the framer when there is
one.

QA finding F-864.

2026-10-27: Don't type the pre-framer quit into a shell on
Disconnect

Once a connection without the framer (the host lacks its
Python) has run the login shell, conductor.sh has unhooked
and nothing reads conductor commands. Disconnect still wrote
the pre-framer quit, so the remote foreground job got a ^C
and the remote shell ran a line of base64. Disconnect is now
unavailable for such a connection, and Conductor.quit()
writes nothing for it. Disconnect still works while
conductor.sh is reading commands during setup.

QA finding F-950.

2026-10-27: Add a release note for the user@host parsing fix

2026-10-27: Report a failed context-menu download instead of
doing nothing

With the browser plugin installed, Download using SSH
Integration examined a file with a known MIME type before
asking whether to download or view it, and returned silently
when that failed. Download it instead so the failure is
reported in the Downloads menu with its reason.

QA finding F-960.

2026-10-27: Don't nest a folder downloaded over SSH
Integration inside itself

The remote archive holds the folder itself, and it was
unzipped into the folder, giving sub/sub/in.txt. Unzip
beside it and move the folder's contents in instead. An
empty folder's archive, which unzip rejects, now gives an
empty folder.

QA finding F-921.

2026-10-27: Fix crash finishing an SSH Integration download
on a user@host connection

it2ssh user@host left the user in the identity's host name,
so the quarantine source URL couldn't be made and its force
unwrap crashed when a Download Files download finished.
Split user@host like ssh does (and honor ssh:// URLs, whose
parsed host was being overwritten), and quarantine without a
source URL when one can't be made.

QA finding F-920.

2026-10-27: Keep a finished program's output out from under
the restart offer

The Session ended announcement covers the top rows of the
session, so a short program's output, like a custom command
that echoes a line, was hidden behind it and looked erased.
When the offer is shown, insert blank rows at the top of the
screen so the content and the message end at the bottom.
Only empty rows below the content are given up, scrollback
is untouched (a cleared screen stays cleared), and marks
move with their lines.

QA finding F-609.

2026-10-27: Test ending a nested it2ssh whose arguments
can't be read

QA finding F-708. When a nested it2ssh's arguments couldn't
be decoded, its conductor was told to quit and torn down at
once. Its hook's unhook then ended the outer conductor, and
its EndSSH went unanswered, so the tab stopped responding.
The F-705 fix on this branch (ignoring a deeper unhook, and
remembering every disconnected connection) covers it; this
adds the regression test at depth 1.

2026-10-27: Translate the unreadable it2ssh arguments banner

Adds the catalog entry for PTYSession.SSHArgumentsUnreadable
(QA finding F-608) with German, Japanese, Brazilian
Portuguese, Russian, and Simplified Chinese translations.

2026-10-27: Accept non-ASCII it2ssh arguments and never
leave the remote waiting

it2ssh quotes its arguments with bash 3.2's printf %q, which
can emit bytes that are not UTF-8. Decode the shell quoting
at the byte level instead of requiring UTF-8. If the hook
still can't be decoded, tell the remote conductor to quit
and explain why in a banner rather than leaving the session
stuck.

QA finding F-608.

2026-10-27: Bury a filtered tmux peer when a switch brings
pane %0 into its slot

A filter's stand-in is not a tmux client, so its tmuxPane
reads 0. When another client swapped the leader's pane %0
into the slot of a filtered peer, the new layout was
compared by tmuxPane, looked unchanged, and was applied as a
resize: the stand-in stayed, the peer was never buried, and
keystrokes went to the parked peer's pane. The comparison
now uses the pane the stand-in shows, as the rebuild already
does. The workgroup attacher's check of which panes a tab
shows does the same, so it no longer re-reads the layout on
every pane-list change while such a pane is filtered.

QA finding F-910.

2026-10-27: Rebuild the it2 binary

Picks up the it2cli change in e7bc90fa2 (QA finding F-911).
Built from this branch's sources rather than taking
18852700c, whose sources differ.

2026-10-27: it2: route by tmux session id on both sides

it2's tmux lookup prefers the iTerm2 client attached to the
pane's own tmux session, but it compared #{client_session}
(a session name, such as A) with the pane's #{session_id}
(such as $0). They never matched, so the order was only
newest attach first, and with two tmux -CC sessions of one
server over two connections, it2 in the older connection's
panes reached the newer connection. Ask list-clients for
#{session_id}, which is the client's session there, in both
it2cli and the Python it2 (submodule bump), and regenerate
the shell-integration outputs.

QA finding F-911.

2026-10-27: Keep macOS metadata out of the utilities archive

copy_shell_integration.sh built utilities.tgz with macOS
tar, which adds AppleDouble ._ entries and LIBARCHIVE.xattr
headers. GNU tar on Linux prints a warning for each header
and extracts the ._ files into ~/.iterm2. Build it with
COPYFILE_DISABLE=1 --no-mac-metadata --no-xattrs (as
6447e3e8b did on master-fixes), and rebuild the archive: the
same 21 entries with identical contents and modes, minus the
21 ._ entries and xattr headers.

QA finding F-881.

2026-10-27: Update shell integration: it2ssh option checks,
it2 tmux lookup, PATH order, nushell

Bumps the shell-integration submodule to qa-fixes-it2: -

--no-shell-integration (F-604, F-808) - 82b4bc3 it2: find
tmux through its server, and don't guess when the tmux
server can't be asked (F-610) - 7b871a5 Move ~/.iterm2/bin
to the front of PATH rather than only adding it when missing
(F-600 follow-up) - 3eadace nushell: load in tmux, put
~/.iterm2/bin first on PATH, keep other builds' it2 (F-616,
F-812, F-813)

Regenerates the bundled scripts, it2.py, the it2 wrapper and
utilities.tgz from it, which also puts the it2ssh fix into
what Install Shell Integration & Utilities installs (F-807).
The it2ssh test runs the submodule's copy again, and new
tests cover the it2 tmux lookup and each shell moving
~/.iterm2/bin to the front, including under zsh options and
