QA findings F-604, F-808, F-610, F-600, F-616, F-812, F-813
and F-807.

2026-10-27: Notice new flow control events once the
monitor's list is full

The controller keeps the last 40 events, so their count
stops changing; compare the latest one's time.

QA finding F-716.

2026-10-27: Redraw the flow control monitor only when it
changes or once a second

The monitor drew its charts on every sample, ten times a
second, which cost an idle iTerm2 about a fifth of a core.
It now redraws at once when what it shows changes (bytes,
window, statistics, events, round trips, the reason flow
control is off), otherwise once a second so the time axis
keeps moving, and not at all while its window is hidden or
miniaturized, catching up when it is seen again.

QA finding F-716.

2026-10-27: Let Cmd-W close the flow control monitor

Shell > Close sends closeCurrentSession:, which the
monitor's panel did not handle. A panel never becomes the
main window, so with it key the action went on to the
terminal window behind and closed a session there. The panel
now closes itself, as its close button does.

Tangential bug found while fixing QA finding F-707.

2026-10-27: Don't claim flow control was never tried on a
stopped connection

A connection whose controller was stopped has no recorded
reason; say only that flow control is not running.

QA finding F-701.

2026-10-27: Keep the flow control monitor on across a
restart

Closing the monitor's window turns off the advanced setting
that shows it, but quitting iTerm2 closes the window too, so
the monitor never came back after a restart. Only a close
the person asks for, which AppKit confirms with
windowShouldClose, turns the setting off now.

QA finding F-707.

2026-10-27: Say why a connection is unpaced as decided when
it started

The flow control monitor worked out why a connection had no
controller from the current setting, so after Pace output
from remote hosts was turned back on, a connection opened
while it was off claimed its framer did not support flow
control. The conductor now records the reason when it
decides not to pace (setting off, waiting for its parent,
the parent not framing it, the framer refusing, flow on
aborted), and the monitor shows that.

QA finding F-701.

2026-10-27: Let Download Files choose any file (F-895)

The ssh Download Files panel filtered files by the AI
provider's attachment types, copied from the chat attachment
picker, so archives, binaries and most other files could not
be selected.

2026-10-27: Say what the tmux pane lookups' origin scoping
actually guarantees

The comments claimed a remote caller can't name another
connection's origin, but any client that evaluates an
expression, including a remote it2 through `it2 session list
--format`, chooses it. The scoping keeps it2's own pane
commands from getting answers about another machine's
servers; it is not access control, and such a client can
already list every session.

QA finding F-867.

2026-10-27: Give up on an unresponsive host quickly during
uninstall and say so

After the uninstall confirmation, a wedged connection held
things up for about two minutes, mostly waiting 30 seconds
at a time for the host's machine token, and the failure then
said iTerm2 couldn't tell whether the host is this Mac.
During uninstall the machine token now has the same 3 second
deadline as the check before the confirmation, and a host
that doesn't answer is reported as not responding.

QA finding F-868.

2026-10-27: Honor Stop Allowing during uninstall even when
the hook stays

Uninstall asks whether to stop always allowing a host to use
the API, but revoked the grant only when the hook was
removed, so for a host it then refused to touch or that
didn't answer, the answer was silently dropped. The grant is
now revoked once the removal finishes, whatever its result,
and for a host that disconnected after the confirmation.

QA finding F-866.

2026-10-27: Treat nu named as the it2ssh command as a shell,
so it gets shell integration

it2ssh host /opt/homebrew/bin/nu started nu without iTerm2's
nu integration. nu was missing from the names that make a
command a shell, so it was run as an ordinary command, which
is never injected (it got only ~/.iterm2/bin on PATH). It is
now a shell named as the command, injected like zsh, fish
and xonsh: NU_VENDOR_AUTOLOAD_DIR points at the uploaded
script, which nu sources at startup.

QA finding F-865.

2026-10-27: Answer the EndSSH of an it2ssh whose connection
aborted

it2ssh prints EndSSH after ssh exits and waits, with the
terminal at -echo -isig, for iTerm2 to send a newline.
iTerm2 sent it for a connection it still had, or one that
had quit, but a connection that aborted was forgotten, so
its EndSSH went unanswered and it2ssh held the terminal
until Return was pressed. An aborted connection is now
remembered for its EndSSH as a quit one is. An EndSSH no
connection of this session started is still not answered,
since any output can contain one.

QA finding F-864 (the repro's exact path is not confirmed;
an abort after the tmux detach is the likely one).

2026-10-27: Refuse an it2 prompt for a tmux pane this
connection doesn't show instead of waiting forever

A pane of another connection's tmux session on the same
server could reach this connection through the socket it
inherited from the tmux server. Its authorization prompt
waited for the pane to appear in this connection's tmux
client, which it never does, so no prompt was shown and that
it2 call, and every later one on the connection, hung. A
prompt for a pane in no window layout of this client is now
refused at once without remembering a decision; one still
waits for a pane in a hidden window. Prompts still waiting
when the tmux client detaches are refused too, so later
calls are not stuck behind them.

QA finding F-863.

2026-10-27: Put ~/.iterm2/bin on PATH once for a shell named
as the it2ssh command

A shell named as the command that could not be injected got
~/.iterm2/bin from both the framer (IT2_PUT_BIN_ON_PATH) and
its command's PATH prefix, so it was on PATH twice. The
framer now does it only for the login shell itself, when
there is no command.

QA finding F-862.

2026-10-27: Don't inject macOS's bash named as the it2ssh
command under another login shell

Whether bash can be injected was decided from getshell's
version string, which describes the login shell. For it2ssh
host bash with a zsh or tcsh login shell on macOS, that
never matched the bash 3.2 check, so /bin/bash ran with
--posix and an ENV it never reads: no shell integration,
POSIX mode left on, and it2 not on PATH. The version now
counts only when the login shell is that bash; otherwise
bash or /bin/bash on a macOS host is taken to be macOS's own
and gets it2 on PATH the way an uninjected shell does.

QA finding F-860.

2026-10-27: Name a remote session's job after the foreground
job on its terminal

A local session's job name is the deepest foreground job
whose stdin or stdout is the terminal, so claude's
caffeinate child, with its stdio on /dev/null, does not
rename it. A remote session could not see file descriptors
and used the deepest foreground job, so Session Status
showed Claude Code (caffeinate) during a turn. framer now
marks foreground processes whose stdio is not a terminal
(from /proc, or lsof where there is none), and the remote
job name follows the same rule as the local one. A session
on a host reached through SSH Integration also looks its job
up by the login shell's pid on that host, which is how its
processes are known, rather than by the local ssh's pid.

QA finding F-840.

2026-10-27: Don't save window restoration state on tmux
attach or detach

Attaching and detaching tmux saved window restoration state
at once, which encodes it on the main thread. They now only
mark it to be saved, at the next resign-active or quit. A
session restored as a gateway after a detach still checks
that tmux runs in it before sending tmux anything, so
restoring after a crash stays safe. The case where iTerm2 is
killed while active, without switching away since attaching,
is no longer covered by an immediate save (QA finding
F-656), by the user's choice; the release note says when it
is.

2026-10-27: Name the filtered session in its close
confirmation

With Find > Filter or Instant Replay open, the close
confirmation named the stand-in (for a tmux pane, tmux), not
the session that closes. It now names the session the
stand-in shows.

QA finding F-846.

2026-10-27: Keep a tmux workgroup's leader in place when
detaching

Detaching ends every tmux session here, and each follows its
profile's end action. A workgroup's members are pinned to
the default action so they stay, like a plain tmux window's
panes, but the leader followed its profile and closed when
that says to close sessions that end, leaving its slot empty
beside the split. While in a workgroup, the leader now stays
at a detach too. Outside a detach it keeps its own end
action.

QA finding F-845.

2026-10-27: Refit filter stand-ins in tmux tabs when their
views change

tmux sizes the grids of its clients, but a filter's stand-in
in a tmux tab is not one, so it kept the size it was given
when it was swapped in. A filter put back while the tab was
still settling after a peer switch from another Mac was then
resized by later layouts and toolbar changes, and the rows
its grid didn't cover drew as a black band. Stand-ins are
now refit after each layout from tmux and after title bar or
toolbar changes.

QA finding F-833.

2026-10-27: Fit a filter's stand-in to its view in tmux tabs

A filter's stand-in starts with the grid of the session it
shows. Outside tmux tabs it is then fit to its own view, but
in a tmux tab it was skipped, though tmux doesn't size the
stand-in. A tmux workgroup member has a toolbar the stand-in
lacks, so the stand-in's view was taller than its grid and
the rows it didn't cover drew as a black band. It is now fit
in tmux tabs too.

QA finding F-833.

2026-10-27: Erase the user's typing before exiting the
installer's helper sh

After Cancel, the installer types exit once the helper sh
shows it reads commands again. Text the user typed in the
terminal in the meantime stayed on the helper's line, so the
helper read something like "echoexit", which is not exit,
and the user was left in the helper.

The exit now follows a Control-U, which erases what is on
the line: the tty does that for an sh without a line editor,
such as dash, and it is the default binding of the line
editors of bash, zsh and others. Only the typed exit has it,
not the commands shown for pasting by hand.

QA finding F-883.

2026-10-27: Start the Internet-Free install's helper sh with
one line

The installer started its helper shells by typing three
lines at once into the user's shell: one that starts an sh,
one that starts a second sh inside it with ENV set, and one
that sets the prompts. While bash's line editor reads the
first line the tty is in raw mode, and when bash puts it
back in canonical mode to run that line, Linux hands
everything still queued to the next read() as one line. So
dash, as the outer sh, read the other two lines at once, ran
the one that starts the inner sh, and kept the rest for
after that sh exited. The inner sh never got its prompts,
and the step waited forever.

The helper is now started with one line, sh -c '', which
every supported shell parses the same way and which sets ENV
and the prompts itself before it becomes the helper with
exec env. It prints the ready marker right before the
helper's first prompt. Nothing is typed until that prompt
shows up, so no line can reach the wrong shell. There is one
helper instead of two, so a step ends with one exit, and the
depth check after a canceled launch expects one.

The real-dash pty test now types the launch in raw mode and
keeps canonical mode off until the helper prompts, so the
outer shell reads everything typed ahead at once, as on
Linux. It hung before this change.  The fake terminals check
that nothing but empty lines is typed ahead of the helper.

QA finding F-882.

2026-10-27: Send the shell integration script to the helper
sh in short lines

The Internet-Free install typed the shell integration script
into the helper sh as one here-document. dash, the sh of
Debian and Ubuntu, reads it from the tty in canonical mode,
and Linux's tty keeps at most 4095 bytes of a line, so the
end of each longer line (the embedded it2.py line is over
10000 bytes) was lost and the written script was broken:
every login shell reported an unexpected EOF.

The script now goes the way the utilities archive does:
base64-encoded in 76-column lines, typed only after sh says
its read loop is reading into a temporary file. sh decodes
it, compares it with the POSIX cksum of the script, and only
then copies it into place and makes it executable, printing
whether it did. The step reports a failure instead of
success otherwise, and Cancel stops typing the script as it
does the archive, writing nothing. The path goes in a
variable so the command stays short of macOS's 1024-byte
line limit.

Tests: the fake dash drops what goes past 4095 bytes in a
line, every install checks that no typed line reaches 1024
bytes, the receive command runs in real sh and dash, and a
script with a line over 4096 bytes is written byte for byte
through real dash in a pty.

QA finding F-880.

2026-10-27: Don't hang the Internet-Free install where sh is
dash

The installer waited for the helper sh prompt at the start
of a row before typing anything after the launch. dash, the
sh of Debian and Ubuntu, has no line editor, so the launch
lines typed ahead are not echoed, and its prompt lands on
the row after the helpers' own default prompts (or an
exported PS1), as in "$ $ >> ". No row started with it, so
the first step never went on.

The line that sets the helper prompt now also prints a
marker with no newline (written with empty quotes, so its
echo can't match), and the helper prompt counts at the start
of a row or right after that marker.  The dotfile step's
first wait uses the same match.

dash also leaves echoing to the tty, which echoes typed text
before sh prints its prompts, so the script step's wait for
"> EOF" would hang next and the dotfile step's wait for the
prompt after fi depended on timing. Both now wait for an
answer the helper prints after the here-document or fi.

Tests run every helper scenario against a fake dash that
doesn't echo typeahead and doesn't redraw lines, and start
the helpers in real /bin/dash in a pty.

QA finding F-870.

2026-10-26: Rebuild the it2 binary

Picks up the it2cli change in 936a28bb2 (QA finding F-610).

2026-10-26: Let Disconnect end an SSH connection before its
framer starts

Disconnect no longer refuses with Cant Disconnect when the
conductor isn't framing, as when it2ssh was killed during
setup or the host lacks the framer's Python.
Conductor.quit() sends the pre-framer quit in that case, and
the forced unhook and the refusal of framer recovery apply
as they do for a framing connection. Without automatic
detection of a killed it2ssh, the refusal left such a
session hooked with no way out.

Removes the warning's strings, which nothing else used, and
Conductor.minimumPythonVersionForFramer, which only the
warning read.

2026-10-26: Remove automatic detection of a killed it2ssh

Removes SSHClientMonitor's watch of the local it2ssh process
and the session's automatic abandonment of the connection
when it exits, the SecretServer's LOCAL_PEERPID recording of
the ancestry of the process that fetched a token, the
SSH_UNFRAMED_PROMPT token and its plumbing from
VT100ConductorParser to PTYSession, and the SSH connection
...  ended unexpectedly announcement (and its string catalog
entries).  Conductor.abandon() and its write guards go too:
only the abandon path called it. Disconnect never used it.

A killed it2ssh is now handled by the user choosing
Disconnect, which still forces the hook off after 3 seconds,
answers every disconnected connection's EndSSH, and refuses
framer recovery and hooks from the disconnected connection.
Disconnect now also stops holding back keystrokes for a
connection that was still being set up, which the abandon
path used to do; otherwise a killed it2ssh during setup
would leave typed keys queued forever.

SSHClientMonitor.swift is renamed
SSHClientProcessLocator.swift and keeps only the check that
a restored session's job still runs ssh (F-786).  The tests
that drove the kept refusals through the abandon path now
drive them through Disconnect. The tests that a conductor
abandoned after its it2ssh died writes nothing go with
abandon().

Undoes QA finding F-482 (24bfcbcb8, the automatic part;
Disconnect's forced unhook from 2ac96e3c1 stays). The
holding of typed keys while the announcement was up (F-820,
1129c9ce2 and 666f2f39f) and sessionDidLetGo() (F-703) were
never applied to this branch.

2026-10-26: Remove killing of orphaned ssh clients

Removes the local SIGKILL of the ssh client a killed it2ssh
left running (the record of it2ssh's ssh child, the
ControlMaster and identity checks, and the iTermLSOF socket
inspection) and the PTYSession calls that sent it.

Also removes F-553's handling of a cut-off framer header in
VT100XtermParser. The header was cut off because iTerm2
SIGKILLed the ssh client while it was writing; an ssh client
that is not killed finishes writing its frame, so an OSC 134
it interleaves with the shell's output is terminated by its
own next write. Ending such a header at a line break would
instead draw the rest of it as text.

Undoes QA findings F-351 (ecd1df6fe), its SIGKILL follow-up
(0932bb993), and the framer header part of F-553
(c2b9f75b3). The framer's killorphan command for a nested
it2ssh (F-704, 85068e0f0) and its fail-safe check (F-782,
1771207be) were never applied to this branch, so there is
nothing of them to remove.

2026-10-26: Remove tty mode repair after a killed it2ssh

Removes TTYModeRepairer: the tty mode snapshot taken when
it2ssh fetches its token, the repair at the prompt and at
OSC 133 C, the watcher for forks of the shell that ran
it2ssh, setting modes through the pty's slave, and the flush
of unread tty input and of PTYTask's pending writes when a
connection was abandoned as soon as its it2ssh was found
dead.  A killed it2ssh is a rare case and this machinery was
far more complex than the problem warranted.

Undoes QA findings F-460 (8a840cd7e), F-541 (4dbb550b8), the
tty mode part of F-553 (c2b9f75b3), and the input flush part
of F-554.

2026-10-26: Don't judge a connection back to this Mac as
having a remote Claude Code hook

The remote hook status had no this-Mac check, and the
installer's pre-marking of the remote step called it
directly, so for a connection back to this Mac the step
could be marked done from this Mac's own settings. The
status now reports not installed for this Mac and unknown
for a host it can't tell apart from this Mac, which covers
every caller.

QA finding F-816.

2026-10-26: Show the Claude Code uninstall confirmation
promptly

Before the confirmation, Uninstall asks each connected host
whether it is this Mac, and waited up to 30 seconds per
question, so a wedged connection left the menu item doing
nothing visible for that long. The question now has a 3
second deadline. A host that doesn't answer in time stays in
the dialog, and the removal checks again and leaves it alone
if it still can't tell.

QA finding F-814.

2026-10-26: Don't edit a remote host's Claude Code settings
when it might be this Mac

When a host's machine token couldn't be read (no answer, a
Mac that couldn't compute it, or a version this Mac doesn't
implement), its locality was unknown, the this-Mac check
said no, and the remote install or uninstall went ahead, so
a connection back to this Mac could still edit this Mac's
settings. Both now refuse such a host and say why.

QA finding F-815.

2026-10-26: Accept a blank remote Claude Code settings.json
and skip this Mac on uninstall

Uninstalling with a live SSH Integration connection whose
settings.json was empty reported that it couldn't be parsed
as JSON. The remote install, status and uninstall now treat
an empty or whitespace-only file as an empty object, through
a parser shared with the local install and uninstall.

Uninstall also had no check for a host that is this Mac
reached over ssh, which Install refuses: the confirmation
named it and the removal edited this Mac's settings through
the connection. Such hosts are now left out of the
confirmation, checked in parallel before it appears, and the
remote uninstall itself leaves this Mac's settings alone. A
second Uninstall click while the hosts are being checked is
ignored.

QA finding F-606.

2026-10-26: Stop calling the Claude Code hook cc-status in
user-visible text

Installs now write an it2 handle-agent-hook command, but the
onboarding list, the uninstall confirmation, the
hook-removal failure alert, the kept-hooks notice and the
broken-hook warning still called it the cc-status hook. They
now call it iTerm2's status hook. The uninstall confirmation
also named a menu item that reads Reinstall in some states;
it now points at the iTerm2 menu instead. Translations
updated for de, ja, pt-BR, ru and zh-Hans.

QA finding F-605.

2026-10-26: framer: remove a connection's /tmp it2 socket
directory when it ends

For a home too long for a socket path, each connection's it2
socket goes in a fresh /tmp/.it2-<hex> directory, which was
never removed. The framer now removes it with the socket on
exit, including on SIGHUP and SIGTERM, and a framer starting
in /tmp sweeps this user's directories that no live framer
holds a lock in.

QA finding F-752.

2026-10-26: Give a remote workgroup peer the PATH of the
user's interactive shell

A peer of a leader on a host reached through SSH Integration
runs as a remote command, which the login shell runs with
-c: zsh skips .zshrc and macOS's bash reads no profile, so
the Code Review peer could not find claude where Claude
Code's installer put it. The peer's /bin/sh script now asks
the user's shell, started as an interactive login shell with
no input, for its PATH and runs the command with it,
~/.iterm2/bin first. Only PATH is taken, so nothing else the
startup files do runs in the peer. A peer started as a pane
of the leader's framer runs the same script.

QA finding F-750.

2026-10-26: framer: take IT2_PUT_BIN_ON_PATH out of its own
environment

The flag was removed only from the environment the framer
gives the login shell, so every other process the framer
started inherited it. The framer now takes it out of its own
environment when it starts and remembers it.

QA finding F-810.

2026-10-26: Exec a shell named as the it2ssh command instead
of running it under the login shell

it2ssh host bash ran PATH=...; export PATH; 'bash' under the
login shell's -c, and bash 3.2 does not exec the last
command of a list, so an extra non-interactive bash sat
between the framer and the user's shell. A shell named as
the command is now exec'd, in the login shell's syntax, when
it is one simple command. The choice of syntax and of exec
is now a tested function.

QA finding F-809.

2026-10-26: it2ssh: refuse --no-shell-integration wherever
ssh reads options after the host

Only the first word after the host was checked, so it2ssh
host -t --no-shell-integration went ahead with shell
integration loaded. Words after the host are now read as ssh
reads them, as options (with their values) until the first
word that is not one or --, and the flag is refused anywhere
among them.

QA finding F-808.

2026-10-26: Align and localize the API access over ssh
button and its sheet

The button in Settings > General > Magic now lines up with
Manage Permissions above it. It and the Remote Hosts Allowed
sheet (and the Always Allow This Host choice the sheet
refers to) are translated into German, Japanese, Brazilian
Portuguese, Russian and Simplified Chinese, and the sheet's
dates follow the UI language as other formatted values do.

QA finding F-611.

2026-10-26: Don't let a remote it2 in an unplaced tmux pane
act on a tmux gateway

A tmux pane inherits the IT2_SOCK of whichever connection
started its tmux server. When it2 could not read the tmux
client records, a call from another connection's pane
arrived on that connection, could not be placed, and
defaulted to the session it arrived on, the tmux gateway:
it2 session set-name renamed the other connection's gateway
and reported success. A tmux gateway is no longer the
default target of a call from a tmux pane, and a remote call
with nothing to act on now fails with a clear message
instead of using the focused session.

QA finding F-610.

2026-10-26: it2ssh: exit 1 after usage, and refuse a
misplaced --no-shell-integration

With set -e and pipefail, ssh's usage status (255) ended
it2ssh before its exit 1. A --no-shell-integration that is
not the first argument, where ssh would read it as an option
(among the options, or right after the host unless -- ended
them), was ignored and the session loaded shell integration
anyway; it2ssh now refuses it and says to put it first. A --
before the host is now recognized; it used to be taken for
an option whose value was the host.

QA finding F-604.

2026-10-26: Put it2 on PATH for a shell named as the it2ssh
command

it2ssh host sh -c ... and it2ssh host bash (macOS bash 3.2)
were treated as custom shells, so they got neither injection
nor the PATH prepend a command gets, and it2 was not found.
An injected shell given -c (it2ssh host zsh -c ...) missed
out too, since the scripts load only in an interactive
shell. Such a shell now runs through
commandPuttingIT2OnPath, in the login shell's syntax. An
interactive injected shell is still left to its scripts.

QA finding F-603.

2026-10-26: Put ~/.iterm2/bin on PATH for a login shell that
gets it2 without shell integration

tcsh is never injected, so nothing put ~/.iterm2/bin on its
PATH and it2 was not found by name over it2ssh although it
was uploaded. The conductor now asks the framer, through
IT2_PUT_BIN_ON_PATH, to prepend the directory to the PATH it
gives the login shell and workgroup panes.

QA finding F-601.

2026-10-26: Turning off host control from the menu revokes
the host's grant everywhere

Unchecking Remote host can control iTerm2 forgot the Always
Allow record but left other live connections to the host
that the grant had let in working with no prompt. It now
revokes the grant as Settings does, so those connections ask
again.

QA finding F-602.

2026-10-26: Keep ~/.iterm2/bin first on PATH after a login
zsh's startup files

The injected .zshenv loads shell integration, which prepends
~/.iterm2/bin, before /etc/zprofile's path_helper and
~/.zprofile run, so over it2ssh the directory ended up last
and an it2 earlier on PATH ran instead of iTerm2's. A
one-shot precmd hook now moves it to the front before the
first prompt.

QA finding F-600.

2026-10-26: Show a local tmux server's pane processes in
Toolbelt Jobs

When tmux runs on this Mac but the gateway session is an SSH
Integration connection (for example it2ssh to 127.0.0.1), a
pane's processes are this Mac's, and its job comes from this
Mac's process cache. The Jobs tool asked the gateway's
provider instead, the connection's remote process table,
which doesn't track the pane, so it stayed empty. It now
uses this Mac's processes for a pane of a local tmux server.
Debug logging at each choice (the provider, the pane pid
fetch) shows which applies if it still doesn't.

QA finding F-612.

2026-10-26: Don't send tmux commands to a shell after
restoring a detached gateway

Detaching from tmux left the saved window restoration state
saying the session was a tmux gateway, since it was saved
when tmux attached. If iTerm2 was then killed, the restored
session took itself for a gateway, printed that tmux sent an
unrecognized command, and typed its tmux commands into the
shell. Restoration state is now saved when tmux detaches
too. And a session restored as a gateway whose foreground
job turns out to be a shell leaves tmux mode before its
delayed writes go out, discarding them.

QA finding F-832.

2026-10-26: Drop the workgroup toolbar from tmux workgroup
members that stay

When a workgroup on a tmux server ends here, as at a detach,
its split and tab members stay as ordinary panes. Their
toolbars were not refreshed, so the tab member, in a tab of
its own, kept its workgroup bar. Each kept member's toolbar
is now refreshed.

QA finding F-831.

2026-10-26: Kill a filtered tmux pane when closing it

Closing a session kills its tmux pane, but with Find >
Filter or Instant Replay open the session closed is the
stand-in, which is not a tmux client, so it was only
terminated along with the session it showed and the pane
stayed on the server. For a member of a tmux workgroup that
left the workgroup half alive: the pane showed as an
ordinary pane, its peers stayed parked, and Exit Workgroup
did nothing. Closing a stand-in now kills the pane it shows,
so the workgroup ends as it does when the member itself is
closed.

QA finding F-830.

2026-10-26: Keep a tmux workgroup peer created this attach
when detaching

Detaching ends every tmux session here, and a session whose
profile closes sessions that end closes then. Adopted peers
and other members are pinned to the default end action, but
a peer whose pane was created during this attach was not, so
it closed and left its slot empty. It is now pinned like the
others.

QA finding F-652.

2026-10-26: Forget the current tmux window when selecting it
failed

After select-window failed because tmux had already closed
the window, the controller still took it as the current
window, and every later reorder of tabs selected it again,
failing each time. It is now forgotten when its selection
fails.

QA finding F-804.

2026-10-26: Ask again where a tmux workgroup's leader is
before ending it

Removing a tmux workgroup from the server ended the instance
whatever its queries returned. If tmux failed to say which
window the leader was in, a parked leader was not brought
out and stayed hidden, with nothing logged. The query is now
asked again before the instance ends, and each failure is
logged.

QA finding F-803.

2026-10-26: Ask before closing a filtered tmux workgroup
member

With Find > Filter or Instant Replay open, Cmd-W closes the
stand-in that shows a session. The check for closing a
member of a tmux workgroup, and the workgroup warning
itself, looked at the stand-in, which is in no workgroup, so
it closed without asking and ended the workgroup. Both now
look at the session the stand-in shows.

QA finding F-801.

2026-10-26: Count a filtered workgroup peer in view as in
its tab

A filter or Instant Replay shows a session through a
stand-in, which its tab holds instead of the session.
Deciding whether a peer is in a tab when its workgroup ends
looked only for the peer, so a filtered peer in view counted
as buried and was terminated in place, or, on a tmux server,
ended instead of being left to tmux. A peer whose stand-in
is in the tab now counts as in it.

QA finding F-800.

2026-10-26: Keep the leader's window when exiting a
workgroup whose peer was shown

A peer that was shown and then switched away keeps its tab
as its delegate while buried. Ending the workgroup took that
as the peer being in the tab and closed it through the tab,
and the tab, holding only the leader, closed with it, taking
the window. A peer now counts as in a tab only if the tab
has it, so a buried one is terminated and the leader stays.

QA finding F-713.

2026-10-26: Refuse workgroups in tmux Integration with tmux
older than 3.2

A workgroup in a tmux Integration tab keeps its state on the
tmux server, which needs tmux 3.2 or later. With an older
tmux, entering one fell back to native splits and tabs,
which tmux's next layout change removed, ending the
workgroup with no message. Entering is now refused there:
the Workgroups menu disables its entries, with a tooltip
saying why, and choosing one another way explains that tmux
3.2 or later is needed.

QA finding F-619.

2026-10-26: Test that closing a tmux workgroup member leaves
its peers to tmux

Closing a member (Cmd-W on the split) ends the workgroup
through the same teardown as Exit Workgroup, so the peer in
view is left for tmux's layout and the leader's window no
longer empties and hides. Covers that path too.

QA finding F-618.

2026-10-26: Ask before closing a member of a tmux workgroup

Closing a member of a workgroup in a tmux Integration tab
ends the workgroup and kills its other members' panes, but
it could close without asking, because tmux hides panes'
jobs and the usual reasons to ask didn't apply. Closing one
now asks first with the same warning that the workgroup will
end and its other sessions close.

QA finding F-618.

2026-10-26: Don't crash making a tab active after it left
its window

tmux can report that a window became current just after
iTerm2 closed its tab, as when closing a member of a tmux
workgroup tears it down. Making that tab active asked the
window's tab view to select an item it no longer had, which
raises an exception. The tab is now left alone when its tab
view doesn't have it.

QA finding F-618.

2026-10-26: Adopt a tmux workgroup when its leader's window
opens later

With more tmux windows than the Dashboard limit, attaching
opens the tmux Dashboard instead of the windows, so a
workgroup's leader had no session when the workgroup would
be adopted and it was skipped. Opening the leader's window
later showed its panes as ordinary panes until the next
attach. Now the workgroup state is read again, once, when
the leader's pane gets a session, which adopts it.

QA finding F-615.

2026-10-26: Leave a tmux workgroup's peers in view to tmux
when it ends

Exit Workgroup swaps the leader back in through tmux and
kills the members' panes, but it also closed the peers in
view here at once. With two peer groups each showing a peer
in the leader's window, that emptied the window's tab before
tmux's layout brought the leader back, and closing a tmux
tab's last session hides its window, so the leader's window
disappeared and was listed as hidden. Peers in tabs of a
workgroup on a tmux server are now left for tmux's layout to
remove, as its other members already were.

QA finding F-614.

2026-10-26: Keep Toolbelt Jobs following its session's root
pid and process provider

Toolbelt > Jobs read its session's root pid and process info
provider only when the toolbelt was refreshed. A tmux pane
on an SSH Integration host can get them later without that
happening, when its connection starts framing or recovers,
so the tool kept reading a provider without the pane's
processes and stayed empty while the tab showed the pane's
job. The job tree now asks its host for both before each
periodic refresh, and setting an unchanged value does
nothing.

QA finding F-612.

2026-10-26: Save window restoration state when a tmux
gateway attaches

Window restoration state is saved only when iTerm2 resigns
active or quits. If iTerm2 stayed active from attaching tmux
-CC until it was killed, the saved state lacked the
gateway's arrangement, so on relaunch its still-running
session was adopted as an orphan showing raw control-mode
output, and tmux integration did not resume. Saving once the
gateway knows its tmux session lets restoration take the
existing path that resumes tmux integration.

QA finding F-656.

2026-10-26: Leave parked tmux workgroup windows out of the
tmux Dashboard

The Dashboard listed windows holding parked workgroup peers
with the hidden windows, where opening one did nothing and
the remove button killed it, ending the whole workgroup.
They are now left out, as the hidden-window menu already
does.

QA finding F-655.

2026-10-26: Bury a filtered tmux workgroup member when a
peer switch parks it

With Find > Filter open on a workgroup member in a tmux
Integration tab, the tab shows a stand-in session in its
place. When a peer switch parked the member's pane, the
stand-in was found among the sessions leaving the tab and
terminated, and so was the member's session behind it, so
switching back built a fresh session from tmux's history.
The stand-in is now ended as leaving a filter does and the
member's session is buried, and its filter is put back when
it returns as the active session.

QA finding F-654.

2026-10-26: Apply user variables a new tmux workgroup peer
sets before its session exists

A peer created in a parked window can print its first prompt
before iTerm2 learns the window is parked and gives the pane
a buried session. That output was dropped and the pane's
screen came from capture-pane, so the user variables its
prompt set were lost until the next prompt. The control
sequences captured from it are now executed when the buried
session is filled, as for a window being opened. A parked
window also stopped counting as awaiting its layout, which
had kept every later new pane's dropped output being
scanned.

QA finding F-651.

2026-10-26: Keep a tmux workgroup's peer in view when
detaching

Detaching terminates the buried sessions, the parked
leader's among them, which ends the workgroup on this Mac.
Ending it closed the peer in view, leaving an empty area in
the window where it had been. When the tmux connection is
gone, peers in tabs now stay in place like any other pane of
a detached window; only buried ones end.

QA finding F-652.

2026-10-26: Keep focus in a tmux workgroup's slot when
switching peers

Switching peers swaps the peer's pane into the slot, and the
tab is rebuilt from tmux's layout. The active member's pane
was gone from that layout, so focus went to its nearest
neighbor, the split member, and iTerm2 then told tmux that
pane was active. Now the peer that takes the active member's
place becomes active, as in a local workgroup, which also
makes it tmux's active pane.

QA finding F-650.

2026-10-26: Don't end a tmux connection selecting a window
tmux already closed

A tmux workgroup's teardown kills its members' panes. The
native tab of a killed window can become current before
tmux's %window-close arrives, and the select-window sent for
it failed, which aborted the whole connection. select-window
now tolerates errors, and a failed one no longer leaves a
window-change notification counted as expected.

QA finding F-653.

2026-10-26: Finish SSH Integration downloads in the
Downloads menu

QA finding F-714. A file fetched through the file panel
(Shell > ssh > Download Files, or attaching a remote file)
is saved by the panel itself, so its transfer was never told
it ended and stayed listed as Downloading 20 MB of 20 MB. It
is now marked finished (or failed) when the panel's download
ends. Also, a conductor transfer stopped counting as a
download once it finished, so Clear All never removed
finished SSH Integration downloads and their notification
said Upload finished; the direction is now kept.

Tests can turn off opening the Downloads menu, which pressed
it through accessibility and stopped the test runner.

2026-10-26: Don't reset a framer that didn't survive a
restart

QA finding F-786. After a restart, a session that reattached
to its job server reset its SSH Integration framers, but
reattaching proves only that the job's shell survived. If
ssh had died while iTerm2 was down, the reset (base64 reset2
and a UUID) was typed into the local shell. The session now
resets only if an it2ssh or ssh client still runs in the
job, and otherwise forgets the saved SSH Integration state.

2026-10-26: Recognize a late unhook by its hook, not just
its depth

QA finding F-787. A conductor nested in one without a framer
has the same depth as its parent, so after Disconnect on the
inner connection its late unhook passed the depth check and
the outer conductor took it as its own end. The unhook token
now carries its hook's ID, and the session ignores the
unhook of a hook whose conductor it already let go of,
unless a live conductor (such as one recovered from the same
framer) still owns it.

2026-10-26: Don't type framer commands into the new shells
of restored SSH sessions

QA finding F-706. A conductor restored from a saved
arrangement reset its framers as soon as it was created,
before the session knew whether it had reattached to the
process that still ran ssh. After Cmd-Q that process is
gone, so the session started a new local shell, and the
reset (base64 reset2 and a UUID, each followed by a newline)
was typed into it. The restored conductor now writes nothing
until the session has reattached; only then does it reset.

2026-10-26: Keep the outer SSH connection going after
disconnecting a nested one

QA finding F-705. Disconnect on the inner hop of a nested
SSH Integration connection unhooked the inner conductor at
once. When the inner hook's own unhook arrived later, the
session handed it to the outer conductor, which took it as
its own end: it turned flow control off and stopped sending
commands, so nothing more was drawn. The inner it2ssh's
EndSSH also went unanswered, since only the outermost
disconnected connection was remembered, so it waited with
echo off in the outer host's shell.

An unhook from deeper than the innermost conductor is now
ignored, and every disconnected or abandoned connection is
remembered so its EndSSH gets the line that lets it2ssh
exit.

2026-10-26: Remove the LIVE badge from the iTerm2 Buddy
session view

2026-10-26: Keep App Nap off while an iTerm2 Buddy phone is
paired

When the displays sleep every window is hidden and macOS
naps iTerm2. The main thread then runs at low priority and
falls seconds behind, so the phone was slow to connect and
kept showing that the Mac was busy. Hold a user-initiated
activity (which still allows idle system sleep) for as long
as a phone is paired, since a paired Mac must answer the
phone at any time.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-25: Extract and translate the strings added in the
QA fix pass

Adds the catalog entries for eight strings introduced by

2026-10-25: Don't type an abandoned SSH connection's
commands into the local shell

QA finding F-554. While the remote streams output, ssh is
busy writing it and leaves the conductor's commands (such as
the autopoll request sent after each autopoll report) unread
in the tty. When it2ssh was killed and the connection
abandoned, those commands were left for the local shell,
which read YXV0b3BvbGw= as a command line. When a connection
is abandoned as soon as its it2ssh is found dead, and the
tty is still in ssh's raw mode with nothing new in the
foreground, the session now drops its unwritten output to
the pty and discards the tty's pending input. Disconnect's
forced unhook seconds later doesn't, since the input may be
the user's by then. An abandoned conductor also never writes
again, so a report handled after the abandonment can't send
anything, nor can a nested connection write through its
still-connected parent.

2026-10-25: Keep drawing the shell's output after abandoning
a streaming SSH connection

QA finding F-553. Restoring the tty modes ssh changed set
them through the pty's master. On macOS that discards the
output waiting to be read and doesn't wake a process waiting
for that output to drain. When it2ssh was killed while the
remote streamed output, zsh restored its own modes with
TCSADRAIN as the killed job ended and slept there, so
nothing it printed reached the session until something else
wrote to the tty. The modes are now set through the pty's
slave, which keeps the pending output and wakes the shell.
This came from 8a840cd7e, not from deac939d3.

The dead connection could also leave an unterminated OSC 134
frame header behind (its ssh died while writing it), which
would have swallowed the local shell's output as the rest of
the OSC. A framer notification header (%output, %autopoll,
%end, and the like) parsed outside the hook now ends at a
line break or at the start of another escape sequence,
neither of which the framer puts in one, and those are then
parsed normally. Other OSC 134 payloads, such as command
replies, are unchanged.

2026-10-25: Exit installer helpers that start after Cancel,
and detect leftover ones

Canceling an Internet-Free install step after it typed the
helper launch but before the helper sh prompted (as when the
user's shell is slow to start a command) typed nothing, so
the session was left in two helper sh at a >> prompt. Trying
again ran discovery in the helper, whose parent is sh (bash
on macOS), and reported bash.

After a cancel while the helpers launch, the installer now
waits up to 30 seconds for their prompt, asks the shell that
printed it how many helpers deep it is
(IT2_INSTALLER_HELPER, which each helper sets one higher
than its parent), and types the exits only if that matches
what was launched.  Otherwise, or if no prompt comes in
time, it types nothing more. A new installer window for the
same session waits for the previous one first.  Discovery
reports when it runs inside a helper shell and asks the user
to exit it, instead of detecting a shell.

QA finding F-552.

2026-10-25: Point localization tests at the tracked string
catalog

Four test classes read sources/Localizable.xcstrings, a
gitignored copy that only the iTerm2 scheme's pre-action
creates and that Xcode's localization sync rewrites, so in a
fresh worktree they failed with file not found and otherwise
checked a modified copy. They now read
xcstrings/Localizable.xcstrings.

Tangential finding T-108 from the QA fix pass.

2026-10-25: Highlight attribute values in View Source

The highlighter looked for values in &quot; or &#39;, but
innerHTML doesn't escape quotes in text, so no value ever
matched. It now matches plain double- and single-quoted
values. The matched text comes from the tag's attribute
text, which can't contain an unescaped < or > or inserted
markup, so wrapping it in a span can't create elements.

Tangential finding T-95 from the QA fix pass.

2026-10-25: Escape the composer's ghost text for file
completions

didFindCompletions picked the inline suggestion from the raw
file completions rather than the shell-escaped ones, so with
AI off an explicitly requested completion for a name with a
space showed unescaped ghost text.

Tangential finding T-85 from the QA fix pass.

2026-10-25: Draw the shell integration installer's Learn
more links in the label font

The link runs had no font attribute, so they drew in
Helvetica 12 next to the system-font text.

Tangential finding T-94 from the QA fix pass.

2026-10-25: Percent-encode + in a URL template's query value

Search the Web for Selection put the selection into the
search URL with NSURLQueryItem, which leaves + unescaped,
and servers decode + in a query as a space, so c++ was
searched for as c  . The query is now edited in its
percent-encoded form: the value is encoded with +, &, =, #
and ; escaped, and the rest of the template's query keeps
its own encoding (before, a %2B elsewhere in it became +).

Tangential finding T-102 from the QA fix pass.

2026-10-25: Don't unpack the utilities elsewhere when cd to
~/.iterm2 fails

The Internet-Free installer's utilities step typed cd
~/.iterm2; echo done, which reported done even when cd
failed (as when ~/.iterm2 is a file), so the archive would
be unpacked in the helper's current directory. Now the cd
answer says whether it worked, the receive command is typed
only after it did, and on failure the helpers exit and the
step reports that the utilities were not installed.

Tangential finding T-130 from the QA fix pass.

2026-10-25: Read a signing certificate's expiration from its
Not After date

SIGCertificate's expirationDate asked for the invalidity
date extension rather than the validity period's Not After
date. It only worked because Security synthesizes an Expires
entry for that OID. It now reads
kSecOIDX509V1ValidityNotAfter, converting its
seconds-since-reference-date value, and checks the types it
gets back. A failed trust evaluation with an underlying
error now uses SIGErrorCodeTrustFailed instead of
SIGErrorCodeTrust, whose description is for failing to
create a trust object.

Tangential finding T-97 from the QA fix pass.

2026-10-25: Ignore malformed trigger parameters from
untrusted dictionaries

A Dynamic Profile trigger whose parameter was an array,
dictionary, or null crashed Bounce Dock Icon and Set Mark
triggers, which call -intValue on it, and Invoke Script
Function passed it on as a string.  Only string and number
parameters are now accepted. A number given to a trigger
that takes text is used as that number's text.

Tangential finding T-40 from the QA fix pass.

2026-10-25: Honor the generative AI lockout for Apple
Intelligence

Apple Intelligence runs on-device, so its requests never
reach iTermAIClient.request, which refuses remote LLM
requests when an administrator sets GenerativeAIAllowed to
NO. AI tab titles, AI chat with the Apple Intelligence
model, and the on-device command safety check kept running
under the lockout.

AppleIntelligenceRunner now refuses complete, generate, and
generateTabTitle with a disallowedByAdministrator error
before creating a session or counting tokens.
AITermController fails an Apple Intelligence request up
front with the lockout message, which the safety check
treats as unsafe (manual approval). AI tab titles are off
under the lockout, so no screen is captured and an existing
AI title is cleared.

Tangential finding T-28 from the QA fix pass.

2026-10-25: Don't overwrite a remote file when an upload's
name check fails

When picking a remote name for an SSH integration upload,
any stat error (a permission error, a dropped connection)
was read as "the name is free", so the upload could replace
an existing file. Now only a definite ENOENT from the framer
counts as free; any other failure stops the upload with an
error before anything is written under the final name. File
operations now report non-ENOENT framer failures as a
distinct error instead of "File not found".

Tangential finding T-41 from the QA fix pass.

2026-10-25: Cancel an scp password request when the password
manager closes

Closing the password manager without choosing a password
dropped the pending completion without calling it, so the
scp transfer thread waited forever. Now the completion is
called once with nil (cancel), it can never run twice, and
the copied block is released (the file is MRC).

Tangential finding T-69 from the QA fix pass.

2026-10-25: Expect the v2 installer URL in the online
installer tests

ec52a96ee switched the typed command to the v2 installer
URL, but the test's expected curl command still used the old
URL, so the fake terminal never replied and all three tests
failed.

QA finding F-540.

2026-10-25: Return to the user's shell after canceling an
Internet-Free install step

Cancel left the terminal inside the installer's helper sh at
a >> prompt: during the utilities archive no exits were
typed, and the script step launched a second, nested pair of
helpers, so its exits closed only one pair. Send Again while
a step was busy typed the launch lines again inside the
running helpers, so each retry left two more shells.

The installer now tracks the helper shells from their output
(their prompt, a step's answers, or an echo that includes
the helper's PS1) and types exit only when it knows how many
are running and that the innermost reads commands. Cancel
exits idle helpers right away, or once an in-progress
here-document or the ended archive read loop is done; before
the launch is confirmed it types nothing. Send Again reuses
running helpers instead of starting more, waiting for them
if they are busy. Discovery exits leftover helpers first.
The script is written without the nested helpers, in one
here-document.

QA findings F-543 and F-544.

2026-10-25: Keep repairing tty modes until zsh records good
ones

QA finding F-541. zsh records the tty modes when a
foreground job ends and applies them when its line editor
returns, before running the command line. After a killed
it2ssh it had recorded ssh's raw modes, but the repairer
disarmed at the OSC 133 D for the killed command, which
arrives right after the repair at the prompt, so zsh
reapplied the raw modes before every later command. Without
shell integration there is no OSC 133 C at all, so the one
repair at the prompt never stuck.

A command's end now disarms the repairer only if that
command started with good modes that the repairer had not
set itself since the previous command ended (or, for a fork,
since the previous command started). Until then the shell's
recorded modes may still be ssh's.

While armed, the repairer also watches the shell that ran
it2ssh for forks with a process dispatch source. zsh forks
to run an external command after applying its recorded
modes, so if the tty then has ssh's modes with ICANON and
ECHO set, the repairer fixes them and zsh records the fixed
modes when the command exits. This works without shell
integration. Forks never disarm; the shell's exit does. All
existing safety rules still apply: the pty must still be the
session's, a tty with ICANON or ECHO off is never touched,
and modes that changed since they were read are left alone.
A reparented it2ssh's parent (launchd) is never watched.

2026-10-25: Restore the tty modes ssh changed when
recovering from a killed it2ssh

QA finding F-460. When it2ssh is killed, its ssh client
can't restore the tty modes it made raw (it is no longer in
the foreground process group and is killed anyway), so after
recovery the session had no signals, output processing, or
flow control. iTerm2 now records the tty's modes when it2ssh
fetches its token, before ssh starts. When the outermost
connection is abandoned or force-disconnected and its
watched it2ssh is known to have exited, iTerm2 restores the
modes ssh cleared through its end of the pty, never writing
to the shell. It does nothing if another connection is
active or has started on the tty since, if it2ssh ran on
another tty (e.g., inside tmux), or if it2ssh ran without
job control. It changes the modes right away only if the
tty's foreground process group is the shell that ran it2ssh,
or it2ssh's job once that job has no members left; otherwise
it only arms. At the prompt it restores only what a line
editor leaves alone. zsh records the modes when a job dies
and reapplies them before each command, so the repairer
stays armed: when a command starts (OSC 133 C) with the tty
in ssh's mode but ICANON and ECHO set, it fixes the tty
again so zsh records good modes when the command exits, and
it disarms once a command finishes with good modes or the
session's shell is replaced. A program that makes the tty
raw itself clears ICANON and ECHO and is never touched, and
modes that change between reading and writing are left
alone. Before every change it checks that the fd is still
the master of the session's pty, so a closed and reused
descriptor is never touched. Without a snapshot it uses the
modes iTerm2 gives new sessions.

2026-10-25: Retain the table of slowly sent pastes

PTYSession is manual retain/release, and 8dcb73f6d stored an
autoreleased NSHashTable in an ivar without retaining it.
The table was freed when the autorelease pool drained, so
the next sendTextSlowly: (the Internet-Free installer's
second command) messaged a dead object and crashed.

QA finding F-542.

2026-10-25: Stop typing the utilities archive when the
installer is canceled

Canceling the Internet-Free install while it typed the
utilities archive closed the window, but the archive, which
takes a minute or more to type, kept being typed until it
was done.

Cancel (and Send Again while busy) now stops that paste
through a new iTermPasteHelper call that aborts only the
pastes it is given, so the user's own pastes are not
touched. Then it types the line that ends sh's read loop,
which the archive would have ended with. Otherwise sh would
keep taking whatever is typed next into its file, including
the commands of a retry. sh now lists the whole archive
before extracting, which checks gzip's CRC, so a partial
archive unpacks nothing rather than overwriting utilities
with partial files. The here-document of a shell integration
script is still left to finish, since ending one cut short
would write a partial script.

QA finding F-463.

2026-10-25: Don't type the utilities archive into sh when it
can't be received

If the helper sh could not create the file the utilities
archive goes into (for example because ~/.iterm2 is not
writable), the read loop's redirection failed and the loop
never ran, yet sh still printed that it was receiving.
Decoding the missing file failed, but tar given no input
succeeded, so sh reported that the utilities were installed.
iTerm2 then typed the whole archive into sh's prompt as
commands, and the installer said the utility scripts were
installed.

The redirection now applies to the group that prints the
receiving marker, so sh prints it only once the file is
open, and prints a distinct marker otherwise. iTerm2 types
the archive only after the receiving marker; on the other
one it leaves the helper shell and shows that the utilities
were not installed. Success now requires that the archive
decoded, that tar extracted it, and that a file from it
exists, each checked separately since POSIX sh has no
pipefail. The temporary files are removed either way.

QA finding F-462.

2026-10-25: Check that the Internet-Free utilities install
unpacked

The utilities archive, hundreds of base64 lines, was typed
into a here-document that sh read through its line editor.
On a busy system lines got lost or spliced, tar failed, and
the installer reported success anyway since it never looked
at tar's result.

The helper sh now reads the archive with a read loop (no
line editor, and the tty stays in canonical mode, where a
full input buffer makes the writer wait instead of dropping
input) into a file, decodes it into tar, and prints whether
tar succeeded. The archive is typed only after sh says the
loop is reading, so none of it can run as commands. On
failure the step shows that the utility scripts were not
installed and offers to try again instead of moving on.

QA finding F-352.

2026-10-25: Never recover or hook from an abandoned SSH
connection's queued output

QA finding F-459. When it2ssh was killed while the remote
streamed output, framer output that was already parsed,
executed, or waiting on the main thread when the connection
was abandoned could still start framer recovery or a
conductor, which typed into the local shell and wedged the
session. Abandoning or disconnecting a connection now
refuses recovery on the main thread at the moment of the
decision, so a recovery requested earlier is dropped and the
terminal leaves recovery mode. The refusal covers the
abandoned connection's depth and every deeper one, so output
parsed inside its framing can't recover either. A hook
parsed inside an abandoned connection's framing, or carrying
an abandoned connection's unique ID, no longer starts a
conductor or lifts the refusal; only a new connection's hook
does.

The basic case (framer output arriving after the forced
unhook) was already handled by 7d023c6bb; QA tested a build
without it. The new tests cover the queued, mid-frame, and
pending side effect cases that it did not.

2026-10-24: Don't type an installer command after Cancel

Since the fix for F-353, iTermExpect holds an expectation's
original until its willExpect callback runs, so a callback
already queued for the main thread runs even if the
installer canceled the expectation in the meantime, typing
the next command (or the utilities archive) after the user
canceled. The installer now ignores callbacks queued before
its last cancel. iTermExpect itself still runs them, since
the bracketed-paste expectation relies on its callback
running after a cancel.

Follow-up to QA finding F-353.

2026-10-24: Type every line of the Internet-Free install's
bash dotfile step

For bash, the dotfile step left the line that loads shell
integration out of the here-document, so ~/.profile got only
an empty line.

The line was lost in iTermExpect. When a wet copy of an
expectation is added, it dispatches the original's
willExpect callback (which types the text) to the main
thread through a weak reference to the original.  sh's echo
of the here-document line and of the empty line after it
answered two expectations in one batch of output, so the
second one completed on the main thread before its callback
ran. Nothing else held it, so the weak reference was nil and
the line was never typed, while the callbacks after it (EOF,
fi) still ran.

The dispatched block now holds the original strongly.

QA finding F-353.

2026-10-24: Ignore an abandoned SSH connection's framer
output instead of recovering

QA finding F-354. When only it2ssh was killed, its ssh
client kept running and its remote conductor kept printing
framer output such as an autopoll report (ESC
]134;:%autopoll <id> ESC \). Once iTerm2 had abandoned the
connection and removed its hook, that OSC 134 arrived
outside any hook and started framer recovery, which typed
base64 "recover" and blank lines into the local shell and
left a recovery conductor taking the keystrokes. Abandoning
a connection (automatically or from the announcement) and
Disconnect now refuse framer recovery at that connection's
parent depth until a new connection hooks there, so its
framer output is ignored and anything else it prints is
drawn. The orphaned ssh client is also killed before the
conductor is unhooked, so less of its output arrives.

2026-10-24: Kill rather than hang up the orphaned ssh client

QA finding F-354. The ssh client a killed it2ssh leaves
behind is now sent SIGKILL instead of SIGHUP. Its process
group is orphaned, so SIGHUP and SIGTERM made it print
"tcsetattr: Input/output error" into the session; SIGKILL
prints nothing. All of the identity and safety checks are
unchanged.

2026-10-24: Hang up the ssh client a killed it2ssh leaves
behind

QA finding F-351. When a watched it2ssh starts, the monitor
records its single ssh child (pid, start time, and
arguments). When the session recovers from the dead it2ssh,
either automatically or through Disconnect's forced unhook,
it sends SIGHUP to that ssh client so it no longer stays
logged in to the remote host on the session's tty. It
signals only when the pid, start time, and arguments still
match, it2ssh is gone and is no longer ssh's parent, and ssh
is not listening on a unix socket (a possible
ControlMaster). Records are forgotten when a connection ends
normally or aborts.

2026-10-24: Detect a killed it2ssh and recover the SSH
Integration session

QA finding F-482. When it2ssh runs on this Mac, the secret
server records the ancestry of the process that fetched the
token, so the conductor can find and watch the it2ssh
process. When it exits without sending EndSSH, the session
ends its SSH Integration state and removes the conductor
hook automatically. When it2ssh can't be watched, a shell
prompt mark arriving outside the conductor's framing shows
an announcement, once per connection, offering to
disconnect. Disconnect can now also remove a nested
conductor's hook.

2026-10-24: Focus the status bar search field under focus
follows mouse

With focus follows mouse including views like Search or
Composer, the window's shared status bar is outside every
pane, so nothing noticed the pointer over its search field.
It now counts as part of the session it shows and tracks
pointer movement.

QA finding F-483.

2026-10-24: Recover SSH Integration sessions on Disconnect
after it2ssh is killed

QA finding F-482. When the local it2ssh process is killed,
neither the framer's unhook nor it2ssh's EndSSH ever
arrives, so the conductor DCS hook swallowed the local
shell's output. Disconnect now forces the outermost
conductor hook off if it is still present 3 seconds later,
and a late EndSSH for a disconnected conductor still
releases it2ssh.

2026-10-24: Keep the Internet-Free utilities install from
hanging at a >  prompt

The utilities step typed each command after the answer to
the one before it: cd waited for the ok from mkdir, and the
here-document line waited for the ok from cd, a line
consisting only of ok. When a shell was slow to answer, as
with tcsh started from zsh, the ok from cd arrived after the
here-document line was typed and landed in the middle of its
echo, so no line was ever just ok and the body was never
sent.

Each command now waits for its own answer, a distinct marker
that its echo cannot contain and that is matched anywhere in
a row. The here-document line only waits for its echo, since
sh reads the body from the tty after it.

QA finding F-481.

2026-10-24: Remove blank lines added to the end of the 3.7
release notes

2026-10-24: Drop Tahoe tab bar insets when using
Sequoia-style tabs. Issue 12498.

With useSequoiaStyleTabs on macOS 26, tabBarInsets still
returned the insets sized for the Tahoe pill, leaving
padding to the left, right, and below the tabs and making
the add-tab button taller than the tabs.  Return zero insets
instead, matching earlier OS versions.

2026-10-24: 3.7.6 release notes

2026-10-24: Show a globe on mention chips for browser
sessions

An @-mention chip for a web browser session showed the
terminal icon. The mention picker now reports the icon with
its choice, and chips rebuilt from tokens resolve the
session to choose it.

QA finding F-480.

Show a globe on sent mentions of browser sessions

A sent message's @-mention of a web browser session showed
the terminal icon even though its chip in the message field
showed a globe. Message bubbles now choose the icon the same
way chips do, and a mention of a session that no longer
exists keeps the terminal icon.

QA finding F-501.

2026-10-24: Return xonsh to its prompt after the
Internet-Free install

The Internet-Free shell integration install left xonsh users
in the helper sh after writing the script. Two things caused
it. The step that writes the script built its nested helper
launch through an out parameter, which ARC passes as nil, so
that launch started a chain of its own and was typed at the
same moment as the outer one. And for xonsh the first
command after a launch was typed as soon as xonsh echoed the
launch. xonsh's line editor reads typeahead in bulk, like
nushell's, so it kept those commands and ran them after the
helper exited, starting a helper sh nobody exits.

The expectation parameters are now inout so each step is one
chain, and for xonsh, as for nushell, the installer waits
for the helper's own prompt (not a wrapped row of the echo)
before typing into it.

QA finding F-436.

2026-10-24: Render a streamed reply's final code block as
code

A streamed AI reply can end with a space after the closing
fence. SwiftyMarkdown treated the whitespace-only last line
as a setext heading underline, turning the last code line
into a heading, and did not accept a closing fence with
trailing whitespace. Normalize fence lines and blank
whitespace-only lines outside code blocks before rendering.

QA finding F-437.

2026-10-24: Keep Session.preferred_size in step with layout
changes in the Python API

A Session's preferred_size was set from its grid size when
the object was created and never refreshed. The App reuses
Session objects across layout changes (update_from copies
the new grid size but left the preferred size alone), so
after async_split_pane() the original session still
preferred its pre-split width. A no-op
Tab.async_update_layout() then sent that stale width and the
server grew the pane and the window back to it, past the
screen edge.

The server cannot tell a stale size from a requested one, so
this is fixed in the client: preferred_size now follows the
session's actual size on every layout refresh, unless the
script has set it and async_update_layout() has not applied
it yet.

QA finding F-500.

2026-10-24: Report -compare-rendering image save failures as
errors

The rendering comparer ignored whether its three PNGs were
written, so run from an unwritable working directory it
printed their paths and RESULT: PASS although none of the
files existed.

Saving and reporting now live in
iTermRenderingComparer.saveAndCompare, which reports each
failed save as an ERROR line, prints RESULT: ERROR, and
returns a nonzero exit status. NSImage saveAsPNGTo: now
returns whether it succeeded, and iTermImageComparison
reports whether the diff image was written. Since
NSApp.terminate always exits with status 0, a nonzero status
is applied from an atexit handler after AppKit finishes
terminating. The comparer's other ERROR paths exit nonzero
too.

QA finding F-435.

2026-10-24: Stop counting the next prompt in Command Info's
output size

The output coord range for a command mark is half-open: it
ends at column 0 of the next prompt's line. PTYTextView
converted it to a line range inclusively, so the Output row
counted the next prompt and any command typed at it ("echo
HELLO" showed 39 bytes in 2 lines, "sleep 2" showed the
27-byte prompt). The conversion now lives in -[VT100Screen
lineRangeOfOutputForCommandMark:] and excludes that line.

Also fix the size of output in scrollback:
numberOfCellsUsedInRange: counted to the end of history for
a range wholly in history, and LineBuffer's
numberOfCellsUsedInWrappedLineRange:width: dropped the
range's last line.

QA finding F-419.

Stop counting zsh's PROMPT_SP padding in Command Info's
output size

When output does not end in a newline, zsh's PROMPT_SP
writes its inverse eol mark (% or #) after it and pads with
spaces until the line wraps, so the next prompt starts on a
new line. The output's last line then held the mark and a
line's worth of spaces, and "printf abc" in a 195-column
window showed 196 bytes.

The OSC 133 D sequence arrives from precmd, after PROMPT_SP
has already written the padding, so the cursor position at D
does not mark the end of the output. Instead, when the
output's last line soft-wraps into the next prompt's line
and ends in an inverse % or # followed by spaces, those
cells are not counted. Spaces before the mark belong to the
output and are kept. If the mark is missing (an empty
PROMPT_EOL_MARK) nothing is trimmed, so real output is never
undercounted.

QA finding F-350.

2026-10-24: Translate Type @ as a verb in the Chinese
Cockpit hints

The Simplified Chinese Cockpit composer hint read @ (Input
type: @ choose sessions), treating Type as the noun . The
Type @ to pick a session status had the same mistake ( @ ).
Use the verb  in both:  @  and
 @ .

QA finding F-512.

2026-10-24: Shorten the German Undo timeout label in profile
Session settings

The German label above the Undo seconds field in Settings >
Profiles > Session measured 561pt in the system font, wider
than its 551pt clipping frame, so the trailing n: was cut
off. Reword it as Sekunden, in denen Rckgngig eine
geschlossene Sitzung wiederherstellen kann: (511pt), and add
a test that every languages label fits the frame.

QA finding F-511.

2026-10-24: Make Find in Settings focus its search field

With the Settings window key over a terminal window, Edit >
Find > Find opened the find bar in the terminal behind
Settings. Settings is a panel, so the terminal stays the
main window, and nothing in Settings' responder chain
handled performFindPanelAction:, so AppKit fell through to
the main window's chain.

PreferencePanel now handles Find by focusing its search
field, and disables the other find panel actions. Settings'
field editor forwards Find to it, since otherwise the field
editor claims the action and disables it.

QA finding F-418.

2026-10-24: Size the Session Status priority popover to its
translations

The Session Status gear popover was a fixed 280 points wide
with a fixed 44 point Notify column, so in German the column
header read Bena and the Arbeitsgruppenstatus zusammenfhren
checkbox was clipped. Measure the localized checkbox titles,

QA finding F-496.

2026-10-24: Dim the FFM subviews option on first show when
FFM is off

In Settings > Pointer, even to views like Search or Composer
was enabled when the pane first appeared with Focus Follows
Mouse off. Its shouldBeEnabled block was assigned after
defineControl:, which had already updated the enabled state
while no rule existed, and nothing updated it again until
Focus Follows Mouse changed. Update the enabled state once
the rule is in place.

QA finding F-417.

2026-10-24: Decode HTML tab title entities even when there
is no tag

With HTML tab titles on, newAttributedStringWithHTML:
treated a title as HTML only if it contained <b>, <i> or
<u>. A title that escaped only & or < (as a program must to
show a literal <b>) was shown with raw entities, while the
same text next to a bold word was decoded.

Also take the HTML path when the title contains &lt; or
&amp;, the two entities the parser decodes. Python API
status bar components, which always go through this parser,
now decode those entities without a tag too, consistent with
how they already did with one.

QA finding F-348.

2026-10-24: Give the tab group context menu the window's
appearance

A tab's context menu takes its appearance from the window
(or, in the Minimal theme, from the decoration background
color), but the tab group pill's menu had none, so it was
drawn in the system appearance when that differed from the
window's.

Move the appearance choice into a helper and use it for both
menus.

QA finding F-416.

2026-10-24: Stamp a command mark's start date once for both
thread copies

Command Info showed Running Time: 0:-1.999 for any quick
command.  setFirstLineOfCommand: stamped startDate with
[NSDate date]. It runs inside a mutateObject block, which is
replayed on the main-thread doppelganger when the threads
next sync. For a quick command the C (command start), D and
next A all arrive before that sync, so the doppelganger's
startDate was stamped after its endDate (captured when D
arrived), giving a running time of about -1 ms. The
formatter then floored that to 0:-1.999.

Capture the start date once, outside the mutation block, so
the progenitor and the doppelganger agree, for both the
shell integration and auto-composer paths. Also clamp
negative intervals to zero in formattedHMS.

QA findings F-415 and F-315.

2026-10-24: Pick the URL search pane with the logical
coordinate

urlActionForClickAtCoord: and urlForCopyAtCoord: limit the
URL search to the pane between column dividers. Divider
columns are found in logical space, but the window was
chosen with the visual coordinate under the pointer. On a
reordered right-to-left row that touches a divider (a
non-integration tmux split, for example), the visual column
can be on the other side of the divider, so the search was
confined to the wrong pane and the URL was not detected.

Now both pass the logical coordinate they already computed.

QA finding F-076.

Hit-test a reordered URL's visual range in its drawn columns

Cmd-hover underlines a URL only when the pointer is inside
the URL action's visual range. That range kept the column
window of the pane around the URL, which is in logical
columns. On a right-to-left row in a non-integration tmux
split, the URL can be logically right of the pane divider
but drawn left of it, so no cell of the URL was ever inside
its own visual range and nothing was underlined. Quick Look,
drag and the context menu use the same check.

visualWindowedRangeForLogical: now widens the column window
to cover the columns the range is drawn in when bidi is on.

The context menu's Copy for a URL action built a selection
from the visual range, but selections are logical, so on a
reordered row it copied the wrong cells (and with the wider
window, more of them). It now uses the logical range.

QA finding F-456 (follow-up to F-076).

Bound every cell of a reordered range when finding its
visual range

Cmd-hover underlines a URL only when the pointer is inside
the URL action's visual range, and otherwise leaves the old
underline alone.  visualRangeForLogical: built that range
from the visual positions of the range's first and last
logical cells. A URL that ends in right-to-left text (such
as https://example.com/ followed by a Persian letter) is not
drawn in one run, so those two cells are not its visual
edges and most of the URL fell outside its own visual range.
Moving the pointer from another URL straight onto that part
kept the other URL underlined.

With bidi on, the visual range now spans the visual columns
of every cell on its first and last rows.

QA finding F-427.

Test URL hover over every cell of a URL ending in RTL text
in a tmux split

QA found that in a non-integration tmux split, Cmd-hover
underlined https://example.com/ followed by a Persian letter
only over its first few drawn cells. The URL action's visual
range was built from the visual positions of its first and
last logical cells, which on that row span only the first
three drawn cells. 89d9078fb (F-427) already fixed this by
bounding every cell of a reordered range.

This adds a regression test with QA's rows (t20l.sh in the
left pane, t20r.sh in the right) that checks every drawn
cell of the URL finds it and falls inside its visual range.
It fails without 89d9078fb.

QA finding F-495.

Drop a redundant nil coalescing in BidiURLDividerTests

VT100GridWindowedRangeDescription returns a non-optional
String, so the ?? "" in two failure messages only produced
compiler warnings.

Found while landing QA finding F-495.

2026-10-24: Make each GPU-unavailable reason a complete
sentence

Help > GPU Renderer Availability built its message by
injecting a separately localized reason fragment into GPU
rendering is off in the current session because %@. The
fragments did not all fit the frame (because This tab has
too many split panes, with a capital and no period), and
translations could not reorder or reinflect them.

Each reason is now its own complete localized sentence,
shown as is, with German, Japanese, Brazilian Portuguese,
Russian and Simplified Chinese translations. A few English
