QA finding F-429.

2026-10-24: Restore a float's place in the z-order on Undo
Close

Undo Close of a float installed it in front of the other
floats. A maximized float fills the tab and is normally kept
behind the other floats so they stay visible, so after Undo
Close it covered them all.

Both the live and the archived Undo Close paths now move the
float just behind the backmost float that the closed tab's
arrangement had in front of it, which is where it was. If
none of those are still in the tab it stays in front, as
before.

QA finding F-428.

2026-10-24: Show an error instead of crashing on a damaged
script archive certificate

Importing a .its script archive whose embedded signing
certificate was corrupt crashed iTerm2 with "CFRelease()
called with NULL". SIGCertificate's initWithData: returned
nil when SecCertificateCreateWithData failed, and its
dealloc then released the NULL SecCertificateRef.

Guard every NULL CF path on the signature verification
route: - SIGCertificate dealloc releases only a non-NULL
certificate, and
  initWithSecCertificate: returns nil for NULL instead of
  retaining it.
- certificateInArray: no longer passes a NULL subject to
CFEqual.  - name initializes its out-parameter so a
certificate without a common
  name does not release stack garbage.
- SIGArchiveVerifier fails with a malformed-certificate
error when the
  signing certificate has no extractable public key, and
  stops when the archive has no certificates instead of
  continuing with an error set.

Verification now fails with
SIGErrorCodeInputMalformedCertificate, which the importer
shows as an alert.

QA finding F-414.

Balance Core Foundation ownership in SignedArchive

SIGIdentity's initWithSecIdentity: stored the identity
without retaining it but released it in dealloc. That only
worked because allSigningIdentities leaked the keychain
result array that held the identities. The initializer now
retains, and the array is released.

Also fix leaks in the same classes: - signingCertificate
released no reference from SecIdentityCopyCertificate.  -
longDescription and serialNumber leaked the CFErrorRef on
failure.  - certificateIsRootWithName: leaked the anchor
certificate array.  - secCertificateWithName: leaked the
keychain result array; it is now
  copySecCertificateWithName:, returning a retained
  certificate that issuer releases.
- SIGTrust's evaluateTrust: leaked the CFErrorRef, and a
failed
  evaluation without an underlying error reported no error,
  so the script importer showed "Unknown error". It now
  reports a trust error.

Found while fixing QA finding F-414.

2026-10-24: Clear the unsaved-settings indicator after Save
Now

With settings loaded from a custom folder and saved
manually, the red "!" in the Settings title bar shows that
local settings differ from the folder's.
UserDefaultsUnsavedController rechecked only on
UserDefaults.didChangeNotification, and Save Now writes the
folder's plist without changing any local default, so the
indicator stayed until the Settings window was reopened.

iTermRemotePreferences now posts
iTermRemotePreferencesSavedPrefsDidChangeNotification on the
main thread whenever its saved copy of the folder's prefs is
replaced, and the controller rechecks when it arrives. When
saving automatically it only rechecks to clear an indicator
that is showing, since an autosave snapshots defaults off
the main thread and an edit made since then would otherwise
flash the indicator.

QA finding F-494.

2026-10-24: Lay out the Minimal title again when only its
subtitle changes

In the Minimal theme a one-tab window draws its title and
the profile's subtitle in place of the tab bar, in a label
sized for one line or two.  A window from Shell > New Window
gets its title before the session's path is known, so the
label was laid out for one line. When the subtitle arrived
later and the title stayed the same, PseudoTerminal only
called -[iTermRootTerminalView setSubtitle:], which updated
the label's text but not its frame, so the second line
stayed clipped until a resize laid the window out again.

setSubtitle: now relays out the window pane decorations when
the subtitle changes, as windowTitleDidChangeTo: already
does for the title.

QA finding F-413.

2026-10-24: Report a dead key's leftover character as the
dead key in CSI u

With kitty keyboard flags that report all keys as escape
codes, a dead key followed by a key it doesn't compose with
(German dead ^ then <) makes Cocoa insert the dead key's
character and then the second key's text, both while the
second key is the current event. Each insertion was encoded
from the current event's key code, so the application got
the second key's report twice and never saw the ^.

The mapper now remembers a dead key press until the next key
down. If the first text inserted for that key press is what
the dead key types on its own (the key translated by the
layout, or its charactersIgnoringModifiers), it is reported
as the dead key's press.  Later insertions, text inserted
outside that key press, and compositions (, or the bare
accent typed with space) are encoded as before.

QA finding F-346.

2026-10-24: Describe an unfinished AI tool call accurately
in the next request

When the AI asked to run a command and the user ignored the
Allow/Deny prompt and sent a new message, the next request
paired that tool call with a stand-in result saying iTerm2
had been restarted and inviting the model to re-issue the
call. Nothing had restarted; the user had moved on, so the
model was told something false and nudged to rerun a command
the user passed over.

The stand-in result now says the call did not complete,
usually because the user moved on without answering the
permission request, possibly because it was interrupted (for
example, by a restart). It says the call's effects are
unknown and asks the model not to run it again unless the
user asks.

QA finding F-493.

2026-10-24: Open a popup browser tab's context menu in that
tab

A browser tab opened by a target=_blank link (including View
Source links) showed no context menu. Its web view is built
from the opener's configuration, so it shares the opener's
user content controller: the context menu script it runs
carries the opener's secret and posts to the opener's
iTermBrowserManager. That manager opened the menu in its own
web view, which sits in a hidden tab, and the script had
already suppressed WebKit's default menu.

Open the menu in the web view that sent the message instead.
Other script message handlers still act on the opener's web
view; they are left for a separate change.

QA finding F-412.

Give a browser tab opened from another its own script
handlers

A tab opened by a target=_blank link or window.open got a
copy of its opener's configuration, including the opener's
user content controller.  Its scripts carried the opener's
secrets and posted to the opener's manager, which acted on
the opener's page: selection and copy on select, hovered
links, find, autofill, the password manager and named marks.
Its URL scheme tasks also went to the opener's manager
through a weak reference, so its iterm2-about pages stopped
loading once the opener closed.

Give such a tab a fresh user content controller and set it
up like any other tab's, keeping the inherited data store
and URL scheme handlers (WebKit requires the former for a
related web view and won't replace the latter). The scheme
handler now hands each task to the manager of the web view
that asked, and fails it if that web view has none. A
manager also ignores any script message from a web view
other than its own, so no handler can act on a page other
than the one that sent it. The new tab is now registered
with the extension manager and gets the ad block rules too.

Found while fixing QA finding F-412.

2026-10-24: Let a session name from Edit Session out-rank an
AI profile's fallback

With a profile whose Title is AI-generated name and no AI
title, the tab title falls back to the program's OSC 1 icon
name before the session name. Editing the session name in
Session > Edit Session left the title components alone for
an AI profile, so after a program had set an icon name the
tab kept it while the window showed the new name.

Editing the name now turns on TemporarySessionName for an AI
profile, as a manual rename or Set Title trigger does, so
the deliberate name wins over both the icon name and the AI
title.

QA finding F-411.

2026-10-24: Let a browser tab's page follow appearance
changes

iTermBrowserWebView overrode
viewDidChangeEffectiveAppearance without calling super.
WKWebView's implementation is what tells the page that the
appearance changed, so a page that supports both color
schemes kept the scheme it had when the tab was created.
Changing the theme from Minimal to Dark left open tabs
light, and a tab opened under Dark stayed dark in Minimal,
where its dark background kept the tab's chrome dark too.

Now the override calls super.

QA finding F-410.

2026-10-24: Show the page source in the browser's View
Source

View Source always showed No source available.
showSourcePage stored the generated page in a per-context
iTermBrowserSourceHandler and then loaded
iterm2-about:source, but decidePolicyFor drops every local
page context before the scheme handler runs, so a fresh
handler with no pending source served the fallback.

The local page manager now owns a single source handler for
its lifetime and reuses it for the iterm2-about:source
context, so the pending source survives the context reset.
resetAllHandlerState also resets it since it may not be in
the active contexts.

QA finding F-387.

Keep View Source on the main thread and drop superseded
source

When the network refetch of a page's source failed,
viewPageSource fell back to reading the DOM by evaluating
JavaScript from the URLSession completion queue. The
completion now hops to the main queue first.

Since the source handler now lives as long as the local page
manager, source stored for a View Source that never got
served could be shown by a later visit to
iterm2-about:source. decidePolicyFor now clears it for
main-frame navigations that stay in the tab and aren't to
the source page.

QA finding F-387.

Keep View Source links with ampersands intact

View Source linkified URLs by inserting <a href="..."> into
the code element's HTML, and the syntax highlighter then
re-read that HTML and wrapped the &amp; inside the real href
attribute in an entity span, whose quote ended the
attribute. Every link containing an ampersand was garbled
and pointed at a bogus URL.

Links are now swapped for placeholders while the highlighter
runs and restored afterward. Link targets are decoded
properly (the serialized text, then the source's own
references in attribute context), resolved against the page
URL, and HTML-escaped. Only http and https URLs (and file
URLs when the page itself is a file) become links, so
JavaScript: URLs in the source are no longer clickable.

QA finding F-409.

description to their text

On the first page of the shell integration installer, the
Also install iTerm2 Utilities checkbox and its one-line,
clipping description had fixed frames sized for English. In
German the checkbox read iTerm2-Dienstprogramm and the

were also too long.

After the page loads, widen the checkbox to its title, up to
the page's right margin, and let the description wrap. The
description grows upward the same way the Internet-Free step
list does: controls above it move up and the page grows, so
the buttons at the bottom stay put. The window is fitted to

QA finding F-406.

2026-10-24: Localize the units in Advanced Paste and Paste
History sizes

Advanced Paste built its chunk size and delay labels by hand
(%@ kB, ms, sec, min), and NSString it_formatBytes, used by
Paste History, the paste-rate toast and the Python runtime
download prompt, hard-coded bytes, kB and so on. In a
non-English UI these English units sat next to translated
text and tick labels.

Add LocalizedUnitFormatter. For an English UI it keeps the
existing strings exactly. For any other UI language, byte
counts go through MeasurementFormatter with
UnitInformationStorage in the UI language, and the Advanced
Paste delay units come from new catalog strings with de, ja,
pt-BR, ru and zh-Hans translations (MeasurementFormatter
left duration units untranslated for ru inside the app). The
language is the UI localization, as with the relative time
strings.

QA finding F-405.

2026-10-24: Show the placeholder in the Toggle Setting and
menu item pickers

SearchableComboView only sets its default title when a
selection fails, so a freshly created combo view shows a
blank title. SettingPopupView and MenuItemPopupView recreate
the combo view on every reloadData, which happens whenever
the action changes in the key binding editor, so the Toggle
Setting picker (and the Select Menu Item picker) stayed
blank until a setting was chosen and the localized Select
Setting title never appeared.

Set the default title right after creating the combo view. A
later selection replaces it.

QA finding F-407.

2026-10-24: Use ps ucomm to find the shell for offline
installer discovery

tcsh overwrites the first character of its argv[0] with -,
so the comm that discovery read from ps showed -csh for a
tcsh started as `tcsh` and -sh for one started as `csh`. The
latter still fell back to $SHELL, so a csh started from a
zsh login shell got zsh-only syntax and the Internet-Free
install hung.

Discovery now asks ps for ucomm (the executable name, which
tcsh can't overwrite) followed by comm, falling back to comm
alone where ps lacks ucomm. It tries ucomm first, then comm
and comm's first word, because xonsh runs under python and
names itself, with its arguments, only in its process title.
csh still maps to tcsh. Otherwise it falls back to $SHELL as
before.

QA finding F-451.

2026-10-24: Fix crash when AI Chats search finds a match

The search results table measured each row by configuring a
fresh ChatSearchResultCell and reading its fittingSize.
Since the chat cells moved from auto layout to manual frame
layout, the cell has no constraints, so fittingSize is zero.
NSTableView raises when a delegate returns a zero row
height, so inserting the first matching result crashed with
an uncaught exception.

Compute the row height with
RegularMessageCellView.cellHeight(for: maxBubbleWidth:),
which mirrors layout(), using the same bubble width the cell
is configured with. If the result is ever non-finite or not
positive, fall back to the height of a single-line bubble.

QA finding F-389.

2026-10-24: Keep the AI Usage report alert a manageable size

When a usage command's output passed the 1 MiB cap, the item
showed Unreadable usage data without mentioning the cap, and
the report alert put the whole diagnostic (about 1 MiB) into
its text. The sheet was taller than the screen, its buttons
were off-screen, and the terminal window was left pushed
down after Escape.

Show at most 2000 characters of the report in the alert (the
clipboard and bug report still get it all), and prefix the
diagnostic with a note when the output was cut off at the
cap. Decode the diagnostic lossily so output cut
mid-character is still shown.

QA finding F-375.

Cap the AI Usage report alert by rows as well as characters

The report alert showed at most 2000 characters of the
report, but a usage command like yes prints two-character
lines, so that was still about 1000 lines: the sheet ran off
the bottom of the screen with its buttons out of reach, and
Escape left the terminal window pushed down.

Also stop after 30 rows, counting a long line as the rows it
wraps to (assuming 80 characters per row). The clipboard and
bug report still get the whole report.

QA finding F-388.

2026-10-24: Retire the global AI custom headers once models
have their own

The one-time migration that copies the former global
AICustomHeaders list into each manual model treats a model
with an empty header list as unmigrated (the editor stamps
empty lists), and it never cleared the global list. On
another Mac sharing the prefs, where the NoSync done flag is
unset, a header the user had removed from a model was copied
back in.

Once configured models exist, turn off
AICustomHeadersEnabled after the migration (also on launches
where an earlier build already set the done flag) and
whenever the models panel saves configurations.  The legacy
single-model path, which still inherits the global list, is
left alone. The list itself is kept for older builds.

QA finding F-091.

Keep a model's emptied custom header list empty during
migration

The one-time copy of the former global AI custom headers
into each manual model treated a model with customHeaders =
[] as unmigrated and gave it the global list. An empty list
is what the model editor saves after the user removes every
header, so on a Mac whose NoSync done flag is unset, with
shared prefs last written by a build that left the global
headers enabled, a removed header came back.

Only a model with no header list at all now gets the global
headers.  The empty-list rule existed because the editor
could stamp an empty list before the lazily run migration;
the models panel now runs the migration before it reads the
configurations, so that can't happen.

QA finding F-386.

Carry the global AI custom headers into a converted legacy
model

A user with a single legacy manual AI model (no model
configurations) sends the former global custom headers with
its requests. Opening the models panel turns that model into
a configuration, but the conversion left its headers out, so
once the panel saved, the model sent none, and the global
list was then retired.

The conversion now copies the global headers when they are
turned on, using the per-model migration's sanitizing, which
it now shares. The sanitizing keeps well-formed entries one
by one, so a header list with a malformed entry, which the
legacy path dropped entirely, now carries its valid headers.
The one-time alert shown when the panel opens no longer says
that editing models loses the headers; it now says only new
models start without them.

Found while fixing QA finding F-386.

2026-10-24: Keep the Minimal theme's page tint from reaching
the web view

In the Minimal theme a browser session's view is light or
dark to match the page's background color. The web view
inherited that appearance, so once a dark page tinted the
session dark, a page that supports both color schemes
rendered its dark variant, whose dark background kept the
session dark through reloads and later pages.

In the Minimal theme, give the web view the app's own
appearance so pages follow the system setting while the
surrounding chrome still follows the page.

2026-10-24: Name settings.json, not its temp file, when a

QA finding F-454.

2026-10-24: Stamp a new chat's model vendor when the chat is
created

ChatBroker.create pins a new chat to the default model but
stored no vendor for the pin; the manual-model stamp from
the F-382 fix was only written by setModel, the launch
backfill, or the refresh before a turn.  So an empty chat
pinned to a manual model on a local server, whose model was
then renamed or removed in Settings, showed OpenAI in the
toolbar and sent its first message (and any attached
terminal context) to api.openai.com instead of failing.

New chats are now built by ChatBroker.makeChat, which stamps
the pin's vendor the same way setModel does.

QA finding F-384.

2026-10-24: Add a regression test for Session Status rows
following a job exit

A Session Status row's name label kept showing the job that
was running when the row was last configured (for example
sleep) after the job exited, while the tab title went back
to -zsh. Rows are configured only when the session's status
changes, so between configurations the label has to follow
the session's name variable on its own, and the label's
evaluator stopped observing that variable once its
asynchronous evaluation finished.

This was fixed by 5b2327dd0. These tests drive
ToolStatusCellView directly: they fail with that commit's
iTermGenericEvaluator.m change reverted and pass with it.

QA finding F-065.

2026-10-24: Substitute browser template placeholders in a
single pass

iTermBrowserTemplateLoader replaced each {{KEY}} in turn, in
dictionary order, so a substituted value that contained
{{OTHER_KEY}} could be expanded by a later pass. That let
untrusted text (a failed URL, an error description, a page's
source) pull other values into unintended contexts.
Placeholders are now replaced in one regex pass over the
template, so values are inserted verbatim; placeholders
without a substitution are left as-is, and COMMON_CSS and
INCLUDE are still handled first. With that, the error page
no longer needs to escape braces itself and goes back to
plain HTML escaping.

The view-source page put the HTML-escaped page URL inside a
single-quoted JavaScript string, where entities are not
decoded, so relative links were resolved against a corrupted
base URL. The URL now goes in an escaped data-url attribute
and the script reads it through dataset. The no-source
fallback fills URL with an empty string.

Found while fixing QA finding F-402.

2026-10-24: Focus the pane under the pointer when a sheet
closes

With Focus Follows Mouse on, moving the pointer into another
split pane while a sheet (such as Edit Tab Title) was open
on the window could not move focus, because the window can't
become key then. Focus follows mouse still counted the
pointer as having entered that pane, so once the sheet
closed and focus returned to the original pane, moving
around inside the pane under the pointer did nothing until
the pointer left it and came back.

When focus does not follow the pointer into a pane that
focus could otherwise move to, that pane is now remembered.
When a sheet on that pane's window ends, the pane gets focus
if the pointer is still in it and it isn't already focused.

QA finding F-403.

2026-10-24: Count each bad Dynamic Profiles file once in the
error alert

After a file's profiles failed to load, the Ignoring dynamic
profiles in  because of an error. line was reported as an
error of its own. That counted toward the alert's N
additional errors may be seen in the log, so N bad files
reported 2N-1 additional errors instead of N-1.

The follow-up line is now only written to the log. Loading a
file and logging that it was ignored is factored into
-loadDynamicProfilesFromFileLoggingFailure:intoArray:guids:
so a test can drive it.

QA finding F-301.

Don't double count Dynamic Profiles errors when the folder
reloads twice

When two bad Dynamic Profiles files appear at once while
iTerm2 runs, the folder watcher can reload twice within a
few milliseconds, before the error alert is shown. Each
reload reported every file's error again and added it to the
pending count, so two bad files reported 3 additional errors
may be seen in the log.

Each reload examines every file, so its errors now replace
the pending errors from the previous reload instead of
adding to them. Whether an alert is already queued is
tracked separately from the count, and if a later reload
finds nothing wrong the queued alert is not shown. The
reload bracketing is factored into -performReloadPass: so a
test can drive it.

The existing release note for F-301 already describes this
fix.

QA finding F-421.

2026-10-24: Let Show menu bar icon change when Dock
exclusion needs hotkey windows

With Exclude from Dock and -Tab Application Switcher on,
Settings showed Show menu bar icon checked and disabled,
since the icon is then the only way back to Settings. That
also happened with but only if all windows are hotkey
windows on, where iTerm2 is an ordinary Dock app whenever a
normal window is open and the icon follows the saved
setting. The box claimed the icon was on while it was
missing, and could not be changed.

The checkbox is now forced only when exclusion does not
depend on hotkey windows, and it refreshes when that setting
changes.

QA finding F-250.

Force Show menu bar icon on while only hotkey windows keep
iTerm2 out of the Dock

With Exclude from Dock and -Tab Application Switcher and but
only if all windows are hotkey windows on, the fix for F-250
stopped forcing the Show menu bar icon checkbox at all. When
no ordinary window is open iTerm2 is out of the Dock and the
menu bar icon is shown regardless, as the only way back to
Settings, but the checkbox showed unchecked and enabled, and
unchecking it did nothing. That is the state a user is in
when they open Settings from the icon.

The checkbox is now forced in that configuration while the
app is actually a UI element, matching how the menu bar icon
decides whether to show. iTermApplication posts a
notification when the UI-element state changes so Settings
refreshes the checkbox as ordinary windows open and close.

QA finding F-423.

2026-10-24: Keep the find field's caret after changing the
window style

Changing a window's style between one with a title bar and
one without replaces the NSWindow. Since the fix for F-139,
focus returns to the view that had it, such as the find
bar's search field, but making a text field first responder
selects all of its text. The next keystroke then replaced
the query instead of continuing it.

The field editor's selected ranges are now remembered before
the window is replaced and put back once the field has focus
again in the new window.

QA finding F-422.

2026-10-24: Escape untrusted text on the browser error page

The internal error page (iterm2-about:error) inserted the
failed URL, the error title and the error description into
its HTML without escaping, and pasted the failed URL raw
into a single-quoted JavaScript string for the Try Again
button. A quote survives in a URL's absoluteString, so a
link to a failing URL such as
http://host.invalid/');alert(1);// could run script in the
internal page, and markup in an error description (which can
include a user-supplied file path) was rendered as HTML.

All text substitutions are now HTML-escaped, with braces
escaped too so a value containing a {{PLACEHOLDER}} can't be
expanded by the template loader's later passes. The failed
URL is put in an escaped data-original-url attribute on the
button and read by the script through dataset, so it never
appears in JavaScript source. The already-escaped TLS
details are left alone, and the certificate chain label is
now escaped for consistency.

Privileges: the error page registers no message handler, and
the local page manager only accepts a message whose name
matches the current top-level iterm2-about: URL, so injected
script could not drive the settings, history or other
privileged handlers. It could run script in a
trusted-looking internal page (spoofing) and navigate to
other iterm2-about: pages, which navigation policy always
allows.

Found while fixing QA finding F-402.

footer

The browsers page-load error page had a translated heading
and message, but its Try Again button and its footer were
English literals in error-page.html. The page shown for an
ssh: URL whose host has no ssh integration connection was
entirely English. Both templates now take those strings as
placeholders filled with localized strings (with de, ja,
pt-BR, ru and zh-Hans translations), HTML-escaped, including
the host name on the ssh page.

2026-10-24: Treat a csh parent as tcsh in offline installer
discovery

On macOS, /bin/csh is tcsh, and ps reports a tcsh started
from a zsh login shell as -csh. Shell discovery stripped the
dash but did not recognize csh, so it fell back to $SHELL
(zsh), typed zsh-only syntax into tcsh, and the
Internet-Free install hung. Discovery now treats a parent
named csh as tcsh.

QA finding F-451 (follow-up to F-260).

2026-10-24: Keep a chat on a removed manual model from going
to OpenAI

A chat pinned to a manual model on a local OpenAI-compatible
server (for example LM Studio or llama.cpp at
http://127.0.0.1:8080) was sent to api.openai.com with its
history once that manual model was removed or renamed in
Settings. The F-380 fix stored each chat's pinned vendor but
deliberately left manual models on non-Ollama APIs
unstamped, since their vendor is guessed from the host and
an unrecognized host counts as OpenAI. An unstamped pin that
no longer resolved then fell back to OpenAI's recommended
model in the chat UI and to the global default in routing.

Such pins are now stamped with
LLMMetadata.manualModelPinVendor, a value outside
iTermAIVendor's range that means "a manual model". A pin
with it resolves only among the configured manual models;
when its model is gone the turn fails with an error that
points to Settings, even if a built-in model has the same
name. A manual model at its vendor's own public API host
(api.openai.com, api.anthropic.com, and so on, but not Azure
or a gateway) is stamped with that vendor instead, so it
keeps falling back within that vendor as before. A
Llama-named manual model on a non-Ollama API is a manual
pin, not an Ollama one.

Chats pinned before this change are stamped at launch while
their manual model is configured, and the stamp is never
changed by the backfill afterwards. Before each turn, a chat
stamped with a cloud vendor or none moves to the manual
stamp if its model is now a local manual model (for example,
its URL was moved off a vendor's host), so a later removal
fails instead of sending turns that went to the local server
to that vendor.  The chat UI keeps showing the missing
manual model so a send fails clearly, and the provider
binding rejects turns that name another provider's model.

QA finding F-382.

2026-10-24: Keep the AI Chats list selection on the chat
that moves to the top

Sending a message moves its chat to the top of the AI Chats
list. The list reloads on the model's change notification
and re-selects the previously selected chats by ID, but it
read those IDs by mapping the table's selected row indexes
through the already reordered model, so it re-selected
whichever chat now sat at the old row. The highlight stayed
on that row while the transcript showed the moved chat.

The list now records the selected chats' IDs whenever the
selection changes, while rows still match the model, and
re-selects those after the reload.

QA finding F-383.

2026-10-24: Resize AI chat @-mention chips when the input's
font changes

An @-mention in the AI chat input is a rasterized image
baked for one font. Changing the font of the input's text
with the Font menu or Font panel enlarged the typed text but
left the chips at their old size; they were re-rendered only
on an appearance change, and always in the text view's
default font.

ChatInputView is now the input's text storage delegate. When
an edit changes attributes, it re-renders each mention chip
in the edited range whose character font differs from the
font its image was rendered for. The appearance-change
re-render also uses each chip's own font.

QA finding F-371.

2026-10-24: Keep a re-bound tmux watch's names while the
reattached pane is unnamed

When a relaunch reattaches a tmux -CC pane, a held watch is
re-bound to the pane's new session as soon as its window
opens and takes its workgroup and role names from that
session. At that moment the pane has no name yet (its
session name is a blank " "), so list_watches, the
status_update, and the chat notice showed a blank workgroup
and role.

The rebind still takes the new workgroup and role IDs, but
keeps the watch's previous names when the new ones are
blank.

QA finding F-332.

2026-10-24: Keep an AI chat's pending timer armed when
orchestration is enabled

Enabling orchestration tore down the chat's dispatcher,
which owns the task that fires a pending register_timer
timer, but kept the timer saved.  Nothing re-armed it, so it
missed its deadline and fired only when the next
orchestration tool call recreated the dispatcher, or at
relaunch.

OrchestratorClient.enableOrchestration now drops the
dispatcher, writes the flag, and, if the chat still has
pending timers or watches, builds a fresh dispatcher at once
so it reconciles and re-arms them.  ChatViewController's
Enable Orchestration uses it.

QA finding F-278.

2026-10-24: Resume AI chat watches at launch and let Stop
cancel them without a dispatcher

At launch only chats with a persisted timer got an
orchestration dispatcher. A chat whose only watcher was a
session watch kept the watch saved but nothing ran it: no
screen polls, no tab-status checks, until something touched
the chat. Because Stop is handled by the dispatcher,
pressing Stop in such a chat neither cancelled the watch nor
showed the cancellation notice, and the watch later fired
and started the turn the user had tried to stop.

Once launch-time session restoration is done, a chat whose
watchers are all watches now gets a dispatcher too, if every
one of its watches can still run (its session was restored,
or it is an orchestration chat's tmux pane watch waiting for
reattach). Any other such chat is still left until the user
opens it, so dropping its watches doesn't start unattended
agent turns at launch.

OrchestratorClient also handles Stop itself: with a live
dispatcher it calls the (idempotent) userDidStop, and with
none it clears the chat's persisted watchers and publishes
the same cancellation notice, which also covers chats left
dormant under the administrator lockout.

QA findings F-133 and F-279.

2026-10-24: Recognize it2ssh's EndSSH mid-frame in a
restored session

When an SSH Integration connection dies abruptly, the
conductor hook treats the local it2ssh's OSC 1337;EndSSH as
an implicit unhook. Inside a %output frame it only does so
when the EndSSH carries this connection's it2ssh unique ID,
so a nested it2ssh's EndSSH relayed in the output is not
mistaken for it. A parser hooked in recovery mode, after a
session was restored by relaunch or by framer recovery,
never saw the SSH_INIT line and so did not know the ID.
Killing the remote sshd while output streamed then wedged
the tab: the local shell was in the foreground, but nothing
was drawn.

The restored conductor knows the ID, so pass the outermost
conductor's it2ssh unique ID to the parser when entering
recovery mode. Nested parsers still get nil, as their IDs
are not saved.

QA finding F-331.

2026-10-24: Draw only the cropped part of a Kitty
placeholder image

A Kitty Unicode-placeholder placement with a source crop
drew the wrong pixels, two ways. Converting a cell's source
rect to the bottom-up coordinates of the crop subtracted the
crop's origin instead of adding it, so a crop of the
top-left quadrant sampled below the image and drew nothing.
And a cell in the letterbox padding around the image sampled
outside the crop, so a strip of the neighboring part of the
image appeared along the crop's edge.

Add the crop origin, clip each cell's source rect to the
crop and shrink its dest rect in proportion, and skip a cell
that falls entirely in the padding. The Metal renderer now
places a dest rect whose origin is not at the bottom of the
cell correctly; before, every dest rect filled the whole
cell so its origin was always zero.

QA finding F-119.

2026-10-24: Decode JSON values in the remaining it2 commands

it2 app get-focus, app theme, app version, profile list,
profile show, profile get, the monitor commands and the tmux
pane lookup all took JSON-encoded profile properties,
variable values and function results and only trimmed the
double quotes, so escapes iTerm2 emitted leaked into the
output: a profile named Q/P "x" printed as Q\/P \"x\ and a
session name with a quote or backslash kept its escapes. A
JSON null also came back as the string null.

Replace trimJSONQuotes with jsonStringValue, which decodes a
JSON string, maps null to the empty string and passes other
values through, and use it at every call site that trimmed
quotes by hand. Rebuilt the checked-in it2 binary with make
paranoid-it2cli.

QA finding F-450.

2026-10-24: Keep Arabic joining at a selection edge in the
legacy renderer

With the GPU renderer off, the text of each background run
was built and shaped separately, and runs were split
wherever the selection or a find match started or ended. A
selection edge inside an Arabic or Persian word therefore
drew the letters on each side in isolated or initial forms
instead of joined.

On lines with right-to-left text, the foreground pass now
combines runs across selection and find-match edges, so the
word is shaped once. The attributed string builder takes the
line's selected columns and picks the selected-text or match
color per cell, and it no longer reuses the previous cell's
attributes when the highlight changes between them. The
legacy drawer already fills each color span of a multi-color
string.  Lines whose text color depends on the background
(minimum contrast, dimming, thin strokes on dark
backgrounds) and faint text still split.

QA finding F-080.

2026-10-24: Keep each pane's size when the API changes a
tab's layout

A real Tab.async_update_layout() change lost a column or row
and shrank the window a little each time. Two problems in
setSizesFromSplitTreeNode:

- A local split view variable shadowed the method's
parameter and was
  nil for a session pane, so the panes were laid out with no
  room for the dividers. When the root then got its computed
  size, the point came out of the last pane, which lost a
  cell.
- When only the split moved and the root kept its size,
nothing fit the
  sessions to their new frames, so they kept their old grids
  and the window was sized from those.

Rename the local and fit the sessions to their views before
fitting the window to the tab.

QA finding F-343.

2026-10-24: Put the cursor right after the session restored
banner

Appending the Session Contents Restored banner already moves
the preferred cursor position onto the banner, but
appendRestoredBanner then added the cursor's whole movement
on top of that. When the cursor had been mid-line, or the
banner wrapped, the rows were counted twice and a blank row
was left between the banner and the shell's first output.

The preferred cursor position is now simply where the cursor
ends up after the banner: the start of the row below it.

QA finding F-324.

2026-10-24: Don't treat a refused proxy icon drag as a click

Dragging the Current Directory status bar component's folder
icon when there was no directory to vend (deleted,
nonexistent, or remote) made mouseDragged return before
setting didDrag, so mouseUp saw a click and opened the
component's menu. Once the mouse moves past the drag
threshold, the gesture now counts as a drag whether or not a
drag session can start.

QA finding F-398.

2026-10-24: Punctuate the Cockpit's skipped-target status
and drop gone chips

When a Cockpit send skipped a closed target, the status
appended Cockpit.SkippedGoneTargets to
Cockpit.SentToSessions with a space, so every language got
two sentences run together with no period. The skipped name
was the chip label, which for a window or tab picked from
the @ picker included the picker's keyboard shortcut (for
example 2), stale once the window closed. The gone chip also
stayed in the composer, so every later send skipped it
again.

The status is now one localized string with a plural
substitution (de, ja, pt-BR, ru and zh-Hans translations),
replacing the separate skipped-targets key. Picker rows keep
the shortcut separate from the name, and a chip is labeled
with the name only. After a send, chips for targets that no
longer exist are removed from the composer.

QA finding F-396.

2026-10-24: Don't archive a tmux integration pane on closure

-hardStop skips archiving a tmux client, but -terminate sets
the session's tmux mode to none before it calls -hardStop (a
tmux client's termination is never undoable, so -hardStop
runs at once), so the check never saw a tmux client. With
Archive On Closure on, closing a tmux pane wrote an archive
that Undo Close could not restore, and such archives just
accumulated.

-terminate now records that the session was a tmux client,
and -hardStop checks that too.

QA findings F-104 and F-335.

2026-10-24: Stop double-escaping the composer's first file
suggestion

With AI command suggestions on, the composer's early result
(the longest common prefix of the file completions, built
from already shell-escaped names, or a history entry) is put
at the top of the completion list that comes back from the
AI request. didFindCompletions then escaped every non-AI
item again, so the row for "my file.txt" read "my\\\
file.txt" and a second, correctly escaped copy of the row
appeared below it.

CompletionItem now has a valueIsFinal flag. The early result
is marked final and the shared escaping helper leaves final
items alone.

QA finding F-277.

2026-10-24: Withdraw the it2 client record on Shell > tmux >
Detach

A tmux integration client advertises itself to it2 with an
@it2_client_<hex> server option. -[PTYSession tmuxDetach]
(Esc in the gateway) withdraws it before detaching, but
Shell > tmux > Detach and the other callers of
-[TmuxController requestDetach] sent only the detach, so the
option lingered on the server. it2 ignores records of
clients that aren't attached, so the only effect was a stale
option.

-requestDetach now withdraws the record before sending the
detach. No release note: there was no visible effect.

QA finding F-068.

2026-10-24: Answer iTerm2-only reports in tmux integration
panes

tmux answers most reports itself, so a tmux integration pane
doesn't send them. OSC 1337 ReportCellSize,
ReportWindowFrame and ReportScreenFrames are unknown to tmux
and pass terminalShouldSendReport:YES, but they were sent
with terminalSendReport:, which drops every report for a
tmux client, so they got no reply at all.

Add terminalSendReportAllowedInTmux:, which skips that
check, and use it for these three. Other reports keep the
tmux check.

QA finding F-058.

2026-10-24: Don't let a dragged workgroup member become a
float

Swap With Session refuses a swap that would make a workgroup
member float, but dragging the member onto a float in
another tab went through -[MovePaneController
dropFloatingPaneInTab:atWindowPoint:], which had no such
check, so the member was installed as a float in that tab.

The drop is now refused for a workgroup member, and
-dropPlacesFloatingPaneOverFloat: says no for one, so a
float doesn't offer itself as a drop target during the drag.

QA finding F-257.

2026-10-24: Anchor tmux floats to the right pane while a
filter is open

-tmuxFloatAnchorsForParseTree: found the tiled session for
each layout leaf by comparing candidate.tmuxPane. With Find
> Filter open on a tmux pane, the tab shows a synthetic
stand-in whose own pane id reads as 0, so when it came
before the real %0 in the tab it was taken for %0, and a

instead.  d1a49cbff switched the other lookups to
+tmuxLayoutPaneForSession: but missed this one.

Add +sessionShowingTmuxPane:amongSessions:, which matches by
+tmuxLayoutPaneForSession:, and use it for the anchors.

QA finding F-131.

2026-10-24: Don't count hidden floats toward the GPU
renderer's pane limit

-updateUseMetal turns the GPU renderer off when a tab has
six or more panes, and counted self.sessions, which includes
every float, hidden or not. Hidden floats already don't use
Metal and don't take part in the other Metal checks, but
hiding floats could not bring Metal back.

The limit now counts the tab's sessions less its hidden
floats.

QA finding F-129.

2026-10-24: Give floats restored from an arrangement the
arrangement's name

-restoreFloatingPanesFromArrangement: passed named:nil to
-_recursiveRestoreSessions:, so a float made from a saved
arrangement did not know which arrangement it came from,
while the tab's tiled panes did. Change Profile in
Arrangement was disabled for such a float, and the nag
offering to fix a broken profile in the arrangement could
not name the arrangement.

The arrangement name is now passed down to the floats as
well. Floats restored from an archive still get no name, as
before.

QA finding F-053.

2026-10-24: Ask tmux to resize a tmux float instead of
resizing it locally

A session-initiated resize (View > Restore Text and Session
Size, the Python API's grid size, and so on) of a float goes
through -[PTYTab sessionResizeFloatingPane:columns:rows:].
It declined tmux tabs, so a tmux float fell through to the
tiled path, which resized the float's session in place and
told tmux nothing. The content was laid out at the new size
inside the float's unchanged frame, tmux kept the old size,
and the float could draw blank until a later relayout.

For a tmux float it now sends tmux a resize-pane by the
difference in cells, as dragging the float's edge does, and
the layout change that comes back resizes the float.

QA findings F-048 and F-130.

2026-10-24: Don't highlight a pane under a float as a swap
target

While picking a pane for Swap With Session, each pane's
SplitSelectionView highlights itself on mouse moved events
from its tracking area. Tracking areas are not
occlusion-aware, so a tiled pane under a float was
highlighted too while the pointer was over the float, and
two panes said they would be swapped. The click itself
already went to the float.

The picker now ignores the pointer, and clears its
highlight, where a click would go to a float other than the
one the picker is in. Other views over the pane, such as its
own subviews, don't hide it.

QA finding F-051.

2026-10-24: Keep focus on a float docked by Control-drag

Dropping a dragged float on a tiled pane with Control held
docks it via -[MovePaneController
reallyDropInSession:half:atPoint:]. Removing the active
float from its tab activates the frontmost remaining float,
and the split that follows only activates the moved session
when its target is the active session. So with another float
open, focus went to that float instead of the docked pane.
With no other float, the tiled pane that took over was
usually the target, which hid the problem.

Docking a float in its own tab now activates the docked
session, as Dock Floating Pane already does.

QA finding F-050.

2026-10-24: Keep a maximized float's earlier frame on Undo
Close

Undo Close of a live float (within the undo timeout)
restores it with
-addRevivedFloatingSession:fromArrangement:, which passed
NSZeroRect for the frame from before maximizing. The
arrangement records that frame for a maximized float, but it
was dropped, so the float came back as a plain float filling
the tab and unmaximizing it could never return it to where
the user had it. The archived path already passed it.

Both paths now read the saved frame from the record the same
way, so a revived maximized float comes back maximized and
unmaximizes to its old frame.

QA finding F-148.

2026-10-24: Grow the Internet-Free installer's step list to
fit its text

The send-shell-commands page shows its steps in one text
field with the fixed height from the nib. The message added
for a failed script write wraps to two lines (three in
German), so the steps after it were cut off: the end of step
4 in English, and all of step 4 in German.

After the steps are rendered, the field is measured with its
cell at its fixed width, keeping the nib height as the
minimum. The field grows upward by the difference, the
controls beside it move up with it, the page grows by the
same amount so the buttons at the bottom stay put, and the
window controller refits the window to the page.

QA finding F-381.

Test that translated installer steps fit the step list

The Internet-Free installer's step list had a fixed height
sized for English, so in German step 4 for nushell was cut
off after two lines.  The step list now grows to fit its
text (sizeStepListToFit). Add a regression test that loads
the real nib, gives the step list the nushell steps in every
shipped language, and checks that the text fits, the list's
top stays put, and the buttons along the bottom neither move
nor overlap the steps.

QA finding F-364.

2026-10-24: Keep a running program's scroll region when
restoring its session

A restored grid read its scroll region (DECSTBM) from the
saved state, but a restored session is created at the wrong
size and then fit to its window, and every resize resets the
scroll region. After a crash and relaunch, a program that
was still running got full-screen scrolling instead of the
region it had set.

The grid now remembers the saved region and the size it was
saved at.  After the window fits its sessions, the region is
put back if the grid is at the saved size again and nothing
has set a region since. A restored session that is not
reattached to its program discards it.

QA finding F-225.

2026-10-24: Keep emoji ZWJ sequences and Base64 pastes
intact when stripping

With the advanced setting Strip zero-width bidi/format
characters when pasting? on, the strip removed every ZWJ, so
emoji ZWJ sequences such as  and  fell apart into separate
emoji. It also ran before Base64 encoding, so an Advanced
Paste with Base-64 encode no longer decoded to the copied
bytes.

Now a ZWJ between two pictographic characters (skipping a
variation selector or skin tone modifier before it) is kept,
approximating the UAX #29 emoji ZWJ rule. Other format
characters, including a ZWJ between letters, are still
removed. The strip is skipped entirely for Base64 pastes.

QA findings F-075 and F-207.

2026-10-24: Draw the copy mode cursor where its character is
drawn

Copy mode keeps its cursor in logical coordinates, as it
moves and selects in logical space, but both renderers drew
the cursor at that coordinate as if it were visual. On a
reordered right-to-left line the cursor appeared in the
blank area on the far side of the line and walked across
empty cells while the selection grew from the real text.

Add visualCopyModeCursorCoord to the drawing helper, which
maps the column through the line's bidi table the same way
the terminal cursor is mapped, and use it in the legacy and
Metal renderers.

QA finding F-082.

2026-10-24: Keep pre-base vowel signs before their consonant
in the legacy renderer

The CoreGraphics renderer moves each CoreText glyph into its
grid column, anchoring every column to its own first glyph.
A pre-base vowel sign such as Devanagari  in  is stored
after its consonant, so it belongs to a later column, but
CoreText draws it to the left of the consonant. Anchoring it
to its own column drew it after the conjunct, so  read    .
Before per-column anchoring (3.7.4) the order was right.

Now columns are gathered into groups first. A glyph that
starts a new column but sits left of the previous group's
baseline and belongs to a later character than any in that
group joins the group, whose baseline becomes its x. The
cluster keeps CoreText's internal layout and starts at its
first cell. A reversed left-to-right island in a
right-to-left line never matches, because its later columns
hold earlier characters, so it is still placed column by
column.

QA finding F-081.

2026-10-24: Start legacy underlines at the leftmost drawn
cell of an RTL run

The CoreGraphics renderer drew an underline or strikethrough
from the position of the first character in the attribute
run. On a bidi line the positions are visual, so for a
right-to-left run the first character is drawn rightmost and
the line started about a word's width too far right: printf
' \e[4m' underlined the end of  and the space instead of .

Now the line, and the underline mask that cuts around
descenders, start at the smallest position in the run, in
both the attributed-string and fast paths. Left-to-right
runs are unchanged because their first position is already
the smallest.

QA finding F-078.

2026-10-24: Anchor the Look Up highlight where an RTL word
is drawn

Look Up finds the word under the pointer in logical
coordinates, then anchored its highlight and popover at
pointForCoord of the range's logical start. On a reordered
right-to-left line that column is far from where the word is
drawn, so the yellow highlight landed over blank cells at
the other side of the row.

Now the anchor is the leftmost visual cell of the word
range, via the bidi-aware extractor. Left-to-right lines are
unchanged.

QA finding F-320.

2026-10-24: Drop alternate-screen bidi tables when
reordering is turned off

Turning on the advanced setting Do not reorder right-to-left
text in the alternate screen while a full-screen app showed
RTL text left the alternate grid's existing bidi tables in
place. shouldPopulateRTLState returned NO in that mode, so
populateRTLState never ran the branch that clears them. The
renderer kept drawing the rows reordered from the grid's
tables while selection treated them as unreordered, so drags
highlighted and copied the mirror-image cells.

Now shouldPopulateRTLState returns YES once more when the
alternate grid still has bidi tables, so they are dropped
and the rows redraw in logical order.

QA findings F-074 and F-313.

2026-10-24: Treat Azure's sovereign and serverless AI hosts
as Azure AI services

With the API key set to Automatic, only *.openai.azure.com,
*.cognitiveservices.azure.com and *.services.ai.azure.com
counted as OpenAI's own host. Azure OpenAI in Azure
Government (*.openai.azure.us, *.cognitiveservices.azure.us)
and Azure China (*.openai.azure.cn,
*.cognitiveservices.azure.cn), and serverless model
deployments on *.models.ai.azure.com, got no key, so users
there had to pick it by hand. The sovereign hosts also
failed the *.azure.com test that picks Azure's request
format, so a key chosen explicitly went out as a Bearer
token instead of in the api-key header.

All of these hosts are run by Microsoft, so add them to
hostIsAzureAIServiceAPI, and have hostIsAzureAIAPI accept
every Azure AI service host. Look-alike hosts are still
refused.

QA finding F-373.

2026-10-24: Don't double the typed directory when expanding
{{FILE}}

Typing please delete logs/er and getting the suggestion `rm
logs/{{FILE}}` produced rm logs/logs/error.log: {{FILE}} is
expanded with the whole typed word plus the completion
suffix, and the model had already written the directory.

When the text right before {{FILE}} starts a shell word
(optionally after ./) with the directory part of the typed
word, substitute the name without that directory.

The prompt also described the file completion suffixes
(ror.log) as the longest common prefix of valid filename
completions. Give it the full names (typed word plus suffix)
instead.

2026-10-24: Stop logging every failed AI plugin validation

QA finding F-109.

2026-10-24: Escape control characters in claude_usage.sh's

json_escape only escaped backslash, quote, newline and tab,

QA finding F-245.

QA finding F-247.

2026-10-24: Let the user stop a running AI Test Connection

Test Connection disabled its button for the whole test.
Against an unreachable host the request only fails at the AI
idle timeout (1200 seconds by default), so the editor sat on
Testing for minutes with no way out short of cancelling the
editor.

AIConnectionTester.test now returns an AIConnectionTest that
reports its outcome once and can be cancelled, which also
cancels the plugin request. While a test runs the button
reads Stop Test and stops it; cancelling the editor stops it

QA finding F-246.

2026-10-24: Return focus to the chat input when the chat

2026-10-24: Use the Internet-free installer when the website

even though the bundled (Internet-free) installer handles

QA finding F-420.

2026-10-24: Restore the find field's caret when focus
follows the mouse back

With focus follows mouse moving focus within panes,
returning the pointer to a pane whose find field had focus
made the field first responder again, and NSTextField
selects all of its text when it becomes first responder. The
next key typed replaced the query.

Before moving focus away, remember the selection of the text
field being edited in the window, and restore it after
making that field first responder again. If the field's
editor is still the window's first responder (focus went to
another window and back), leave it alone instead of making
it first responder again.

QA finding F-038.

2026-10-24: Keep a failed pairing's error when AI is toggled

refreshGateState rebuilds the ready-to-pair state when AI
availability changes so its AI advisory stays current,
unless a pairing is in progress, which it detected with
isListening. A failed pairing is no longer listening, so
turning generative AI on while the window showed Pairing
failed:  wiped the error, its tooltip and the QR code and
went back to the idle Show QR Code state.

Track whether the top shows a pairing failure (cleared
whenever the top changes state) and don't rebuild while it
does.

QA findings F-100 and F-336.

2026-10-24: Forget cached password manager account names
when auth is revoked

Fetching accounts saves their display names in a class-level
cache so the Open Password Manager trigger's parameter popup
can list them. The cache was never cleared, so after a
screen lock revoked authentication the trigger editor still
showed saved account and user names without
re-authenticating.

Observe the data source provider's revocation notification
for each password manager class and clear its cache then.
Clearing posts the accounts-loaded notification, so an open
trigger editor reloads.

QA finding F-323.

2026-10-24: Don't animate status bar sparklines in Low Power
Mode

In Low Power Mode the sparkline's scroll animation set
preferredFrameRateRange to 1 fps, but that is only a hint:
on an M1 in real Low Power Mode the graph kept moving
smoothly between updates, so the energy saving didn't
happen.

In Low Power Mode, move the graph in one step per update
without an animation, as the

QA finding F-391.

2026-10-24: Tint the workgroup git arrows for the toolbar's
appearance

The workgroup toolbar's git item supplies labelColor as the
tint for the ahead/behind arrow bitmaps, and the tinting
resolves that dynamic color against the app's theme
appearance. With the Minimal theme the toolbar takes its
appearance from the terminal background, so a dark
background in a light system gave light text but black
arrows.

Resolve labelColor against the toolbar view's effective
appearance before handing it to the string maker. The item
already rebuilds its string when that appearance changes.

QA finding F-334.

2026-10-24: Stop swallowing keys while an announcement
slides away

A Press any key to dismiss announcement (such as the
clear-scrollback one shown after `clear`) stays the session
view's current announcement for the 0.25 second slide-out
after the first key dismisses it.  handleKeyDown: kept
returning YES for dismissOnKeyDown announcements during that
time, so every key typed then was eaten: typing `echo
LOSTTEST4` right after `clear` ran `OSTTEST4`.

Once the announcement is dismissing, return NO so the keys
reach the session. Only the key that dismissed it is
consumed.

QA finding F-282.

A session started with a command is saved with both a

is Yes with the same command.  RiskAnalyzer added the
program count and the custom command count, so one command
was reported as 2 custom commands that will run when the
arrangement opens, for tiled panes and floating panes alike.

Count a session as running a command once if either its
Program or its profile names one.

QA finding F-329.

2026-10-24: Keep -suite when LetsMove relaunches the app

LetsMove's Relaunch() ran `/usr/bin/open <path>` with no
arguments, both after moving the app to Applications and
after the de-translocation relaunch. An instance started
with -suite NAME came back on the default settings domain
and opened that domain's restored windows.

Forward a -suite argument from the current process to the

QA finding F-392.

2026-10-24: Fail -compare-rendering when another instance
holds the lock

-compare-rendering suppresses alerts by auto-answering them
with the first button. When another instance held the
suite's instance lock, that picked Quit on the iTerm2 Is
Already Running warning, so the process exited with status
0, printed nothing and ran no comparison.  A script would
treat that as success.

In compare-rendering mode, print a RESULT: ERROR line naming
the other process to stderr and exit with status 1 instead
of showing the warning.

QA finding F-220.

2026-10-24: Leave a tab alone when the API sets an unchanged
layout

Tab.async_update_layout() with no changes sent back the tree
it was given, and apiServerSetTabLayout:handler: applied it
anyway. Turning each pane's grid size into a view size and
fitting the sessions back to those views can lose a cell, so
every no-op call shrank the panes and the window by a column
(a point for a browser pane).

When every session's requested grid size matches the current
one, report success without touching the layout. A real
change still goes through setSizesFromSplitTreeNode:, whose
rounding is not addressed here.

QA finding F-073.

2026-10-24: Return JSON null for built-in functions that
return NSNull

functionInvocationDidCompleteWithObject:error:completion:
encoded the result with it_jsonStringForObject, which
returns nil for a top-level NSNull. Functions that succeed
with no value (set_floating_pane_frame, raise_floating_pane,
dock_floating_pane, and move_session_to_new_tab or _window
for tmux) return NSNull, so the response had an empty
json_result and the Python client raised JSONDecodeError
instead of returning None.

Encode NSNull as "null" in a small class method used for the
result.

QA finding F-128.

2026-10-24: Keep Remember unticked when the basic-auth
prompt returns

After the user opened the password manager from the
basic-auth prompt and backed out of the picker, the
coordinator re-prompted with rememberByDefault set whenever
Remember was offered, so the checkbox came back ticked
although the user never ticked it, and OK then tried to save
the credential.

Carry the checkbox state with the open-password-manager
outcome, store it on the challenge context, and restore it
on the re-prompt. Without a recorded state, use the first
prompt's rule (on only after a saved credential was
rejected).

QA finding F-033.

2026-10-24: Count Advanced Paste preview lines correctly

The Advanced Paste stats label counted lines with -[NSString
numberOfLines]. NSStringITerm defines that with
lineRangeForRange:, but SmartSelectionController's
NSString(iTermTextDataSource) category also defines
numberOfLines as the number of LF-separated components, and
that one won at runtime. So 1\n2\n3\n4\n5\n read as 6 lines,
and the same text with Convert CRLF and LF to CR checked
read as 1 line.

Rename the NSStringITerm method to it_lineCount so it can't
collide, and compute the label from the preview string
through a new class method that uses it. The Russian
translation also put the line count in the nominative ( 1 );
use the prepositional forms.

QA finding F-303.

2026-10-24: Let Cmd-W close the Cockpit

Shell > Close is wired to closeCurrentSession:, not
performClose:. When the Cockpit panel was key nothing in its
responder chain implemented it, and PseudoTerminal's
validation enables the item only when the key window's
controller responds to it, so the item was disabled and
Cmd-W could not close the panel.

Implement closeCurrentSession: on the Cockpit window
controller to perform a close, as the Companion pairing
window and other panels do.

2026-10-24: Clamp SetWindowFrame to the title bar's minimum
width

The SetWindowFrame clamp used the largest tab minimum plus
decorations and the window's minSize as the smallest allowed
frame. For a titled window that is far narrower than a user
can drag it: AppKit also keeps a titled window at least as
wide as its title bar buttons need, so
SetWindowFrame=0;0;0;0 produced a 24-point sliver with a 2x2
grid while drag-resizing stopped at about 87 points.

Also enforce NSWindow's minimum frame width for the window's
style mask when it is titled, the same limit live resize
hits.

QA finding F-395.

2026-10-24: Disable Prefer integrated GPU on a Mac without

The Advanced GPU Settings sheet's view controller disabled

Factor the check into +canPreferIntegratedGPU and give the

QA finding F-254.

QA finding F-222.

2026-10-24: Show the default in a string popup whose saved
value isn't in its menu

Settings selected a string popup's item with
-indexOfItemWithRepresentedObject: and skipped the selection
only when that returned NSNotFound. It returns -1 when
nothing matches, so a value missing from the menu (for
example a Dynamic Profile naming a progress bar color scheme
that doesn't exist) went to -selectItemAtIndex:-1, which
cleared the selection and left the popup blank.

Add -it_selectItemWithRepresentedObject:fallback:, which
falls back to the setting's default value, and use it for
string popups. A nil or NSNull value still leaves the popup
alone, and when neither value is in the menu it still shows
no selection, so the keyboard locale popup stays blank when
no locale is set instead of showing some other input source.

QA finding F-265.

2026-10-24: Don't move a hotkey window to a tmux window's
saved position

With Settings > General > tmux set to open tmux windows as
tabs in the attaching window, running tmux -CC attach in a
hotkey window that held only the gateway tab applied the
tmux window's saved origin to the hotkey window, leaving it
mostly off screen until it was hidden and shown again.

A hotkey window's position comes from its profile, so the
saved origin is no longer applied to one. The decision is
factored into
+shouldApplySavedOriginUsingOriginalWindow:initialTabs:initial:isHotKeyWindow:
so it can be tested without a tmux server.

QA finding F-273.

2026-10-24: Update pane title bars for sessions exchanged by
Swap With Session

-swapSession:withSession: refreshed each tab's pane titles
and refit its sessions before updating the tabs'
view-to-session maps. -sessions finds sessions through that
map, so each tab skipped the session it had just received.
Swapping the lone pane of a one-pane tab into a split tab
left it without a title bar beside neighbors that had one,
gave the one-pane tab a title bar, and could change the
window's height.

The maps are now updated right after the sessions' delegates
change, before anything asks either tab for its sessions.

QA finding F-317.

2026-10-24: Don't copy Lock Location to a duplicated window

Window > Duplicate Window opened the copy from the
original's full arrangement, which includes its Lock
Location state. The copy was locked to the original's frame,
so it opened exactly on top of it and snapped back there
when moved.

The duplicate is now built with representsThisWindow:NO, the
same way a tab group saved as an arrangement is, which
leaves the lock out.

QA finding F-037.

2026-10-24: Keep keyboard focus in a window after changing
its style

Switching between a window style with a title bar and one
without (for example Window > Window Style > No Title Bar)
replaces the NSWindow. The replacement was only ordered
front, so after the old key window closed there was no key
window and the new one's first responder was the window
itself. Typing went nowhere, and menu items that act on the
current window, including Window Style, were disabled until
the user clicked the window.

The replacement window now becomes key when the old one was.
Its first responder is the view that had focus before, if
that view is inside the content view that moves to the new
window (such as the find bar), and otherwise the current
session's view.

QA findings F-139, F-154, and F-040.

2026-10-24: Keep Cmd-H, Cmd-Q, Cmd-W, and Cmd-M out of
alerts' automatic shortcuts

An alert's buttons get an automatic Cmd-<letter> shortcut
from their labels, so in the Cant Find LastPass CLI alert
Cmd-H pressed Help (opening a web page) instead of hiding
iTerm2. Those four letters are the system-wide Hide, Quit,
Close, and Minimize shortcuts, so automatic shortcuts now
skip them. A shortcut the caller assigns is still kept.

QA finding F-337.

2026-10-24: Keep Cmd on an alert's shortcuts when it is
shown again

makeAlert wrote the shortcuts it chose back into each
action's keyEquivalent: the Return or Escape that NSAlert
gave a button, and the automatic Cmd-<letter> taken from the
label. When the same warning was built again, as Permanently
Forget Saved Selection does to re-show it, those values
looked like ones the caller had chosen and were copied to
the buttons with no modifier mask, so a plain letter pressed
a button (bare a opened Advanced Paste).

The automatic shortcut now lives in a private
automaticKeyEquivalent property that is recomputed each
time, and the action's keyEquivalent is never changed, so
every build of an alert gets the same shortcuts.

QA findings F-028 and F-366.

2026-10-24: Keep a local-model chat on Ollama when its model
disappears

A chat created with Llama (Local) while Ollama was running
is pinned to the discovered tag, e.g. qwen-fake:1b. If that
tag later disappeared from Ollama's list, the pinned name no
longer resolved. Routing only refused unresolved names
containing "llama", so AIConversation fell back to the
global default vendor and the chat UI mapped the name to
OpenAI's recommended model: the chat and its history were
sent to api.openai.com (or DeepSeek, for a deepseek-named
tag).

Each chat now stores the vendor of its model pin
(Chat.modelVendor, a new migrated column), stamped whenever
the pin changes. Existing chats are backfilled when the chat
list loads, wherever the name still identifies the vendor
(it resolves, it is the discovery placeholder or a
Llama-family name, or it is an Ollama-style name:tag); other
chats keep the old behavior. Manual models on non-Ollama
APIs are not stamped, because their vendor is guessed from
the host and an unrecognized local server counts as OpenAI.

LLMMetadata.resolvePinnedModel resolves a pin within its
vendor. An Ollama pin resolves only among Ollama models;
when its model is gone the turn fails with an error instead
of going to any other vendor or silently to a different
local model. Other vendors keep their behavior: the pinned
model, or the vendor's recommended model for a retired one.
The rule is applied to chat turns (including turns from the
phone), chat renaming, icon generation, the provider binding
check, and the chat UI, which keeps showing the missing
model on Ollama so a send uses it and fails clearly.

QA finding F-380.

2026-10-24: Don't read a partial line as the zsh dotfile
directory

The Internet-Free installer asks zsh for its dotfile
directory and matched the reply with ^My dotfiles go in(.*),
treating an empty capture as $HOME. Expectations are also
checked against partial lines, so on a slow link the reply
could match as soon as My dotfiles go in arrived, and a user
with ZDOTDIR set got $HOME: the script went to $HOME and
.zshrc would have been amended there, which zsh never reads.

The reply now ends in a marker the regex requires.

QA finding F-325 (T-24).

2026-10-24: Detect the shell reading the offline installer's
commands

The Internet-Free shell integration installer decided which
shell to install for from $SHELL. When the session runs a
different shell, such as tcsh started from a zsh login
shell, it typed zsh-only syntax into tcsh, which rejected
it, and the installer waited forever.

The discovery command now also reports the name of sh's
parent process, which is the shell the commands are typed
into, and uses it when it is a supported shell. It falls
back to $SHELL when ps is unavailable or the shell runs
under an interpreter (xonsh under python). The reply ends in
a marker so a partially received line is not taken for the
answer.

QA finding F-260.

2026-10-24: Give settings.json the default mode when
replacing a dangling symlink

Installing the Claude Code hooks when
~/.claude/settings.json is a dangling symlink replaced the
link with a regular file, as intended, but the atomic write
carried the symlink's own mode over, so the new
settings.json was 0755: executable and world-readable, where
a newly created one is 0644.

When the path is a dangling symlink, write a new file beside
it and rename it over the link, so it gets the mode any new
file gets.

QA finding F-072.

2026-10-24: Accept an empty settings.json when uninstalling
Claude Code hooks

Uninstalling the Claude Code integration with an empty or
whitespace-only ~/.claude/settings.json reported that the
file couldn't be parsed as JSON and asked whether to
continue, because JSONSerialization rejects empty input.
Install already treats such a file as an empty object. It
has no hooks to remove, so the strip now succeeds without
touching it.

QA finding F-365 (T-52).

2026-10-24: Keep hand-written triggers across a profile
reload

On a profile reload, PTYTriggerEvaluator reuses each
unchanged trigger so it keeps its state, matching the
profile's trigger dictionaries against the cached triggers'
dictionaryValue. The profile side was only lightly
normalized, so a hand-written trigger (in a Dynamic Profile,
say) that omits keys dictionaryValue always writes, such as
contentregex or matchType, never matched. Every reload
rebuilt it and lost its state, and a partial-line trigger
then fired a second time on the same line.

Build the trigger from the profile dictionary and match on
its own dictionaryValue, so both sides are normalized the
same way.

QA finding F-266 (T-39).

2026-10-24: Show a restored session status in the tab bar

A session's status came back from a saved arrangement into
the Session Status tool and session.status, but the tab bar
showed no subtitle or dot for it. The tab caches the winning
session's status and refreshes it only when a session
reports a change or the tab's sessions change. Restoration
adopted the status silently, and it can finish after the
session has already joined its tab, so the tab never learned
of it.

Adopting a restored status now reports a change like any
other, flagged as a restoration so PTYSession doesn't post a
status-change notification for it.

string

The API takes a variable's value as JSON and ignores one
that doesn't parse, so it2 session set-var user.x hello
printed success and exited 0 while setting nothing; only a
quoted '"hello"' worked. Send a value that already parses as
JSON unchanged and encode anything else as a JSON string.
Rebuilt the checked-in it2 binary with make paranoid-it2cli.

2026-10-23: Make browser sessions work in iTerm2 Buddy

Streaming a web browser session showed a black screen, and

stream snapshots of the web view and are interactive from

- Tell the phone when a text field has focus, checking the
