2026-10-22: Send arrow keys and Tab an IME hands back after
committing, not just Return and Backspace. Issue 13030

An input method can commit its composition and then hand the
keystroke back as a command selector. Only deleteBackward:,
its decomposing variant, and insertNewline: were sent to the
terminal. Any other selector, such as moveLeft: for an arrow
key or insertTab: for Tab, was dropped along with its key.

Add insertTab:, insertBacktab:, moveLeft:, moveRight:,
moveUp:, and moveDown: to the selectors that are sent.

Changes from the original version of this commit, made in
review:

- It originally sent every selector except noop:. A key
bound to several
  selectors (Option-Up is (moveBackward:,
  moveToBeginningOfParagraph:)) calls doCommandBySelector:
  once per selector, so the key reached the terminal twice.
  It also would have started sending keys nobody had
  checked, such as Ctrl-letters bound to editing selectors.
  An explicit list keeps the change to the keys that were
  tested.
- The key is now sent at most once per keypress, even if a
user's
  DefaultKeyBinding.dict binds a key to a listed selector
  more than once.
- Added tests for a key bound to several selectors and for
unlisted
  selectors.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-22: Make tmux divider drags end where the window
showed them (#796)

When a divider in a tmux tab was dragged, the resize was
sent as resize-pane to the first pane of the subview before
it. tmux moves the divider of the nearest enclosing split of
the same direction as seen from that pane, so with nested
splits (three columns whose first column is itself split
left/right) it moved the inner divider and the dragged one
snapped back. Some dividers have no pane that can address
them at all.

Now the drag is sent as select-layout with a layout built
from tmux's last layout, in which the two cells beside the
divider get the sizes their panes were shown at, rounded to
whole cells. Nested splits beside the divider share the
change as the window previewed. Everything else keeps tmux's
sizes. resize-pane, sent to a pane whose split owns the
divider, is the fallback when no layout can be built
(floating panes, a maximized pane, or a resize still waiting
on tmux, since the layout would be stale). If neither works,
the layout is fetched from tmux again.

- The layout is quoted; unquoted, tmux parses its braces as
a command
  block.
- A moved-divider layout counts as an outstanding resize
until tmux
  replies. If tmux rejects it, the layout is re-synced.
- Commands that tmux should always be able to run can now be
flagged to
  report a malformed-command error (a critical error in
  debug builds, an RLog otherwise) instead of being
  tolerated silently.
- iTermTmuxLayoutBuilder placed nested splits at the wrong
offsets.  - The new controller methods do nothing after a
detach.

Based on a fix by breakerr, who found the nested-split case.

Co-authored-by: breakerr <breakerr@qq.com>

2026-10-21: Harden the x-man-page URL handler. Issue 5298.

Validate man page names, apropos patterns, and sections
against an explicit allowlist instead of the
sanitizedCommand blacklist. The old code let a URL name a
file path, which /usr/bin/man renders directly and whose .so
directives it evals, and let an apropos pattern begin with a
dash, which man.sh's second getopts pass reads as an option.

An operand must now start with an ASCII letter, digit, or
underscore and may contain only ASCII alphanumerics and . _
+ : @ -. Sections must be ASCII alphanumeric. Empty operands
get their own warning instead of the disallowed-character
one. The unused sanitizedCommand: helper is removed and the
validators are exposed as class methods with unit tests.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-21: Fix vertically cropped Kitty images in the GPU
renderer. Issue 13146.

A direct Kitty placement's source frame is stored with a
bottom-left origin, but the Metal renderer used it as a
top-left texture rect, so a y=/h= crop sampled the mirrored
band. Flip it first, as the unicode placeholder path already
does. Adds unit tests and a manual repro script.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-21: Evaluate trigger-local interpolated parameters
without pausing

A trigger parameter written as an interpolated string was
always evaluated with the session's variable scope, which
costs a paused side effect. Token execution then stops until
the main queue flushes side effects, which takes up to a
second for a session that isn't visible.  tmux panes share
TaskNotifier's reader thread, so a paused pane whose token
queue fills blocks output in every session. Idempotent
triggers like Make Hyperlink are rescanned constantly, so
this happened often.  Issue 13160.

Parameters that refer only to matches[N] and line are now
classified once, cached, and filled in from a template on
the mutation queue with no pause. Everything else is
evaluated as before, but performBlockWithScope: now uses an
urgent paused side effect so background sessions wait for
the foreground flush period instead of the background one.

2026-10-21: Fix the Make Hyperlink trigger's interpolated
default parameter

The interpolated default was https://\(match0), but no
match0 variable exists. Trigger expressions get `matches`
(an array) and `line`, so use https://\(matches[0]).

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-21: Fix tab bar corruption when unpinning a tab left
of the pin boundary. Issue 13154

The reorder after a pin change and the pinned-zone clamp in
moveTabAtIndex:toIndex: both computed the destination from
the last pinned index in the current order. When the moving
tab sat left of that boundary (unpinning any pinned tab but
the rightmost, or mid-way through unpinning a group), the
destination was one past the end, so PSMTabBarControl threw
after removing the cell and its group chips, leaving the bar
out of sync with the tabs.

Count the other pinned tabs instead, guard against
out-of-range destinations, unpin group members right to left
so the group keeps its order, and add logging along the
path.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-20: Make Hide Activity Indicator also hide the tab

OSC 9;4 progress drove the tab's activity indicator
directly, bypassing the Hide Activity Indicator setting,
which only gated the busy state.  PSMTabBarControl now asks

the window re-pushes processing state when settings change
so toggling the setting takes effect immediately.

2026-10-20: Honor disabled ligatures when not preferring
speed to ligature support

When the advanced setting preferSpeedToFullLigatureSupport
is off, the legacy renderer shapes every run with CoreText
but left the ligature level at the font's default even when
ligatures were disabled. Fonts with a standard "fi" ligature
drew it in one cell and left a gap. Set the ligature level
to 0 when ligatures are disabled for the character's class.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-20: Keep the kitty keyboard protocol across
XTMODKEYS requests. Issue 13138

CSI > Ps ; Pv m and CSI > Ps n used to zero the kitty
keyboard flags and empty the push stack, so an app that
pushed kitty flags and then asked for modifyOtherKeys (as
Claude Code does) fell back to the modifyOtherKeys mapper,
which never reports Command keys. kitty ignores these
sequences and Ghostty keeps the kitty protocol in either
order, so now they only record the modifyOtherKeys-style
setting. Kitty flags already take precedence in the key
mapper, and the recorded level takes effect once the app
pops its flags. Full terminal resets still clear everything.

Also fix an off-by-one in the CSI > Ps n bounds check.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-19: Fix Caps Lock reports when it switches input
sources. Issue 13136

With the Kitty keyboard protocol on and macOS set to switch
input sources with Caps Lock, macOS changes the lock state
with synthetic flags-changed events that have keyCode 255.
These had no key number, so they were reported as malformed
sequences like CSI 1:3 u, which some apps read as Control-C.

Treat a lock-state change from an unknown key code as Caps
Lock. When the setting is on, report nothing for a tap that
switches input sources, and report a press and release when
a hold or tap leaves the lock state changed. Never send a
flags-changed report without a key number.

Add unit tests for each recorded event sequence, harness
steps for them, and a script that prints the key reports a
terminal sends.

2026-10-19: Recycle Snippets tool cells and reset their
icons on reuse

The cell views never set their own identifier, so
makeViewWithIdentifier: never recycled them. Set it so it
does. Folders and snippets share a reuse identifier, so also
set the icon and tint each time a cell is used rather than
only when it is created; otherwise a recycled cell shows the
other kind's icon.

2026-10-19: Keep Auto Layout out of the terminal window.
Issue 13118.

With the Minimal or Compact theme and the Snippets tool
enabled in the toolbelt, a window could be made wider but
not narrower. The inline AI chat panel caused the same
thing.

Any constraint in a terminal window engages the layout
engine for the whole window. AppKit then turns every
manually laid out view's autoresizing mask into constraints
and clamps live resizes to the content view's fitting size.
Several hidden views keep stale frames whose fixed margins
become a minimum width: the hidden tab bar backing
(CGRectZero since the layout calculator refactor, so the
minimum was the current width and the window ratcheted), and
the hidden toolbelt and fake title label (a fixed floor when
the tab bar is shown).

The Snippets cells pinned their text field and icon with
constraints.  Lay out IconTableCellView by hand in layout(),
and use it for the plain cell too. ChatInputView turned off
translatesAutoresizingMaskIntoConstraints on its subviews
even though it lays them out by hand; that gave its buttons
and hint label content-size constraints. Stop doing that.

An audit of code, xibs, the browser plugin and the inline
chat found no other constraints in the terminal window.

2026-10-19: Size tmux panes with each pane's own right
gutter

When applying a tmux layout, every leaf was sized with the
window profile's right extra and no session, so right-gutter
panels (clippings, inline chat) and per-pane adjacent
timestamps were ignored. The pane showing them was too
narrow for its grid, the panel covered the ends of its
lines, and the window was left with an empty strip on the
right.  Annotate each leaf with its session's
desiredRightExtra alongside the toolbar flag and use it in
the geometry pass, falling back to the profile only when the
pane has no session yet.

2026-10-17: Fix a main-thread hang waiting on the Metal
layer lock. Issue 13139

iTermMetalLayerBox holds its lock across
CAMetalLayer.nextDrawable() on the get-drawable queue, and
the layer's drawable timeout was disabled, so a fetch could
hold the lock forever when the drawable pool drained (for
example under heavy memory pressure). The main thread then
blocked on the same lock in per-frame property accesses and
never recovered.

- Re-enable the layer's drawable timeout so nextDrawable()
holds the lock
  for at most about a second. A fetch that completes with a
  nil drawable ends the frame instead of retrying, so the
  retry loop can't spin.
- Skip the lock in the presentsWithTransaction setter, which
runs every
  frame, when the cached value already matches.
- Move the zero-size check into
iTermMetalLayerBox.nextDrawable() so it
  runs under the lock. It throws, the fetch rejects its
  promise, and the retry loop no longer asserts that every
  rejection is a timeout or reuses a failed promise.
- Add TimedMutex and use it for the layer lock. Setting the
drawable size
  defers while our own fetch is outstanding; fetchDrawable
  waits on that fetch (bounded by the drawable timeout) and
  then applies the size, and won't hand out a drawable until
  it has. Main-thread waiting per frame stays bounded by the
  drawable timeout.
- Add iTermPromiseErrorDomain instead of repeating the
literal.

2026-10-16: Push relay: second instance with peer
replication; apps fail over

The push relay was a single instance, so a VPS outage at its
host lost every push for hours. Add a second instance
(push2.iterm2.com) and client failover, with no load
balancer, so nothing is billed per request.

Apps: CompanionPushRelay lists both hosts. The phone
registers with the first that answers; the Mac pushes
through the first that delivers. Both share one loop
(tryInOrder) and move on unless the relay itself says the
request is bad (400), or, for the Mac, rate limited (429),
the APNs rejection is one any relay would get, or the secret
was revoked ("bad secret"). After a failover the Mac prefers
the host that worked for 5 minutes.

Relays (host/peer.js, Node host only; src/worker.js
unchanged): each relay queues every registration in a SQLite
outbox before replying to the phone and forwards it to its
peer's /peer/register, authenticated with a shared secret.
A single drain loop sends live rows before backfill, backs
off failing rows, and gives up a pass when the peer looks
down. The receiver keeps the later registeredAt (relies on
NTP) and writes atomically; a phone registration is stamped
after anything the relay already held. Each relay resends
everything at startup, on SIGUSR2, and when the peer's
instance or boot id changes.  Shared APNs 4xx rejections go
out as 422 so Cloudflare passes the reason through. SQLite
now runs synchronous=FULL.

Off unless PUSH_PEER_URL and PUSH_PEER_SECRET are set. See
"Redundancy (two instances)" in
Companion/PushRelay/ops/DEPLOY.md.

2026-10-16: Make the live refusal scenario opt-in and soften
its prompt

The refusal scenario sent a bank phishing request to every
model on every live sweep, and OpenAI flagged the account
for suspected fraud.

It now runs only when ITERM2_AI_LIVE_REFUSAL is set.
"missing" sends the prompt only to models that lack a
committed fixture; "all" refreshes every model. Cassette
replay still runs it offline without the opt-in.  This
replaces ITERM2_AI_LIVE_REFRESH_REFUSAL_FIXTURES. The prompt
is now a low-severity request with no fraud or cyber
framing.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-16: Keep launch-time prompts out of test hosts

A modal window at launch blocks button and menu actions in
the tests that follow, failing dozens of unrelated tests.
Two could appear:

- The crash reporter, whenever iTerm2 had crashed since it
last reported.
  Test hosts now skip it.
- Sparkle's prompt asking whether to check for updates
automatically.
  Sparkle takes its suite from the -suite argument, so it
  uses the shared base suite rather than the test host's
  copy, and prompts when that lacks its settings. Test hosts
  now give Sparkle a delegate that never prompts or checks.

2026-10-16: Report archive failures and fix more values that
broke saved files

Session archives and window arrangements saved to a file are
property lists. Follow-ups to the hyperlink fix for issue
13143:

- propertyListData returned the empty contents of a temp
file when
  serialization failed, so archiving wrote an empty archive
  and reported success. It now returns nil, and archiving
  shows its failure notification. The output on success is
  unchanged.
- Bidi info stored ranges as NSValue, which broke saving
lines with
  right-to-left text. Ranges are now [location, length]
  pairs. Older saves still load, and malformed or
  overflowing ranges are dropped instead of crashing.
- Automatic profile switching stored NSNull for missing
values. Missing
  values are now omitted, saved entries are type-checked on
  load, entries without an original profile are dropped, and
  popping the stack can no longer loop forever when an
  original profile is nil.

Issue 13143

2026-10-16: Isolate test hosts' settings and stop hung test
runs

Parallel test hosts shared one settings domain, Application
Support folder, and config folder. A setting one test
changed was visible to the other workers, only one worker
could open the chat database, and a worker that crashed or
was killed before its teardown left the setting changed for
every later run. A test host launched with -suite NAME now
uses its own suite, NAME.<pid>-<random>, seeded from NAME,
which is never written.  State left by test hosts that are
no longer running is removed at launch.

run_tests.expect now treats 300 seconds without output as a
hang. It saves each test host's process state and stacks to
tmp/test-stall-*.txt, prints which test each host was in,
and kills the run. The live harness scripts allow 30 minutes
because a single live test can wait minutes.
RUN_TESTS_STALL_SECONDS overrides the limit, and 0 turns it
off.

2026-10-16: Don't crash highlighting a composer that has no
delegate

iTermMinimalComposerViewController forwards the syntax
highlighter request to its own delegate and returns nil when
there isn't one, but ComposerTextViewDelegate declared the
result non-optional, so Swift crashed calling highlight on
it. This happened in the Cockpit composer tests and could
happen when a rate-limited highlight fires after a
composer's delegate is gone. Make the result optional and
skip highlighting when it's nil.

release_stable_noninteractive.sh is the unattended
counterpart of release_stable.sh, modeled on
release_beta_noninteractive.sh. It reads secrets from the
keychain, takes release notes from docs/notes-<version>.txt,
and runs all cheap checks before building.

Stable-specific behavior: - Releases from master or a
release branch, as long as HEAD matches
  origin.
- Writes full_changes.txt, the stable changelog, .htaccess,
and both
  final appcasts.
- Publishes the shell integration commit this release ships
to the
  website, after checking that the submodule matches HEAD
  and is pushed.
- Fails after the build if it modified tracked files, which
means the
  string catalog and translations are out of date.

2026-10-16: PushRelay: recover from dead APNs connections;

A long-idle HTTP/2 session to Apple could die silently (no
TCP keepalive), after which every push timed out until a
restart, and nothing was logged because the worker turns the
throw into a 500.

replace sessions
  idle > 5 min; open at most one new connection per second
  per APNs host so an outage can't turn every push into a
  connect attempt; telemetry hook.

metrics: push_failed, apns_rejected{reason}, APNs latency
histogram, connection
  health counters, session gauges, last-delivery timestamp.

2026-10-16: Export and import Password Manager passwords.
Issue 7471.

Passwords saved with the Keychain password manager can be
exported to a passphrase-encrypted .iterm2passwords file and
imported on another Mac, from the password manager's menu,
by opening the file, or as an optional part of Export/Import
All Settings and Data.

- SignedArchive gains passphrase encryption: AES-256-GCM
with a PBKDF2
  key (NFC-normalized passphrase, bounded iteration count),
  the header and metadata bound as AAD. The extract tool can
  decrypt.
- Import asks about each conflict, with Always/Never
Replace, skips
  identical passwords, and reports partial progress on
  failure.
- Export and import always authenticate first.  - SSKeychain
reports an error for an empty or non-UTF-8 password instead
  of returning nil without one.
- The Makefile builds non-Development configurations into
their own
  intermediates folders so switching doesn't leave a stale
  PCH, and adds run-deployment.

2026-10-16: Fix saving arrangements and archives that
contain hyperlinks

Window arrangements saved to a file with contents and
session archives are written as property lists, which only
allow string dictionary keys.  iTermExternalAttributeIndex
encoded hyperlinks, underline colors, and similar per-cell
attributes in a dictionary keyed by NSNumber, so
serialization failed. The arrangement file was silently
never written and the session archive was empty.

Encode the index with string keys and accept both string and
legacy NSNumber keys when decoding, so existing restorable
state still loads.  Show an error when writing an
arrangement file fails instead of discarding it.

Issue 13143

2026-10-16: Strip HTML tags from the profile name in window
titles

With Support basic HTML tags in tab titles on, the window
title strips tags from every title source except the profile
name, so a profile named <b>Work</b> with the Profile Name
or Profile & Session Name component showed the literal tags
in the native window title.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-16: Render HTML title tags in pane title bars

With Support basic HTML tags in tab titles on, tabs render
<b>, <i>, and <u>, but the per-pane title bar set its label
from the plain string, so the tags showed up literally. It
now runs the title through the same parser the tab bar uses
before applying PUA fonts.

The native window title is plain text, so a window title
shows the text without the tags. That already held for
titles from the session name, but a window name set by a
program (OSC 0/2) or by tmux was returned verbatim, tags
included. Every window-title source is now stripped.

2026-10-15: Let KeePassXC remember browser basic-auth
passwords

The browser's basic-auth store names each remembered
credential after its protection space, such as
http://localhost:8022 (Realm). The KeePassXC adapter uses
the account name as the keepassxc-cli entry path, and
keepassxc-cli treats every / as a group separator with no
way to escape it, so add-account always failed and Remember
this password showed Could not save the password. Any
account name containing / failed the same way, including a
rename in the password manager window, which deleted the
entry before the re-add failed.

The KeePassXC data source now stores / in titles as U+2215
DIVISION SLASH, which looks the same in KeePassXC, and maps
it back when listing so names still match. Entries are still
addressed by the adapter's own entry path. Other adapters
receive account names unchanged. The fix is on the iTerm2
side so the signed adapter binary does not need to be
rebuilt.

QA finding F-342.

2026-10-15: Keep text view contents out of the undo-safety
debug log

With debug logging on, the undo-safety scrub that runs when
an undo-enabled text view leaves its window logged the
NSTextStorage with %@. Its description is the full text, and
the text view is often the field editor of a secure text
field, so passwords typed into the password manager or a
KeePassXC prompt were written verbatim to the debug log.

The log line is now built by iTermUndoSafetyScrubLogMessage,
which describes the text storage by class, address and
length and the text view by class and address, never by
contents.

QA finding F-341.

2026-10-15: Don't offer a deleted directory from the status
bar proxy icon

The Current Directory status bar component's folder icon
could still be dragged after its directory was deleted. The
container computed the icon's URL, including the existence
check, only when it updated the component, and the icon kept
that URL. Deleting the directory from elsewhere changed
nothing the container watched, so a drag still vended a URL
to a folder that no longer existed.

The icon now asks for its URL through a provider when a drag
begins, and the component checks then that the path is a
local directory that exists. The check moved into a small
helper so it can be tested, and the stat on every status bar
update is gone.

QA finding F-214.

2026-10-15: Open the status bar component menu when its
proxy icon is clicked

Since the Current Directory status bar component's icon
became a draggable proxy icon, a plain click on it did
nothing. The container's click gesture recognizer delays
mouse events until it fails. On a click it recognized, so
the icon never got the mouse-down and mouse-up it uses to
tell a click from a drag, and clickRecognized: deliberately
ignored clicks on the icon to avoid opening the menu twice.
Drags still worked because the recognizer fails on movement
and then delivers the delayed events. Hostname and Username
share the same proxy icon class and had the same problem.

Make the container the recognizer's delegate and decline
every event of a gesture whose mouse-down lands on the proxy
icon. The decision is latched at mouse-down so one press and
release is never split between the icon and the recognizer.
The icon then handles the click itself, opening the same
menu as clicking the text, and dragging still works.

QA findings F-213 and F-105.

2026-10-15: Disable session-only composer actions in the
Cockpit

The Cockpit's docked composer has no terminal session behind
it, but Edit > Paste Special > Advanced Paste (Opt-Cmd-V)
and Session > Open Command History (Shift-Cmd-;) stayed
enabled there: Advanced Paste did nothing and command
history opened an empty popup outside the composer.  The
composer's help popover also listed them, along with AI
lookup (Cmd-Y), which the Cockpit already disables.

The composer now has a sessionless mode, which the Cockpit
turns on. It disables those two menu items and leaves them
and AI lookup out of the help popover.

QA finding F-377.

2026-10-15: Keep chips' attachment characters out of Cockpit
commands

An @-mention chip in the Cockpit composer is an attachment,
which is the character U+FFFC in plain text. Copying text
with a chip put U+FFFC on the pasteboard, and since the
composer pastes plain text, pasting it back dropped the chip
but kept the invisible character. Shift-Return then sent it
to the shell as part of the command ("command not found"),
because only a selection send stripped U+FFFC, not a
whole-field send.

A send now never includes U+FFFC. With plain-text paste
forced (the Cockpit), copying removes it from the copied
text, including a copy with several cursors, and pasting
text that contains it inserts the text without it as its own
undo step.

QA finding F-376.

2026-10-15: Put @-mention chip edges on whole points from
the baseline

After 87c573476 the chip image's top and bottom sat a
fraction of a point from the text baseline (for example 11.6
above and 2.53 below for the 12 point monospaced system
font). Plain text's baseline lands on the pixel grid, and
the chip's fractional edges were rounded to it when drawn,
so on a 2x display the name inside the chip sat one device
pixel (0.5 point) below the adjacent typed text.

The chip's ascent and descent are now rounded outward to
whole points when the line box has room for it, which keeps
the name on the text's baseline at 1x and 2x. When the line
box isn't a whole number of points and rounding outward
would make the line taller, the exact edge is kept so
nothing is clipped.

QA finding F-330.

2026-10-15: Draw the Cockpit composer's placeholder once

ComposerTextView named its placeholder property
placeholderString, which overrides NSTextView's own
(private) placeholder property. AppKit then drew a wrapped
copy of the placeholder whenever the field was empty, on top
of the composer's own single line. When the text fit on one
line the two copies coincided, so it showed up only for a
long placeholder in a narrow Cockpit, such as the Brazilian
Portuguese one at 380 points.

The property is now composerPlaceholderString, and the
composer's own placeholder ends in an ellipsis when it
doesn't fit instead of being cut off at the edge.

QA finding F-263.

2026-10-15: Keep the floating Cockpit panel from covering
sheets

The Cockpit panel floats above iTerm2's windows. iTerm2's
warnings for a terminal window, such as the Broadcast Input
confirmation, are sheets on that window, at its normal
level, so the Cockpit covered them and hid their buttons
until the user moved the panel.

While another window shows a sheet, the panel now drops to
the normal level just behind that window, and it floats
again once no window has a sheet up (including when a window
closes with its sheet still showing).  App-modal alerts
already sit above floating panels.

QA finding F-249.

2026-10-15: Skip closed targets when sending from the
Cockpit

When a session chipped in the Cockpit composer had its tab
closed, Shift-Return refused the whole send with "Unknown
target @ptys_..." (the session's internal id), so the
targets that still existed got nothing.

A chip whose session, tab, or window no longer exists is now
skipped: the command goes to the targets that remain, and
the status line names the skipped ones by their chip names.
If every target is gone, nothing is sent and the status
names them.

QA finding F-242.

2026-10-15: Close the Cockpit's session picker when the
caret leaves the @-run

The Cockpit composer drove its @-mention picker from the
first word-start @ anywhere in the text, whether or not the
caret was in it. After typing "email me @ noon" the bare @
still counted, so the picker stayed open on every keystroke
and Return committed a chip in place of the @ instead of
inserting a newline. Esc hid the picker only until the next
keystroke.

Only an @-run that contains the caret now drives the picker.
Esc remembers the run it dismissed and keeps the picker
closed until the caret leaves that run, and a key routed to
an open picker first checks that the caret is still in the
run (a click doesn't change the text).

QA finding F-240.

2026-10-15: Keep the stoplight hotbox off a browser's
navigation bar

In a Compact or Minimal window with no tab bar at the top,
pointing at the top-left corner reveals the window buttons
unless a clickable decoration lies beneath them, in which
case only a chevron toggle appears. The check knew about the
session toolbar, title bar and status bar but not a browser
session's navigation bar, so the window buttons covered the
back, forward and reload buttons and swallowed clicks meant
for them.

The browser view controller now reports its visible toolbar
as a clickable decoration, and SessionView includes it in
the check.

QA finding F-339.

2026-10-15: Update a browser tab's minimal theme tint when
the page's color arrives

In the minimal theme a browser tab's tint lagged one page
load behind: it showed the previous page's background color.
The page color was read only once, in didFinish, but WebKit
updates underPageBackgroundColor a moment after the load
finishes, so didFinish still saw the old page's color and
the new one was never delivered until the next load.

Observe underPageBackgroundColor with KVO and forward
changes once the load has settled and the web view is no
longer loading, so WebKit's transient values during a load
still do not flip the tint. Repeated colors are not
forwarded, so a stale value at didFinish does not use up the
session's rate limit and delay the real color.

QA finding F-039.

2026-10-15: Keep long SSH integration framer commands from
being cut in two

Conductor encoded a framer command by base64-encoding each
argument, joining them with newlines, and then splitting the
whole string into 128-character lines that end in a
backslash continuation. When the newline between two
arguments fell exactly on a 128-character boundary, the next
line began with that newline, so an empty line went over the
wire. framer.py treats an empty line as the end of a
command, so it ran the first half with a mangled last
argument and then the rest as a separate command. Commands
with several long arguments, such as login, save, and file
suggestions, could hit it.

Split each argument into continuation lines on its own
instead, so the only empty line is the one that ends the
command. framer.py rejoins continuation lines regardless of
where they were split, so existing remotes decode the new
output unchanged. The pre-framer conductor.sh encoding is
unchanged.

Found while adding the F-061 regression test. Tangential
finding T-74.

2026-10-15: Stop Engage AI from crashing in the Cockpit
composer

Edit > Engage Artificial Intelligence (Cmd-Y) in the
Cockpit's docked composer crashed iTerm2. The Cockpit
composer belongs to no session, so its variable scope is
nil. The composer hides its AI controls there, but the menu
item and key equivalent still reached
performNaturalLanguageQuery:, which handed the nil scope to
AITermControllerObjC and on to AIPromptTemplateEvaluator,
whose force-cast of the scope's copy trapped.

AIPromptTemplateEvaluator now takes an optional scope and
falls back to an empty one, and the composer's scope and
AITermControllerObjC's scope parameter are declared
nullable. The large composer now disables Engage AI where
its AI controls are force-hidden (only the Cockpit), and the
action does nothing there. Ordinary composers keep the item
enabled so the generative AI lockout explanation (F-115)
still appears.

QA finding F-370 (resolves T-45).

2026-10-15: Write the nushell shell integration script in
order and verify it

The Internet-Free installer for nushell launched its helper
sh and then typed the next command as soon as any output
appeared, which the echo of the launch line itself provided.
nushell's reedline reads typeahead in bulk, so commands
meant for sh were consumed by nushell, a nested helper sh
started late, mkdir landed inside the heredoc, and the
autoload directory was never created. The step still
reported that it wrote the script because it only waited for
a prompt.

For nushell, wait for the helper sh's own prompt before
typing anything else (this also covers the utilities step).
The script step now runs in the first helper sh with no
nested one: it sets and creates the autoload directory and
prints a sentinel, opens the heredoc only after that, and
sends the script, terminator and a check in one piece. The
check prints whether cat succeeded and the file is
non-empty, and the step advances only when the file was
written. Otherwise it shows an error and lets the user try
again.

Adds nushell tests with a fake terminal that loses typeahead
to nushell the way reedline does, and updates the discovery
command the existing installer tests expect.

QA finding F-363.

2026-10-15: Don't ask to copy settings at quit just because
a migration ran

With settings loaded from a custom folder and manual saving,
quitting asks to copy settings to the folder whenever local
prefs differ from the folder's plist. One-time launch
migrations of synced keys (AlternateScreenBidi=NO becoming
DisableBidiInAlternateScreen=YES, and a saved session status
priority list gaining done and error) changed local prefs
while the folder kept the old values, so every quit
prompted. Choosing Lose Changes did not help because the
migration ran again on the reloaded folder prefs.

Migrations of synced keys now run through
+[iTermUserDefaults migrateKeys:inUserDefaults:block:],
which reports the values they changed.
iTermRemotePreferences applies those changes to its copy of
the folder's prefs (only for keys whose folder value is the
one the migration started from) and, in manual-save mode,
compares local prefs against that. The folder is not
rewritten for a migration alone; the migration just runs
again next launch. Automatic saving keeps its old behavior
of writing the migration to the folder once.

QA findings F-216 and F-069.

2026-10-15: Truncate the restorable state WAL after
housekeeping and at quit

The saved-state database runs in WAL mode. Launch
housekeeping ran a passive checkpoint and then VACUUM, which
writes a full copy of the database into the WAL. Nothing
ever truncated it, and the connection is never closed at
quit, so restorable-state.sqlite-wal stayed one to two times
the database size and was left on disk after a clean quit.
It grows with scrollback.

Housekeeping now runs wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE) after VACUUM.
The synchronous save at quit also queues a TRUNCATE
checkpoint on the database thread. It runs asynchronously so
the main thread is not blocked; if the app exits first, the
abandoned checkpoint is harmless and the next launch's
housekeeping truncates the WAL.

QA finding F-221.

2026-10-15: Add regression tests for focus in iTermWarning
text prompts

QA finding F-018 suspected that text-field and password
prompts built with iTermWarning lose keyboard focus because
they rely only on initialFirstResponder. Runtime QA (F-030,
F-031, F-034) did not reproduce it: typing without clicking
landed in the field for app-modal alerts and sheets alike,
including the scp password prompt. The code agrees: every
path that shows a warning builds the alert with makeAlert,
which hands the field to the alert window's
initialFirstResponder, and AppKit focuses it when the alert
is shown. No code change is needed.

These tests pin that mechanism for a plain text field, a
secure field (as in the scp prompt), and a field chosen from
a container of several. They fail if makeAlert stops setting
the window's initial first responder.

QA finding F-018.

2026-10-15: Add a regression test that keystrokes typed
during a paste stay after it over SSH integration

QA saw a stray "~" command run after pasting into an it2ssh
session and pressing Return, and suspected the Return was
being inserted inside the paste's bracketed-paste
terminator. iTerm2 does not reorder this input.  PTYTextView
holds key-downs while the paste helper is pasting and
replays them after the last chunk is written; in an SSH
integration session both paste chunks and keystrokes go
through Conductor.sendKeys, whose queue is first in, first
out and whose pipelining only appends; and framer.py runs
one command at a time, writing each payload to the pty
before reading the next.

The symptom comes from zsh instead. Its bracketed-paste
reader gives up when the next byte takes longer than
KEYTIMEOUT (0.4 s by default) to arrive. If the final "~" of
the closing ESC[201~ arrives late, zsh ends the paste early,
inserts the "~" as a typed character, and Return runs it as
a second command. A local pty experiment reproduces exactly
this with a 0.6 s gap before the "~" and nothing else. On a
loaded remote, the shell's pty can fill while zsh reads,
which delays the last bytes.

The tests decode what the conductor writes the way framer.py
does and check that the remote receives the whole paste and
then Return: for every order in which the remote's replies
can arrive relative to the writes (both with and without
command output, which moves the conductor into its executing
state), for keystrokes queued behind a command that is still
in flight, and end to end from the paste helper with Return
pressed mid-paste. There is no timing dependence: timers are
fired by hand and replies are delivered explicitly.

QA finding F-061.

2026-10-15: Keep the space between LTR and RTL text when
copying part of a bidi line

With right-to-left support on, a character drag is
decomposed into one logical subselection per contiguous
logical run. Dragging over the start of L4    end4 and part
of the Hebrew yields the runs L4 and   . Copy extracted each
run separately with trim whitespace on copy on, so the space
after L4 was trimmed and the words fused: L4   instead of L4
.

Mark a decomposed run that is followed by another run on the
same line (precedesSameLineBidiPiece, kept across copies and
resizes). The selection extractor tells the text extractor
to keep the trailing whitespace at the very end of such a
range while still trimming before each hard newline inside
it.

QA finding F-321.

2026-10-15: Never send a Llama (Local) chat to the cloud
default vendor

Picking Llama (Local) in an AI chat while no Ollama model
was discovered pinned the chat to the discovery placeholder
model. At send time LLMMetadata.model(named:) did not
recognize the placeholder, so AIConversation used no
provider override and the turn went to the global default
vendor, e.g. OpenAI: it opened that vendor's key sheet or
sent the message to api.openai.com while the picker still
showed Llama (Local).

The placeholder now resolves to the Ollama vendor's
recommended local model once one is discovered. Until then,
and for any unresolved Llama-family pin, the turn fails
before any request or key sheet with an error asking the
user to start Ollama. Chat renaming and icon generation,
which send the chat's content and title, now run on the
turn's model instead of the global default.

OllamaModelCache gains a test hook so the new tests never
reach a real Ollama server or have a fetch overwrite their
cache state.

QA finding F-283.

2026-10-15: Don't act on a browser prompt answered after the
script timed out

load_url and browser_eval_js ask the user before acting, and
the prompt never times out, but the script's call does: the
Python API used the default 30 second invoke timeout. A user
who took longer to answer got an RPCException: Timeout in
the script while the prompt stayed up, and clicking OK
afterward still loaded the URL or ran the JavaScript, after
the script was told the call failed. A script that retried
would act twice.

The server-side timeout in iTermScriptFunctionCall dropped
the method's late completion, but the method never learned
it had been abandoned.  Each call with a timeout now carries
an iTermScriptCallCancellation that is cancelled when the
timer fires. iTermBuiltInMethod injects it into an argument
named cancellation, the way it injects connectionKey,
without adding it to the public signature. load_url and
browser_eval_js check it when the prompt is answered (and
browser_eval_js before showing it) and do nothing if the
script stopped waiting.

Session.async_eval_javascript and Session.async_load_url
take an optional timeout that defaults to ten minutes, long
enough to read the prompt.

QA finding F-360.

2026-10-15: Validate and clamp OSC 1337 SetWindowFrame

SetWindowFrame=x;y;width;height parsed each field with
doubleValue and passed the result straight to
-setFrame:display:. Non-numeric fields became 0, and nan,
zero, negative or huge values made the window 0x0 (so it
vanished) or put it tens of thousands of points off screen.
Any output printed to the terminal could do this when
session-initiated window resizing is allowed.

Parse strictly: the payload must have exactly four fields,
each a finite number, or the sequence is ignored. When
applying the frame, clamp its size between the window's
minimum size (the largest tab minimum plus window
decorations, as live resize does) and the union of the
screens' visible frames, and move it onto the nearest screen
unless its top edge and at least 40 points of it are already
on one.

QA finding F-059.

2026-10-15: Return focus to the terminal when a focused
session note is hidden

When a script cleared or hid the session note through the
Python API while the user was typing in it, the note view
was removed from the window and AppKit left the window
itself as first responder. Every keystroke after that was
dropped until the user clicked the terminal.  Toggling the
note closed with Edit Session Note while it had focus did
the same.

-[SessionView hideSessionNote] now checks whether keyboard
focus is inside the note before removing it and, if so,
gives focus back to the session, the same way collapsing a
focused note already does. Hiding an emptied note when it
loses focus is unchanged, since focus is already moving to
wherever the user put it.

QA finding F-308.

2026-10-15: Never put a native session into a tmux tab as a
split pane

With a split new-instance open style (NewInstanceOpenStyle 2
or 3) and a tmux integration tab current, opening a file
such as a .command added a native split to the tmux tab. The
next %layout-change terminated it, so the open was silently
lost (before 35a475674 it took over tmux pane %0 instead).
Open URL in a split pane in a tmux tab made a tmux shell
pane and dropped the URL.

PseudoTerminal now has canInstallNativeSplitPane, which
refuses tmux tabs. When it refuses, the session launcher
resolves split styles to a new tab (and reports the tab
object type), and Open URL falls back to its existing new
tab path via canOpenURLInSplitPaneOfTerminal:, the same
fallback floating panes use.

QA finding F-122 (and F-124).

2026-10-14: Add a regression test for tmux detach releasing
Prevent Sleep

Detaching from tmux while the windows stay open marks each
tmux pane's session exited ("tmux detached"). QA saw those
panes still counted as Prevent Sleep requesters, keeping the
idle sleep assertion held. That build predated 81dafd414,
which made PTYSession post PTYSessionExitedDidChange
whenever its exited flag flips; tmuxDidDisconnect goes
through setExited:YES, so the coordinator already recomputes
and drops the detached panes on master. A reattach builds
new sessions (closeAllPanes forgets the old panes), which
PTYSessionCreated covers.

Add a test that calls the real tmux disconnect handler on
two real sessions in a real window with Prevent Sleep on and
windows kept open after detach, with the coordinator using
the real session enumeration, and checks the count drops 2,
1, 0 and the assertion is released. It fails with the count
stuck at 2 if the exited-change post is removed.
liveRequesterGUIDs becomes internal so the test can use it.

QA finding F-212.

(cherry picked from commit
a578625277b8e3b76108c79e42f86664be246bb7) (cherry picked
from commit 246fca93c8c3d6f07bacf62955d429688bb90521)

2026-10-14: Free each AI request's URLSession, delegate and
JSContext

Every AI request leaked its URLSession, the session's
delegate, its JSContext (about 150 KB each), and anything
the request's closures captured, such as its Cancellation,
so memory grew about 1 MB per AI chat message. Each
request's URLSession was never invalidated, so it kept its
delegate (and through the JS callback, the JSContext) alive
forever. The blocks registered into the JSContext also
captured the context strongly, a retain cycle that kept it
alive on its own. And the request's Cancellation, which
callers keep, held the per-call PluginClient.

The delegate now invalidates its session when the task
completes, which covers success, failure, and cancellation.
The registered blocks and the promise handlers capture the
context weakly (the call keeps it alive until it finishes),
and the Cancellation holds the client weakly.

Adds a test seam that reports the objects each call creates,
and tests checking that after a completed streaming request,
a cancelled request, and a call cancelled before its
transfer starts, the delegate, client, JSContext and
captured closures are all freed.

2026-10-14: Keep text editing shortcuts for an alert's text
field

iTermWarning gives each button without a key equivalent an
automatic Cmd-<letter> shortcut from its label. Those
shortcuts won over the field editor, so in the
paste-with-tabs alert Cmd-A opened Advanced and Cmd-C
pressed Convert tabs to spaces (which pastes) instead of
selecting or copying in the tab-size field.

When the accessory holds selectable or editable text,
automatic shortcuts now skip A, C, V, X, and Z. Shortcuts
the caller assigned are kept.

The set of letters already used was never updated because
the result of setByAddingObject: was discarded, so two
buttons could get the same shortcut, or repeat one the
caller assigned. It is now a mutable set, and actions that
already have a key equivalent keep it.

2026-10-14: Keep a manual model's economy requests on the
manual model

AIMetadata.economyModel(for:in:) fell back to the economy
model of the catalog entry sharing the configured model's
name. Manual models never carry an economy pointer, so that
fallback applied exactly to them: a manual "gpt-5.4-mini" at
a gateway with no designated economy model sent every
command safety check and screen-watch poll to the gateway as
"gpt-6-luna". A gateway that serves only the configured
model rejects those, the safety check fails closed, and
every AI-proposed command is flagged as potentially
dangerous.

Only a model's own pointer now selects an economy model, and
only catalog models have one. A manual model without a
designated economy model gets none, so the safety check and
poller use the manual model itself, with its name, URL,
headers and credential. Catalog and recommended models are
unchanged.

2026-10-14: Stop localizing the Get Info version string

sources/InfoPlist.xcstrings carried CFBundleGetInfoString as

that the build stamps into the base Info.plist, so
non-English users saw 3.7.dev in Finder Get Info on release
and beta builds.

(which alone does not stop existing translations from being
compiled), so the OS falls back to the base plist value. Add
a hygiene test that keeps version keys out of the localized
overlays, and correct the Info.plist localization doc.

2026-10-14: Authenticate every open of the standalone
password manager window

The standalone password manager window, used when no
terminal window exists, was created once and reused. Its
open gate was made only when it was created, so in Require
Authentication on Every Open mode a second open showed the
accounts with no prompt, and in the screen-lock modes
reopening after a lock showed an empty window with no
prompt. Each open now goes through the gate again, as a new
window would: a closed window is reopened by replacing its
gate and authenticating, and when a prompt is needed the
rows, search text and two-factor field from the earlier open
are cleared first. A prompt result from an earlier open is
ignored.

Also, a failed or cancelled prompt from any caller, or a
screen lock, revokes the provider's authentication and locks
every window, but windows kept showing the account names and
user names they had loaded.  The provider now posts a
notification when its authentication is revoked. Each
unlocked window's gate then ends its open, so it stays
locked even if another caller authenticates later, and the
window clears its rows, ends its sheets and closes, as a
screen lock already did. A fetch that finishes after a lock
shows no rows.

tools/sanitize_iterm2_plist.py left the user-chosen Ollama

the password-manager adapter path (PathToExecutable_<id>,
which contains the user name) in sanitized output. Add the

to PREFIX_CONTENT, with matching audit lines.

triggers (iTermSetProfileBooleanTrigger), which is only a
profile key plus 0/1, leaving the trigger as junk. Keep that
parameter when it has exactly the key\x01<0|1> shape; other
trigger parameters are still redacted.

2026-10-14: Hide password manager accounts until an
every-open prompt succeeds

In Require Authentication on Every Open mode, once the
password manager had been unlocked earlier in the launch,
reopening it showed the account list (account names and user
names) behind the authentication prompt. The provider stays
authenticated for the whole launch, and the window decided
whether to load and show accounts from that flag alone, so
it filled the list while its own prompt was still up.

Each window now owns a gate that counts the provider's
authentication only when the mode lets an earlier unlock be
reused, or once this window's own authentication succeeds.
Every check in the window that used the provider's flag now
asks the gate, so the list stays empty until the prompt
succeeds, and a failed or cancelled prompt closes the window
as before. Other modes behave as before.

2026-10-14: Cancel Companion wizard setup when the wizard is
closed

Closing the Companion setup wizard with Cmd-W while it was
downloading did not stop setup. The Close menu item sends
closeCurrentSession:, which the wizard did not handle, so
the action went to the terminal window behind it (closing a
session there) and the wizard kept going: it installed the
plugin, raised the administrator password prompt, and on
Don't Allow showed Setup Could Not Be Completed.

The wizard now handles closeCurrentSession: by closing its
window, which cancels the install. The install steps move
into CompanionWizardSetupRunner, which checks for
cancellation before every step with side effects, treats an
error after cancellation as a cancellation rather than a
failure, and silently rolls back any consent or API key it
wrote. Cancellation no longer depends on the window being
visible, so a panel hidden while the app is inactive does
not abort setup. The plugin installer stops before
installing into Plugins if cancelled during the unzip.

QA finding F-036.

(cherry picked from commit

from commit 190c9f2fde4f190222f032d864d25685c662e463)

2026-10-14: Keep tmux floats in place on attach by pinning
window sizes first

On attach, iTerm2 sent refresh-client -C with the profile
size before listing windows, so new windows would get that
size. With per-window sizes, a control client's size also
applies to every window it has not sized itself, so tmux
immediately shrank each existing window to the profile size
(typically 80x25). tmux clamps floating panes into a
shrunken window, and they stay there when the window grows
back: a float at x=68 in a 157-column window came back at
x=37, and tmux's own layout was rewritten.

On tmux 3.8 and later (where floating panes exist), the
client size now waits for the initial list-windows. Then
iTerm2 sets the client size to the largest window size
(which can only grow a window), pins every window at its own
current size, and finally sets the profile size, which then
only applies to windows created later. Older tmux keeps the
previous attach order.

QA: please also verify that attaching with tmux older than
3.8 (for example 3.3, 3.4 and 3.7) behaves as before with
regard to window and session size: windows should come back
at the size tmux had for them (or the profile size, as in
3.7.4), not be shrunk, grown or resized unexpectedly, and
tiled pane layouts should be unchanged after attach.

QA finding F-049.

(cherry picked from commit
e93ca9c9f05c38d2a08bc9deadfeb63722138270) (cherry picked
from commit c352aadfc76fb96f63458a5569e18c45a098098a)

2026-10-14: Keep @-mention chips on the text baseline

A session @-mention chip in the Cockpit composer and the AI
chat input is an image attachment. Its image was sized to
the laid-out line height of its contents (rounded, with
leading) and positioned from the font's exact ascender.
TextKit 1 puts a plain line's baseline at a rounded baseline
offset, so the chip reached above or below the box plain
text gets: a line with a chip was taller, the text after the
chip sat lower than on a line without one, and the name
inside the chip was drawn off the adjacent text's baseline.

The chip image now spans the font's own TextKit line box
(without leading, clamped to the ascender and descender) and
draws the name at an explicit baseline. When the name's
glyphs reach further (an emoji), the box grows only as far
as the line the name would get typed as plain text, so a
chip never changes the line more than its name would and the
name is not clipped inside that line.

QA finding F-032.

(cherry picked from commit
87c573476f3b269146363861a2626c3e4ac649db) (cherry picked
from commit 3240bb3b84db8a2f507dd61d2d68f4b6e62ebe46)

2026-10-14: Recover from an SSH Integration connection that
dies abruptly

When an it2ssh connection ended without the framer's clean
exit (the remote sshd killed, or the local ssh client
killed), the session wedged: the local shell was back in the
foreground, but nothing it printed was drawn and keystrokes
were queued forever.

On a clean exit conductor.sh prints an OSC 135 unhook, which
drops the DCS 2000p conductor hook, and then the local
it2ssh prints OSC 1337;EndSSH=<id>, which tears down the
conductor. On an abrupt end no unhook arrives, so the hook
stayed in place. It only recognized ST-terminated OSCs, so
the BEL-terminated EndSSH and all later output, including
the local prompt, were swallowed.

The conductor parser now remembers the it2ssh unique ID from
its init line and treats that it2ssh's EndSSH as an implicit
unhook. It leaves the EndSSH unconsumed so the regular
parser executes it, just as after a clean exit. An EndSSH
relayed inside %output is honored only for this connection's
ID, so a nested it2ssh's EndSSH is not mistaken for it.  The
OSC parser also now backs up to the ESC when an OSC is
interrupted by another one, instead of losing the ESC, so an
EndSSH that follows a framer OSC cut off by the dying
connection is still recognized.

2026-10-14: Restore an archived floating pane as a float on
Undo Close

With Archive On Closure on, Undo Close of a floating pane
whose undo window had run out restored nothing. The session
was gone, so recreateTab: had no live session to put back as
a float. It stripped the float records from the tab
arrangement and rebuilt the tiled layout, which succeeds
without the float, so the archive was silently dropped.

Now recreateTab: finds archives whose session has a float
record in the tab arrangement and restores each one as a
float from that record (its frame, container size and grid),
using a per-record helper extracted from
restoreFloatingPanesFromArrangement:. An archive restored
this way does not also get a tab of its own. If the float
can't be restored, or its tab is gone, the archive gets its
own tab as a tiled pane's does.

2026-10-14: Type a space instead of .  for double-space
period substitution

With macOSs Add period with double-space on, pressing space
after a space makes the text input system call insertText:.
with a replacement range covering the space before the
cursor. The keyboard handler backspaced over that space and
sent . , and in vim (where it doesn't backspace) the period
repeated the last command.

The fix for issue 13123 sets
NSAutomaticPeriodSubstitutionEnabled to NO in iTerm2's user
defaults, but NSSpellChecker reads the standard user
defaults. When iTerm2 runs with a custom defaults suite
(-suite), the override never reaches AppKit and the global
setting wins, which is how QA saw it after using the
Composer.

The keyboard handler now recognizes the substitution (a .
insertion replacing one character during a space keypress,
with no marked text) and inserts the space the user typed.
Dictation, Voice Control, and input methods committing
marked text are unaffected.

(cherry picked from commit

2026-10-14: Don't show the locale prompt as a sheet on an
unseen hotkey window

When no valid UNIX locale matches the user's language and
region, the first session of a dedicated hotkey window asks
the user to pick one.  That session is launched while the
hotkey window is ordered in but still has an alpha of 0 (it
only rolls in after its session exists). The prompt attached
itself as a sheet whenever the window was visible, so it
became a sheet on a fully transparent window: the user saw
nothing, key focus was trapped in the hidden sheet, and the
hotkey window could not appear until the sheet was answered
blind.

Only use a sheet when the window is visible, has nonzero
alpha, and is not miniaturized. Otherwise activate iTerm2
and show an app-modal alert.  The same rule applies to the
wrong-encoding warning shown for a custom non-UTF-8 locale.
The session still launches with the locale the user picks,
as before.

QA finding F-306.

(cherry picked from commit
4514cefcbda025da1ce107a17d3759ba511f2b74) (cherry picked
from commit 4ab632807c49f3117d39bc02f1e898279a91812e)

2026-10-14: Coalesce title changes so a title flood cannot
swamp the main thread

Each OSC 0, 1 and 2 added its own side effect. A window
title in a session with no remote host also asked the main
thread for the working directory, which sent one XPC request
to the pidinfo service per title, armed a timeout timer on
the main queue, and on completion went back to the mutation
queue to log the directory, which queued another side effect
that saved the recent directories database and updated the
path variable. A program setting the title as fast as it
could write queued hundreds of thousands of these. The main
thread fell minutes behind and memory grew to gigabytes,
held by the outstanding requests, their timers and closures,
and the side effects they queued, until the backlog drained.

Title changes with no other side effect between them now
share one batch and one side effect, using the token
executor's side-effect serial as the OSC 7501 batches do, so
they keep their order relative to bells, reports and other
side effects. The batch keeps only the latest window title,
icon title and subtitle, whether to poll the local
directory, and the first and last line at which to log the
working directory, which is fetched once per batch. A change
that needs the directory does not join a deferred batch, so
it is not delayed as it was not before.

2026-10-14: Show the basic-auth sheet instead of prompting
in every-open mode

In Require Authentication on Every Open mode, once the
browser password manager had been unlocked, every basic-auth
challenge for a remembered site raised the system open the
password manager prompt. The basic-auth store only
auto-fills when the password manager can be read without a
prompt, but its check treated any earlier unlock as
reusable. Every-open mode never reuses one, so the silent
lookup prompted instead.

The check now counts an earlier unlock only when the mode
allows it to be reused. In every-open mode a challenge shows
the plain basic-auth sheet, and the user can still choose
the password manager from it, which authenticates as usual.

2026-10-14: Revert the session.path ssh-exit heuristics
built on the F-103 fix

Reverts f72f61f2b, 4d2232a7a and 4b107878f. They tried to
let a polled local directory replace a remote session.path
after ssh exits, for a local shell that has no shell
integration while the remote one does. Each rule exposed
another edge case (subshells, tmux panes, sudo -s, screen, a
race recording the foreground process group), and a review
found the latest one could leave the path stuck on the
remote directory.

This keeps 01b50a4b1 (QA finding F-103), which restores the
original Objective-C rule: a polled local directory never
overwrites the path while a remote directory is current.
After leaving ssh into a local shell without shell
integration, session.path stays on the remote directory
until the next directory report, as in 3.6. QA findings
F-134, F-138, F-141, F-142 and F-144 are known limitations.

2026-10-14: Name the reattached tmux pane's workgroup in a
re-bound AI chat watch

When an orchestration chat's watch on a tmux -CC pane was
held across a relaunch and re-bound to the pane's recreated
session, only its session reference was updated. Its
workgroup ID still named the old session (session:<old
stableID>), while list_workgroups lists the pane under its
new stableID, so list_watches and the watch's status_update
named a workgroup that no longer exists and the model could
not match them.

The re-bind now also takes the workgroup ID, workgroup name,
role ID, and role name from the new session, using the same
context list_workgroups uses, before persisting the watcher
and re-arming it.

QA finding F-145.

(cherry picked from commit
222fbdced7227a14bd08887ebaf1956a49bf9de4) (cherry picked
from commit e63f326eac0d6d8bab34d1dec1b7c02aeaa8aa96)

2026-10-14: Cancel only the stopped AI request, not another
chat's

PluginClient was a singleton that remembered only the most
recently started HTTP task, and every AI request's
Cancellation cancelled through it. With two chats replying
at once, pressing Stop in the chat whose request started
first cancelled the other chat's request (it ended with
"HTTP request failed with cancelled") while the stopped
chat's transfer kept running to completion, still using
tokens. Cancelling a request that had already finished could
also kill another chat's request.

Each plugin call now gets its own PluginClient, and
Plugin.load ties the request's Cancellation to that client,
so cancel stops exactly that request's transfer. The client
also remembers that it was cancelled so a cancel that lands
before the HTTP task is created keeps it from starting.

Adds test seams (URLProtocol classes, a plugin built from
test code, and a plugin override on iTermAIClient) and tests
covering two concurrent requests, cancelling a finished
request, and cancelling before the transfer starts.

QA finding F-268.

(cherry picked from commit
58bd67892e9f09b265c63a9d25437e1070f5848f) (cherry picked
from commit 8e30bfd5d1a81d19024cc266d15efcde3da3ff7f)

2026-10-14: Keep the Claude Code hook when the config dir is
an alias of ~/.claude

Installing the Claude Code integration merges the cc-status
hooks into settings.json in the resolved config directory,
then strips them from the previously used directory if it
changed. The "changed" check compared standardized path
strings, so a CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR that names the same
directory as ~/.claude through a symlink (~/.claude ->
~/dotfiles/claude) or a case variant on a case-insensitive
volume (~/.Claude) looked different, and the strip removed
the hooks Install had just written.

Decide sameness by file identity (device and inode via stat)
for both the directories and their settings.json files,
falling back to comparing symlink-resolved paths,
case-insensitively only on a case-insensitive volume, when
either can't be stat-ed. The strip step is now
stripStaleCCStatusHooks(previousDirectory:newConfigDirectory:)
so it can be unit tested.

QA finding F-274 (also F-013, F-064).

2026-10-14: Keep AI chat watches on tmux panes across a quit
that restores windows

On an ordinary quit with window restoration on, an
orchestration chat's watch on a tmux -CC pane was still
dropped when the pane closed, because the
session-termination handler treats a tmux client session as
never restored. Since watches registered from an
orchestration chat now record the pane's tmux identity, and
the next launch holds such a watch until the pane reattaches
and re-binds it, there is no reason to drop it at quit.

When a tmux client session terminates for a quit after which
windows will be restored, the dispatcher now keeps that
session's orchestration-chat watches that carry a tmux
identity. If the pane does not reattach at the next launch,
the existing five-minute deadline drops it. Real pane
closes, Option-Quit, quits without restoration, watches with
no tmux identity, and session-bound chats keep the old
behavior.

2026-10-14: Tell the user when Stop or Disable Orchestration
cancels AI chat timers

Stop and Disable Orchestration cancel a chat's pending
register_timer timers and watches without telling the model,
so nothing told the user either, and they could keep waiting
for a timer that would never fire.

Both now publish one chat notice counting what was
cancelled, such as Stop cancelled 1 pending timer. or
Disabling orchestration cancelled 2 pending timers and 1
watch. The notice is client-local, so it starts no agent
turn and sends nothing to the model. Nothing is published
when nothing was pending. Disable Orchestration's drop,
clear, and notices move into
OrchestratorClient.disableOrchestration so the counts are
read from the persisted set just before it is cleared, with
or without a live dispatcher. The strings are pluralized and
translated into German, Japanese, Brazilian Portuguese,
Russian, and Simplified Chinese.

Follow-up to QA finding F-110.

(cherry picked from commit
a16ee361ca209b19a5266b18d7c991667dd3d50b) (cherry picked
from commit 471808c3086e24f2ca559093f45189b822d37af7)

2026-10-14: Keep AI chat watches on tmux panes across a
relaunch

After a kill -9 and relaunch, an orchestration chat's watch
on a tmux -CC pane was dropped at launch with "could not be
restored", which also started a paid agent turn. Watchers
persist only the session's stableID, but a tmux client
session is not restored by window restoration: the restored
gateway reattaches tmux after the restoration gate has
opened, and each pane comes back as a new session with a new
stableID.

Watchers now also record the pane's tmux identity (the tmux
session's @iterm2_id and the pane number) when registered
from an orchestration chat.  At reconcile, a tmux watch
whose session is missing is held instead of dropped, and is
re-bound to the pane's new session when tmux opens its
window. It is dropped if that tmux session detaches (the
detach notification now carries its @iterm2_id) or if the
pane has not reattached five minutes after reconcile. A held
watch gets no screen backstop until it is re-bound, and a
held copy that duplicates a watch registered again on the
reattached pane is discarded. Session-bound chats keep the
old behavior, since their link still names the old pane
session.

QA finding F-126.

(cherry picked from commit
098675fcf2fb8bd1b6d672df4b5d0a5c3ec29e9f) (cherry picked
from commit 2fe05b81ae21dfa68eee5a63a267e8d73f1db16e)

2026-10-14: Keep pinned tabs out of unpinned groups on Move
Tab Left/Right

Move Tab Right on a pinned tab whose right neighbor was an
unpinned tab group made the tab join that group in place.
Joining copies the group's pinned state onto the tab, so the
pin was silently lost. The symmetric case, Move Tab Left on
an unpinned tab next to a pinned group, silently pinned the
tab.

iTermTabGroupOrdering.singleTabMove now takes the tabs'
pinned states and makes the join a no-op when the
neighboring group is across the pinned boundary, matching
how a plain reorder across the boundary is already clamped.
Joins on the same side of the boundary are unchanged.

(cherry picked from commit

2026-10-14: Keep AI chat timers when a chat is unlinked from
its session

Unlinking a session-bound chat from its terminal session,
which also happens when the linked session closes, cancelled
every watcher on the chat, including register_timer timers.
A timer targets no session, so the model was told it would
fire, but nothing fired and list_watches came back empty.

The unlink path now drops only the session watches it
orphans and leaves timers armed and persisted, both with a
live dispatcher and in the client's no-dispatcher path. Stop
and Disable Orchestration still cancel timers: Stop exists
so no later event can wake the agent, and disabling
orchestration clears all watchers.

QA finding F-110.

(cherry picked from commit

2026-10-14: Expand {{FILE}} in AI suggestions with the whole
filename

File completion items carry only the text to insert after
the word being typed, so typing er with error.log present
yields ror.log. AI command suggestions substituted that
suffix for {{FILE}}, so rm {{FILE}} was offered as rm
ror.log, which runs rm on the wrong name.

Rebuild the name from the typed word plus the suffix and
shell-escape it, since AI suggestions are inserted without
the escaping applied to plain file completions. An append
whose text begins with {{FILE}} is inserted right after the
partial word, so it still gets just the suffix there.

QA finding F-114.

(cherry picked from commit
e44fceefb41bd3814d585111a6f461fdc1b73811) (cherry picked
from commit 1d7f17088d010446b26534bd8a5ce7362ecd53be)

2026-10-14: Keep one rate-limit timer per program status
notifier

ProgramStatusNotifier treated a non-empty held set as
meaning its post timer was scheduled. Handling a batch of
OSC 7501 reports can drop every held notification (the
record went back to working) while the timer is still due,
so the next notification inside the interval scheduled a
second timer for the same deadline. Under a flood, batches
ran between those timers and each timer posted, giving two
or three notifications within moments of each other instead
of one per three seconds.

The notifier now tracks whether its timer is outstanding
explicitly, clears that when the timer fires, and holds new
notifications while it is outstanding.

QA finding F-326.

(cherry picked from commit
a434348d5bffd30cd137df4d737b8643aaa7196f) (cherry picked
from commit c8bbd5a75d0769df0475eea8a608bfdfc51ea7d4)

2026-10-14: Bound AI Usage command memory and kill its whole
process group

A custom AI Usage command that floods stdout, such as
/usr/bin/yes, grew iTerm2's memory by gigabytes before the
20 s timeout even though captured output was capped at 1
MiB. iTermCommandRunner's read loop called -availableData
outside its per-iteration autorelease pool, so every chunk
read stayed alive until reading finished. The read now
happens inside the pool, which bounds memory for every
caller of the runner.

The timeout also killed only the direct child. A wrapper
whose child kept stdout open (for example a script running
sleep 60) left the runner reading until that child exited,
and orphaned children piled up.

iTermCommandRunner gains an opt-in terminatesProcessGroup,
which makes -terminate SIGKILL the command's process group
(NSTask makes the command its group leader), and
iTermBufferedCommandRunner gains an opt-in
terminateWhenOutputExceedsMaximum, which terminates the
command once the output cap is exceeded. The AI Usage item
enables both; other callers keep their existing behavior.

QA findings F-116 and F-117.

(cherry picked from commit
4cd0c197329ee9e1d8bec03f643af5156cc961f2) (cherry picked
from commit e043a7a738cda93c2bcd2f8f3c0b35f3611c02d7)

2026-10-14: Hold a remote session.path only while ssh's
process group is in the foreground

Three fixes to session.path (F-103, F-134, F-138) each added
a special case: a polled cwd baseline taken after a remote
push, and a shell/other classifier of the foreground job.
Both broke in new places. ssh started from a local subshell
kept the remote path after exit, because the subshell never
classified as the shell (F-141). In a tmux integration pane
the baseline was taken after the first local cd, which was
then swallowed (F-142).

Replace them with one rule. When a remote shell first
reports its directory, record the local terminal's
foreground process group (the group ssh runs in, or the root
when the profile's command is ssh). The remote directory
stays current only while that group is still in the
foreground. A valid poll that finds anything else there, or
can't read it, makes the polled local directory current.
Later pushes in the same remote stretch keep the recorded
group, so an OSC 7 processed after ssh exits still ends it.
tmux integration panes have no local terminal to read, so
polls win there as in 3.7.4. The group is saved in the
arrangement so a session whose ssh survives a relaunch keeps
its remote path; a new shell goes local on the first poll,
and older arrangements behave as in 3.7.4.

Known limit: wrappers that run their own pty (sudo with
use_pty, script, screen, a local plain tmux) stay in the
foreground, so a remote path set inside them lasts until
they exit, as it did before this change.

QA findings F-141 and F-142.

(cherry picked from commit
f72f61f2b72ceda3349eb89a68e3b52756fac24a) (cherry picked
from commit 0c600af155b138da17880c3f1ec1f1426d48101f)

2026-10-14: Don't overwrite an unparseable Claude Code
settings.json on install

Installing or reinstalling the Claude Code integration read
settings.json with try? and fell back to an empty object
when the file couldn't be read or parsed, then wrote the
result. A settings.json with a single syntax error was
replaced by a file holding only the cc-status hooks, losing
the user's permissions, env, model, and every other setting
without warning.

Move the read/merge/write into mergeCCStatusHooks, which
refuses to write an existing file that can't be read or
doesn't parse as a JSON object and returns a result the
wizard turns into a localized alert naming the file and
asking the user to fix or remove it and try again. A missing
or blank file still starts from an empty object. The
uninstall path already left such files untouched; tests now
pin that too.

2026-10-14: Give the AI key hint room for its longest

The hint under API key in the manual AI model editor had a

English too.

unchanged), and moves the controls below it and the window's
height by the difference. In Settings the hint wraps to two
lines, which every shipped language fits in the width
visible in the tab, and the Timeout row and safety checkbox
move down to make room. Should a future translation need

2026-10-14: Update session.path when the local shell returns
from ssh without a cd

After ssh'ing somewhere whose shell reports its directory
with OSC 7 and then exiting back to a local shell without
shell integration, session.path kept the remote directory
until the user changed directory locally. The fix for F-134
only accepted a polled local cwd that differed from the
first one seen after the remote push, and the local shell
comes back in the directory it ssh'ed from.

The directory tracker now asks its delegate what is in the
foreground of the local terminal. PTYSession reads the
terminal's foreground process group from the kernel and
reports whether the polled process (the shell, or login's
child) leads it. Once another job such as ssh has been seen
in the foreground while the directory is remote, a valid
poll that finds the shell in the foreground again becomes
the current directory even if it did not change. Requiring
another job first keeps the remote directory for profiles
whose command is ssh itself, and tmux integration panes
report unknown and keep the earlier behavior.

QA finding F-138.

(cherry picked from commit
4d2232a7ac5f180239e62e91507d76a215620990) (cherry picked
from commit 425e2720c8d43d425643e12e4cdf5a30278d083c)

2026-10-14: Coalesce OSC 7501 reports so a flood cannot
swamp the main thread

Each OSC 7501 report added its own deferred side effect, and
each one ran the full program-status update on the main
thread: comparing records, posting the records-did-change
notification, building display records, calling the notifier
and re-applying the tab status. A program flipping a task
between two states as fast as it could write queued about a
million side effects in ten seconds, so the session fell
minutes behind drawing, memory grew to gigabytes, and
rate-limited notifications kept arriving until the main
thread caught up.

Reports with no other side effect between them now share one
batch and one side effect. The token executor exposes a
side-effect serial so a report only joins a batch whose side
effect is still last in the queue, which keeps reports in
order relative to resets, OSC 21337 and other side effects.
The batch keeps only each record's latest report, at the
position of its last write, with clears as barriers, so a
flood that cycles among a few records holds one report per
record.

The program status controller handles a batch at once: the
records change once, the tab status is set once, and each
record whose state changed over the batch, or that was
written after a clear, is announced once in its final state.
States a record only passed through within a batch were
never on screen and are not announced.

2026-10-14: Send the OpenAI key automatically only to
Azure's AI service hosts

Under the Automatic key choice, any host ending in
.azure.com counted as OpenAI's own host, so it got the
user's OpenAI key. But anyone with an Azure VM chooses the
label of its public DNS name
(<label>.<region>.cloudapp.azure.com), so a third-party or
company gateway there received the key, and Settings said it
would.

Key binding now uses a stricter predicate that matches only
Azure's AI service endpoints (*.openai.azure.com,
*.cognitiveservices.azure.com, *.services.ai.azure.com). The
broader .azure.com test still picks the Azure request
format, so a key the user chooses explicitly for such a host
is still sent in the api-key header.

QA finding F-135.
