2026-10-14: Let the local cwd update session.path again
after leaving ssh

After ssh'ing somewhere whose shell reports its directory
with OSC 7 and then exiting back to a local shell without
shell integration, session.path stayed on the remote
directory forever, even after cd. The fix for F-103 stopped
the polled local cwd from writing the path, and once any
shell has pushed a directory the poller never creates marks
again, so nothing could update the path.

The poller reads the login shell's cwd, which cannot change
while a foreground job like ssh runs. The tracker now
remembers whether the last directory was remote and records
the first valid poll result after a remote push. A later
valid result that differs means the local shell is back and
changed directory, so it becomes the current directory. When
the last directory is not remote, a differing poll result
becomes current as before the F-103 fix. The remote flag is
saved in the arrangement ("Last Directory Is Remote",
already read for backward compatibility) and inferred for
older arrangements, so a restored session whose ssh job
survived a relaunch keeps its remote path.

2026-10-14: Show the AI lockout alert for Engage AI in the

model, but the user saw a confusing Plugin not allowed error
instead of the 3.7.4 alert, and the backstop does not cover
Apple Intelligence.

The action now runs the gatekeeper check up front, as the
shell-prompt Engage AI path does, and sends nothing when it
fails. Under the lockout this shows Feature Unavailable /
Generative AI features have been disabled. This also
restores the 3.7.4 must enable AI alert when AI is turned
off in settings and a manual model is configured, which had
been sending the composer text anyway.

2026-10-14: Let a click replace Cockpit chips from clicked
rows that are now hidden

To keep a chip the user typed for a collapsed or
filtered-out session, a click on another row stopped
replacing any chip whose row was hidden.  That also kept
chips the user got by clicking a row whose group was then
collapsed (or hidden by a refresh). The next click added a
target instead of retargeting, the chip survived the clear
after a send, and every later send also went to the hidden
session.

The Cockpit now remembers which chips came from rows the
user selected.  When the user replaces the selection
(selects a row and either had nothing selected or dropped a
visible row), those chips are replaced even if their rows
are hidden. Collapsing a group, which only removes rows from
the selection, and Cmd-clicking to add a row replace
nothing.  Chips the user typed for hidden sessions still
stay, and the clear after a send never drops a hidden chip
on its own.

QA finding F-264.

from commit 5e37e6d3ee57bedff9230059b10565c7092261df)

2026-10-14: Send a vendor's key automatically only to that
vendor's own host

A manual AI model whose API key choice was Automatic sent
the stored key of whatever vendor the model resolved to, and
an OpenAI-compatible model at an unrecognized host
(OpenRouter, z.ai, Qwen, MiniMax, a company gateway)
resolves to OpenAI. So the user's OpenAI key was sent to
third parties. A model name such as gpt-*, claude-* or
llama-* at a gateway had the same effect with that vendor's
key. Choosing a gateway preset also reset the key choice to
Automatic, which could turn None into sending the OpenAI
key.

Automatic now sends a vendor's key only to that vendor's own
host (api.openai.com and Azure for OpenAI, Anthropic, Google
and DeepSeek); elsewhere it sends none and the user picks a
key explicitly. Name inference still picks the request
dialect. Ollama's key stays unbound for native Ollama
servers. When a gateway refuses the keyless request, the
error explains why and where to choose a key, and the
editor's hints and Automatic title say no key is sent.
Gateway presets select None.

Configurations saved by older builds have no stored key
choice and relied on the old behavior, so an absent choice
is read as the key Automatic used to send, and new builds
always store the choice (Automatic as "automatic"). This is
decided on read rather than by a one-time migration so that
prefs loaded from a custom folder or synced from an older
build are covered. The legacy single manual model keeps its
key the same way.

2026-10-14: Run the AI safety check migration at launch so
it never undoes a new choice

The one-time migration that moves the AI command safety
check off on-device Apple Intelligence only ran when the
first AI controller was created. On a Mac without Apple
Intelligence, a user who turned on Use AI to check safety of
commands before running them before ever using AI chat was
mistaken for a 3.6 user: the first controller cleared the
setting and the nag flag, and the next proposed command
asked whether to enable safety checking again.

Run the migration at launch, after user defaults are
initialized and before Settings can be opened, and have the
Settings checkbox mark the migration done so an explicit
choice is never migrated regardless of ordering. Users
upgrading straight from 3.6 are still migrated once.

QA finding F-092.

2026-10-14: Give same-named files in one SSH upload batch
distinct remote names

Uploading several files over SSH integration (Upload and
Paste Paths) starts every upload at once. Each one chose its
remote name by checking what existed on the remote host, so
two files named x.txt both found x.txt free before either
was moved into place. The second overwrote the first, both
transfers reported success, and the same path was pasted
twice.

The conductor now keeps the names claimed by uploads in
progress. A name is skipped if another upload has claimed
it, re-checked after the existence query returns, and
queried again if another upload placed a file while the
query was in flight. The claim is released once the file is
in place or the upload fails.

QA finding F-102.

(cherry picked from commit
d81fddb8728a286511e2f742db3d63b7abef9001) (cherry picked
from commit b76666bd7a23289fcca7b6d78213f219453bfd95)

2026-10-14: Load triggers that have no regex instead of
crashing

A trigger dictionary without a "regex" key (or with a
non-string one) made +[Trigger triggerFromUntrustedDict:]
pass nil to -setRegex:, and NSRegularExpression threw on the
nil pattern. Every session creation loads the profile's
triggers this way, so a hand-written event trigger in a
Dynamic Profile, which has no use for a regex, crashed
iTerm2 before it could open a window.

Treat a missing or non-string regex as "", which is how
Settings stores an empty regex, so these triggers load and
behave the same as ones saved from the UI.

QA finding F-203.

(cherry picked from commit

from commit bd929c28ccbbf5f24eb9060c683cac572bc4ce40)

2026-10-14: Keep session.path on the remote directory when
the local cwd is polled

In a plain ssh session (no ssh integration) whose remote
shell reports its directory with OSC 7, session.path
switched to the local ssh process's working directory after
running a command, so Upload and Paste Path sent files to
the wrong remote directory.

The directory tracker's lastLocalDirectory didSet wrote its
value into the path variable. A remote directory push leaves
lastLocalDirectory unpushed, so when the working directory
poller (triggered by a key press and line feed) returned the
local cwd, it was stored as the last local directory and
clobbered the path. Whether the next OSC 7 landed before or
after the poll result made it intermittent. The path write
was added when directory tracking was extracted from
PTYSession; the original setter never touched the path
variable. Remove it so only setLastDirectory updates the
path.

QA finding F-103.

(cherry picked from commit
ea39eac26adf75341bdcd697bc1c69e31bbd3fca)

2026-10-14: Keep focus and floats right for a filtered tmux
pane on layout change

With Find > Filter open on a tmux integration pane, the tab
shows a synthetic stand-in session in place of the tmux
session. The stand-in is not a tmux client, so its tmux pane
id reads as 0, the same as tmux pane %0. On a layout change
from tmux:

- The active pane was computed from the stand-in's own id,
so focus
  moved to %0 instead of staying on the filtered pane.
- The loop that decides which leftover sessions become
floats used the
  stand-in's id, so a filtered pane that tmux made a float
  was terminated (taking its live session with it) and a
  duplicate float was created, and the stand-in could be
  taken for %0 becoming a float.
- Float reconciliation and placement matched sessions by
their own id,
  so a filtered float was not found, was marked hidden, and
  was never placed.

Add +tmuxLayoutPaneForSession:, which maps a stand-in to its
live session's pane and a non-tmux session to -1, and use it
for the active pane, the leftover-float decision, float
reconciliation, and float placement. The earlier part of
this bug, where the stand-in took %0's slot, was fixed by
keying reused views the same way.

QA finding F-097.

(cherry picked from commit
d1a49cbff21a3fcec14a9a6f342149ab58aa3754)

2026-10-14: Keep the Cockpit composer's chips and caret when
the outline changes

While a selected session changed status, the Cockpit moved
its row to another status group by removing and reinserting
it. That cleared and then restored the row's selection, and
each change rewrote the composer from the selection, so the
caret jumped to the end and text typed mid-line landed at
the end. Clicking a row also replaced every chip with chips
for the selected rows, silently dropping a chip for a
session whose row was collapsed or filtered out and
retargeting the command.  Sending did the same when it put
the chips back.

Selection changes made while the outline refreshes no longer
touch the composer. After a refresh, the only chips removed
are ones whose session, tab, or window has gone away. When
the user changes the selection, the selected rows replace
only chips the user could see and did not select (plus what
a container row they just deselected had contributed). Chips
for hidden rows stay, and the clear after a send keeps them
too.

QA finding F-241.

(cherry picked from commit
965774c55e5f07594236a0e9c70155ab735c7ac9)

2026-10-14: Keep AI chat links and watches across a quit
that restores windows

An ordinary quit closes every window, so each session
terminates and posts iTermSessionWillTerminate. The AI chat
treated that like a real close: ChatViewController unlinked
the chat from its terminal session, which also cancelled
every watch and timer the chat had registered and posted
This chat is no longer linked to a terminal session., and
the orchestration dispatcher dropped watches on the session
and reported them to the agent.  With window restoration on,
the session comes back at the next launch with the same guid
and stableID, so the link and its watches were lost for no
reason.

Both observers now leave everything in place when the
session is going away for a quit that window restoration
will undo: the app is quitting, windows will be restored at
the next launch (Option-Quit discards saved state, so it
still unlinks), and the session was not a tmux client (tmux
tabs are not restored). Because -[PTYSession terminate]
clears tmuxMode before posting, it now passes whether the
session was a tmux client in the notification's userInfo.
Watches whose session is not restored are still dropped by
the launch-time reconcile.

QA finding F-088.

(cherry picked from commit
be21b798c0aa7b2db4755450e8aa25bd004bf5f3)

2026-10-14: Stop the companion wizard from asking for a key
the AI model won't use

The full (AI) setup path always showed an API key field and
Provider popup, stored the pasted key under the chosen
provider, and then verified that the default model could
authenticate. With a manually chosen model that has no key,
the provider choice had no effect, so verification failed
with The AI and companion plugins did not load after
installation. even though both plugins loaded, and the whole
setup was rolled back.

The screen now decides how to handle AI credentials with a
pure, tested function. If the default model can already
authorize, the key controls are hidden and Install neither
requires nor writes a key or vendor. If a manual model can't
authorize, the key controls are hidden, a note points to
Settings > General > AI, and setup finishes without AI
working yet. Only a recommended model with no usable key
still shows the key field and Provider popup. Verification
now tells a plugin that did not load apart from a model that
can't use the entered key, and Install rebuilds the screen
if AI settings changed while it was open.

QA finding F-101.

(cherry picked from commit
127fffefd2fe44fc45a97a987193f6c9d8dd4e2f)

2026-10-14: Send only the selected text from the Cockpit
composer

With text selected in the Cockpit composer, Shift-Return,
Opt-Return and Opt-Shift-Return sent the whole field instead
of the selection and then cleared it. The composer reported
each selection separately, but the Cockpit ignored the
reported text and always sent the field's typed text. With
two selections the second report found only the target chips
left and showed an Add a command after the @mention error
after the first send had already gone out.

In the Cockpit the composer now reports all selections as
one command, one line per selection in document order. The
Cockpit recognizes a selection send, sends just the selected
text (minus any chips) to the chips anywhere in the field,
and leaves the field as it was, the same as the regular

QA finding F-239.

(cherry picked from commit
7debbdf916be6ea05382fdb9c3697ee1fa20e47d)

2026-10-14: Revoke password manager authentication on screen
lock without its window

In the Once per Launch and after Screen Locks mode, a screen
lock only revoked the cached password manager authentication
through a class observer that the password manager window
registered in awakeFromNib. The browser password manager can
be unlocked by saving a basic-auth credential without that
window ever loading, so no observer existed and a lock left
it unlocked: remembered basic-auth credentials were filled
in silently and the browser password manager opened without
authentication.

The data source provider now observes the screen lock
itself, so the cached authentication is revoked however it
was obtained. The window keeps its own observer, which
closes an open window.

QA finding F-098.

(cherry picked from commit
a41515c79c87c95f0ff2e1ecf8395be710bf3f51)

2026-10-14: Pass the shell's CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR to the AI
Usage command

The AI Usage workgroup toolbar item ran `claude -p /usage`
without CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR. Users whose Claude Code login
lives in a custom CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR exported from their
shell startup files got not signed in or another account's
usage.

AdapterShellEnvironment's single cached login-shell run now
reports CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR along with PATH, using one /bin/sh
printf command that exits 0 whether or not the variable is
set. Password manager adapters still get only HOME and PATH;
the AI Usage item reads both values through a new
shellValues(_:) API, so the login shell is not run any more
often.

The item sets CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR from, in order: the value
the shell exports (startup files run after the inherited
environment, so this is what claude in a terminal uses), the
value in iTerm2's own environment, and, only when the shell
could not be read, the directory recorded when the Claude
Code integration was installed
(NoSyncClaudeCodeConfigDirPath).

QA finding F-244.

(cherry picked from commit
62ee85941b1c888737cd35824c43b1e86f557c1d)

2026-10-14: Treat a non-string bell sound in a profile as
the system alert sound

A dynamic or hand-edited profile whose Bell Sound was not a
string (for example "Bell Sound": 5) crashed the app the
first time the bell rang.  PTYSession read the value with
stringForKey:inProfile:, which returns the stored object
unchecked, so an NSNumber reached a Swift String parameter
of iTermBellSoundPlayer and the bridge threw. Opening the
profile in Settings would also have crashed calling -length
on it.

Add iTermBellSound helpers that return the value only when
it is a string (logging and returning nil otherwise, which
means the system alert sound) and use them for the bell and
in Settings. Settings now selects the bell sound itself so a
malformed value shows the system alert sound rather than an
empty popup.

QA finding F-106.

(cherry picked from commit
b1c5bad030aa241ba55620c40593f6876d304448)

2026-10-14: Keep AI chat timers and watches from reaching
the LLM under the AI lockout

A timer registered by an AI chat kept working after an
administrator disabled generative AI with the
GenerativeAIAllowed advanced setting. At the next launch the
persisted timer was re-armed, fired, and its timerFired
status update started an agent turn that sent the chat
history to the configured LLM endpoint, while every other AI
entry point was blocked.  Watches registered by a chat had
the same problem.

The orchestration dispatcher now checks the lockout wherever
a timer or watch would wake the agent. A blocked timer,
tab-status fire, or screen-poll result leaves its watcher
persisted but dormant, so turning AI back on does not lose
it; it is re-armed at the next launch. Screen polling and
escalation checks, which ask an LLM to judge the screen, do
not run under the lockout, and launch-time timer re-arming
is skipped.  As a backstop, iTermAIClient.request now
refuses every remote LLM request while generative AI is
disallowed.

QA finding F-089.

(cherry picked from commit
5f5d78d13f0ff3e39e3907b087aab26ccc76dcfb)

2026-10-14: Close only the detached tmux windows that asked
when answering Always

Answering Always to the Close tmux windows after detaching?
announcement closed every session in the app, including the
gateway (a plain shell again after the detach) and unrelated
sessions. Every session's nagging controller observes the
notification that broadcasts the answer, and each one closed
its session unconditionally.

Only a leftover tmux window asks the question, from
tmuxDidDisconnect.  The nagging controller now remembers
that it asked, and only controllers that asked act on the
answer. Restarting the session forgets it.

QA finding F-252.

(cherry picked from commit
02abd97be1f45290a8b757e367587e5469eb2a51)

2026-10-14: Fix crashes acting on tmux windows left open
after detaching

After a tmux -CC detach that leaves the windows open, the
controller's gateway is nil. Several actions on those
windows built command lists from the gateway as array
literals or with addObject:, which threw
NSInvalidArgumentException on the nil entries: Maximize
Active Pane (toggleZoomForPane:), swapping panes, arranging
panes (select-layout), splitting a pane, resizing a floating
pane, and creating a window from the dashboard.

Return early with a DLog when the controller is detached or
has no gateway, as setCurrentWindow: already does. A split
with a completion block reports failure (-1) so the window
undoes its pending-split state and notifies callers.

QA finding F-096.

(cherry picked from commit
ea06c6c830d4f64be0ea46c300d555ff6e5a907d)

2026-10-14: Fix offline shell integration install hanging
for zsh

The Internet-Free install discovers a zsh user's dotfile
directory by typing an echo after learning the shell. It
typed that echo before registering the expectation that
waits for its output. Expectations reach the trigger
evaluator only on the next sync, so a fast shell's output
was checked before the expectation existed and the installer
waited forever, including after Send Again. Type the echo
only once the expectation is in place, as every other step
does, and fall back to $HOME when the reported directory is
empty.

Skip on the discovery step advanced with the shell
defaulting to bash and no dotfile directory, so later steps
typed paths like (null)/.iterm2_shell_integration.bash.
Discovery can no longer be skipped.

QA finding F-236.

(cherry picked from commit
aabb1b162c9fc0ad0c2ee5709137ac49c6d953f0)

2026-10-14: Keep a cleared screen in place when restoring a
session

A restored session is created at the wrong size and then fit
to its window. When the screen had been cleared (ED 2) with
the cursor moved down, that resize round trip trimmed the
blank rows below the cursor and pulled the pre-clear lines
from scrollback onto the screen. Restoring the saved cursor
position afterwards then left the cursor pointing into the
old text, above the new content.

Restoration now records where the cursor was right after
restoring. When the window has been fit, if only a resize
slide moved the content (the cursor's absolute line is
unchanged and the window is not taller than the saved grid),
the screen is scrolled back up by the slide so the content
returns to the rows it had when the state was saved, and
then the cursor is restored as before.

QA finding F-224.

(cherry picked from commit
8dc7345326f237d031601d924ef751cdd2eedd66)

2026-10-14: Keep each color of a mid-word SGR split on the
legacy renderer

On a bidi line, runs that differ only in foreground color
are appended to the same attributed-string builder so
CoreText shapes the word as a whole. When the builder was
still eligible for the fast path (zippy text drawing, or
ASCII text), changing its attributes after characters had
been appended snapshotted them into a cheap single-attribute
string that shared the builder's character buffer.
Characters appended after the color change landed in that
same buffer, so the final string gave every character the
first run's attributes: with the GPU renderer off, printf '
\e[31m\e[32m' drew all of  in red.

Changing attributes on a non-empty builder now disables the
fast path, so the builder produces a real attributed string
with per-range attributes and the CoreGraphics renderer
fills each span with its own color while the word stays
joined.

QA finding F-077.

(cherry picked from commit
dd2424dfcb3e9e67eca696e750c249e789d7eea6)

2026-10-14: Redact AI custom header values and keys from
debug and wire logs

A debug log of one AI chat send contained every per-model
custom header value in clear (Authorization,
Proxy-Authorization, X-Auth-Token, ...), because
WebRequest.debugDescription and several JSFetch DLogs
printed the request headers, and callAsync logged the
JSON-encoded request. The raw AI wire log, which is meant to
be safe to attach, fingerprinted only four header names and
wrote other custom header values verbatim.  URLSession
errors, RLogged always, also printed the failing URL, which
carries the API key for Gemini.

The wire log sanitizer now fingerprints every header value
except a short allowlist of harmless headers (Content-Type,
Accept, anthropic-version, ...), keeps an auth scheme prefix
only for Authorization and Proxy-Authorization, and scrubs
key-like URL query parameters. The debug log uses the same
sanitizer for request descriptions, URLs, error text, and
response and JS console output, and the encoded request
arguments are logged as a count only. Nothing sent on the
wire changes.

QA finding F-085 (also F-016).

(cherry picked from commit
6ea2a4b65f8e131110e98b3b5527eafa28e722de)

2026-10-14: Cancel an AI chat turn when its message is
deleted

Deleting a user message whose turn was still running did not
cancel the turn. ChatService dropped removed messages only
from the queue's undispatched tail, so the reply arrived
later and was appended and persisted after the cut,
answering a message that no longer existed.  ChatService now
cancels the turn when the truncation removes its head
message, and discards that turn's streamed chunks and reply.

Cancelling exposed a second leak. AITermController is reused
for the next turn, and cancelling does not always stop the
streamed transfer, so a cancelled request's late words and
final response were parsed as the next request's reply:
after a delete (or Stop) the next message "answered" with
the rest of the old reply. A cancel during the deferred send
also still sent the request. A request generation, bumped on
cancel, now drops a cancelled request's deferred send,
streamed words, response, and tool-call follow-up.
Cancelling while an attachment uploads, previously a fatal
error, now fails the upload quietly.

QA finding F-086, part A.

(cherry picked from commit
d900c8ad25a0b7d277e1dff5e3e18e72cb800657)

2026-10-14: Keep observing variables an async evaluation
reads late

Session Status rows stopped following a session rename. The
row's name label evaluates
\(iterm2.private.session_name(session: id)), and that
built-in reads its default name and profileName arguments
from the scope only after its explicit argument has been
evaluated. For the asynchronous evaluation, that happens
after iTermGenericEvaluator had already snapshotted its
dependencies, so it observed only "id". The synchronous
evaluation's dependencies, which did include "name", were
replaced and freed when the autorelease pool drained, so
nothing observed the rename.

This was latent before, but every table reload rebuilt the
label's evaluator, which hid it. Reusing the evaluator when
the string and scope are unchanged exposed it.

Install the dependencies again when an evaluation completes,
guarded by a generation counter so only the newest
evaluation installs them and skipped after invalidate. While
an asynchronous evaluation is in flight, merge what it has
read so far into the existing dependencies instead of
replacing them, so a change in that window is not lost.

QA finding F-071.

(cherry picked from commit
4abc7207f570a78647af69d0568d0144039463b7)

2026-10-14: Rebuild it2 to pick up session list --format and
new status help

The bundled it2 is the checked-in prebuilt it2cli/bin/it2,
last rebuilt in c77dbd257. It predated 9e65c39eb (it2
session list --format) and 49936d3a5 (set-status help
listing done and error), so "it2 session list --format ..."
failed with "Unknown option --format".

Rebuilt from a clean scratch directory with the sandboxed
"make paranoid-it2cli UNIVERSAL=1": universal arm64 and
x86_64, minimum macOS 13.0, Developer ID signed with the
hardened runtime, linking only system libraries.

The paranoid-it2cli target now limits PATH to system
directories. The Makefile's own PATH puts Homebrew first, so
with no cached package checkouts SwiftPM tried to clone with
Homebrew's git, which the sandbox blocks.

QA finding F-070.

(cherry picked from commit
7aa50e08ace6c77aeb6e42a7208ea139ba1d3c60)

2026-10-14: Never put a native session into a tmux tab as a
floating pane

A session iTerm2 runs itself (a shell from an external open
with the Floating Pane open style, or a web page from Open
URL in Floating Pane) could be installed as a float in a
tmux integration tab. The launcher and Open URL paths only
checked canCreateFloatingPane, which allows tmux tabs whose
server supports floats, and when floats were not allowed
(older tmux, instant replay) the launcher's floating pane
case fell through into the split case, which installed the
float anyway or made a native split. On the next
%layout-change the native session, whose tmux pane id reads
as 0, was laid out in place of tmux pane %0, hiding %0 and
taking its input.

Native floats now require canInstallNativeFloatingPane,
which also refuses tmux tabs; when it refuses, the launcher
opens a new tab and reports the tab object type. The
create_floating_pane function no longer falls back to a
native float in a tmux tab. As a safeguard, a tmux layout
change only reuses sessions that are tmux panes, keying a
filtered pane's synthetic session by its live session's
pane.

QA findings F-046 and F-047 (and F-008).

(cherry picked from commit
35a475674d153933afb18e19b5ba082045ec76b4)

2026-10-14: Make Opt-Return and Opt-Shift-Return send in the
Cockpit composer

In the Cockpit, Opt-Shift-Return (send each) and Opt-Return
(enqueue each) erased the typed command and the session chip
and sent nothing.  Those actions extract each command as a
plain string and delete it from the composer before calling
the delegate. The Cockpit ignores that string and reads the
composer's attributed contents to find its target chips and
the command, so by then there was nothing left to send.

The Cockpit composer holds a single command plus target
chips and has no command queue, so when rich text (chips) is
enabled, send each and enqueue each now act like Send
(Shift-Return) and leave the contents in place for the
delegate to read. Other composers are unaffected.

QA finding F-001.

(cherry picked from commit
3f627dfe06f48f29b138576f62b590cfbb438f75)

2026-10-14: Do not treat acknowledging a status as a change
for Notify on Status Change

Typing in a session whose status was done or error turns it
idle. PTYSession wraps that in acknowledge{} so it posts no
notification, but Notify on Status Change observes
SessionStatusController directly and never saw the flag, so
it popped a Session status changed alert and disarmed the
watch.

SessionStatusController now tracks which sessions are
acknowledging, and TabStatusController.acknowledge marks the
session there too. The notify controller absorbs a debounce
burst whose status-text changes were all acknowledgments: no
alert, still armed, and the next real change is measured
from the acknowledged value. Changes to other fields in the
same burst do not count as news.

The alert also said the status changed from none. The
controller is created lazily, and its window-scope baseline
was filled only by changes it observed, so a status set
before it existed had no baseline. It now seeds the baseline
from existing statuses, which also stops a restated status
from alerting.

QA finding F-002.

(cherry picked from commit
8a3ae030dd44dabd61ce365e487c07a53c1038a3)

2026-10-14: Run the AI Usage command with the user's shell
PATH

The AI Usage workgroup toolbar item ran its command with
iTerm2's own environment. When iTerm2 is launched from the
Dock, PATH is only /usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin, so the
bundled script's `command -v claude` failed for installs in
~/.local/bin or /opt/homebrew/bin and the item reported that
claude was not found.

Before each run the item now asynchronously gets the login
shell's PATH from AdapterShellEnvironment, the cached lookup
the password manager adapters already use, and runs the
command with iTerm2's environment with PATH replaced.
~/.local/bin, where Claude Code's native installer puts
claude, is appended when missing because the lookup's
fallback lacks it.  The item never calls prefetch(), so it
does not run the login shell periodically. The environment
provider and command runner are injectable so tests can
check what reaches the runner.

QA finding F-011.

(cherry picked from commit
b85e309c05207ea90f78a131801ab93d5398b05b)

2026-10-14: Defer AI chat watcher reconciliation until
session restoration finishes

At launch, applicationDidFinishLaunching re-arms persisted
AI chat timers, which builds an orchestration dispatcher for
each chat with a timer. The dispatcher's init reconciled its
persisted watchers immediately, but window restoration had
not run yet, so every watched session looked gone. Each
watch was dropped, saved, and reported to the agent as a
watcherDropped event, and each event started a paid agent
turn.

Add a one-shot SessionRestorationGate. A dispatcher now
reconciles the watchers persisted at its creation only once
the gate opens, and ignores tab-status changes for sessions
it has not seeded yet, so restoration-time status updates
are not mistaken for transitions. The app delegate opens the
gate once restoreWindowsWithCompletion: has finished (which
includes native full screen windows), the post-restoration
steps (buried sessions, workgroup reconstruction, orphan
adoption) have run, and no window is still decoding its
restorable state. Without the restorable state controller
the gate opens immediately.

QA finding F-010.

(cherry picked from commit
a8aa8c061c32ef5d0603c05450070121d0ea8a13)

2026-10-14: Truncate hidden rows and dequeue removed
messages on AI chat delete

Deleting or editing an AI chat message truncates the log
from that message onward, but two things leaked past the
truncation. The view names only the rows it displays, so
hidden tool results and resolved tool requests after the
chosen message stayed in the store and were sent to the LLM
in the next turn's history. And ChatService ignored
messagesRemoved, so a message typed while a turn was running
and then deleted stayed in the pending queue and was still
sent when the turn ended.

ChatListModel.truncate now removes every stored row from the
earliest requested ID to the end, except chat-state rows
(permissions, vector store, rename) that the agent reads
back from history. The broker fans out every removed ID, and
ChatService drops them from the queue's undispatched tail,
leaving the in-flight head for finishTurn to pop.

QA finding F-009.

(cherry picked from commit
f556062a9284d0edd8acccbaeba3dd8cb0b590e8)

2026-10-14: Keep a manual model's auth on its economy
variant

AIMetadata.economyModel(for:in:) copied only the url and api
from the configured model onto the cheaper catalog model
used for command safety checks and screen-watch polling. A
manual model at a gateway that authenticates with custom
headers, or that chose a specific API key, lost that auth on
the economy request: the gateway answered 401, the safety
check failed closed so every command needed manual approval,
and polling failed too.

Copy the custom headers, credential choice, manual
configuration ID and vendor (which picks the key for
automatic credentials) as well, so the economy request goes
to the same endpoint with the same key and headers as the
configured model.

QA finding F-006.

(cherry picked from commit
93725756557bd662fde0f6b056f0359e92954963)

2026-10-14: Re-take the Prevent Sleep assertion after
Restart Session and Undo Close

Restart Session (on a live shell or after the shell exited)
and Undo Close brought a Prevent Sleep session back without
re-taking the idle sleep assertion until some unrelated
event recomputed.

Restart Session cleared the session's exited flag in
replaceTerminatedShellWithNewInstance but nothing told the
sleep prevention coordinator, which had already dropped the
session when the old process died. PTYSession now posts
PTYSessionExitedDidChange whenever its exited state flips,
and the coordinator recomputes on it.  This also replaces
the explicit recompute in brokenPipe.

Undo Close: -revive posts PTYSessionRevived before every
caller puts the session back in a window, so the synchronous
recompute could not see it. The coordinator now defers that
recompute to the next main-queue turn, coalesced, which is
after all revive callers have re-added the session.

The coordinator takes an injectable environment (session
source, power gate, scheduler, assertion) and notification
center so these paths are tested deterministically.

(cherry picked from commit
81dafd414aaaf66ff5ab015a87f94b43f6fcba8c)

2026-10-14: Wire the Prevent Sleep checkbox to
settingChanged:

The Prevent the system from sleeping while a session has
this setting on. checkbox in Settings > Profiles > Session
(and Edit Session) had an outlet but no action connection in

Connect the button's action to settingChanged: on the

preferences view controller.

QA finding F-210.

(cherry picked from commit
1e5fa97524c74a43b39ffb6ac070b9bd9ada95e9)

2026-10-14: Fix crash when a tmux window becomes key after
detaching

After a tmux -CC detach that leaves the windows open, making
one of them key called -[TmuxController setCurrentWindow:].
Since 94a40e856 that method builds an array literal of
command dictionaries from gateway_, which is nil after
detach, so the literal threw NSInvalidArgumentException.
Before that change it sent a single command to nil, which
was a no-op.

Return early when the controller is detached or has no
gateway.

QA finding F-211.

(cherry picked from commit
817cb9315602e6a5680459d69c71afe351b9b4f2)

2026-10-14: Fix crash when a Set Profile Setting trigger
fires

The Set Profile Setting trigger scheduled its work with an
ordinary side effect. PTYSession handles it by calling
setSessionSpecificProfileValues:, which reloads the profile
and joins threads. Joining from inside a non-paused side
effect trips the assertion in -[VT100Screen
performBlockWithJoinedThreads:], so any such trigger crashed
iTerm2 as soon as it matched.

Use a paused side effect and unpause after the delegate
call, the same pattern the rename trigger and other
profile-changing side effects use.

Add a regression test that fires a real
iTermSetProfileBooleanTrigger through the side-effect path
and checks that joining threads is legal when the delegate
is called.

QA finding F-201.

(cherry picked from commit
699619fd36be4ea0c2ec265bde5e76f75eb1c870)

2026-10-14: 3.7.5

2026-10-12: Bump the Python API module version to 2.30

2026-10-12: Join windowed and connected selection lines in
the Python API

Selection.async_enumerate_ranges told its callback to add a
newline after ranges that iTerm2 joins, so async_get_string
split lines that copying in iTerm2 keeps together. Two bugs,
both differing from iTermSelection.m:

- In a selection limited to a column range, it marked only
the last
  line as connected. iTerm2 connects every line except the
  last.
- It looked up connectors by a range's inclusive end, but
connectors
  are recorded at the exclusive end, so no range ever
  matched one.  Connected sub-selections got a newline too.

2026-10-12: Fix bugs and add guards in the Python API

Behavior changes from PR 782 by djpadz, split out from the
type annotations so they can be reviewed on their own.

Changes that could affect existing scripts:

- SubSelection.enumerate_ranges always calls its callback
with
  (subselection, range). Windowed selections used to pass
  (range) and others (range, subselection), which made
  Selection.async_enumerate_ranges raise TypeError for
  non-windowed selections.
- Rename the internal module globals tmux.DELEGATE,
  tmux.DELEGATE_FACTORY, window.DELEGATE_FACTORY, and
  transaction.CURRENT_TRANSACTION with a g prefix.
- Add __all__ to the iterm2 package, which limits import *.
- _async_subscribe returns the token when unsubscribing
instead of
  None.
- A tab group reports collapsed as False instead of None
when iTerm2
  doesn't send the field.
- Profile customizations passed to rpc.async_create_tab send
None and
  empty-string values as JSON null.

Bug fixes:

- Subscribing to profile change notifications referenced
api_PB2 and
  always raised.
- ProxySession passed its session ID where Session expects a
layout
  link.
- Color.hex raised for non-integer components, such as those
from
  Color.from_dict. It now rounds and clamps them.
- rpc.async_create_tab with a command passed a profile
object where a
  dict was expected. It now layers the command over any
  other customizations.
- async_list_prompts named errors with the wrong status
enum.  - Nested submenus in mainmenu.py are enum
non-members, so their items
  are reachable on Python 3.12 and earlier.
- Selection.async_enumerate_ranges consumed an iterator
twice, so it
  never removed indexes from indexes_to_add.
- The script name sent in the connection headers is always a
string.  - LSBackgroundContextManager raised NameError on

  bundle already had LSBackgroundOnly set, instead of
  restoring it.

New guards, which turn a crash or assertion into a clean
result or error:

- App.get_session_by_id(None) returns None,
  get_tab_and_window_for_session(None) returns (None, None),
  and current_window returns None when there's no current
  window.
- Profile.background_image_mode, title_components, and
title_function
  return None when unset, and async_make_default raises
  BadGUIDException without a GUID.
- Connection.iterm2_protocol_version returns (0, 0) and
  async_send_message raises RuntimeError when not connected.

doesn't match the new types, a type: ignore marks it, and

2026-10-12: Add submenu and visible_when to Python API
context menu providers

ContextMenuProviderRPC.async_register takes two new optional
arguments.  submenu groups items under a submenu with that
title, merging items from any script that share a title.
visible_when is an expression evaluated in the clicked
session's scope when the menu opens; the item is shown only
if it yields a true or nonzero number. When omitted, items
are always shown, so existing scripts are unaffected.

visible_when is evaluated synchronously with no RPCs so
opening the menu never waits on a script. Registration
rejects expressions that fail to parse or that call
functions. Strings are not truthy, matching the expression
language and Automatic Profile Switching rules; the docs
recommend `user.flag == 1`, which matches both a Python
boolean and a string set from the shell with
iterm2_set_user_var.

Protocol version 1.23. The iterm2 module (2.28) warns when
these arguments are passed to an older iTerm2, which would
show the item everywhere, and adds
capabilities.supports_context_menu_submenus_and_visibility.

Includes a manual test
(tests/run_context_menu_submenu_visibility_test.sh).

Issue 13007

2026-10-12: Translate the Manage Permissions button in
Settings > General > Magic

2026-10-12: Add Python API to run JavaScript in browser
sessions

New built-in method for browser sessions:
iterm2.browser_eval_js(js:) runs a function body in the page
world (it may await) and returns the result as JSON;
returning nothing gives null. Like load_url, the first use
on a site asks the user, and the answer is remembered per
origin (scheme, host and port), as
NoSyncBrowserJavaScriptAllowed_<origin>.  Only http and
https pages can be scripted.

The origin is read from the page (location.origin) rather
than webView.url, which names the destination while a
navigation is pending.  The script runs only if the page is
still at the origin the user approved, checked in the same
call that runs it.

Errors reach the script: exceptions and syntax errors come
back with their messages, and a session that closes while
waiting for approval reports a failure. The WebKit code
lives in a Swift extension on iTermBrowserViewController, so
PTYSession.m does not import WebKit.

Python: Session.async_eval_javascript(), gated on API
protocol 1.22 (advertised version bumped from 1.21). Module
version 2.27.

Settings > General > Magic gets a Manage Permissions button
that opens a panel listing the sites where scripts may run
JavaScript or load URLs.  Sites can be added, removed,
allowed or denied there. Edits clear the site's entry from
the Suppressed Alerts list so it does not show a stale
answer.

load_url now keys its remembered answer by the lowercased
host, and moves answers saved under a mixed-case host to the
lowercased key.

Also: sanitizer rules for both kinds of remembered answer,
unit tests for the permission keys and their storage,
translations, a rewritten smoke test
(tests/browser_eval_smoke.py) that serves its own page, and
tests/browser_eval.sh, which runs JavaScript in a browser
session from the command line.

2026-10-12: Add a Python API to hide a browser session's
toolbar

New built-in method
iterm2.browser_set_toolbar_hidden(hidden:) and Python
Session.async_set_browser_toolbar_hidden(). When hidden, the
navigation buttons and URL bar are removed and the page
fills the session, which suits a browser pane a script uses
to show its own page. Open Location shows the toolbar again
so the URL bar stays reachable. If the URL bar has focus
when the toolbar is hidden, focus moves to the page. The
setting is runtime-only and is not saved with the session.

tests/browser_toolbar_test.py is a manual end-to-end check
against a dev build: the page's innerHeight grows by the
toolbar's 44 points and shrinks back, and terminal sessions
are refused.

Changes made when landing (George Nachman): added a Web >
Show Toolbar menu item that toggles the toolbar and shows a
checkmark while it is visible, so it can be hidden and shown
without a script. Updated the docstring and release note to
mention it.

Closes https://github.com/gnachman/iTerm2/pull/785

2026-10-12: Keep the manual size of centered hotkey windows.
Issue 13125.

Showing a hotkey window with a Centered style canonicalized
its frame with preserveSize:NO, which snapped it back to the
profile's grid size and undid any manual resize. Preserve
the size and only re-center, as canonicalizeWindowFrame
already does for these styles since issue 12918.  Also cap a
preserved centered size to the screen's visible frame so a
large window does not hang off a smaller display.

2026-10-12: Only apply appearance settings from Instant
Replay recordings

An .itr recording embeds a profile that was used as-is to
create the replay session, so a crafted recording could
supply triggers (for example, an idle-event Run Command
trigger) that executed when the file was opened. Recordings
can come from anyone, so build the replay session's profile
from an allowlist of drawing-only keys whose values pass the
profile validators, migrate the deprecated keys that still
affect drawing, and always assign a fresh GUID.

Every profile key is now explicitly classified as allowed or
denied for recordings, and a test fails when a new key is
added without a decision.

Issue 13128.

2026-10-12: Point translation docs at the tracked string
catalog

The docs told translators to edit
sources/Localizable.xcstrings, which is a gitignored build
copy, so their edits could never reach a pull request. Point
them at xcstrings/Localizable.xcstrings and recommend
editing its JSON by hand instead of in Xcode's editor, which
reorders the whole file.

2026-10-12: Add Rename Tab to the tab context menu

The tab title dialog could only be reached by
double-clicking a tab, which is not discoverable. The menu
item opens the same dialog for the tab that was clicked.

Changes by the maintainer while merging: the action now
checks that the clicked tab is still in the window before

longer causes the dialog to rename whichever tab is current.
The unrelated string catalog churn was dropped in favor of

2026-10-12: Stop leaking the multiserve launch-request
buffer. (#793)

copyLaunchRequest: encodes into a temporary protocol buffer
and parses a private copy of the strings. The server frees
that temporary after the same encode/parse. The client left
it allocated, and the decode-failure path allocated a second
buffer that shadowed the first.  2026-10-12: VM tests:
require --host instead of defaulting to vm1

The developer decides which agent gets which VM, so there is
no default host and no ITERM2_TEST_VM fallback.

2026-10-11: Add tools for agents to do hands-on QA on a test
VM

tools/vm/qa.sh builds the working tree (and optionally a
baseline ref such as origin/master) on a test VM, launches
it with its own settings suite, drives the pointer and
keyboard, reports which element has focus, presses menu
items, edits profiles, and takes screenshots, all over ssh
so nothing happens on the developer's Mac. vmctl does the
input and accessibility work on the VM.

docs/vm-qa.md explains the one-time permissions, the
workflow, and what was learned doing QA this way, such as
confirming focus by typing because the accessibility tree
can lag. Every command needs --host: the developer decides
which VM an agent uses.

2026-10-11: Rework Focus Follows Mouse so it stops taking
focus from fields in a pane

Focus follows mouse used to move focus to the terminal when
the pointer left the find bar, composer, or another field in
the same pane. Now one controller, PaneFocusFollowsMouse,
decides from what is under the pointer instead of tracking
areas spread across SessionView and PTYTextView.

By default focus moves only between panes. Entering a pane
restores whatever had focus in it, and moving within a pane
changes nothing. A new setting, even to views like Search or
Composer under Settings > Pointer (off by default), lets
focus follow the pointer onto those fields and back onto the
terminal: the find bar, composer, session note, inline AI
chat, clippings, a browser pane's URL bar, and a per-pane
status bar's search field.

Behavior kept from before: no pane switching during drags, a
still pointer doesn't take focus, stealing key focus,
aggressive activation, hotkey windows, Spaces, and floating
panes.

Also, collapsing a session note that had keyboard focus now
focuses its terminal instead of leaving nothing focused.

2026-10-11: VM tests: clean up after hung runs, and free a
lock whose connection died

A hung or cancelled run could leave its test host, the
host's pidinfo service, or a harness's private tmux server
running, outside the job's process tree. A leftover pidinfo
service made every later test host stop at launch. The VM
side now kills those before each run and when it cancels
one, and samples the test host's main thread before
cancelling a run that went quiet, so a hang leaves its stack
in the log.

The lock's connection now carries a heartbeat. A connection
that died without the VM noticing used to hold the lock for
hours; now the VM releases it after two minutes of silence.
A dropped connection during the copy is retried once.

2026-10-11: VM tests: cancel a run that goes quiet, and run
any command after --

A test hung, or waiting on a modal alert nobody on the VM
can answer, used to hold the VM forever. A run that prints
nothing for 15 minutes (--idle-timeout) is now cancelled
with a message saying so, and exits 124.

Anything after -- is now run as a command, as the usage
says, instead of only paths in the repo. The script is read
whole before it runs, so editing it during a run no longer
changes that run.

2026-10-11: Answer iTerm2's first-run update prompt on test
VMs

The test host keeps its preferences in the iterm2-tests
suite. On a fresh account Sparkle asks there whether to
check for updates automatically, in a modal alert nobody
answers. While it is up AppKit delivers no actions outside
it, so menu and warning tests failed on a new VM. The
provisioning script now answers it.

2026-10-11: Run the tests on a VM instead of this Mac

tools/run_tests_vm.sh takes the same arguments as
run_tests.expect, or any command after --, and runs it on a
macOS VM: it copies the working tree, uncommitted changes
and submodules included, to a checkout kept on the VM, runs
the command in the VM's GUI session (an app-hosted test
needs one), streams the output, keeps the log in
tmp/vm-tests, and exits with the command's status. It
refuses this machine and anything that is not a VM, holds a
lock per VM for the life of its connection, and picks the
first free VM from a list. ITERM2_ONSCREEN_TESTS is
forwarded only when it is set here.

tools/vm/provision_test_vm.sh prepares a VM once: the same
Xcode as this Mac, its license and Metal toolchain, tmux,
ssh to itself, and no sleep, screen saver or crash dialogs.

2026-10-10: Keep Script Console entries alive while their
script is

A launched script's entry now follows its process, not just
its connection. It keeps observing API shutdown until the
process exits, and reconnecting keeps the launched PID.

Connections are counted per entry, so the entry stops only
when the last connection with its key closes. A rejected
request no longer overwrites the PIDs of an existing entry
or stops it.

The built-in entries (iTerm2 App, Automatic Profile
Switching, Dynamic Profiles, Smart Selection Actions, Web
Browser) never stop, so an API shutdown can no longer let
Clear Terminated remove them.

An entry is finished only when it is stopped and has no
launched process still alive. The 200-entry cap, Clear
Terminated, and the Clear Terminated button's enabled state
all use that, and the console is told when a stopped entry's
process exits.

2026-10-10: Update the Snippets tool's comments about
leaking reloads

Its reloads go through reloadPreservingExpansionAndScroll,
which now uses -it_reloadDataWithoutLeaking, so they no
longer strand disclosure buttons.  The comments also claimed
that -reloadItem:reloadChildren: leaks, which is only true
when reloading the nil item.

2026-10-10: Stop the job tree from leaking disclosure
buttons on every tab switch

The Jobs toolbelt tool sets a new pid on every tab switch,
and the job tree answered by discarding its root and calling
-reloadData, which strands a disclosure button and two
notification center registrations for every visible
expandable row. It did the same whenever the process info
for its pid was briefly unavailable. A memory graph of an
eight-day-old process had 14,034 stranded disclosure
buttons, nearly all from six job trees.

Use -it_reloadDataWithoutLeaking for every whole-tree
reload, and treat a changed root pid as a reload instead of
clearing the root first. When the process info is missing,
only reload if there was a root to remove.

2026-10-10: Stop a finished Script Console entry from
observing the API

Each history entry registers for the API server's message
notifications and for the API shutting down, and kept those
registrations until it was deallocated, long after its
script or connection had ended. That wasted notification
center space for every finished entry. Worse, on API
shutdown, apiDidStop: sent SIGKILL to the entry's pid even
if the process had exited long ago and the pid had been
reused.

Remove the registrations in stopRunning, and add
resumeRunning to restore them. A stopped entry can come
back: it2 over SSH shares one connection key per ssh
session, and a client may reconnect with its old key, so
connectionAccepted: finds the stopped entry for that key and
reuses it. It now resumes the entry, so its traffic is
logged again, and the entry counts as running so the cap on
finished entries can't evict it mid-connection.

When it resumes a stopped entry, connectionAccepted: also
takes the new connection's pids, since it may come from a
different process, and resumeRunning clears
terminatedByUser, which described the previous run.

Several connections can share a key at once: a script can
open more than one, and a reconnecting client can be
accepted before the server posts its old connection's close.
So entries count their open connections and stop only when
the last one closes; otherwise a connection that was still
open would stop being logged and could be evicted by the
cap. Stopping for any other reason, like the API shutting
down, resets the count.

2026-10-10: Cap the Script Console's finished entries at 200

Every API connection adds a Script Console history entry,
and nothing removed finished ones unless the user clicked
Clear Terminated. Clients like it2 connect once per command,
so a long-running process accumulated them without bound: a
memory graph of an eight-day-old process held 62,749
entries. Each one also kept three notification center
registrations, which made every observer removal in the app
slower, and lookups by identifier scan the whole list.

Keep at most 200 finished entries, dropping the oldest when
a new entry is added. Running entries are never dropped.

Also ignore adding an entry that's already in the list. The
connection rejected path and the script launcher's exception
path re-added existing entries, so they appeared twice.

2026-10-10: Add an NSOutlineView reload that doesn't leak
disclosure buttons

Reloading a whole NSOutlineView, with -reloadData or
-reloadItem:nil reloadChildren:YES, permanently strands a
disclosure button for every visible expandable row, and each
stranded button leaves two registrations in the default
notification center. That slows every view teardown in the
app as it grows. The leak is AppKit's; reloading individual
items and the batch insert/remove API don't trigger it.

-it_reloadDataWithoutLeaking replaces every top-level row
through the batch API, then restores expansion, selection,
and the scroll position. It counts the old top-level rows
from the view itself because -numberOfChildrenOfItem: asks
the data source, which may already describe the new model.
In a harness with a nested tree, 200 reloads held steady at
20 disclosure buttons where -reloadData reached 2986.

It differs from -reloadData in a few ways. Expansion is
matched by item, and an expanded item under a collapsed
parent is remembered rather than forgotten. Selection
follows the selected items rather than their row indexes,
falling back to the row indexes when no selected item can be
found, as when items are recreated without isEqual:. The
delegate is told about the restored selection and expansion.

reloadPreservingExpansionAndScroll, used by the Snippets
tool, now reloads this way. Also correct the reproducer's
note, which said any -reloadItem:reloadChildren: leaks; only
reloading the nil item does.

2026-10-10: Improve clipboard image pasting and upload
performance over SSH

From PR #745:

- SSH Integration uploads send 16 KiB chunks instead of 1
KiB, about three
  times faster. Cancelling cleans up the remote temporary
  file and no longer leaves a finished transfer stuck in
  "Cancelling...".
- Pasted clipboard images in formats agents may not read
(such as TIFF
  screenshots) are converted to PNG, and each gets a unique
  filename so uploads from different temporary directories
  cannot collide remotely.
- "Upload and Paste Path" uses the tmux gateway's SSH
Integration connection
  in tmux integration panes, and falls back to the
  conductor's host and home directory when shell integration
  has not reported a host or directory.

Changes made while rebasing onto master:

- Master had rewritten upload-and-paste to handle several
files at once,
  using a generation counter to ignore completions after a
  cancel and pasting where each file actually landed. Kept
  that design and dropped the PR's single-upload identifier,
  which it made redundant.
- Moved the PR's check that nothing is pasted if the session
exited or its
  host or ssh connection changed during the upload into
  -finishUploadAndPasteWithRemotePaths:destination:conductor:.
- That host check no longer requires the old and new host
names to match
  exactly. It also accepts two names the conductor
  recognizes as its own host (the ssh alias, its ssh config
  HostName, or hostname -f), so a host that shell
  integration reports under its full name mid-upload no
  longer silently suppresses the paste.
- Merged the PR's upload availability into master's
  -nonTextPasteHelperDestination:. Upload is offered only
  when there is a destination path, and a tmux gateway's
  conductor counts as SSH Integration.
- Release notes moved into master's Improvements and Bug
Fixes sections,
  leaving out items master already covered.

2026-10-10: List OSC 7501 tasks in Session Status and notify
per task

A session whose program reports more than one OSC 7501
record now lists up to four of them under its row in the
Session Status tool, most urgent first by the user's status
priorities, with a "+N" row for the rest.  Each child row
shows its label (the record's path, using reported titles),
status, progress and message. The session row drops its
detail while the records set its status, since a child
carries it.

With merged workgroups, every member's records are listed
under the one row, prefixed with the member's name, and
members without records appear by their plain status.
Clicking a child reveals the peer it came from, and the
selection follows the active session in both directions.
Snoozing a child row snoozes only its peer; snoozed peers'
children dim and sort last.

Each record the program moves into a state whose priority
entry notifies posts its own notification, rate-limited per
session: the first goes out at once, later ones are held
newest per record and sent together when the interval ends,
and held ones are dropped once their record leaves a
notifying state. Changes the records make to the tab status
no longer also post the session-level notification.

translations to match their changed English, fix missing

model labels), and add zh-Hans to the crash reporter.

2026-10-09: Support the Program Status Protocol (OSC 7501)
and add done/error statuses

OSC 7501 lets a program report that it is idle, working,
blocked on the user, done, or failed, as records keyed by
id. iTerm2 validates each report against the spec's grammar
and limits, answers the feature query, keeps up to 256
records per session, and shows the most urgent one as the
session's tab status. Done and error records survive the
next prompt and process exit and turn idle when the user
types in the session. Records also drive the progress bar,
without starting or ending OSC 9;4 operations, and give back
any 9;4 bar they only paused or reddened.

Session Status gains "done" and "error". The default
priority order is now waiting, done, error, working, idle,
and saved lists get the two new patterns below waiting with
idle's notify setting. Typing in a session showing done or
error turns it idle without posting a notification.
cc-status reports a finished Claude Code or Codex turn as
done and an API failure as error. Workgroups and the
orchestrator treat both as idle.

Adds a paused-indeterminate progress state, drawn as a still
yellow band in the session and a pause glyph in the tab. OSC
9;4;4 over a spinner and a blocked record now use it instead
of a yellow bar at 10%.

2026-10-09: Play custom bell sounds as alerts through System
Sound Services

NSSound plays at full volume on the default output device
and ignores the Flash the screen when an alert sound occurs
accessibility setting, so a custom bell sound behaved
differently from NSBeep(). Play it with
AudioServicesPlayAlertSound instead, which follows the alert
volume, the Play sound effects through device, and the flash
setting. Playing a sound that is still playing restarts it,
so a burst of bells still sounds like a burst.

System sounds are created from files, so a name is resolved
to a file the way NSSound(named:) does it, and looked up
again if that file changes or the Sounds folders do. This
also keeps the bell from changing the shared NSSound that
mark alerts play.

tests/bell_alert_sound.swift compares the ways of playing a
file by hand.

2026-10-09: Let each profile choose the sound its bell makes

Adds a Bell sound setting to Settings > Profiles > Terminal.
It can be the system alert sound (the default), a sound
installed in a Sounds folder, or any audio file. Issue 2635.

From GitHub PR 761.

2026-10-08: Skip tests that put windows on screen unless
ITERM2_ONSCREEN_TESTS=1

Tests built on TerminalWindowTestFixture make a terminal
window key and order it front in every setUp, the find
cursor tests left a screen-sized borderless window up for
the rest of the run, and a few others order windows or
alerts on screen. All of that gets in the way of using the
machine while the suite runs.

OnscreenTestGate skips them by default. To run them, set
TEST_RUNNER_ITERM2_ONSCREEN_TESTS=1. tearDown methods
tolerate a skipped setUp so they don't restore preferences
they never saved.

2026-10-08: Add --format to it2 session list. Issue 13088.

it2 session list --format takes an interpolated string,
evaluated in each session's context, such as
'\(session.path)\t\(session.jobName)'. It prints one line
per session in place of the usual columns, or with --json
adds the result to each session as "formatted". Default
output is unchanged.

A new built-in function, iterm2.interpolate(string:),
returns its argument; since a string argument is an
interpolated string, invoking it in a session evaluates the
format there. it2 probes for it up front so an older iTerm2
fails cleanly. A session whose evaluation fails gets an
empty string with a note on stderr, a format that fails in
every session is an error, and a transport failure
mid-listing ends the listing rather than blanking the
remaining sessions. APIClient.invoke now throws
IT2Error.functionFailed for errors the function itself
reports so callers can tell them apart from transport
errors.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-08: Tag AI tab-title corpus records by session and
keep reworded titles

Corpus records now carry an opaque sessionToken (truncated
SHA-256 of the session GUID) so the grader can measure churn
within a single tab instead of mixing interleaved records
from concurrent tabs. The capture script writes the same
token and the corpus grader prints a per-tab churn summary.

A regenerated title with the same words as the applied one,
ignoring case and order ("Deploy app" for "Deploy App", "Zsh
Update" for "Update Zsh"), no longer renames the tab. Issue
12971

2026-10-08: Remove obsolete macOS 12.x compatibility code
(#738) 2026-10-07: Keep session mentions linkable when
markdown pairs their underscores

Two stableIDs in one chat message (@ptys_A... and
@ptys_B...) put an underscore before each body, which
markdown treated as an emphasis pair and consumed, so the
ids rendered as dead ptysXXXX text. Mentions are now swapped
for alphanumeric placeholders before the markdown pass and
restored afterwards, on both the Mac and the Companion app.
Code blocks, indented code lines, inline code, link
destinations, and URLs are left alone so a placeholder never
leaks into a copy button or a link target.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-07: Make Voice Control corrections replace text
instead of appending

Voice Control corrects a word by selecting it through
accessibility and then inserting the new text with no
replacement range, expecting the insertion to replace the
selection. iTerm2 ignored the selection, so replace world
with there produced worldthere. Now, when an insertion
arrives outside a keypress and the selection was set by
accessibility, iTerm2 backspaces over the selected text
first. It does so only when the selection ends at the cursor
and soft alternate screen mode is off; otherwise the
insertion is dropped.

Also fix insertText:replacementRange:, whose backspaces went
through -doCommandBySelector: and were dropped unless
experimental key handling or the accent menu was on.
Backspaces are now sent directly, counted in characters
rather than UTF-16 code units, and read across soft-wrapped
rows. In soft alternate screen mode they are still sent only
when the accent menu or experimental key handling is on, as
before.

Text for these counts comes from a new
-screenTextInNSRange:, split out of
attributedSubstringForProposedRange:actualRange:, which
doesn't clamp to the range length since a cell can hold
several UTF-16 code units.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-07: Leave cc-status hook commands that also run the
user's programs

Uninstall removed, and Reinstall rewrote, any hook entry
whose command mentioned a cc-status path anywhere. A command
like `~/.config/iterm2/cc-status && say done` lost the rest
of its work, and `echo done > /tmp/cc-status` was mistaken
for our hook. PR 781 reported the same over-matching while
fixing issue 13099.

CCStatusHookCommand.runsOnlyCCStatus decides whether running
cc-status is all a command does, apart from guards: [, [[,
test, true, false, :, exit, type, which, command -v, and
if/then/elif/else/fi. Guards may only redirect to /dev/null
or a file descriptor, and command substitution anywhere
outside the cc-status path counts as the user's code.
Special parameters such as "$@" and $? and plain $((...))
arithmetic do not. The lexer now treats redirection targets,
including bash's &>, as files rather than references to
cc-status.

Uninstall removes only such commands. Reinstall rewrites a
broken one, and next to a broken command that also runs
something else it adds a fresh cc-status entry instead.
After Uninstall, a warning lists any commands it left in
place, and the installed flag is set from the same check
reconcileHooksCache uses, so the menu doesn't change on the
next launch.

Co-authored-by: Heath Dutton <heathdutton@gmail.com>

2026-10-07: Archive to a default folder when none is set.
Issue 12980

Archive Sessions on Closure with an empty folder resolved
the archive path against the working directory, which is the
read-only root of the startup volume, so every archive
failed. Turning archiving on with an empty folder now fills
in ~/Library/Application Support/iTerm2/Archives.  Profiles
that already archive with no folder save there too, and
Settings shows the default as the field's placeholder and
checks it for the writable indicator without creating it.
Typed folders now have ~ expanded when saving, matching the
Settings check.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-07: Stop leaking the tmux window-change ignore
counter.

setCurrentWindow: incremented
_ignoreWindowChangeNotificationCount for every select-window
it sent, expecting one %session-window-changed echo for
each. tmux sends no notification when the window is already
current, so a redundant select left the count too high, and
it then swallowed the next real notification, such as
another client switching windows. iTerm2 kept showing the
old tab until the next change.

Send the select-window in a command list after a display of
the current window, and increment only when that window
differs from the target.  tmux runs a client's command list
without running other clients' commands in between, so the
check can't race with another client, and it holds back
notifications until the list's output is written, so the
echo always arrives after the increment. Both hold back to
tmux 1.8.

2026-10-07: Keep help popovers from clipping their last line

In a popover, the text view's clip view adopts 6pt top and
bottom content insets. Since the popover is sized to exactly
fit the text, that cut off the last line and let the text
scroll. Turn off the automatic insets, and don't rubber-band
when all the text fits.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-07: Turn off automatic spelling correction,
capitalization, and smart quotes and dashes

The text input system can read the text before the cursor in
the terminal, so these could rewrite what the user types, as
the period substitution did in issue 13123. Text replacement
stays on because it's useful in text views and the terminal
implements it separately.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-07: Turn off Add period with double-space in iTerm2.
Issue 13123.

Since PTYTextView started returning screen contents from
attributedSubstringForProposedRange: (for Voice Control),
NSTextInputContext's double-space period substitution can
see the space before the cursor and replaces it with ". ".
In vim the period repeats the last command.
NSTextInputTraits doesn't control this, so override
NSAutomaticPeriodSubstitutionEnabled alongside the other
system preference overrides.

2026-10-07: Move the menu bar item to Settings and show a
bug on evaluation errors

The menu bar item is now configured in Settings > Appearance
> General with a Show Menu Bar Icon checkbox and a text
field for the interpolated string, replacing the
MenuBarItemString advanced setting. While iTerm2 is excluded
from the Dock the checkbox is shown as on and disabled,
since the icon is the only way back to Settings; the saved
value comes back once iTerm2 is in the Dock again.
MenuBarItemDrawsBadge stays an advanced setting.

The string is evaluated strictly so a reference to an
undefined variable is an error rather than an empty string.
When evaluation fails the item shows a ladybug and its menu
gains a Show Menu Bar Item Error item that explains the
failure.

2026-10-07: Menu bar status item for busy AI agents

Adds iterm2.workingSessionCount and
iterm2.waitingSessionCount application variables, counted
from sessions whose status text is exactly "working" or
"waiting" (what the cc-status hooks write).

A new menu bar item shows an interpolated string evaluated
in the global scope, the same way badges and the status bar
component do. The MenuBarItemString advanced setting holds
the string, and an empty string means no item.
MenuBarItemDrawsBadge (off by default) draws a one-character
result inside the square icon instead of next to it, with 0
or empty showing the plain icon and + for 10 or more. The
icon shown when iTerm2 is hidden from the Dock now uses the
same status item and behaves as before.

2026-10-07: Report BDSM as permanently reset when
right-to-left support is off (issue 13102)

With the global right-to-left text setting off nothing is
ever reordered, so DECRQM for mode 8 now answers 4
(permanently reset) instead of the escape sequence's state,
which would misleadingly report implicit mode.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-07: Honor tmux new-window -d in tmux integration.
Issue 13112.

A window that tmux created on its own always opened as the
selected tab, so `tmux new-window -d` still took focus.
%window-add does not say whether -d was used, but tmux
reports whether the new window became the session's current
window: without -d, %session-window-changed for the new
window arrives just before its %window-add, and the
window_active field in the display reply we already send
says the same.

TmuxController now tracks tmux's current window from that
ordered stream. It records every %session-window-changed
before the ignore counter can drop it, and it uses
window_active only when the reply describes our session,
since a window may be linked into several. The window opener
decides at tab-creation time and opens tmux-created windows
in the background unless tmux reports them as current. The
tmuxWindowsOpenInBackground advanced setting still forces
background.

2026-10-07: Remove a leftover merge conflict marker from the
release notes

2026-10-07: Substitute the user's name and email placeholder
in the crash report message again

The localization change renamed the tokens to {USERNAME} and
{EMAIL} in code, but the crash report template in the xib
still used %%LONGUSERNAME and %%EMAILADDRESS, so they were
shown raw.

2026-10-07: Keep Session Note open when shown from Open
Quickly

When Open Quickly invoked Show Session Note, the terminal
window became key after the note took focus.
windowDidBecomeKey did not treat the note's text view as a
preferred first responder, so it moved focus back to the
session, which ended editing and hid the empty note.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-07: Let a manual AI model choose which API key it
sends

A manual model authorized with whichever vendor key its name
and URL suggested, so a model named gemini/gemma-4-31b-it on
an Open WebUI server asked for a Gemini key even though the
user had an OpenAI key (issue 13105).

The manual model editor gains an API key setting: Automatic
(the default, unchanged inference, now showing what it
resolves to), a specific vendor's stored key, a key stored
for that model alone in the keychain, or none. An explicitly
chosen vendor key is sent even to a local endpoint.
Configurations saved before this read as Automatic.

The chat controller and the checks that run before a chat
exists (Explain, Codecierge, the command safety check,
Companion setup) now resolve the key through one shared
function, so they agree on whether the default model can
authorize and which vendor to prompt for.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-07: Hotkey window: skip the resign-active wait when
nothing could flash

On macOS 26 the hotkey window waits for iTerm2 to resign
active before ordering out, to keep a background iTerm2
window from flashing to the front. When there is no such
window the wait does nothing useful, and in practice the
resign-active notification often doesn't arrive before the
0.3s fallback fires. Meanwhile the invisible hotkey window
stays key and swallows keystrokes meant for the previous
app. Order out immediately when no other visible window on
the active space could be promoted. Issue 11372.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-07: Split environment entries on a literal '='
(#789)

-[iTermFileDescriptorMultiClientChild
initWithReport:thread:] and +[PTYTask
mutableEnvironmentDictionary] found the '=' in KEY=value
with -rangeOfString:, which compares composed character
sequences. When the value starts with a combining mark the
'=' and the mark form one sequence, so the search fails.
ITERM_PROFILE carries the profile name, so a profile named
e.g. U+20E4 hit assert(false) in initWithReport.  That
crashed iTerm2 when the session launched and again on every
relaunch while iTermServer still reported the child.

Use NSLiteralSearch so the split happens at the first '='
code unit.

Co-authored-by: Vlad Orlenko
<27573+orlenko@users.noreply.github.com> Co-authored-by:
Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com> 2026-10-07: Tell the
TUI safety check which session it is judging and when
watches fire

The screen-aware keystroke check held a plain message the
user dictated for another coding agent. Haiku could not tell
the screen belonged to the session named in the transcript,
so it worried the text would go to the wrong place, and it
never saw the watch the request was conditioned on fire,
because watcher events were dropped from the safety
transcript.

- Name the target session outside the screen fence and say
the keystroke
  is typed there; mark a trailing send_text row as the
  pending action.
- Tell the model the destination is fixed, and to judge text
typed into a
  coding agent by what it asks the agent to do.
- Project watcher events into the safety transcript as
iTerm2 event rows,
  built only from iTerm2-controlled fields (no role name or
  detail).
- Add the real failing case and an unsafe twin to the TUI
eval fixtures.

2026-10-06: Add floating panes

A floating pane floats over a tab's split panes. It can be
moved, resized, raised, hidden, maximized, docked into the
split panes, and dragged to other tabs and windows. Floats
are saved with window arrangements and session restoration,
can be created and controlled from the Python API, it2,
AppleScript, key bindings and pointer actions, and work in
tmux integration, which shows tmux's own floating panes.

This squashes the floating-panes branch. See that branch for
the individual commits.
