2026-10-06: Warn when another instance is using the same
settings domain

A process-lifetime flock in $TMPDIR keyed by -suite (or the
bundle ID when there is no suite) detects a second instance.
It shows an iTermWarning offering to quit and switch to the
running copy, or to launch anyway.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-06: Make offline shell integration install work
without bash

The offline installer launched bash to get a Bourne shell
with known prompts, but bash isn't installed by default on
FreeBSD or OpenBSD.  When it was missing, the commands ran
in the user's shell, and tcsh never terminates <<'EOF' on a
plain EOF line, so the install hung.

Use POSIX sh as the helper shell instead, replace echo -n
(which macOS /bin/sh prints literally) when picking the bash
dotfile, and fall back to b64decode -r when base64 is
unavailable (OpenBSD).

2026-10-06: Use a secure text field for the AI API key
prompt

The registration sheet that asks for an AI provider's API
key used a plain text field, so the key was visible as you
typed it.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-06: CLAUDE.md: use iTermWarning rather than NSAlert
so the phone can answer alerts

2026-10-06: Keep verified OSC 7 locality from being
overridden by stale third-party reports

A third-party precmd hook (e.g. oh-my-zsh's
omz_termsupport_cwd) can send a tokenless OSC 7 right after
shell integration's verified one. When the shell's $HOST is
stale, that report names a host we don't recognize and
turned a local session remote, so file paste offered only
upload actions.

- Ignore a tokenless OSC 7 that arrives after a verified
report and before the
  first of 133;B, 133;C, or input written to the pty. A
  report from a remote shell reached by plain ssh always
  comes after input, so it is still honored.
- Notify the session when only a host's locality or its
verification changes,
  not just its hostname.
- Record whether a host's locality was proven (ssh
integration, machineID
  token) or inferred from the hostname, and persist it.
- When remoteness is only inferred, the file and image paste
dialogs also
  offer to paste a local path, after the upload actions.

Issue 13117.

2026-10-06: Restore the braces lost rebasing the pairing
controller onto master

Two conflict resolutions in the rebase of the companion
modal-alert work dropped closing braces, and the park
supervisor's fault event now reports through reportFailure,
which master added meanwhile.

2026-10-06: Refuse a second answer to an alert that was
already answered, and stop storing room secrets at unpair

An alert that blocks stays registered, and in front, until
its modal run loop ends, and a second remote press in that
window would override the first. The registry now marks an
alert answered after an accepted press and refuses later
ones. A press the alert itself refused leaves it open to
another.

Unpairing deletes the stored room secret and then says
goodbye to the phone a moment later. The link is now told to
stop storing room secrets before any key material is
deleted; the store and that flag share a lock, so a store
already under way finishes first.

2026-10-06: Fix review findings in how the phone answers and
tracks Mac alerts

The card names the alert it drew, and an answer or Not now
for an alert the Mac has since replaced is dropped rather
than applied to the new one.

The wait that follows an answer belongs to that answer: it
is numbered, cancelled by the next answer, and cancelled
when the alert goes away, so a leftover wait from an earlier
answer cannot cut a later one short.

Clearing the Mac's status when a connection ends no longer
forgets which alerts were set aside with Not now; the same
alert reported again after a reconnect stays set aside.

The status is also cleared when a reconnect attempt tears
down or replaces its connection, and the liveness probe's
verdict is ignored if the connection it pinged has been
replaced since.

A rejected answer clears when anything in the Mac's status
changes, including whether it is blocked, not only the list
of alerts.

2026-10-06: Hide buttons that only make sense at the Mac
from the phone

Every place a warning is shown was read for buttons whose
effect is to open something on the Mac that the companion
app cannot see or operate: a file panel, Finder, Settings or
another window, a web page, or a session revealed on the
Mac's screen. Those 49 buttons are now marked as not offered
remotely. On the Mac nothing changes.

To make that a one-token change at a call site, the actions
array of the showWarningWithTitle: methods accepts an
iTermWarningAction in place of a label, and
+[iTermWarningAction localOnlyActionWithLabel:] makes one.

A button stays visible when hiding it would leave the phone
no way to dismiss the alert, or when it opens a terminal
window, which the phone can see and drive.

2026-10-06: Fix review findings in how warnings are
published and answered

A sheet no longer counts as showingWarning. That flag pauses
redraws in every window and keeps the hotkey window from
hiding, and many alerts that used to be plain sheets are now
warnings.

A remote answer sets the warning's own "don't ask again" box
both ways, so a box ticked at the Mac is not honored when
the phone did not ask for the choice to be remembered.

The "Advanced" button on the paste warnings is not offered
to the phone: it opens a panel on the Mac. Actions can be
marked this way by label.

A warning can mark its text as secret to keep it out of
logs. The reveal-password alert does.

2026-10-06: Refuse scroll-wheel reports during a stall and
deliver stream acks off the main actor

A scroll over a program that takes mouse reports is written
to the terminal as input, so like keys it is refused while
the main thread is blocked instead of being replayed when
the alert closes.

Stream acknowledgements now go from the link straight to the
streamer.  The streamer stops emitting frames once it is
about half a second ahead of the last one, so waiting for
the main actor stalled the video for as long as an alert had
the main queue frozen.

2026-10-06: Act only on the phone's current connection, and
close ones it gives up on

Events and close notifications are ignored unless they come
from the connection in use. A connection the liveness probe
or the connection check declares dead is closed, so its
pending requests fail at once and its late close cannot tear
down the connection that replaced it.

When the probe finds the connection dead while a reconnect
is under way, clear the Mac status and close that
connection. The reconnect was waiting on a request held
because the Mac said it was blocked; this fails it so the
reconnect tries again.

2026-10-06: Remove the debug menu items for freezing the
main queue

They existed to test the companion app against a modal that
freezes the main queue while that work was in progress.

2026-10-06: Don't report a cancel after an accepted
natural-language query prompt

After OK, the prompt's completion was called with the text
and then again with nil. The only caller ignores nil, so
nothing visible went wrong, but a completion should be
called once.

2026-10-06: Convert most plain NSAlerts to iTermWarning so
the phone can answer them

About 125 alerts across the app become iTermWarnings, which
publish themselves to the companion app. On the Mac each
keeps its text, its buttons in the same order, what each
button does, whether it blocks, and its parent window.
Prompts with a plain text field publish it as a remote input
and take focus through initialFirstResponder instead of
laying out the alert and setting first responder by hand.

Left as plain alerts: those with custom controls (combo
boxes, popups, profile lists, a progress indicator, a
multi-line text view), those shown during launch,
termination or when the app bundle is damaged, the title
prompts that set the alert window's appearance,
script-defined alerts with an unbounded number of buttons,
the account picker, the expression editor, the AI API key
sheet, and the debug logging toggles.

2026-10-06: Add a debug menu item that shows the password
prompt from a main-queue block

For testing the companion app against a password prompt that
has the main queue frozen. Afterwards a warning reports what
the prompt received.

2026-10-06: Let the phone answer the password prompt and the
scp password prompt

Both can come up with nobody at the Mac: the password prompt
when a trigger opens the password manager, the scp prompt
some time after a transfer starts. Both are now
iTermWarnings that publish the password as a secret input.
The password prompt also publishes its user name field and
its Remember checkbox. The Password Manager button on each
opens a window on the Mac, so it is not offered to the
phone.

The password prompt's accessory is a stack view laid out by
constraints, which has no frame until it is in a window, so
it goes in a container with a real frame for iTermWarning to
size.

2026-10-06: Let the phone enter secrets, set an accessory
checkbox, and skip Mac-only buttons

Three additions to what a warning can publish for the
companion app:

A secret input, for a password field. The phone shows an
obscured field.  What the Mac's field holds is never
published: the remote input, the registry and the link each
send an empty value. A secret the phone leaves blank is not
sent, so the Mac's field keeps what it has.

A remote checkbox: a checkbox in the accessory that the
phone shows and sets the way it does a warning's own "don't
ask again" box. The alert carries whether the box starts
checked.

An action can be marked as not offered remotely, for a
button that only makes sense at the Mac. It stays in the
list so indexes match, the phone does not show it, and an
answer that names it is refused.

2026-10-06: Only interrupt the phone for alerts that block
the Mac or concern the session on screen

An alert being up no longer counts as the Mac being blocked.
When an alert that can block the app appears, the stall
monitor probes the main queue at once and reports a stall
after 0.3 s instead of 2 s. Keys from the phone are refused
only during an actual stall, and the phone holds its request
timeouts only then.

Warnings shown over a terminal window carry the stable IDs
of the sessions in that window's current tab (or one session
named by the caller). The phone shows the full card when the
Mac is blocked or when the alert is about the session on
screen, and the pill otherwise. The session list marks
sessions with an alert waiting.

The video frame timer now runs in common modes so live video
continues while an alert is up.

2026-10-06: Fix a crash listing sessions for the phone when
a tab has no title yet

PTYTab.title returns nil for a tab that was just inserted,
and the session lister runs from the notification that the
number of tabs changed. Opening a tab with a phone connected
could crash. Send "Tab N" for such a tab, as an untitled
window is sent as "Window N".

2026-10-06: Let the phone fill in the values a warning asks
for

Two warnings ask for input in an accessory view: pasting
text with tabs (the tab size in spaces) and adding a Python
script dependency (its name). The phone could see and answer
them but not see or change the value, so answering meant
confirming contents the user had not seen.

A warning can now describe such controls with remoteInputs:
free text backed by a text field, or a whole number with a
range. The alert registry and the mac status message carry
each input's label, kind, current value, and range. iTerm2
Buddy shows a field for each on the alert card and sends the
values back with the answer. Before clicking the button the
Mac checks every value and then puts each into its real
control, as if typed there. If any value is unacceptable the
press is refused and no control changes.

Declaring inputs says they are everything in the accessory
that matters to the answer, so the phone no longer says more
is shown on the Mac.

The card's window takes key status when it has an input,
since a text field only gets the keyboard in the key window,
and gives it back when it goes away.

This extends companion protocol revision 14, which has not
shipped. The input kind is a string so a later Mac can add
kinds without breaking this phone.

2026-10-06: Fix review findings in the companion modal-alert
work

Unpairing left the Mac's status in place in iTerm2 Buddy, so
the alert card stayed up over the pairing scanner and held
timeouts and the liveness probe outlived the pairing. Both
unpair paths now clear it.

A warning shown as a non-blocking sheet stayed in the alert
registry forever if its parent window closed first, because
AppKit does not call the sheet's completion handler then. It
now unregisters when the parent closes.

Park supervisor events are read on the main actor after a
delay, so one from a run that had since been stopped could
be applied after a newer start: marking the supervisor
inactive while it parked, or installing a bridge for a link
nobody watched. Each command now has a generation, events
carry it, and the controller drops events from any other.

A press on a disabled or hidden button was reported as a
success. It is now refused, and the phone's "sending" state
gives the buttons back after ten seconds if the Mac never
acts.

A sheet registered after an alert that blocks the app became
the one answerable alert, though the blocking alert was
still in front. Sheets are now kept behind every app-modal
alert.

The stall monitor started measuring a second after a phone
connected and reported only as of its last tick. It now
starts at once and answers as of the current time, so a
phone connecting during a freeze is told sooner.

The link acted on an alert answer from a phone that had not
completed hello at a revision that carries the status, and
forwarded keystrokes and pastes during a freeze, to be typed
into the session when the alert was dismissed. Such answers
are now ignored and such input refused.

Also: one factory for the production link, one shared weak
observer list for the registry and the monitor, and a header
comment that claimed an opt-out no call site uses.

2026-10-06: Show and answer the Mac's alerts in iTerm2 Buddy

The phone side of companion protocol revision 14. The Mac
now reports which modal alerts are showing and whether its
main thread is blocked, in its hello reply and in
macStatusChanged events.

An alert that blocks the Mac is shown as a card in front of
everything, in its own window so it appears over a presented
sheet: the Mac's heading and text, its buttons, a checkbox
for "don't ask again" when the alert has one, and Not now.
Setting it aside, or an alert that does not block the Mac,
leaves a pill in the banner area that reopens it. If the Mac
could not press the button, the card says to answer at the
Mac.  When the Mac is blocked by something the phone cannot
answer, a banner says so.

Requests that the Mac's main thread has to serve now hold
their timeouts while the Mac reports itself blocked, and get
the full timeout again each time it unblocks. Before, they
timed out against a Mac that was only waiting for an alert,
which tore down a healthy connection and looped
reconnecting. The handshake and the foreground connection
check stay strict. While requests are held, a liveness probe
pings every ten seconds, since the Mac answers pings without
its main thread, so a connection that really died is still
noticed.

Release notes for both apps.

2026-10-06: Tell the phone which alerts are showing and
whether the Mac is blocked

Companion protocol revision 14. The hello reply gains
macStatus: the modal alerts the phone may answer and whether
the Mac's main thread is blocked. It rides the reply, rather
than a following event, so the phone knows before it sends
requests that would time out. Changes afterwards go out as
macStatusChanged, always the full state. The phone presses a
button with answerModalAlert; if the Mac could not press it,
it sends modalAlertAnswerRejected and then its status again.

Everything is additive. The hello field is omitted for an
older phone, the new events are sent only to a phone at
revision 14 or later, and minimumPeer stays at 11.

CompanionLink subscribes to the alert registry and to a new
CompanionMainThreadMonitor, which posts a probe to the main
queue about once a second while a phone is connected and
reports the main thread blocked when the probe has not run
for two seconds. That covers what the registry cannot: a
plain NSAlert, an open panel, or a busy app. The monitor's
clock does not advance during sleep. An answer is handled
apart from the receive loop, so a press that cannot run yet
does not hold up other requests.

The pairing controller now skips link-up events for links
that have already closed. A phone that does not know to wait
reconnects over and over while the main actor is frozen, and
each attempt used to get its own short-lived bridge once it
thawed.

2026-10-06: Publish modal warnings to a registry that other
threads can answer

A modal alert started from a main-queue callout freezes the
main queue until it is dismissed. The companion app's
connection now survives that, but it had no way to see the
alert or press a button on it.

ModalAlertRegistry records the alerts on screen.
iTermWarning registers each alert just before its modal loop
starts and unregisters as soon as the loop ends, for
runModal, the sheet form, and runModalAsync. Other threads
read a lock-protected copy of the list, and observers are
told synchronously, so both are current before the main
queue can freeze.

An answer is delivered to the main thread through a run loop
block in the common modes, which still runs inside such a
modal loop. It is refused if the alert is no longer the
newest one, the index is out of range, or some other modal
session is in front, since a click would then end that
session instead. Pressing a button checks the suppression
box only when the warning shows one and the action may be
remembered.

A warning can opt out with remotelyAnswerable. Nothing reads
the registry yet.

For tests, iTermWarning gains a headless mode: it builds its
alert, never shows it, and blocks in a nested modal-mode run
loop until a button is clicked programmatically.

2026-10-06: Let a paired phone reconnect while a modal
freezes the main queue

Parking in the relay room, accepting the phone, the Noise
handshake, and re-parking after a drop all ran on the main
actor, driven by the bridge's onClose. With a modal alert up
that was started from a main-queue callout, the Mac never
noticed a dropped connection and never parked again, so
iTerm2 Buddy could not get back in until the alert was
dismissed.

CompanionParkSupervisor, an actor, now does this for an
established pairing: park, accept, handshake, check the
pinned phone key, start a CompanionLink, and park again when
that link drops. The pairing controller sends it start,
adopt, and stop commands through a synchronous call and
reads its events on the main actor. Fresh pairing (QR and
SAS entry) stays on the main actor and hands its live link
to the supervisor once it succeeds.

A connecting phone couriers the relay room secret before its
hello and waits for the ack, so the link now answers that
message too. It skips the keychain write when the secret is
the one already held.

The retry policy is a pure function with its own tests. A
live connection that ends because it was displaced or
evicted by a reshard now waits before re-parking, the same
as a park that ends that way; before, only a quota teardown
waited.

ModernTests now links iTermSwiftPackages, so tests can use
the Companion protocol types and run a real Noise handshake.
The in-memory transport moves from the app target to the
tests.

2026-10-06: Keep the companion connection alive while a
modal freezes the main queue

A modal alert started from a main-queue callout (a
@MainActor task or a DispatchQueue.main.async block) stops
the main dispatch queue and every @MainActor job until it is
dismissed. CompanionHostBridge is @MainActor and did all of
its own network I/O, so iTerm2 Buddy went dead for as long
as such an alert was up: even its foreground ping timed out.

CompanionLink, an actor, now owns the transport, the receive
loop, and the outbox drain. It answers hello and ping itself
and forwards every other message to the bridge in wire order
through an event stream, so the bridge's handlers are
unchanged and simply lag during a freeze.  Main-actor code
reaches the link only through synchronous nonisolated calls,
never through a Task that would start on the frozen actor.

Classification for the presence warning moves from the
messagesSince and syncSince handlers to one pure function
applied before dispatch.  The link also drops pushes queued
for a version-blocked peer, except the unpair farewell.

Tests use a harness that spins a modal-panel run loop inside
a main-queue callout and asserts the freeze really happened,
plus an in-memory transport. That transport lives in the app
target because ModernTests cannot link CompanionProtocol.

Development builds get three menu items that show a modal
from a main-queue callout, for testing this by hand.

2026-10-06: Preserve -suite when relaunching after importing
settings

2026-10-05: Keep a symlinked settings.json when writing the
Claude Code hook. Issue 13098 (#775)

Install, Reinstall, and Uninstall of the Claude Code
Integration wrote settings.json with an atomic write to its
unresolved path. An atomic write renames a temporary file
over the path it is given, so when settings.json was a
symlink into a dotfiles repo (GNU Stow, chezmoi), the rename
replaced the link with a regular file. The link's target
kept its old contents, Claude Code read the new copy from
then on, and the copy did not inherit the target's mode: a
0600 file came back as 0644 on macOS 26.7 (the issue reports
0755 on macOS 27), readable by other users, although
settings.json can hold API keys.

Both write sites now go through one helper that resolves
symlinks before the atomic write, so the rename happens next
to the real file.  The link survives, and the real file
keeps its mode. A dangling link resolves to itself, so
writing through one still replaces it, as before.

The new tests strip the hook through an absolute and a
relative (Stow-style) link and check that the link survives
with the same destination, that the target has the change,
and that the target is still 0600.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 (1M context)
<noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-05: Fix frame of views in
settings>general>ai>prompts

2026-10-05: Don't let a skipped nanny update swallow later
ones

dbf0fa199 made iTermTitlebarAccessoryNanny.update() return
without clearing needsUpdate when the window has no title
bar, meaning to stay pending. But requests only trigger
update() when needsUpdate goes from false to true, so with
it stuck at true every later add, remove, or min-height
change was a no-op, and updateIfNeeded() is only called from
a few places. A window that regained its title bar never got
its accessories installed or resized.

Clear needsUpdate when skipping. viewControllers and
pendingUpdates keep the desired state, so the next request
applies all of it. Latent today, since PseudoTerminal checks
for a title bar before using the nanny.

2026-10-05: Take the texture pool's lock when the power
manager empties it

iTermTexturePool requests and returns textures under
@synchronized(self) on the Metal queue, but
powerManagerMetalAllowedDidChange: emptied the array and
bumped the generation on the main thread without the lock.
Emptying the array between -requestTextureOfSize:'s count
check and its removal threw "removeObjectsInRange: range {0,
1} extends beyond bounds for empty array". Take the lock
there, and in stampTextureWithGeneration:, which reads the
generation and is also called from iTermPooledTexture
without it.

Not covered by a test: the race depends on thread timing.

2026-10-05: Skip smart selection rules that have no regex

Smart selection rules can come from hand-edited, dynamic, or
imported profiles. +[SmartSelectionController regexInRule:]
returned the value unchecked, so a rule without one passed
nil to RegexKitLite, which threw "The regular expression
argument is NULL." Return nil unless the regex is a
non-empty string, and have the text extractor, the context
menu, and PTYSession's combined smart selection regex skip
such rules.

2026-10-05: Keep Metal frame statistics alive as long as the
frames using them

iTermMetalGlue gave each frame's
iTermAttributedStringBuilder raw pointers into timing
statistics stored in the glue itself. Frames can outlive the
glue: a session that closes frees its glue while frames are
still in flight on the Metal queue, and building rows and
completing a frame write the builder's statistics. Those
writes went into freed memory, which can corrupt the heap or
whatever object reused it.

Move the statistics into a small object that the glue owns
and each builder retains through a new statsOwner property.
The frame does not retain the glue itself, so the glue's
last release can't move to the Metal queue.

2026-10-05: Don't unwrap a nil iTermController when
GlobalJobMonitor starts

GlobalJobMonitor's initializer picks up existing sessions
with iTermController.sharedInstance().allSessions().
sharedInstance() imports into Swift as an implicitly
unwrapped optional, and it is nil once
+releaseSharedInstance has run at termination. If a quit
(Cmd-Q, or a logout or restart Apple event) was handled
before launch finished, ClaudeWatcher.start() created the
monitor from applicationDidFinishLaunching with the
controller already gone, and the unwrap trapped. Treat a
missing controller as no sessions, as 88228e721 does for
sleep prevention.

Not covered by a test: reproducing it needs the app's shared
controller to be nil, and releasing it can't be undone in
the test host.

2026-10-05: Keep the grid size finite when a font measures
as zero

Adding a tab to a window sizes the new session from the
existing view's size divided by the profile's cell size.
TerminalWindowSizeHelper's
gridSize(forContentSize:cellSize:) divided by the raw cell
size, so a font that measures as zero made it infinite,
Int32(clamping:) turned that into Int32.max columns, and
allocating the grid's lines threw "-[NSConcreteMutableData
initWithLength:]: absurd length". Treat a cell as at least
one point, as the other sizing paths do, and never return
fewer than one row or column.

Also fix Int32(clamping: CGFloat), which compared against
Int.max and Int.min, so values between those and Int32's
limits reached Int32(value) and trapped.

2026-10-05: Don't let tab accessibility elements outlive
their cell unsafely

Each tab cell vends an accessibility element, plus one for
its close button, that refers back to the cell through an
assign property, and nothing cleared it when the cell was
freed. Assistive apps can keep an element after its tab
closes and then ask for its parent, which messaged the freed
cell and crashed in -[PSMTabAccessibilityElement
accessibilityParent]. Make the reference weak.

2026-10-05: Don't split a pane that left the window during
an async split

asyncSplitVertically: captures the target session and looks
up the working directory before it splits. If the target was
closed or moved to another window meanwhile (or there was no
current session, the "should be impossible" path, which
passes a nil target), the split fell back to the current tab
and PTYTab asserted that the target's view was in its split
tree.

Before creating the new session, require the target to be in
one of this window's tabs; otherwise log it and report
failure through the completion block.

2026-10-05: Don't trust value types in Dynamic Profiles
files

profilesInFile:fileType: assumed the types of the JSON or
property list it read and crashed on: a "Profiles" value
that is not an array, an entry that is not a dictionary, a
"Keyboard Map" that is not a dictionary (the deprecated key
mapping check subscripted it), and JSON null values (a null
parent name reached bookmarkWithName:, and any null made the
NSUserDefaults write throw "Attempt to insert non-property
list object").

Report a non-array "Profiles" and skip entries that are not
dictionaries. Treat null as absent by removing it throughout
each profile, drop and report a non-dictionary "Keyboard
Map", and ignore a parent name or GUID that is not a string.

2026-10-05: Fix a crash Cmd-hovering over a composer
autosuggestion

The hovered character index covers the prompt prefix, the
typed text, and the gray autosuggestion. To find the command
under it, characterRangeOfCommand(atCharacterIndex:) removes
the prefix with withoutPrefix, and clearing the prefix also
clears the suggestion. An index that was in the suggestion
was then past the end of the shorter text, and
paragraphRange(for:) threw "Range {17, 0} out of bounds;
string length 3". Return nil for an index past the end.

2026-10-05: Keep the titlebar accessory nanny away from
windows without a title bar

AppKit throws "titlebarAccessoryViewControllers not
supported for this window style" when you get or change a
window's titlebar accessories without
NSWindowStyleMaskTitled. On exiting Lion fullscreen,
PseudoTerminal restores the saved style mask, which can lack
it (seen with tmux integration windows), and then on macOS
26 calls forceReaddAll, which read the accessories and
crashed.

The nanny now only touches a window that has a title bar:
forceReaddAll and probation review do nothing otherwise, and
update() stays pending until the window has one.
PseudoTerminal already skipped the nanny for untitled
windows when managing the tab bar accessory.

2026-10-05: Don't load the Settings window when the app
hotkey is pressed

To decide whether the hotkey was pressed while the Settings
window's hotkey field had focus, the handler called
-[PreferencePanel window], which loads the Settings nib the
first time. If Settings was never loaded it can't be in use,
so check isWindowLoaded first. Besides being wasted work,
the forced load turned a damaged bundle (an old process
whose app was replaced on disk) into a crash on an unrelated
keystroke.

2026-10-05: Explain and exit instead of crashing on other
missing bundle resources

Several resources the app can't run without crashed when
they could not be loaded, which happens when the app's
bundle is moved, deleted, or replaced while it runs. Use
AppSignatureValidator.warnAndExit(reason:) for them, so the
user is told the app is damaged and it exits without a crash
report:

- iTermLocaleGuesser's encodings list and the triggers
status bar icon,
  which warned and then called it_fatalError.
- The default smart selection rules, which warned only when
the file
  was missing and then hit ITCriticalError.
- Browser page templates and their included files, which
called
  it_fatalError without explanation.
- iTermGraphicSource's bundled graphic maps, which passed a
nil path to
  NSData and raised.

warnAndExit can now be called from any thread: off the main
thread it hands the alert to the main queue and parks the
calling thread so it never continues without the resource.
It is exposed to Objective-C.

2026-10-05: Explain and exit when the AI model catalog can't
be loaded

AIModelCatalog asserted when neither the bundled
ai-models.json nor the downloaded cache could be read. That
happens when the app's bundle is moved or deleted while it
launches: for example, a Jamf App Installers staged copy
gets launched, the "Move to Applications folder?" prompt
holds up launch, and the installer removes the staged copy
before the user answers. The bundled catalog always ships,
so this means the app is damaged.

Now the catalog reports that it is unavailable, and launch
calls the new AppSignatureValidator.warnAndExit(reason:),
which tells the user the app is damaged and exits without
producing a crash report.

Also fix AppSignatureValidator's messages, which were
swapped: an official signature said it did not match the
official distribution, and any other team's signature was
called valid "against all odds". Remove a leftover print of
the signing information.

2026-10-05: Skip sockets when exporting all settings and
data

it2 over SSH creates live Unix domain sockets in
~/.iterm2/it2.  "Export All Settings and Data" copies
~/.iterm2, and deepCopyContentsOfDirectory called copyItem
on the socket, which can't be copied, so the whole export
failed with "Failed to copy file: "<hex>.sock" couldn't be
copied to "it2"."

Exclude ~/.iterm2/it2 from the export, and make both
deep-copy helpers skip sockets, FIFOs, and devices, which
are runtime state rather than data.

2026-10-05: Fix a crash importing all settings that change a
secure setting

ImportExport called
SecureUserDefaults.instance.deserializeAll(dict:), a
mutating method on the static `instance`, which held
exclusive access to it for the whole import. Writing a
changed secure setting posts secureUserDefaultDidChange
synchronously, and observers such as AIModelCatalogUpdater,
the companion pairing gate, and the composer read
SecureUserDefaults.instance, so Swift's exclusivity checker
trapped with "Simultaneous accesses".

serializeAll and deserializeAll are now static. They take
the list of settings with a brief access to `instance` and
read or write them with no access held, as setBatch already
does.

Adds SecureUserDefaultTestHooks.privilegedWriteRunner
(ITERM_DEBUG only) so tests can exercise the write path
without an administrator prompt.

2026-10-05: Fix a crash from NSFont keys in the Metal font
ID bijection

-[NSFont it_metalFontID] keyed a bijection by NSFont. A font
made by it_fontByAddingToPointSize: (Cmd-+ and Cmd--) is
-isEqual: to the same font made any other way, but hashes
differently, because CTFontCreateCopyWithAttributes with an
explicit matrix adds an identity matrix attribute to its
descriptor that -hash includes and -isEqual: ignores.
Lookups for the zoomed font usually missed, so it was linked
a second time under a duplicate key, and when that insert
grew the dictionary Swift trapped with
KEY_TYPE_OF_DICTIONARY_VIOLATES_HASHABLE_REQUIREMENTS.

The bijection now recognizes objects conforming to
BijectionIdentifiable, which supply their own identity, and
keys by it while still returning the linked objects.
NSFont's identity is its descriptor attributes, minus a
matrix attribute that is the identity transform, so equal
fonts share one ID and fonts that render differently
(feature settings, variations, a real matrix) keep their
own.

Computing that identity is slow, so the ID is now computed
once and carried with the font. PTYFontInfo computes its
font's ID when the font is set, and
iTermAttributedStringBuilder puts it in the attribute
dictionary as iTermMetalFontIDAttribute next to
NSFontAttributeName, so cheap and NSAttributedStrings both
carry it. The GPU renderer reads the stored ID, which also
removes a lookup it used to do for every glyph.  Only
CoreText runs that use a substituted fallback font still
call it_metalFontID, once per run. The bijection lock is now
an os_unfair_lock.

2026-10-05: Fix heap corruption from a stale find indicator
range

Selecting a find match computes the indicator range and
shows it later in a dispatch_async. If the session got
narrower in between, start.x was past the end of the line.
TextClipDrawing.drawClip copies each line at the current
width and blanks 0..<start.x with
copyByZeroingVisibleRange:, which wrote zeros past the end
of the malloced copy. Heap corruption crash reports have
zeroed free-block headers in whole 12-byte strides, matching
this write.

The indicator is now skipped if the width changed before it
is shown, drawClip refuses a range that starts past the
width, and the zeroing methods ignore cells outside the line
and RLog when asked to zero them.

2026-10-05: Fix crash loops in the window state database
from overlapping saves

A synchronous save (applicationWillTerminate) could run
while an async save was still pending. updateSynchronously:
rejected only async saves while updating, so the sync save
built its delta against the same self.record as the pending
save, and both inserted every node new since then. The
resulting duplicate sibling rows persisted in the database.

On every later launch, for arrays of more than 16 elements,
the delta encoder looked up the previous revision through an
index in which the last duplicate won, while the save
enumerator and restoration take the first. The mismatch
either skipped inserting new children, so the next save
threw MissingRowID and hit fatalError, or paired records
with different rowids and failed assert(before.rowid ==
after.rowid).

A sync save now waits for a pending save to finish and
publish its result before building its delta, so saves never
overlap. The index now keeps the first duplicate, which
stops the crash loop for databases that already contain
duplicates.

2026-10-04: Let undo close restore sessions from their
archives

When a profile archives sessions on close, a closed session
whose undo window runs out is archived and its undo close
entry keeps the archive's path. Undo restores it from the
archive in its original pane, tab, and window, and revives
any sessions in the group that are still alive. The restore
methods that revive sessions substitute each archived
session's arrangement into the saved layout. A session
restored this way reuses its archive if it is closed again.

The 20 most recent fully archived undo close entries are
saved in app restorable state and restored at launch. Their
tabs are identified by GUID and looked up when undone, since
windows may not be restored yet.  State is saved as soon as
an entry becomes fully archived and after an archived entry
is undone.

Also: - Give archives unique filenames so split panes with
the same name
  don't overwrite each other's archives.
- Don't warn that an archived session ended soon after
starting.  - At quit, stop restorable sessions once, before
saving state, without
  skipping entries; and don't record undo entries for
  windows closed while quitting, which re-archived their
  sessions.

2026-10-04: Stop old key bindings from hijacking the A key
in physical-key mode

Older versions stored some key bindings as
0xchar-0xmods-0x0, using keycode 0 to mean "unknown".
Keycode 0 is also kVK_ANSI_A, so with "Interpret key
bindings based on physical key" on, bindings like Forward
Delete fired when you pressed A, and Ctrl-A matched
Ctrl-Home, which wrongly triggered the offer to enable
physical-key matching.  Issue 13110.

- Keystrokes with a keycode now serialize as four parts
  (0xchar-0xmods-0xkeycode-0x1), meaning the keycode is
  real. Older versions parse the first three parts and
  ignore the fourth.
- When parsing a three-part key, keycode 0 is trusted only
if the
  character is one the A key can type: a/A, or what keycode
  0 types in an enabled keyboard layout. Otherwise the
  binding has no keycode and matches by character.
- A one-time migration rewrites three-part keys in the
global key map,
  profiles, and rewritable dynamic profiles, but only where
  the answer doesn't depend on the enabled layouts.
- Physical-key lookup scans the binding dictionary once and
falls back
  to keycode-less character matches.
- The Python API writes four-part keys and leaves old
keycode-0 keys
  unchanged so iTerm2 can interpret them.
- Fix dead-key handling in UCKeyTranslate calls that passed
the
  NoDeadKeys bit index instead of its mask, and stop reading
  an uninitialized buffer when no characters are produced.

2026-10-04: Fix histogram sampler weight overflow after
weeks of uptime. Issue 13103

The global Metal histograms live for the whole process and
merge every frame, so after weeks of uptime the sampler's
32-bit weight overflowed.  That corrupted the merge math and
tripped an assertion in merge_from.  Make the weight 64-bit,
draw reservoir indices with 64 bits of randomness once the
weight passes 2^32, and clamp the per-side merge counts so a
bad weight can never read past either vector or overfill the
reservoir.

2026-10-04: Keep it2 from relaunching iTerm2 while it quits

When iTerm2 quits, closing sessions hangs up their jobs, and
hooks run on the way out (Claude Code's SessionEnd runs
cc-status, which runs it2 set-status) connect to the API and
then request a cookie with an AppleScript tell. If iTerm2
has exited by the time the Apple event arrives, the tell
relaunches it. During a macOS restart, loginwindow then
quits that short-lived instance and its empty window list
replaces the saved state, so nothing is restored at the next
login.

it2 now asks for a cookie only if iTerm2 is running, as the
Python library has always done. iTerm2 also stops accepting
new API socket connections once a quit is confirmed, so
those hooks fail at connect instead of reaching osascript.
Existing connections are unaffected.

Issue 13106

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-04: Fix crash when a new tab joins the current tab's
group

iTermApplicationDelegate.m is not ARC, so the didMakeSession
block literal in -newTabAtIndex: was never copied to the
heap. Its storage ended with the enclosing if statement, and
iTermSessionLauncher later copied the dead stack block,
crashing in objc_copyWeak. Copy it explicitly. Issue 13107.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-04: Make live harness tab title corpus tests opt-in

The Apple Intelligence tests that walk the captured tab
title corpus make one or more on-device generations per
record, so with a real corpus a bare tools/run_ai_live.sh
ran one experiment for ten hours. Skip them unless
ITERM2_AI_LIVE_TAB_TITLE_CORPUS=1.

2026-10-04: Fix live AI harness failures caused by vendor
changes

- Attachment matrix: move the Gemini lane from
gemini-2.5-flash, which is
  now restricted to existing users and returns HTTP 500 for
  an inline DOCX, to gemini-3.5-flash-lite. Gemini 3.5 reads
  TIFF and DOCX and accepts ZIP and octet-stream without
  reading them, so re-baseline those cells.  Re-record the
  lane's cassettes and remove the orphaned 2.5 ones.
- Audio transcription: gpt-audio now declines to say what a
clip contains,
  so use gpt-audio-1.5, which answers the probe for both mp3
  and wav.
- Legacy completions: OpenAI shut down every /v1/completions
model on
  2026-09-28. Exercise the legacy prompt request shape
  against Ollama's OpenAI-compatible /v1/completions
  instead, gated like the other Ollama tests.
- LLMProvider: drop a stale comment example claiming Gemini
rejects TIFF.

2026-10-04: Fix a race that could drop a new session's first
tokens

TwoTierTokenQueue initialized its per-priority queues with a
lazy var.  The reader thread appends tokens while the
mutation queue checks and executes them, and Swift lazy
initialization is not thread safe: when a session's first
append raced the first read, each thread could build its own
queues and tokens appended to the losing set were silently
lost.  Initialize the queues eagerly.

This surfaced as an intermittent TmuxKeyReportingModeTests
failure: turning on tmux mode schedules a paused side effect
whose unpause wakes the mutation queue at the moment the
test sends its first bytes. Add a direct regression test,
which lost the token in about 30% of races before the fix.

2026-10-04: Update the AI model catalog to current vendor
lineups

Rebuild ai-models.json (catalog version 4) from each
vendor's published model docs:

- OpenAI: add gpt-6-astra (now recommended), gpt-6.1-sol,
gpt-6-sol,
  gpt-6-luna (now the economy model), gpt-5.4-pro and
  gpt-5.4-mini. Drop o4-mini, which shuts down 2026-10-23.
  gpt-6-astra and gpt-6.1-sol reject reasoning effort
  "none", so they think at "low" when thinking is off.
  Correct gpt-4.1/gpt-4.1-mini limits and per-model tools
  and service tiers to match the model pages.
- Anthropic: add claude-opus-5-5 (now recommended),
claude-fable-5-1,
  claude-sonnet-5-5, claude-fable-5 and claude-opus-5, none
  of which accept temperature. Opus 4.6+ and Sonnet 4.6+
  have a 1M context window by default; Sonnet 4.6 and 5
  allow 128K output.
- Gemini: add gemini-3.8-flash (now recommended);
gemini-3.5-flash-lite
  is now the economy model.
- DeepSeek: deepseek-v4-flash is a legacy alias; use
deepseek-flash.

The new Claude models can decline at the API layer with
stop_reason "refusal", no content, and the reason in
stop_details. The parser ignored those fields, so a refusal
showed as a blank reply. Surface the explanation in both the
streaming and non-streaming parsers.

Capture refusal fixtures for the new models; gpt-6-astra,
gpt-6.1-sol and gpt-6-sol block the refusal prompt at HTTP
400 and are fixture-exempt.

2026-10-04: Fix a crash and oversized panes when the API
sets a layout with a browser session (#783)

Tab.async_update_layout() converted each pane's grid size to
points with the profile font's cell size. A browser
session's grid is measured in points (its cells are 1x1), so
a browser pane came out several times too big, squeezing the
panes beside it. A 1444x1024 browser grid became a pane
about 10,400 x 16,400 points.

A pane that big holds so many one-point cells that
saveToDvr's int byte count overflowed. The negative length
got past DVR's size guard and failed the length assertion in
-[DVREncoder _appendKeyFrame:length:metadata:info:].

Use the session's own cell size for browser sessions, and
compute the DVR frame length in 64 bits, skipping frames too
big for an int.


Claude-Session:
https://claude.ai/code/session_01UZV8mwBt1GHX6NWmWrEH1A

Co-authored-by: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
2026-10-04: Keep the stoplight hotbox from covering
clickable toolbars and status bars. Issue 13100

In compact windows without a top tab bar, hovering the
top-left corner revealed the stoplight hotbox over whatever
was beneath it and swallowed clicks there, so the leftmost
item of a workgroup toolbar or a top status bar could not be
clicked.

When the hotbox overlaps a session toolbar, pane title bar,
or status bar, it now stays collapsed: a sliver of the
hotbox slides in with a chevron toggle, and clicks elsewhere
pass through. Clicking the chevron slides the hotbox fully
in or back out. Without such an overlap the hotbox fades in
on hover as before.

The slide is driven by a single offset property animated
with a cancelable NSAnimation subclass (iTermValueAnimation)
so every moving view starts from where it is and a canceled
slide can't overwrite later state. The hotbox's tracking
area is now created once (it tracks the visible rect) so
sliding doesn't produce spurious mouseEntered: events, and
its mouse state is cleared when it is hidden or changes
windows.

2026-10-04: Keep user vars and shell integration state set
while a tmux pane opens. Issue 13006

Output for a tmux pane is dropped until its session exists,
because its effect on the screen is recovered from
capture-pane. Control sequences that change state only
iTerm2 keeps were lost with it, so a badge using a user
variable stayed empty until the shell's second prompt.

While a pane is being opened (or a just-added window's
layout is pending), its dropped output is parsed and the
last SetUserVar per variable, RemoteHost, CurrentDir, and
ShellIntegrationVersion are kept. Panes in hidden windows
are not scanned. The opener applies captured user vars to
the controller, which saves them in @uservars and overrides
the stale value fetched during the open, and passes the
tokens in the pane state so setTmuxState: executes them once
the cursor is restored.

2026-10-04: Recognize cc-status hook commands that use
$HOME, ~, or a wrapper

Claude Code runs a hook command with /bin/sh -c and never
inspects it, so users write $HOME/.config/iterm2/cc-status
(to share settings.json between Macs) or a wrapper that
exits quietly when cc-status is missing.  The health check
treated the whole command string as a file path, so those
setups got a false "Looks Broken" alert on every launch, and
Reinstall did not recognize them and added a second
cc-status entry to each event (issue 13099).

CCStatusHookCommand splits a command into words the way sh
does and finds references to cc-status: a word whose last
path component is cc-status, or a bare cc-status in command
position. It expands ~, $NAME and ${NAME} from a supplied
environment and never evaluates anything that would run
code; such words, and bare names missing from PATH, are
unresolvable and count as working. Install, uninstall and
the health check all use it.

The health check and the installer get the environment from
one place, a login interactive shell, and only when some
cc-status command needs it, so they agree on which commands
are broken and the common case launches no shell. Reinstall
keeps a working custom command and only replaces a plain
absolute path or one that cannot run. The health check is
skipped when the prompt is silenced, and the alert no longer
claims Claude Code rewrote the file.

2026-10-04: Align the password manager's Working label with
its Cancel button's baseline

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-04: Make Find treat cells the cursor skipped over as
spaces

When a program moves the cursor past a cell (CHA, CUF, CUP)
instead of writing a space, the cell stays nul. It draws as
a blank but the search haystack held U+0000, so multi-word
searches failed across it. Search now converts nuls before a
line's last written cell into spaces, one unichar per cell
so deltas are unchanged. Trailing nuls are untouched.

Fixes #13101

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-10-03: Fall back to a GitHub Pages mirror of the
companion shard map

Some networks can't reach resolver.iterm2.com (Cloudflare),
and since the relay is the only transport, pairing and every
cold reconnect failed with "The request timed out." The
shard map loader now also fetches a mirror published from
the same file to GitHub Pages.

- The phone fetches the mirror alongside the primary. The
Mac only asks the
  mirror when the primary is slow, failing, or out of date,
  to stay clear of GitHub Pages rate limits.
- The primary is authoritative. A mirror map, or a primary
map on a cold
  start, only wins if the loader would adopt it; a stale
  copy counts as a failed attempt instead of ending in "no
  host assigned".
- When no source works, the error names each server and what
went wrong
  with it (DNS failure, no response, HTTP error, captive
  portal, outdated copy), and suggests another network only
  when every failure was in the network. Failures recorded
  before an overall timeout are kept.
- The phone shows the explanation in a Details section on
the pairing
  screen. The Mac shows a short message with the full text
  in a tooltip and the debug log.
- The Mac's plugin HTTP bridge now passes URLError codes
through, so the
  Mac explains failures as specifically as the phone.

2026-10-03: CLAUDE.md: drop the note about the deleted
iTerm2XCTests target

2026-10-03: Make the Bitwarden integration report real
errors and recover from rejected sessions

The Bitwarden adapter hid bw's errors and could prompt or
hang. It now runs bw with --nointeraction and a null stdin,
keeps stdout and stderr apart, drains both while bw runs,
passes the session in BW_SESSION rather than on the command
line, and ignores SIGPIPE. Folder lookup and creation report
bw's message instead of "Failed to create or find folder", a
missing "iTerm2" folder produces an explanatory warning
rather than an empty list, a wrong master password is
reported as such, and login checks that bw accepts the
session it just issued.

In iTerm2, a rejected session now rebuilds the command on
retry instead of rerunning one that still carries the old
token, which also affected 1Password. Rejections are matched
to the session the command was built with (AdapterSession),
so a stale failure can't discard a newer session, and a
session rejected before it ever worked stops with an error
rather than prompting for the master password in a loop. A
wrong master password reopens the prompt with the reason.
Adapter error messages are shown and logged instead of "Not
authenticated."

Adapters find their CLI on the user's PATH before asking,
and the open panel keeps the path the user picked instead of
resolving symlinks, which pinned one installed version and
broke on upgrade.

Debug logs no longer include stdin or stdout of password
manager commands, passwords in request structs, or
environment values.

Issue 13046

2026-10-03: Run password manager adapters with the user's
shell PATH

Adapters were launched with an empty environment, so a CLI
that is a script rather than a native binary could not find
its interpreter.  Homebrew's bw is a Node script with a
"#!/usr/bin/env node" shebang, so the Bitwarden integration
could not run it at all (issue 13046).

AdapterShellEnvironment reads PATH from the user's login,
interactive shell through the pidinfo service, appends the
standard install directories, and caches it in memory. Only
the first adapter command waits, with a timeout after which
it uses the fallback while the fetch continues. Refreshes
happen only when the password manager opens, at most every
ten minutes, and only when an adapter is the chosen source.

To support that, the pidinfo shell runner takes a run mode
(bare, interactive, or login-interactive) instead of a BOOL.
The argument choice lives in iTermShellArguments, which adds
-l only for shells known to accept it, since tcsh, csh and
elvish reject it. Existing callers keep their previous
behavior. The gateway's two shell methods now share one
exactly-once delivery path.

2026-10-03: Make the shell environment reply nullable and
let the composer recover from a failed PATH read

exfiltrateEnvironmentVariableNamed:shell:completion: passed
nil when the shell could not be run despite a nonnull
annotation. The header, implementation and callers now say
nullable.

LocalFileChecker had been bridging that nil to an empty PATH
and marking every command invalid until the session ended.
It now treats a failed read as unknown, retries after a 60
second backoff rather than on every edit, and has its
gateway calls and clock injected so this is unit tested.

2026-10-03: Add backward search tests with the match in the
final cell

A review asked whether a backward search with offset 0 that
starts on the last character of the line buffer reports that
match twice, on the theory that the stop position one cell
past the start does not exist there and
SearchRequest.stopPosition falls back to the start itself.
It does exist: it is the buffer's end position, equal to
lastPosition(), so the fallback is not taken. These two
tests pin that the match is reported once, with and without
a trailing null cell.

2026-10-03: Correct the Semantic History release note

The note said the wrong path was sent for a space unless a
line number was present. That described TextMate and BBEdit;
for MacVim it was the other way around, and the fix also
covers #, ? and literal percent escapes. Describe the
symptom without the per-branch qualifier.

2026-10-03: Send MacVim an escaped file URL and TextMate the
raw path

Commit 07b59768a made Semantic History send every
scheme-based editor the raw path after file://,
percent-encoded once. That is what TextMate wants, but not
MacVim, and the two cannot share a format:

- MacVim (MMAppController parseOpenURL: and
handleGetURLEvent:) decodes
  the url value once and parses the result with
  URLWithString:, so the decoded value must be a well-formed
  file URL with its path escaped.  Its source calls this
  double encoding the canonical form
  (mvim://open?url=file:///foo%2520bar) and names iTerm2 as
  a tool that sometimes sent a single encode. With the raw
  path a literal %41 in a filename was decoded twice, and
  MacVim builds without the compatibility re-encoding also
  broke on space, # and ?.
- TextMate (handleTxMtURL:) decodes once, strips file://,
and uses the
  rest verbatim as the path. An escaped file URL makes it
  look for a file literally named with %20.

So choose the form by editor: MacVim gets
fileURL.absoluteString, TextMate and BBEdit get the raw
path. Both are then percent-encoded once with & = + # and %
escaped, in a single assignment, with or without a line
number. BBEdit is closed source and is reached through
TextMate's scheme, so it keeps TextMate's form; that is
unverified.

The tests now model each editor's decoding and check the
path it would open for a space, &, #, ? and a literal %41.
The five expected-failure tests from the review are replaced
by passing ones.

2026-10-03: Add expected-failure tests for Semantic History
editor URL values

Since 07b59768a the url query value sent to MacVim, TextMate
and BBEdit is the raw path after "file://", percent-encoded
once. An editor that decodes it once and parses the result
as a URL gets a string that is not a well-formed file URL: a
"#" or "?" in the path becomes a fragment or query and
truncates it, a literal "%41" is decoded a second time, and
a space survives only where the URL parser escapes it
itself.

These five tests assert that the once-decoded value parses
to the original path, and for a space that it equals the
escaped file URL.  They fail today and are wrapped in strict
XCTExpectFailure until the format is settled against the
real editors. The space test conflicts on purpose with
testEditorURLWithoutLineNumberEncodesSpaceOnce, which pins
the current raw-path form.

2026-10-03: Correct the all-null selection check's comment
and add resize tests

The comment on coordRangeContainsNonNullCharacter: claimed
it was valid wherever runByTrimmingNullsFromRun: is. That is
not true when the height shrinks: only some of the primary
grid's rows are appended to the line buffer before the check
runs, and getLineAtIndex: then serves a row past them from
the top of the grid, so the check can wrongly report content
for an all-null range below the cursor. It is harmless today
only because the range conversion that follows cannot
convert a range past the appended rows. Say so, so nobody
relies on the check there.

Two tests with text on the top rows and a blank selection
below the cursor: a width-only resize, where the whole grid
is appended and the check is what drops the selection (this
fails if the check is removed), and a one-row height shrink,
where the conversion drops it.

2026-10-03: Release notes for the Semantic History, CSI u,
selection and state-saving fixes

2026-10-03: Drop a selection of only nulls when the screen
is resized

A selection containing nothing but nulls survived a resize
as a phantom sub-selection with no content, so hasSelection
stayed YES and copy, auto-copy and selection-gated UI acted
on an empty selection. Since commit 5e401d357,
-[VT100ScreenMutableState runByTrimmingNullsFromRun:]
(VT100ScreenMutableState+Resizing.m) trims nulls only within
the run's first and last lines, which is deliberate for
notes and marks on blank lines, so an all-null multi-line
selection trimmed to a null cell,
positionRangeForCoordRange: succeeded, and didResizeToSize:
re-added it.

Before converting each sub-selection to the new width, on
both the main and alternate screen paths, skip it when no
cell in its range holds a non-null character. Trimming,
notes and marks are unchanged, and a selection whose only
text is on a middle line still survives.

2026-10-03: Stop a wrapped backward search before the match
it already reported

A backward search with offset 0 that wrapped around reported
the match at the starting coordinate twice.
SearchRequest.stopPosition in SearchEngine.swift used the
initial start as the wrapped pass's stop regardless of the
offset, and -[LineBuffer findSubstring:stopAt:] keeps a
backward match that starts exactly at the stop (it only
drops matches before it, whereas the forward filter is
half-open since commit 44da563ea). That inclusivity is what
lets an offset-1 backward search find the skipped start
match last after wrapping, so the first pass with offset 0
had already reported the match at the start and the wrapped
pass reported it again.

For a backward wrap, place the stop one cell past where the
first pass began (offset 1 - offset from the initial start):
offset 1 still stops on the start itself, offset 0 stops one
cell beyond it. Forward searches and LineBuffer are
unchanged.

2026-10-03: Send the meta byte for Option+key in the termkey
key mapper

With Option configured as Meta and CSI u disambiguation off,
iTermTermkeyKeyMapper dropped Option+ASCII keypresses. In
sources/Keyboard/iTermTermkeyKeyMapper.m,
termkeySequenceForCodePoint:modifiers:keyCode: took the
NSData from dataForOptionModifiedKeypress (which sets the
high bit on the first byte, e.g. 0xE2 for b), decoded it as
an NSString in the session encoding and postCocoaData
re-encoded it. A lone byte >= 0x80 is not valid UTF-8, so
the string was nil and nothing was sent. Present since the
mapper was added in 0ac126e7c.

The fix hands the Option-modified NSData back through an out
parameter so postCocoaData sends the raw bytes, matching
iTermStandardKeyMapper.  The Esc+ and Option-normal cases
produce the same bytes as before, and the pre-Cocoa string
path, CSI u disambiguation and every other path are
unchanged. The regression test in TermkeyKeyMapperTests no
longer expects failure.

2026-10-03: Pad screen char arrays with null cells instead
of the EOL code

-[ScreenCharArray paddedToLength:eligibleForDWC:]
(ScreenCharArray.m) copied the whole continuation cell into
the padding since commit ca0dd907f, which extended the
continuation so background colors draw correctly from
scrollback. The continuation's code is the EOL type, not a
character, so a soft-wrapped line was padded with code
EOL_SOFT, which equals DWC_SKIP. The later check that
upgrades the last history line to DWC_SKIP plus EOL_DWC when
the grid starts with a double-width character (commit
c54cccb04) tests for a null last cell and therefore never
fired.

Copy the continuation's colors and attributes into the
padding with a zero code so the padding is genuinely empty
and the EOL_DWC upgrade works again.

2026-10-03: Encode editor URL query values exactly once in
Semantic History

Semantic History builds mvim://, txmt:// and x-bbedit://
URLs in iTermSemanticHistoryController.m, in
-openFile:inEditorWithBundleId:lineNumber:columnNumber:
(introduced by commit 8d5f938e1). Without a line number it
percent-encoded fileURL.absoluteString, which already
contains escapes, so a space in the path became %2520 and
TextMate looked for a file that does not exist. With a line
number it assigned decoded queryItems back to
percentEncodedQueryItems, so the two branches disagreed.
Both used URLQueryAllowedCharacterSet, which leaves & and =
raw, so a path containing an ampersand truncated the url
query value.

Now both branches build the raw file URL (file:// plus the
unescaped path), encode it exactly once with a character set
that also escapes & = + # and %, and assign the items to
percentEncodedQueryItems together. MacVim, TextMate and
BBEdit each decode the value once, so the path round-trips.
The tests read the query items through URLComponents and no
longer expect failure; the legacy no-line expectation now
has literal slashes, which decode to the same value.

2026-10-03: Skip the merged DWC_RIGHT when the cursor sits
on the predecessor's spacer

Appending text with the cursor on the DWC_RIGHT half of the
previous character wrote the merged buffer's spacer at the
cursor, producing [null][DWC_RIGHT][text][DWC_RIGHT] with
the cursor two columns too far.  This is a regression of
issue 9852: commit 4c8d54a8d rewrote the predecessor merge
in appendStringAtCursorSlowly: (VT100ScreenMutableState.m)
to consult only predecessorIsDoubleWidth, which
coordinateBefore:movedBackOverDoubleWidth: leaves NO when
stepping back from the spacer itself, and dropped the
augmentedResultBeginsWithDoubleWidthCharacter check from the
original fix (commit f0ed6987f).

Also skip the spacer when the predecessor adjoins the cursor
and the grid cell under the cursor is already a DWC_RIGHT.
The appended text then overwrites the spacer, which erases
the orphaned left half as usual, and the character after it
is preserved.

2026-10-03: Mark the optional iTermPythonArgumentParser
properties nullable

script, module, statement and their escaped variants were
declared inside NS_ASSUME_NONNULL but are nil unless the
matching argument was parsed. Swift callers therefore saw an
empty string instead of nil and could not tell an absent
argument from an empty one. Annotate them nullable to match
the implementation; the regression tests check that Swift
now sees nil.

2026-10-03: Actually enable WAL mode in the graph database

Commit fc83f8a5c changed the journal_mode=WAL and
wal_checkpoint pragmas in iTermGraphDatabase from
executeUpdate to executeQuery so the result set could be
closed cleanly, but it never stepped the result set. FMDB's
executeQuery only prepares the statement; sqlite3_step
happens in FMResultSet next. So the pragma never ran, every
database created or recovered since then stayed in
rollback-journal mode, and the synchronous=NORMAL setting
added later assumed a WAL that was not there.

Step the result set before closing it in both places. The
regression test asserts a fresh database reports
journal_mode wal.

2026-10-03: Port the legacy iTerm2XCTests to ModernTests in
Swift and delete the old targets

The iTerm2Tests app target and the iTerm2XCTests bundle had
been broken since the VT100Screen mutable-state refactor;
eight of their files were wrapped in #if 0 and the rest had
not been run in years. Every test that still makes sense now
lives in ModernTests as Swift XCTest cases (1,107 new test
methods across 39 files), with multi-scenario legacy methods
split into one test per behavior.

Dropped on purpose: the PTYTextView golden-image drawing
tests and the 299 PNGs under tests/Goldens, the Toolbelt
tests that launch real windows, and a handful of tests whose
subject no longer exists or is unreachable from Swift.

Tests that exposed real production bugs are kept as strict
XCTExpectFailure cases so the suite stays green and flags
itself when the bug is fixed: the graph database WAL pragma
that is never stepped, the termkey mapper dropping
Option-as-Meta ASCII keys, scheme-editor URL encoding in
semantic history, and the nonnull-but-nil Python argument
parser properties.

Supporting changes: - Expose the headers the ports need
through the bridging header, with
  nullability annotations on two headers so it compiles.
- Look up the shell history Core Data model by the base
class so a test
  subclass in another bundle can construct the controller.
- Move iTermSelectorSwizzler into sources/Infrastructure
since
  production code still uses it.
- Remove both targets, the iTerm2Tests scheme, and their
plist, prefix
  header and localized resources from the project.
